# The broker's CRM: how it is being built

The spec is `docs/BROKER_CRM_STORIES.md` (stories `CRM-…`), `docs/QUOTING_PLATFORM_STORIES.md` (stories `K-` to
`R-`) and `docs/BROKER_CRM_PRD.md`. This page holds the founder's decisions of 2026-11-13, the epics, and the
rules every story's build follows. Where this page and a story disagree, this page wins.

## Decisions (founder, 2026-11-13)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1 | Build every `CRM-` story, and the `K-` to `R-` stories they need: firms and memberships, a broker starting an intake for a client, the employer's PEO authorization, sending from the product, reading replies and quote documents, the comparison |
| D-2 | One pull request per epic. Each story is its own pull request into its epic's branch |
| D-3 | Epics are stacked: each epic's branch starts from the epic before it and its pull request targets that branch. Epic 1 starts from `claude/keen-brahmagupta-n7raz0` |
| D-4 | A story's pull request is merged into its epic branch by the orchestrator once its gate passes and its review is Ready. Epic pull requests are merged only by the founder |
| D-5 | Words: **firm** and **agent** in code and on screens; lead, account, contact, opportunity, quote. Roles: `owner`, `lead`, `agent` |
| D-6 | `firms` has a `parent_firm_id` column. No rule, policy, function or screen reads it yet |
| D-7 | The workspace (leads, accounts, quotes, inbox, reports, settings) uses the grey-and-blue look of `docs/prd-screens/`. The employer's intake keeps today's look |
| D-8 | Today's advisor console pages keep working, scoped to the firm, until the screen that replaces each ships. A page is removed only in the story that ships its replacement |
| D-9 | Reading a PEO's reply or quote document only suggests. Nothing is saved until an agent confirms |
| D-10 | An opportunity's stage moves by itself: the first send moves it to In market, the first recorded quote to Quoted. This changes D44 |
| D-11 | A second sign-in factor is required for password accounts only |
| D-12 | Sending to PEOs and the employer's PEO authorization are built switched on, with draft wording. All legal wording lives in one module so counsel's text is one change, and every pull request that shows it says so |
| D-13 | Tests are written at four seams and nowhere else: (1) database rules and functions, through SQL tests in `supabase/tests`; (2) pages and forms, through component tests of what a person sees and does; (3) a few end-to-end browser flows in `e2e/`; (4) server functions, through their request and response with the mail provider and the model faked |
| D-14 | Sonnet builds every story. Opus does every review. From Epic 2 on, see D-53 |
| D-25 | Stories in an epic are built at the same time wherever one does not need another. Up to three run side by side, each in its own working copy with its own test database; a story starts as soon as the stories it needs have merged |

## Epic 1 decisions (2026-11-13)

The founder said to take the recommended answer on each decision a scout finds before an epic, and keep going.
These came from reading Epic 1's stories against the code.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-15 | In Edgar Allan Advisory, today's admins become owners and today's advisors become agents, and that firm is set to "everyone sees everything", so nobody loses sight of a submission. Submissions get no owner in Epic 1 |
| D-16 | A platform `admin` is not a member of any firm and stops reading firms' intakes. Assigning held mail shows only the File ID and the PEO |
| D-17 | Firm addresses (subdomains) are built in Epic 1. The code reads the host; the DNS, hosting and sign-in provider settings it needs are listed in the pull request for the founder to apply |
| D-18 | The database checks that the caller is a member of the record's firm. The address only chooses what the screen shows; it is never the lock |
| D-19 | A second factor is asked for when this sign-in used a password, not whenever the account has one |
| D-20 | The teams table and the visibility rules are built in Epic 1. No screen shows a team field until CRM-X1.1 |
| D-21 | Q-1.2 is built as the admin's approval of a firm only. No licence check, since nothing records a producer number yet |
| D-22 | CRM-P2.5 (the employer ends a firm's access) moves to Epic 5, after firm links exist |
| D-23 | CRM-P1.4 replaces the admin's organisation list: `/advisor/organisations` and `claim_advisor_role_by_organisation` are retired in that story |
| D-24 | An acceptance criterion about something a later epic creates (exports, send buttons, the Settings page, tables for leads, accounts, opportunities and quotes) is met in the story that creates that thing. The Epic 1 story builds the mechanism and applies it to what exists, and its pull request lists what was deferred and to which story |
| D-26 | A firm address is refused as a PEO's only when, with everything but letters and digits removed and in lower case, it is exactly a listed PEO's name or short name. No partial, prefix or contains matching: three review rounds showed any fuzzier rule both lets real look-alikes through and refuses innocent names. Platform admins approve each firm before it can send (Q-1.2), which is where a look-alike is caught |
| D-27 | What a firm address shows. **A firm that exists** (approved or still waiting for approval) shows its name and its sign-in page, because a waiting firm's members can work before approval; approval gates sending, not signing in. **An address no firm holds** shows a plain not-found page to everyone: no sign-in is offered there and the sign-in callback pages are not mounted; if a session somehow exists at that address, the not-found page carries a sign-out. A signed-in person who is not a member of an existing firm's address sees "You aren't a member of this firm" with a sign-out. Signing out on a firm address ends that address's session only; signing out on the bare domain keeps today's behaviour (every session of that person). When firms can be suspended or closed (CRM-P1.8, CRM-P1.7), a suspended or closed firm's address looks the same as one no firm holds |
| D-28 | The check that a table holding firm data has a firm rule is by behaviour, never by reading the policy's text. Every public table is listed in the isolation tests with what it holds (a firm's data, platform-wide data, a person's own data). Every table listed as a firm's data must have a two-firm probe that inserts a row for each firm and proves a member of the other firm reads, counts, changes and deletes nothing; a firm table with no probe, or a table not listed, fails the build. A text check can be fooled by a policy that mentions a firm helper without tying it to the row being read |
| D-29 | A rule of the form "at most one of these per person or per firm" is enforced by a database constraint (a unique or partial unique index), not only by a lock inside a function. A constraint is tested in one session by trying the second insert and seeing it refused; a lock on its own cannot be tested that way and reviews kept finding it unprotected. A lock may stay beside the constraint only to give a friendlier message, and then the constraint is what the test proves. First use: one firm waiting for approval per person who signed up (CRM-P1.1) |
| D-30 | A person has at most one firm waiting for approval at a time. Signing up another is refused ("You already have a firm waiting for approval") until the first is decided, so nobody holds addresses they have no use for or fills the admin's list. A broker who runs two firms signs the second up after the first is decided |
| D-31 | A refused firm behaves, at its address, like an address no firm holds: no name, no sign-in, and its founder is not let in (D-27). The address stays taken by the refused firm, with no end date, until a later story decides otherwise: a refused founder signs up again at a different address |
| D-32 | A lock that guards against two things happening at the same moment stays only if a test goes red when it is removed. Where the rule can be a constraint, D-29 applies. Where it cannot, and the clash it guards against cannot happen through anything a person or job can do today, the lock is removed and the story that first makes the clash possible adds it back with its test. Where the clash can happen today, it is tested with two database sessions; a lock no test protects is not merged. First use: the lock on an intake's firm while a funnel step is written (CRM-P2.1b) |
| D-33 | A firm never sees who at Nevermore acted on it. When a platform admin who is not a current member of the firm does something recorded in that firm's activity (approving or refusing it, later support access), the firm's owners see the actor as "Nevermore": no name, no email, no user id, in the list, in the person filter and in anything the reading functions return. The real actor is still stored, for the platform's own record only. A member of the firm who also holds a platform role and acts as a member is shown as themselves. Reason: every outside party who signs up a firm would otherwise learn staff sign-in emails |
| D-34 | Who can invite, by the firm's state. A firm still waiting for approval can invite and its invitations can be accepted (its people can work before approval, D-27), but it may hold at most five pending invitations until it is approved, so an unapproved sign-up cannot use Nevermore's mail to send in bulk. A refused firm cannot invite or resend, and a pending invitation to a refused firm cannot be accepted (D-31). An invitation link is accepted only at the address of the firm it belongs to; opened at another firm's address it is refused and joins nothing. The secret in an invitation link travels in the part of the address after `#`, which browsers do not send to servers, and the page removes it from the address bar as soon as it has read it |
| D-35 | Who receives a firm's alert when a send is retried. The recipients of an alert are fixed when it is first prepared: the firm's current members at that moment. On a retry, anyone who has since left the firm is dropped (a former member is never sent a firm's alert), and the message is rebuilt only for that reason. Someone who joined between the first attempt and the retry does not receive that one alert; they receive every alert prepared after they joined. Reason: rebuilding a message to add a new member changes its send key, so people who already received it would receive it twice |
| D-36 | How much invitation mail a firm waiting for approval can send. On top of D-34's five pending invitations, a waiting firm may send at most twenty invitation emails (first sends and resends together) in any twenty-four hours; the next is refused with a plain message saying when it can send again. There is no lifetime limit: a waiting firm whose invitations were accepted can invite more. Both limits end when the firm is approved. Reason: without a rate limit a sign-up nobody has approved could withdraw and re-invite in a loop to send mail in bulk; a lifetime limit would strand a real firm that is simply waiting |
| D-101 | (Epic 1 decisions continue from D-101; D-37 to D-99 belong to Epic 2.) Referral links and which firm an intake belongs to. Today every intake a prospect starts belongs to the default firm (Edgar Allan Advisory), whatever address or link they came by; starting an intake for another firm arrives with New quote (Epic 5), which must take the firm from the address on the server, never from the browser's word alone. Until then a referral partner's link is offered only for the default firm. For any other firm the Referral Partners page keeps the partner and its code but shows no link, and says links come when the firm can take intakes. A code is matched to a partner only within the intake's own firm, so another firm's code used on the bare domain attributes nothing |
| D-102 | Adds one case to D-35. When a firm's alert is retried and NONE of the people it was first prepared for is still a current member, the alert goes to the firm's current members, rebuilt with a new send key, so that it is not lost. In every other case D-35 stands unchanged: recipients are fixed when the alert is first prepared, leavers are dropped, and someone who joined later does not receive that alert. D-35's own text is not rewritten; a change to a decision is a new decision that names the one it changes |

### Epic 1 build order

P-1.1 says roles are owner and member; D-5 wins: owner, lead, agent. P-1.2 and P-1.3 are replaced by CRM-P1.2,
CRM-P1.5 and CRM-P1.1.

| # | Story | What it covers | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | P-1.1 | Firms, teams, dated memberships, the parent column, a firm on every intake; Edgar Allan Advisory seeded with its members and every existing submission | nothing |
| 2 | CRM-P2.1a (with CRM-S5.1) | The visibility helper; every table policy and storage policy scoped to the firm; two firms, two agents and a lead in the SQL tests; the check that fails on a table with no firm rule | 1 |
| 3 | CRM-P2.1b | Every advisor and admin function that reads or changes firm data filters by the caller's firm | 2 |
| 4 | CRM-P2.1c | What has no submission behind it: referral partners, the advisor alert's recipients, the new-mail mark, mailboxes, held mail (D-16) | 2 |
| 5 | P-1.4 | The existing console pages show only the firm's records; the front end asks "is a member", not "is an advisor" | 3 |
| 6 | CRM-P1.3a | The firm address: the host chooses the firm shown; "You aren't a member of this firm"; a session per address | 2 |
| 7 | CRM-P1.3b | A second factor for password sign-ins, including the test gateway's password sign-in | 2 |
| 8 | CRM-P2.3 with Q-3.1 | The record of who did what, read by owners; export alerts when exports exist | 2 |
| 9 | CRM-P1.2 | Invite a member | 2 |
| 10 | CRM-P1.5 | Remove or suspend a member | 4, 8, 9 |
| 11 | CRM-P1.6 | Leave a firm | 10 |
| 12 | CRM-P1.4 | Join by organisation (D-23) | 9 |
| 13 | CRM-P1.1 with Q-1.2 | Sign up a firm; an admin approves it | 6 |
| 14 | CRM-P1.8 | Suspend a firm | 13 |
| 15 | CRM-P1.7 | Close a firm | 8, 14 |
| 16 | CRM-P2.2 | Files, tokens and reply addresses | 3, 4 |
| 17 | CRM-P2.4 | Help from Nevermore, logged | 3, 8 |

New migration files are named to sort after `20261115140000`.

### The three lines of work, brought together (2026-11-14)

Epic 1's late stories (CRM-P1.5, CRM-P2.2 and their fixes) were merged into `crm/epic-01-firms-security` only, CRM-P1.8 and
CRM-S1.1b went to `main`, and Epic 2's review fixes stayed on `crm/epic-02-shell-tables`. All three are merged into one line
on `claude/friendly-feynman-wlpj9j`, and every remaining story is built there, one after another, in the build order below.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-103 | Where two lines disagreed about a member of several firms, one firm at a time wins (D-40, ADR 0016): the Members page, like every member page, lets them in at a firm's address and sends them to "Choose a firm" on the bare domain. The Members page sits inside the workspace shell and is linked from Settings, for owners |
| D-104 | Six database objects were each rewritten by two lines that did not see each other, so the later file undid the earlier one's rule (a suspended firm's members were let back in; suspending a member failed). `20261117160000_epics_reconciled.sql` states each once with every rule. The two migrations that restate the list of activity kinds (`20261116220000`, `20261116230000`) were edited to accept each other's kinds: without that, whichever is applied second is refused on a database that already holds the other's entries. This is the one edit to an old migration, and it changes nothing on a database where both are already applied |
| D-105 | A story's SQL test loads every migration, not only those up to its own, whenever a later migration restates something the story made. Migrations for the stories still to build are named to sort after `20261117160000`, in the order they are built; the timestamps in the Epic 2 table below are replaced by that rule |
| D-106 | An outbox row belongs to an intake or, for a firm-level notice such as "a member left" (CRM-P1.6), to a firm and no intake. Firm-level rows are not shown on the firm's Outbox page (that page lists events through their intakes), are withheld while the firm is suspended, and are deleted 90 days after they were queued, by the same daily job that prunes the firm's record (`prune_firm_activity`, then `prune_firm_outbox_events`), with the same window |
| D-107 | A suspended member, and any member of a suspended firm, cannot leave a firm: they are not current members, so there is nothing for them to end. An owner removes a suspended member (CRM-P1.5) |
| D-108 | One organisation (a Google domain or a Microsoft tenant) may be listed by several firms. A listing grants nothing by itself: an owner of the firm approves each person who asks to join from it (CRM-P1.4) |
| D-109 | A declined request, and an ended membership of that firm (removed or left), are final for the automatic ask: signing in again lists the person for no one, and an owner brings them back only by invitation. Removing a listing leaves the requests already open, which an owner can still approve or decline (CRM-P1.4) |
| D-110 | A platform admin never asks to join a firm: they join only by an owner's invitation, so a firm that lists Nevermore's domain learns no staff email (D-33). A sign-in made with a password is vouched for by no provider, even if the account once linked Google or Microsoft. The token does not say which provider a provider sign-in used, so an account with both is matched through either (CRM-P1.4) |
| D-111 | The old platform-wide organisation list was not copied to any firm: its two tables are kept read-only as a record, and people who got the advisor role through it keep the role (CRM-P1.4, D-23) |
| D-112 | Owners are not emailed when someone asks to join: they see the request on the Invitations page. "Approved with a role and a team" is built as a role only; the team waits for CRM-X1.1 (D-20) (CRM-P1.4) |
| D-113 | A firm waiting for approval can ask to close, since it is theirs. A refused firm and a suspended firm can't (CRM-P1.7) |
| D-114 | A suspended firm is not closed: the daily job and `close_firm_now` leave a due request open while the firm is suspended, and the firm stays as the suspension leaves it. Lifting a suspension of a firm with an open request moves its closing date to the later of what it was and seven days from the lift, so its owners have time to withdraw, and the banner shows the new date. A suspended firm's owners still can't withdraw (they get nothing while suspended). A platform admin can stop a closing with a reason (`admin_stop_firm_closure`: recorded as Nevermore's under D-33, owners emailed, shown on the Firms page) but can neither ask to close a firm nor withdraw for its owners (CRM-P1.7) |
| D-115 | A closing firm is read-only by a guard on every write path (`assert_firm_writable`), not by changing `firm_role`, because its members still read. The guard refuses members of that firm only, so nobody learns a firm is closing. The paths are functions, a trigger (a note on an intake) and the two manuscript storage policies; a standing test lists every write function and policy and fails on one that is neither guarded nor exempt with a reason. Each guarded write holds the firm's row `FOR KEY SHARE` (D-122). Opening a quote request's thread stays allowed: it only marks it read (ADR 0023) |
| D-116 | After closing, the address looks like one no firm holds (D-27). The firm's row stays; its address is held 12 months from the closing day and released only when a new firm asks for it, by renaming the old row to `x.<id>` (accepted by the table's check, refused by `sign_up_firm`, so no sign-up can ever take it). Who asked, withdrew or stopped is kept as bare ids with no foreign key, so deleting an account neither erases nor blocks it |
| D-117 | Closing deletes by one list (`firm_closing_plan`), checked against the isolation registry in `data_isolation.sql`: a story that adds a table that holds a firm's data adds a line. Teams are deleted after the team is cleared from the ended memberships, under a narrow exception in the membership history trigger that exists only while the closing runs |
| D-118 | Alerts continue while a firm is closing (its members still read). At closing the firm's waiting alerts and emails are ended as cancelled ("the firm closed"), and any queued for it afterwards are ended as they are queued. The emails to owners that a closing was asked, withdrawn or stopped are one outbox kind, `firm_closing`. A firm that fails to close is an ops event (code `firm_close_failed`, the firm's id in the id column) as well as a warning |
| D-119 | Mail for a closed firm's reply addresses is still received and threaded onto the intake, which stays with the employer. Nobody can read it, and held mail can't be assigned onto a closed firm |
| D-120 | Between the 30 days ending and the next daily run the firm stays read-only and the request can no longer be withdrawn |
| D-121 | The default firm (Edgar Allan Advisory) can't be closed: it is refused when asked and by the closing itself, and Settings doesn't offer it. Every intake is stamped with it (D-101), so closing it would send every later intake to a firm nobody reads. This holds until an intake can belong to another firm: New quote (Epic 5) must revisit it |
| D-122 | The firm's row is the one lock that orders closing, suspending, lifting, deciding, withdrawing, stopping, asking and members' writes: guarded writes take it `FOR KEY SHARE` (inside `assert_firm_writable` and the manuscript storage helper), everything that decides the firm's state takes it `FOR UPDATE` first and checks after. A key share conflicts only with `FOR UPDATE`, so ordinary writes never wait for or deadlock each other. Each use has a two-session test (`close_a_firm_races.sql`, `close_a_firm.sql`) that fails without it. The one known limit: file bytes written to the object store outside the database transaction (ADR 0023) |
| D-123 | Support (CRM-P2.4) reads at owner level: what any member reads, plus the members and the firm's record, and the records an owner sees (`can_see_firm_record`). It does not read invitations, requests to join or listed organisations (people who are not members yet). It opens no file: no stored file opens (the database), and no download control is offered in the firm's workspace, one plain line says so (the screen). The screen part is a screen rule: a person with the rows on screen can still copy them |
| D-124 | The story asked for the firm to see "the admin's name". D-33 wins: each use is in the firm's record as "Nevermore", with the reason and the times, and never who. The admin's id and email are kept in the platform's table only |
| D-125 | A suspended firm can be looked at (that is when it needs looking at). A refused or closed firm cannot be started, and closing or refusing a firm ends an open session |
| D-126 | The hour is the server's clock, is exactly one hour and cannot be extended (start another). Its end is recorded when it is next seen (starting another, ending, the Firms page, the firm's address) or by the daily job; what stops it is the clock, so one nobody looked at stops all the same |
| D-127 | Becoming a member ends the session: accepting an invitation to the firm, or an owner approving the admin's request to join, ends that person's open session for that firm in the same transaction, recorded as ended ("became a member"). Starting one on a firm they belong to is refused |
| D-128 | An export function must refuse a support session. A test calls every function the API roles can run with "export" anywhere in its name, as a support session at the firm's address, and fails on one that does not raise PT423 (CRM-S3.8b's function must call `refuse_support_session(firm)` first). `refuse_support_session(NULL)` refuses a caller with any open session, so passing NULL fails closed |
| D-129 | How a record's history is built (CRM-S5.2, D-45). One generic table (`record_history`) and one trigger function (`record_history_capture`) whose arguments are the table's short name (a fixed list, the domain `record_history_table`), the key column(s) within the firm, and the fields to track, so the list is written in the migration that attaches it and nothing else can enter. Creation is one entry (kind `created`), each changed field one entry (kind `changed`), the fields of one save sharing a `change_id`. Values are jsonb: text, number and boolean as themselves, a date as ISO text, no value as SQL null (never the word "null"). A record's key within its firm is stored as text (a partner's code; a uuid or number later; a composite key as a compact JSON array of its parts, so no two keys can be confused (`["a/b","c"]`); a timestamp in UTC; a JSON null in a jsonb column and no value are the same to the history), and the trigger refuses a change of a tracked record's firm or key so no history is orphaned. History starts when it shipped: nothing is backfilled |
| D-130 | Nobody edits a history. The table has no grant or policy for a browser's role; the service role may read it and nothing else. A trigger refuses every UPDATE, every TRUNCATE and every DELETE except one made by the role `record_history_remover`, which has no login, belongs to no API role, holds only read and delete on this table and owns the one function that deletes, `record_history_remove`: the guard reads who is running the statement, never a setting (ADR 0012; the first build used a session setting, which the table's owner could set). The function deletes a record's history only once the record is gone (D-45; the capture trigger calls it after the record's own deletion) and a whole firm's only while that firm's closing is running (`close_firm_now` calls it for the plan's `record_history` line, ord 55, before the partners at 60, D-117). Only a superuser who becomes the role can still delete, as for the firm's record. Referral partners cannot be deleted through the product today, so the record's-own-deletion path is tested with the table's own DELETE. `record_history_track(table, name, key columns, fields)` attaches the capture trigger and a guard against emptying the table together, and refuses at attach time a table, key or field that does not exist |
| D-131 | Who is recorded, and shown. The actor is `auth.uid()` (null for the service role: shown as "System"). The staff stamp is decided by one helper, `firm_actor_is_staff`, moved out of `firm_activity_insert` unchanged and used by both, so the firm's record and a record's history cannot disagree about "acted as a member or as Nevermore". Stamped when written; a staff entry is read as "Nevermore", with no id and no email, and the reading function returns no id at all and, for staff, no email: `who` is a member's email, "Nevermore" or "System" (D-33) |
| D-132 | Reading a history. `record_history_list(firm, table, key, limit, after_at, after_key)` takes the firm because a record's key is only unique within its firm (D-18, D-40). It refuses a table not on the list (22023) and gives the one refusal `PT404 "That record isn't available"` for a record of another firm, a made-up one, a caller who is no member (former, suspended, wrong address, no second factor, platform admin with no session) and a missing key. Who may see a record is one internal function per table (`record_history_can_see`); for a referral partner it is any current member (`is_firm_member`, which a support session also satisfies, reads only). Paged by the time and random key of the last entry seen, 1 to 100 a page, newest first |
| D-133 | The unit of the menu's two counts (CRM-S1.1c). D-39 says "quote requests" for both; the Inbox count is instead the number of intakes that have at least one quote request with new PEO mail, because Inbox opens the queue filtered to "has new PEO mail" (D-38) and the queue lists intakes: a number that counted threads would contradict the rows it opens (an intake with two unread threads is one row). Quotes stays quote requests with status `sent` (an intake sent to three PEOs counts three), which the queue's "waiting on a PEO" filter shows as fewer rows; its page, the whole queue, shows no filter to contradict. The label says what it counts: "open", "with new mail". Until the queue page applies the Inbox link's `mail=new` (CRM-S3.5a moves it onto the table frame, CRM-S3.3a adds the filter), Inbox opens the whole queue, so its count is smaller than the rows shown; the page says so in one line ("Showing every submission. The ones with new PEO mail are marked “New PEO mail”.") shown only when the address carries `mail=new`, and CRM-S3.3a removes the line when it applies the filter. Both counts are of the intakes the queue shows: the queue's test for hiding an empty guest draft is one function (`queue_hides_empty_guest_draft`) used by `advisor_list_submissions` and `menu_counts` Note, 2026-11-14 (CRM-S3.5a): the queue now applies `mail=new` as the "has new PEO mail" filter, so the Inbox count and the rows it opens agree and the one-line notice is gone (D-136). The rest of D-133 stands. |
| D-134 | How the counts stay honest (CRM-S1.1c). One query, `menu_counts()`, returns a jsonb object so a later story adds a key (leads, others) and no caller changes; a caller with no firm (a platform admin with no support session, an advisor role with no membership) gets zeros in the same shape, and a caller the console refuses is refused (42501) as the queue refuses them. "New PEO mail" is defined once, as the relation `quote_requests_with_new_peo_mail()` (a SQL function with no SET clause that returns rows, so Postgres folds it into the calling query; the first build made it a scalar function of one quote request, which cannot be folded, 1.3 s against 0.38 s for the old inline test over 240,000 requests, 0.26 s as the relation), used by both `advisor_list_submissions` (the queue's mark) and `menu_counts`. The browser refreshes on focus and every 60 seconds, keeps the old numbers while the next arrive and does not retry within a refresh (the next one is the retry); a failed refresh shows nothing; the numbers are dropped only once refreshes have been failing for five minutes (counted from the first failure after the last good answer), so a stale number is never shown as fresh for ever, and a tab that merely sat in the background keeps its numbers until the refresh on return answers. The badge caps at 99 ("99+"); zero shows none. The page that marks a thread read, and the calls that change a quote request or the status (a send recorded, a quote answered, Won or Lost), invalidate the query so the menu follows at once. Cost, measured on 60,000 intakes, 240,000 quote requests and 360,000 messages over 21 firms: the count reads one firm's intakes through `chamber_sessions_by_firm` (the new index on `chamber_sessions (firm_id)`, which the plan uses: without it every firm's sessions were read) and its quote requests through `quote_requests_one_per_peo`, the guest flag by key, and each request's mail through `quote_request_messages_by_request` and the thread-read key; so the cost is linear in the one firm's quote requests, not the whole table's (about 0.16 to 0.2 s for a firm with 3,000 intakes and 12,000 requests). No index on messages was added |
| D-135 | The table address (CRM-S3.5a, D-43). One codec, `src/components/workspace/table/address.ts`, reads and writes a table's whole state to the page's search parameters, for every table: `page`, `sort`, `filter` (repeated), `view`, `q`. It is total: for any address and any typed text it returns an address and never throws (text is counted in characters, as the database counts them, and never cut through one; half a character becomes U+FFFD). What it can't read is dropped (an unknown field or operator, a value of the wrong shape or one the database would refuse for its shape such as blank text, a number that isn't one, a short search, a view the table doesn't know), and the address is rewritten cleanly in place. Filters are written in one canonical order, so the same filters are one address, one key and one bookmark. It never drops what only the database can judge (an unlisted choice value, a filter on a person): the database refuses it with a reason and the page shows that reason (D-138). The parameters and encodings are in "The table address" below |
| D-136 | Inbox is the filter, not a mode (CRM-S3.5a, changes D-133's last two sentences). The queue reads `mail=new` as the filter "has new PEO mail is yes" and rewrites the address to `filter=has_new_peo_mail:is:true`. The menu's Inbox link keeps `mail=new` and marks Inbox for either spelling; Quotes is marked otherwise. The interim line ("Showing every submission…") and its two tests are gone, and the Inbox badge and the rows it opens are the one rule (proved in the page's test with the filter sent, and in `e2e/inbox-and-pages.spec.ts`) |
| D-137 | The queue's status tabs are its built-in views, and the view's id is the stage's own name (`view=won`). The tabs are the frame's `ViewTabs`, drawn from the definition's views. A tab's number is what its rows would total under the search and filters on screen, from `queue_view_counts`, not the whole firm's count of that stage, so a number and the rows it opens always agree. A failed refresh of the numbers keeps the last ones and shows no error, and they are dropped only once refreshes have been failing for five minutes: the rule of the menu's counts (D-134), the same code (`src/lib/failureClock.ts`). CRM-S3.4a extends `ViewTabs` with saving, renaming and sharing and decides whether a tab counts under the filters |
| D-138 | What a table shows when the database refuses the query. The frame, not the page, recognises it (`refusedQueryReason`): code 22023 (a filter, sort, page, view or time zone it doesn't take) and 0A000 (a filter on a person, until records have an owner, CRM-A1.1). It shows the database's own reason plainly, and a link that clears the filters and the view; never "Try again", which cannot work for the same address. The reasons are safe to show as they are: every `RAISE` in `workspace_filter_check`, `queue_filters_checked`, the queue's sort, page and view functions is a plain sentence naming a field, an operator or a limit and echoing at most 64 characters of what was sent, and none holds anything of the firm's data. Every other failed load is the page's `error` slot, which CRM-S2.3 filled with `LoadFailed` (D-175). The panel's heading says what was refused when the reason lets you tell (a sort, a page, filters) and is neutral otherwise ("This view of the submissions can't be shown."); the way back is a link to the table's bare address (no sort, filters, view, search or page), so a refused sort is not kept by the way out |
| D-139 | How a table pages. 50 a page, from the database; the range ("1 to 50 of 375 submissions", "0 of 0 submissions", and "…matching “pat”" for the search the rows were found for) is one polite live region that changes only when the new rows arrive and not when a refresh brings nothing new; Previous and Next are real links (a new tab and Back work) that step from the page the address asks for, and are plain text with `aria-disabled` at the ends; the rows stay on screen, dimmed and marked busy, while the next page, filter or refresh loads; a page past the end is corrected to the last page by rewriting the address (to the first when nothing matches); a page above the database's own limit (100000) is not a place and reads as page 1. Any change to the address that holds more than the page goes back to page 1, in the writer, so no control can forget. Nothing is asked until the workspace knows its firm: the key holds the firm and the hook waits for it (`useFirmReady`), so a load asks once, not once for no firm and again for the firm The rows are keyed on the effective query (the view's filters and the address's, the sort, page and search), and each tab's count on that view's effective filters, so a saved view edited under the same id is asked again at once. Tab counts go to the database in calls of at most 20 views (the limit of `queue_view_counts`); a failed call loses only its own views' numbers, which keep their last value through a failure and go after five minutes of failing |
| D-140 | The queue page's own controls until the stories that replace them. The two "only" checkboxes (needs advisor action, waiting on a PEO) and the Sort box stay, writing the address through the codec: the checkboxes write the filters, the Sort box one of three sorts (newest first, longest wait on a PEO, quote expiring soonest). CRM-S3.2a (sort headers) removed the Sort box (D-147 to D-150), CRM-S3.3a (the filter row) the checkboxes. Refresh asks again for everything the page shows (the rows, the tab numbers, the bounced-email marks). The queue now pages 50, not 25, as D-44 says. `listSubmissions` and its tests are gone (`advisor_list_submissions` stays in the database, which its own SQL test and the first migration still use) |
| D-141 | A table's views and fields can be "not known yet" (CRM-S3.5a, for CRM-S3.4a's saved views and CRM-X3.1's custom fields). A definition says so with `viewsStatus` and `fieldsStatus`: `ready` (the default, and always for what is in code), `loading` or `failed`; a saved view is added to `views`, a custom field to `fields`, when it arrives. While a part is `loading` the codec keeps what it can't judge (a view id the table doesn't list, a filter or sort on a field it doesn't list) exactly as written in the address's `held` part, the address is not rewritten for it, and the table asks for no rows and no counts: the frame shows its loading slot, so the page never loads the wrong rows first. Once `ready`, what is still unknown is dropped and the address rewritten once. If it is `failed`, the table falls back to what it has, drops what it can't judge, and says so in one line ("That view isn't available, so this shows every submission."; "A filter on a field that isn't available was left out.") until the next change of the address. The queue's own definition is in code and always ready; its `useQueueTable()` is where CRM-S3.4a adds the saved views Lines the table says (one plain line each, gone with the next change of the address): a view the table does not list once its views are ready or failed ("That view isn't available, so this shows all statuses.", naming the table's own "all" tab), and a filter on a field it does not list once fields are ready or failed ("A filter on a field that isn't available was left out."). A held filter is replaced by any change that states the whole filter list (a checkbox, a removed chip, "Clear filters"). Contract for a hook that builds a definition (CRM-S3.4a, CRM-X3.1): return a memoized definition (the address is memoized on the definition object); keep the last views and fields while asking again, reporting `loading` only before the first answer and `failed` only when there is none to keep, or the tabs blank on every refresh and the address is read again (D-169 changes the filter row's part: it keeps held filters through other changes and removes only the one chip, or all of them with "Clear filters"; `changeAddress` still replaces them when a caller states the filters without `heldFilters`.) |
| D-142 | The frame owns what a view means for the query (CRM-S3.5a). `queryFor(definition, address)` is the one effective query: the view's filters, always counted first, then the address's, with the database's limit of 20 filters applied to the total (the address takes only as many as remain; the codec drops the rest and rewrites). The row fetcher and the tab counts both receive it (`effectiveFilters` per tab), so no table combines a view with the address itself. A definition holds its fields once: `fields` lists each field's id, label, kind and choices, mirroring the database's list, and a column refers to fields by id (`sortField`, `filterField`) and carries no kind. A column may give a `text` accessor, the plain value CRM-S3.8a writes to an export (a column without one is left out), and cells get the page's lookups through the frame's `context`, so a definition is a constant and never rebuilt when a lookup arrives `defaultSort` is the sort a table has when the address holds none: a column's sort, or `null` for the table's own order that no single column shows, with `defaultSortLabel` for the line over the table ("Order: Newest first") and `defaultSortReset` for the button back to it (D-149). The queue's is `null` ("Newest first": certified submissions first, latest certification first, then latest change), so its header shows no sort until one is chosen. The operators of each kind of field, with their words, are `OPERATORS` in `operators.ts` beside the codec, for CRM-S3.3a's filter row. How the 20-filter limit is applied to the total: the view's filters count first and the address takes the rest; the rows use the current view's room and a tab's count its own view's room, so under a view with many filters a tab can count with fewer of the address's filters than the rows do. CRM-S3.4a adds a view's `sort` and `columns` to `TableView` and makes `queryFor` and `sameAddress` apply the view's sort; CRM-S3.1a adds the visible and ordered columns input |
| D-143 | What "needs the data" means for each control while a page has nothing to show yet (CRM-S2.1). The test is "does it change the QUESTION, or act on the ANSWER". Controls that change the question stay usable, so a person can correct a slow search: the search box, the filters (the queue's two "only" boxes, later the filter row), the sort headers, the view tabs, the outbox's status buttons and the activity page's Kind list. Controls that act on the answer are not usable: Refresh (nothing to ask again; "Try again" is the way), the pager (the frame draws it only once rows exist; the activity page only when ready) and the activity page's Person list (its choices come with the answer). A control that is not usable is `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`: it stays in the tab order, has an accessible description that says why ("Available when the list has loaded") and ignores the click. Nothing acts on rows yet (selection is CRM-S3.6): when it does, its bar stays absent while there are no rows. The menu and New quote are outside the page and are never touched. The frame hands its toolbar `{ loading, slowLine }` (`toolbar` may be a function) and the page decides per control |
| D-144 | A refresh is not loading, and a changed question over rows is not loading either (CRM-S2.1, D-49). Grey rows and the slow-load line appear only when the page has nothing to show yet: no rows have ever arrived, or the address holds something the table cannot judge yet (`tableIsLoading`). Everything already on screen STAYS while the next answer arrives, on every page: the rows, the counts and any alert. A refresh of the question already on screen changes nothing on screen (no dimming, no busy mark). Rows that answer a DIFFERENT question than the one now asked (the next page, tab, filter or status) are dimmed and marked `aria-busy`: the dimming is what says they belong to the question just left, and it ends when the new rows replace them in the same table. The one exception to "marked busy" is an EMPTY body (CRM-S2.2, D-162): when the empty answer is for a question already left it is dimmed but not marked busy, because the status inside it must stay outside a busy region (a busy region can hold announcements back) and its words do not change while it waits. The queue (through the frame), the outbox and the activity page all follow this rule; the outbox's summary, counts and stall alert never blank during a reload or a status change (an alert is not removed and put back, so it is not announced again). The one exception is a different FIRM, never "the same list, dimmed": the activity page clears its entries and its Person list (people's emails) at once when another firm is chosen on the bare domain, and shows grey rows. "Refresh" cancels a request still on its way before asking again (`useRefreshTable`), so a stuck request for the next tab has a way out. The submission page keeps the submission on screen while it reloads after a change. Things that appear above or beside the data only when the answer holds them (the outbox's stall alert, the submission's email-problem alert, "PEO threads", "Changed since signing", the activity page's choice of firm on the bare domain for an owner of several) cannot be reserved in advance and may push the data when they appear; what is known in advance is reserved (the outbox's "N notifications, newest first" line, the status buttons' count room, the activity page's "Firm:" line at a firm's address) |
| D-145 | Ten seconds are counted from the first draw of the wait, continuously, through the "held, not judgeable yet" state and the time before the firm is known, until the page has something to show: when the data arrives or the load fails the count stops and the line goes, and a later wait counts from nothing. "Try again" asks for everything again (`useRefreshTable`: it drops the request still on its way first, because a plain refetch would hand back the same stuck request) and starts the ten seconds over. CRM-S2.2 extends the wait: a question the person has changed whose answer has not arrived, over the previous answer on screen (dimmed rows, or the dimmed empty words), is a wait too, and after ten seconds of it the same line shows with "Try again" (the frame counts it for rows and for the empty state alike; the rows and words stay, dimmed, meanwhile). A refresh of the question already on screen is still no wait. While nothing has been asked (the address holds something the table cannot judge yet because a saved view or custom field is still loading, or the firm is not known), there is nothing to ask again, so the line says what it is waiting for ("Still waiting for the table's views and fields." / "Still waiting for your firm's details.") and offers no button. CRM-S3.4a and CRM-X3.1, whose definition hooks load views and fields, may add a `retry` to the definition contract (D-141) and the frame would then offer "Try again" for that wait too; today the queue's definition is in code and never waits. The line is a polite status that is on the page, empty, from the first draw of the wait and filled at ten seconds, and the one "Loading <what>" status is on the page empty and filled just after its first draw, because a live region inserted already holding text is not reliably announced; neither is ever inside an element marked busy. The table frame owns the wait for its table (`useSlowLoad`, `tableIsLoading` and the refresh are wired once in the frame, and the line is handed to the toolbar as `slowLine` for the page to place under its title); a page not on the frame wires `useSlowLoad` itself. The submission page's "Try again" at ten seconds while the first opening is still running does not send a second opening: the page marks a submission as opened when it SENDS the call (the database records an opening when it handles the call, and collapses repeats only within 30 seconds), asks again as a plain read, and sends an opening again only after the first call failed. A call that is lost without any answer is therefore not re-sent as an opening: the cost of that edge is one missing entry, and the alternative is a double one |
| D-146 | How a loading table is drawn (CRM-S2.1). The FRAME draws one `DataTable` always, with grey rows as its body while it waits and the real rows after, so it is the same element before and after (focus on its scroll box and the sideways scroll survive) and it is built from the frame's own `columns`, `sort` and `renderHeader`, so a page cannot pass a loading table that is out of step with the real one (CRM-S3.2a's sort headers and CRM-S3.1a's visible columns show while loading with no work). The frame's `loading` slot is gone. Eight rows, each cell a grey bar sized for its column (the name column widest, then by the kind of field, right-aligned where the column is), in cells with the real cells' padding. The rows are hidden from assistive technology and ONE polite status reads "Loading <the noun's plural>". The shimmer is `motion-safe:animate-pulse`: nothing moves for a person who asked for less motion. The bars are `muted-foreground` at 30% (about 1.56:1 on a white card in the workspace look and 1.58:1 in the default look, 1.24:1 and 1.25:1 at the pulse's low point; the page's own muted tone was 1.10:1 and 1.05:1), decorative. A table not on the frame (activity, outbox) draws its own header from one list of columns and gives `PlaceholderBody` the same list's shapes, in one table that is never rebuilt when its rows arrive. A record page (the submission) draws its own frame (back link, each panel's real heading) and puts grey lines in the data areas with `PlaceholderLine`, `PlaceholderLines`, `PlaceholderBlock` and `PlaceholderPanel` under `RecordLoading`; only the placeholder parts are marked busy, never the statuses. It draws only the panels every submission has. The submission is one request, so its panels share one placeholder state; the PEO profiles, which load apart inside two panels (`PeoNeedsPanel`, `ManuscriptPanel`) keep their own line until CRM-S2.3, which gave them the shared pieces (done, D-177) |
| D-147 | How a column header sorts (CRM-S3.2a, and its review fixes). The frame, not the page, draws it: a column with a `sortField` has a real `<button>` filling the whole header cell (`DataTable`'s `onSort`; `renderHeader` still replaces it), a column without one is a plain header with no button and no `aria-sort`. This follows the WAI-ARIA sortable table example: the button's name is the header's words alone ("Company"), the `<th>` carries the state in `aria-sort` (`ascending` or `descending` on the one sorted column, `none` on the other sortable ones), and the arrow is decorative (`aria-hidden`), so a screen reader reading the header at every cell move hears the name once and the state once. What a click will do ("Sort descending") is the button's `title`, which is its description, never part of the name. The mark is a shape (an up or down arrow on the sorted column, a faint up-and-down mark on the others), never colour alone. One column at a time. A first click sorts a column in its field's first direction, a click on the sorted column reverses it, and a third click reverses again: nothing clears a sort, because the story says "clicking again reverses". The first direction is `FieldDefinition.firstDirection`, and without it follows the kind: a date starts newest first (what people expect of "Submitted"), everything else ascending (A to Z, smallest first, a choice in its own order). The queue sets "asc" on "Waiting since" and "Quote expires" (the oldest day needs attention) and "desc" on "Quotes" (most requests first: an intake with none has the number 0 and would fill the first page). The headers work while the table loads (the grey rows) because the same `DataTable` draws both (D-146). A table's `aria-busy` and dimming are on its body only, never its headers, so the button a person just pressed stays bright and usable while the sorted page loads. A sort equal to the table's default is no sort: the address never holds it |
| D-148 | What a sort click does to history. A sort that puts a NEW column in the address pushes a history entry; one that only reverses the column already there replaces it. Back therefore undoes the choice of a column, and asc, desc, asc on one column is one Back, not three. (The first draft pushed every click; a reviewer's rule replaced it.) Filters and search replace; a tab is a link and pushes; a page change pushes. A sort changes only the order of the same rows, so it does not change the tab counts: their query key holds the views' filters and the search, not the sort, and a page test asserts a sort does not ask for them again (D-168 supersedes "Filters and search replace" for filters: the filter row's apply, remove and clear push; only the search replaces.) |
| D-149 | How a person gets back to a table's own order (CRM-S3.2a). No header click can clear a sort, so the frame draws one short line over the table, `OrderLine`, and its button is the only way back. With no sort in the address and a table whose order is no single column's (`defaultSort: null`, the queue), it says "Order: Newest first" (`defaultSortLabel`), so "the header shows the direction" stays honest when no header can. With a sort in the address it says "Sorted by Company, ascending." and offers one small button with the definition's own words (`defaultSortReset`, "Back to newest first": written out, not made by changing the label) that clears `sort` and nothing else (tab, search and filters stay), pushing a history entry and starting at page 1. The button disappears when pressed, so focus goes to the table's scroll box, which is always there. A table whose default is one column shows it in that column's header, has no line while the address holds no sort, and gets the line and button once another sort is set. The line is plain text, not a live region: `aria-sort` already says the state, and a live region on top made one click three announcements |
| D-150 | The queue's sort columns (CRM-S3.2a). The database sorts every field in `queue_field_kind`; a header sorts a column only when the column names one field. Sortable: Company (`company_name`), Contact (`contact_name`), States (`states`, by its codes as text), Employees (`employee_count`), Submitted (`submitted_at`, newest first; a draft has none, so drafts come last either way), Status (`pipeline_status`, in pipeline order, not alphabetical), Quotes (`quote_requests_sent`, how many were sent, most first; a row with none says "None", not a blank, so what sorts as 0 reads as a value), and two columns that were lines inside Quotes and are now columns of their own, so each header carries one field: Waiting since (`oldest_waiting_on`; the cell shows the day the wait began with how long that is under it, so ascending, oldest day first, is the longest wait first, and the header, the cell and the direction agree) and Quote expires (`earliest_quote_expires_on`, the day alone). No sort: Completeness, which the browser works out from the row (`completenessOf`) and the database cannot order. The interim Sort box is gone; its three orders became: newest first the table's own order (the line and "Back to newest first"), longest wait the Waiting since header (ascending) and quote expiring soonest the Quote expires header (ascending). Fields the database can sort that no column shows (signer, file ID, the item counts, `start_month`, the yes/no marks) are reachable only by an address; one in the address is kept and sorts, and no header is marked. `start_month` is free text ("March 2026"), so a text sort would not order it as a date: it must not get a column with a sort until it is a date field. Every date column's plain `text` for an export is an ISO day (YYYY-MM-DD, the UTC day for a moment); Submitted's was "Oct 1, 2026" and changed to match (a draft, which has no date, keeps the words that say why) |
| D-151 | The three sort rules are the database's, checked again here (CRM-S3.2a), and one did NOT hold. Empty values come last in both directions (the first sort key is "is empty"; tested for company name, headcount, days, states and a date with empty values) and a date or moment is compared as a moment or a day, never as its text: both hold. Text ignoring case held only for ASCII: the key was `lower(x)` compared under the database's collation, which is C or C.UTF-8 in the test databases (code-point order), so "Émile" and "Ünal" sorted after "zeta". `20261118150000_queue_sort_collation.sql` restates `queue_page_full`, the function that holds the ORDER BY (from its latest definition: the first migration's, renamed by the faster path), and lowers and compares every text key (company, contact, signer, File ID, the note, target start) and list-as-text key (states, missing items) under the ICU root collation "und-x-icu", as saved view names already are (`20261117120500_table_settings_fixes.sql`, point 5), stopping with a clear message if the collation is missing. Every rule, refusal, tiebreaker (the queue's own order, then the id), limit and grant is as it was; the faster path sorts no text and is not restated; text search and the contains and is filters are not changed. `queue_query.sql` tests accented and mixed-case names ascending and descending for company, target start, the advisor's note and contact, and loads every migration to the newest; `queueApi.test.ts` reads the full path's checks from the new definition (its latest) |
| D-152 | What raises a record's version (CRM-S2.4b, D-46). A trigger (`record_version_bump`) raises it by exactly one when one of the record's own editable fields really changes, and not otherwise. The editable fields are the very ones the history tracks (a partner's name and kind): `record_track` attaches both from one list, so the two cannot disagree. A save that changes nothing, or only a stamp (updated at, who saved), raises nothing, so a person who saves a form twice without editing it is not refused the second time. Nobody sets the version: an UPDATE's value for it is put back, and a new row is 1 whatever it said. Existing partners start at 1. A table that later gets a version calls `record_track(table, name, key columns, fields)` in place of `record_history_track` (history and version together, so neither can be added by mistake without the other; `record_history_track` and `record_version_track` stay for a table that means to have only one). `record_version.sql` has two standing checks over every table: one with a history trigger and not a version trigger (or the reverse) fails, and so does one whose two triggers list different fields, so a table that attaches them separately cannot let the version and the history disagree |
| D-153 | The history says which version each entry produced (CRM-S2.4b, changes D-129 by adding a column; D-129's text is not rewritten). `record_history.version`, a new nullable column in `20261118160000_record_version.sql`; the capture trigger reads it from the record's own `version` (null for a table with none), and the creation is version 1. No row is backfilled: entries from before this shipped have none, and cannot matter, because every partner was at 1 when it shipped and every later change has a version. "What changed since version N" is therefore exact: entries with a version above N and up to the version the record is at |
| D-154 | What a save without a version does (CRM-S2.4b). It is refused (22023: "This save did not say which version of the partner it started from, so it was refused. Reload the page and try again."), never read as "from the current version", because that would overwrite silently. `advisor_update_referral_partner` is now `(code, name, kind, firm, version)`: the version is the last argument and its default is NULL, which is what lets an old four-argument call arrive and be refused with that sentence instead of failing as an unknown function. A version higher than the partner's current one is refused too (22023, "This save names a version of the partner that does not exist, so it was refused. Reload the page and try again.", `20261118160500_record_version_fixes.sql`): nobody changed anything, so it is not a conflict. It is checked after "No partner has that code", so a missing code is still PT404. The only caller in `src/` (`updateReferralPartner` in `advisorApi.ts`, from the partners page) now sends it, and `advisor_add_referral_partner` is not changed (it returns the row, which now carries `version`). The refusals that come before a version is looked at are unchanged and in the same order: the advisor check, the firm to act for (D-18, D-40: a non-member, a removed or suspended member, a wrong address), the write guard (a support session and a closing firm, PT423, D-115), then the input checks, then the version |
| D-155 | The shape of a conflict (CRM-S2.4b). A normal answer, not a raised error. The save is `{conflict: false, record: <row, with its new version>}` or `{conflict: true, currentVersion, current: {name, kind}, changed: [{field, value, who, at}]}`; `changed` holds each field whose value now differs from what it was at the version the person started from (a field changed and changed back is no difference), with its value now and the last entry that set it, ordered by field name. Why not a raised error (a distinct SQLSTATE with the detail as JSON): a conflict is an expected outcome, so it should not be logged or counted as a failure, the browser reads one typed object instead of parsing an error's detail text, and the form's `explain` hook is not needed to tell it from a real failure. "Changes nothing" holds either way: the one `UPDATE ... WHERE version = <sent>` touches no row. Every part of the answer is checked in the browser (`readSaveAnswer`, with zod): an answer with a missing or malformed part is a failed save (the plain failure line), never a success and never an empty conflict. The generic layer calls the saved record `record`; `updateReferralPartner` maps it once to `partner`. The client is `updateReferralPartner(firmId, code, name, kind, version)`, which returns `{conflict: false, partner}` or `{conflict: true, currentVersion, current: {name, kind}, changed: ChangedField[]}` (`ReferralPartnerConflict`, built on the generic `RecordConflict<Current>` in `src/lib/recordVersion.ts`); a failure is still thrown. The value is a JSON string, number or boolean, or null. CRM-S2.4a puts the dialog on this: it already has the person's own typed values from the form, the current values and the changed list with who and when, and the current version to save again from. **What the form holds (review fix).** A save sends the version the person STARTED editing from, never the row's live version: `useWorkspaceForm` holds it (`startedVersion`), set when the person opens Edit (`begin(values, version)`), moved only by that form's own successful save (`versionOf`, from the saved record's version) or by `startFrom(version)`, and never by a list reload under the open form (a reload used to swap the version and let old typing overwrite someone's change). `save(values, { version })` receives it. A successful save also updates the row the page shows at once from the answer, so editing again before the list reloads starts from the new version and does not conflict with the person's own save. For CRM-S2.4a: as built (D-183, which changes this): "keep mine" saves again from `currentVersion` once, per field (the person's changes, and their change on what was left alone), and the form takes that version only if the save works; "keep theirs" takes their values for the fields they changed through `begin`, starts from `currentVersion` and keeps the rest of what was typed |
| D-156 | How who changed it is named in a conflict (CRM-S2.4b, D-33, D-131). By the history's own rule, written once: `record_history_who(actor, is_staff)` returns a member's email, "Nevermore" for staff (no id, no email), "System" for the server, "A deleted account" for a member whose account is gone. `record_history_list` is restated to use it, so the history and a conflict cannot name a person differently. No id of a person or a firm is in a conflict. Staff's stamp is the one `firm_actor_is_staff` decided when the change was written. The conflict is only built after the save's own checks, so it is never given to someone who is refused, and another firm's values are never in an answer (proved with a non-member, a removed member, a suspended member, a person at the wrong address and a platform admin, each sending a stale and a current version and getting the same refusal) |
| D-157 | Ordering two saves (CRM-S2.4b, D-29, D-32). The version check is the concurrency control and it is a constraint-like single statement: `UPDATE ... WHERE firm_id AND code AND version = <sent>`. A second save from the same version waits for the first's row, then finds the version moved and touches nothing, and is answered with the conflict. No new lock is taken (the guard's `FOR KEY SHARE` of the firm's row, D-122, is the only one, unchanged). The answer reads the record's current version once and only the history entries up to it, so a save that lands while the answer is built is not half in it. `record_version.sql` proves with two real sessions (dblink) that the second save is seen waiting, exactly one wins, the loser gets the winner's version, values and who, and the row, its version and its history hold the first save only; the proof goes red when the `AND version = <sent>` is removed |
| D-158 | How the two kinds of empty are told apart, and the rule on each page (CRM-S2.2). **Nothing yet** means the firm has no records of this kind; **nothing matches** means the question was narrower than the whole list. The queue: an address with no view, search or filter that comes back with no rows is nothing yet; an address with any of them is nothing matches (`isNarrowed`). A sort or a page is not a narrowing. The queue does not know how many submissions the firm has without narrowing: its tab numbers are counted under the search and filters on screen (D-137), so the "all" count is not the firm's total, and one more cheap call (a count with no filters and no search) was not added. Instead nothing matches is worded to be true whether or not submissions exist ("No submissions match these filters") and it never says some exist and never offers to create. The outbox knows: its summary counts every status whatever is chosen, so no notification anywhere is nothing yet (even with a status chosen, since "No notifications are dead" would suggest others exist) and a status with none while others hold some is nothing matches ("No notifications are dead", with "Show all notifications"). The activity page has no total: an unfiltered first page that comes back empty is nothing yet; a person or kind chosen is nothing matches ("No entries match these filters"); a page AFTER the first that comes back empty, filtered or not (older entries aged out meanwhile), is neither: "There are no older entries" with "Back to the newest", which moves focus to the Person list. Invitations, organisations and asked to join have no filter, so only nothing yet. The words always describe the question the empty answer was FOR, which is the previous one while the next is on its way |
| D-159 | What each way back clears (CRM-S2.2, changed by its review). Each button does the one thing its label names. **"Clear search"** when only a search narrows it (a tab may also be in force), **"Clear filters"** for filters, **"Clear filters and search"** for both: all three clear those and KEEP the tab and the sort. When, after that, only the tab narrows the list, the state is nothing matches with only "View: Won" in force and the button is **"Show all statuses"** (the table's own all-view name), which leaves the tab. The sort stays in every case: it only orders the same rows and has its own way back (D-149). A search that found nothing adds the table's hint (`searchHint`: "Search covers company, contact and File ID."). Every way back is a step Back undoes (`push`, as a tab and a new sort column are): from `/advisor?view=won&q=zzz`, "Clear search" then Back returns to the search; the outbox's "Show all notifications" (its status buttons replace the entry, but leaving an empty status is a place to come back to) and the activity page's buttons (its filters are not in the address, so there is no history) follow the same words. On the queue it is one `change(...)` that also clears held filters and goes to page 1 (D-135, D-139); the search box follows the address. After the press, focus goes to the table's scroll box (queue), the Person list (activity) or the All button (outbox), since the pressed button is gone |
| D-160 | What the queue's nothing yet offers (CRM-S2.2). One sentence, "Submissions appear here once a prospect has started filling in an intake", and no action. The words are true to `queue_hides_empty_guest_draft`: the queue hides a guest's intake that was never certified and holds nothing (no answers, provenance, transcript, chosen PEO or document), so an intake nobody has filled in does not appear. A member cannot make a submission today: New quote arrives with CRM-Q2.1 (D-38), which gives the empty state its primary action then; the import action (CRM-X4.1) is a prop on `NothingYet` that nothing passes yet. Deferred to those stories, not invented here |
| D-161 | The Inbox's special words (CRM-S2.2). The Inbox is the filter "has new PEO mail is yes" in the address (D-136), so its way back is "Clear filters". When it is the only thing in force (no tab, no search, no other filter), an empty queue says "No submissions have new PEO mail", which is true whether or not the firm has submissions, instead of "match these filters". It still lists "Has new PEO mail: yes" and offers "Clear filters". The wording is given by the queue page (`headline` on `NothingMatches`), not by the table definition: it is one filter on one table, and a definition-level list of special sentences was not worth building for one case. When CRM-S3.3a removes the two checkboxes, this stays as it is. Any other combination uses the generic sentence |
| D-162 | One announcement, once (CRM-S2.2). Each empty state is ONE polite status holding its headline and the list of what is in force, on the page empty from its first draw and filled just after (the same rule as `LoadingStatus`, D-145); the actions are outside the status. A refresh that still finds nothing changes nothing in it, so nothing is said again; a change of the words (another filter) is said once. An empty body that answers a question already left is dimmed but not marked busy (see D-144): its words do not change while it waits, and a status inside a busy region can be held back. A wait over it that lasts ten seconds shows the slow-load line (D-145). Each of the invitations page's three lists has its own status. The empty cell is one component, `EmptyBody` (states folder), used by the frame and by the activity and outbox tables. The frame draws the empty state as one cell in the table's own body under its real headers, so the table, its scroll box and a focused control survive from grey rows to rows to empty and back (D-146), and the non-frame tables (activity, outbox) draw the same one table with the empty cell, their old stand-alone messages being gone |
| D-163 | The activity page's nothing yet is true after the ninety days (CRM-S2.2, D-106). "Nothing has been recorded for your firm in the last 90 days: entries appear here as its people sign in, open submissions or change who belongs." A firm whose entries have all aged out reads it correctly; it replaces "Nothing has been recorded for your firm yet", which would have been false for one |
| D-164 | The invitations page's actions (CRM-S2.2). Each list is one line (sentence and action, `layout="line"`). Pending invitations and Organisations each have an action, "Invite someone" and "List an organisation", which moves focus to the first field of the form directly above the list, rather than a second control that sends or lists anything: the form is already the primary action on the page, and a button that only moves focus adds no second way to do the same thing. Asked to join has no action (people ask by signing in; an owner cannot make a request). The empty list inside a record (one line with its add action, heading and count of zero) is not built: D-24 gives it to CRM-A2.1, CRM-C1.1 and CRM-O1.1, which use `NothingYet` with `layout="line"` |
| D-165 | The words for a filter (CRM-S2.2's review; CRM-S3.3a's chips reuse them, `filterInWords`). A choice reads by its label; one value is a plain statement ("Status is Won", "Status is not Won", "States includes CA", "States does not include CA"); two or more keep the list ("Status is any of Won or Lost", "…is none of Won, Lost or Quoted", "States includes any of CA or NY"). A pair is put in order, the smaller or earlier end first, whichever way it was written ("Employees is between 10 and 50"; the address keeps what was written, and the database accepts either). A count of days follows the database, which reads N as today and the N days after (or before) and 0 as today alone: "is in the next 3 days", "is in the next day", "is in the last day", "is today". A value the table does not list is shown in quotes as written ("Status is “zzz”"), never as a bare id; choices ignore case as the database does. The queue's "Missing required items" lists its choices from the same list the Reckoning uses (`REQUIRED_ITEMS`, plus `RECOMMENDED_ITEMS`' census) and "States" lists the state codes. A person field lists no choices and must be given a way to name people (`filterInWords(..., names)`, CRM-A1.1): an id is never shown |
| D-166 | The filter row (CRM-S3.3a). A generic piece of the frame, `FilterRow` in `src/components/workspace/table/`, which a page places in its toolbar: it reads the table's definition and the address, writes through `change`, and knows no table. A definition gives `listedFilters` (required; the fields shown as buttons always, in that order); "Add filter" offers every other field in `fields`, by label, searchable. The editor (`FilterEditor`) is driven by the field's kind and `OPERATORS`, so it offers exactly the operators the codec and the database take, and reads what is typed with the codec's own `readValue` and the limits in `columns.ts` (200 characters, 100 values, 0 to 3660 days; nothing restated). It is react-hook-form with a zod schema (D-51) with errors under the field as the workspace form does them, but it does NOT use `useWorkspaceForm`: that hook's unsaved-changes guard would block navigation over a half-typed filter, and a filter editor has nothing to lose (Escape or a click away just closes it). Native selects and date and number inputs, as the workspace form's own select is: they work with a phone's picker and a keyboard. A list of values the table does not list is typed, separated by commas, and a backslash keeps a comma (or a backslash) inside its value ("a\\,b, c" is "a,b" and "c"), the codec's own escape, so a value with a comma survives changing the filter. The editor checks once: the schema hands on the finished filter, so what is checked is what is applied. Changing the condition to one that asks for a different kind of value empties the boxes, so a value typed for the old condition is never applied unseen. A "between" takes its ends in either order, so its messages say "one end" and "the other end", not smaller and larger |
| D-167 | One filter per field in the row. The database combines filters with "and", and two filters on one date field (after X, before Y) are legitimate but "between" says it, so the row keeps one: once a field has a chip, its listed button and its place in "Add filter" are gone and the chip, clicked, changes it. An address written by hand that carries two filters on one field still shows two chips, each changeable and removable on its own (nothing is lost). A person's "is any of" is offered only when the field lists people to choose from (`choices`, CRM-A1.1); without a list only "is me" and "is my team" are offered. `listedFilters` is typed by the table's own field ids: a table whose fields include loaded custom ids (CRM-X3.1's definition must type them in `fields`, so its `keyof` holds them) lists one with no cast |
| D-168 | What a filter change does to history (changes D-148's "filters and search replace", for filters). Adding, changing, removing and clearing a filter each PUSH a history entry and start at page 1, as "Clear filters" and the other ways back from empty already did (D-159): each is a deliberate step ("Apply", a click) and a new question, so Back undoes it. Only the search replaces (it follows typing, so every keystroke would be a Back step). A filter applied while a tab is chosen keeps the tab. The 20-filter limit is counted as `effectiveFilters` counts it: the tab's own filters first, then the address's (held ones included) |
| D-169 | Chips show the address's filters only. A tab's own filters are not in the address, so there is no chip to remove: the tab is their control. A filter held for a field that has not loaded yet (D-141) is a quiet dashed chip with its text as written and a remove button; it is not a button that opens an editor. While `fieldsStatus` is "loading" the listed buttons and "Add filter" are `aria-disabled` with the reason "More filters are available once the table's fields have loaded." (D-143's way: in the tab order, a description that says why, the click ignored); the known chips stay changeable. Held filters are kept through any change to the others: `changeAddress` takes `heldFilters` (the held texts that stay), so removing or adding a filter no longer drops them unseen, and removing a held chip drops only that one. A held chip's removal and "Clear filters" are the only ways a held filter goes before its field arrives |
| D-170 | Focus and announcements in the row. Opening an editor puts focus in its first control; Escape or "Cancel" closes it and focus goes back to what opened it; clicking elsewhere closes it and focus stays where the person clicked. Applying puts focus on the chip made or changed; removing puts it on the next chip, else the one before, else "Add filter"; "Clear filters" puts it on "Add filter". The row announces NOTHING of its own: the range line already says once what a new filter left ("1 to 1 of 1 submission") and the empty state says it once when nothing matches (D-162), and focus on the new chip reads its words; a third announcement would be the triple the sort review found. Validation messages are `role="alert"`, announced as they appear. The listed buttons are named "Filter by Submitted" (not "Submitted") so none shares a name with a column's sort button. Two "Clear filters" buttons can show at once (the row's and the empty state's, D-159); both send focus to "Add filter" (the empty state's through the row's button, given to the page as `addFilterRef`); a "Clear search" or "Show all statuses", which has no such button, still goes to the table. When Back or Forward takes away the chip that had focus, focus goes to "Add filter" rather than the page (the row remembers the last control in it that had focus; a person who clicked blank page first and then pressed Back also lands there, a small cost accepted). Applying a filter that leaves the address as it was writes nothing and pushes no entry: the editor closes and focus returns to what opened it |
| D-171 | The queue's listed filters (CRM-S3.3a). The three yes-or-no marks the old checkboxes and links stood for (Needs advisor action, Waiting on a PEO, Has new PEO mail; "Status" is the tabs), then the three columns a broker narrows by most (States, Employees, Submitted). Every other field (company, contact, the counts, dates, intake, the missing items and the rest) is under "Add filter". Every filterable field in `QUEUE_TABLE.fields` already has a label and, where its kind needs them, its choices (status, intake, states, missing items); a page test checks that "Add filter" offers exactly the fields that are not listed. The two interim checkboxes are removed (D-140). The queue's `?status=`, `?action=1`, `?waiting=1` and `?mail=new` links are unchanged (D-136: the codec's `legacy`) and now show as chips. The tab numbers refetch on a filter change (their key holds each tab's effective filters) and not on a sort (D-148); a page test asserts both |
