| D-172 | The count and a colleague's view. "The count above the table is of what matches": the range line counts the effective query's rows, which now includes the chips' filters (page test: a filter changes "1 to 50 of 375" to "1 to 1 of 1"). "A colleague sees the same filters over their own visibility": the address carries the filters and the database applies the viewer's own membership and visibility to the same question (D-43, D-138's neighbours), so there is nothing to build; a page test shows the same address asks for the same query and shows the same chips |
| D-173 | Deferred by CRM-S3.3a (D-24). Person filters on a real table: the editor's person branch is built (me, my team, and "is any of" over a field's listed people, reached by a test-only definition) and the database still refuses them with 0A000 until records have an owner, so the queue lists none; CRM-A1.1 must give Accounts an `owner` field of kind `person`, and supply its people in ONE of two ways: as that field's `choices` (each `{ value: the user id, label: the name }`: the editor lists them to choose from and the chip reads the label, D-174), or as the `names` lookup that `filterInWords` and `inForceInWords` take (the chip reads the name; a person nobody can name reads in quotes as written, never as a bare id). The editor's "is any of" needs `choices`; without them only "is me" and "is my team" are offered. It also lists "Owner" in Accounts' `listedFilters` and lifts the database's 0A000 refusal. Custom fields: CRM-X3.1 adds them to `fields` and `fieldsStatus`, so "Add filter" offers them with their kind's operators and nothing else changes. A saved view (CRM-S3.4a) stores exactly the address's filters; when one is active its filters are the tab's own (not chips, D-169) and a person's extra filters show as chips over it, whose Clear filters leaves the view; "modified view" is `sameAddress` differing from the view's. "Save this as a view" and the columns menu are not built |
| D-174 | Fixes found in review (CRM-S3.3a). A person's chip is worded by the `names` lookup, else by the matching choice's label, else in quotes as written; an id is never shown bare. The codec drops a day in year 0000: Postgres takes 0001-01-01 to 9999-12-31 and refuses 0000 (checked by hand: `select '0000-01-01'::date` fails, `'0001-01-01'` and `'9999-12-31'` pass), so the address no longer lets the database fail a whole table for it. The choice list's message is tied to the list (`aria-describedby`) and a failed Apply puts focus on its first choice, not the search box above it |
| D-175 | The failed load (CRM-S2.3). ONE piece, `LoadFailed` in `src/components/workspace/states/`, fills every page's data area when it could not load, and replaces `AdvisorLoadError` (gone). It says "We couldn't load the submissions." (the page's noun, with its article) and "This is usually a dropped connection or a problem on our side, not something you did.", with a "Try again" button. It NEVER shows the error's own text (the database's "canceling statement due to statement timeout", the network's "Failed to fetch"): the error is read only for its kind, as before: a lapsed session ("Your session has expired", the person is signed out and sent to sign in), a refused role (42501: "You no longer have access to this firm's records", no retry) or anything else (retry). D-138's refused-query panel is unchanged and stays the frame's own: it shows the database's reason because D-138 says those reasons are safe. "Try again" is the page's own retry (a table's `data.retry`, a page's reload); nothing in it touches the address, so a table's filters, sort, view and page are kept, and the toolbar and tabs above it stay. Focus (`retryLanding.ts`): the button is gone as soon as the load starts again, so when it is removed while it still had focus, focus goes to what stays (the page's `main`; for a panel, the panel); when the retry fails too, the new failure takes focus on its own "Try again", so the keyboard person can press it again and the new alert is read once. A press is believed for ten seconds only, so a failure a minute later (a refresh) never takes focus. A failure has a key (its noun, or `focusKey`): two panels can wait on one read, and only the one whose button was pressed takes focus back; a press whose button never went (the failure stayed on screen while it asked) is spent at once. The landing element is made focusable for that moment only and plain again on blur. A page that keeps the failure on screen while it asks again (a hook that holds `failed`) must take it off, as Settings' "can this firm be closed" check now does with a "Checking…" status, or a second failure is not a new alert. Moved onto it: the queue, activity, held mail, outbox, members, partners, firms, PEOs, funnel, mailbox, settings (can this firm be closed), the submission record page and the invitations page |
| D-176 | "This submission isn't available" (CRM-S2.3). One state, `NotAvailable` (noun, link back, label), for a record that was removed, one that is in another firm, and an address whose id is no id at all, in the same words ("This submission isn't available." and "It may have been removed, or you may not be able to open it.", with "Back to all submissions"), so a person cannot learn from the page whether a record exists that they may not open. The database already answers all three with nothing (`open_submission` returns no row for a missing or foreign submission; the row security hides the rest); an id that is not a uuid is refused with 22P02, which the page now reads as the same state rather than as a failed load that "Try again" can never fix. It is not an error and not a wait, so it has no alert, no status and no "Try again" (an e2e step asserts neither exists). When it appears focus goes to its heading, which is what tells a screen reader that the page they opened has nothing on it. Replaces "Submission not found." Later record pages (accounts, contacts, leads, opportunities, quotes) use it with their own noun: "This account isn't available" is CRM-A2.1's to place |
| D-177 | A failed panel (CRM-S2.3). A record's panels are independent already (the submission is one request, but what loads apart does not hold the rest). `PanelFailed` is the small alert inside a panel's body: its own sentence, its own "Try again", focus to the panel when the button goes. The two panel-level PEO profile reads now use the shared pieces, as D-146 said they would: while the profiles load, `RecordLoading` (one status "Loading the PEOs' profiles", outside any busy element) over `PlaceholderLines` (hidden from assistive technology, `aria-busy`), and in the Advisor packet panel the visible reason Generate waits ("You can generate the packet once they're in."); when they fail, `PanelFailed` with the panel's own sentence. "Load the profiles again" is now "Try again", the one name for the one action. Generate stays disabled until the profiles are in. The Invitations page's Organisations and Asked to join sections are panels too: each fails on its own, in plain words, and tries again on its own button, with the rest of the page untouched. One read, two panels: the Advisor packet panel and "What each PEO still needs" wait on the same PEO profiles, so the page says so ONCE (D-146): the first panel is the live one, and the packet panel's grey lines, words and button are not a second status or alert (`announceCatalog`, `silent`), except when the first panel is not showing the profiles (no certified version to read). `PanelFailed` reads the same three kinds as `LoadFailed` (a lapsed session or a refused role: nothing to retry); the invitation and organisation reads carry `forbidden` for a 42501 so they say it |
| D-178 | A failed save (CRM-S2.3). It keeps what the person typed, says what failed in plain words next to the buttons, and never closes the form. Forms built on `useWorkspaceForm` already did (CRM-S4.1): their message is now the shared `SaveFailed` and their rule for "is this text meant for a person" is now `failureWords` (`src/components/workspace/form/failureWords.ts`), one rule for every failed save and every message the product shows, checked against every `ERRCODE` the migrations raise: an error's own sentence is shown when it is meant for people, otherwise the caller's fallback sentence. Shown: the product's 22023, 42501, PT4xx, 55000 (about 70 plain refusals), P0002 and P0001 (the default of a plain RAISE), 23514, 23505, 23502, 40001 ("Another version was generated meanwhile") and EA00x. Not shown: the client's own failures ("Failed to fetch", "Edge Function returned a non-2xx status code"), codes PostgREST and the server raise (PGRST, 08, 53, 57, 58, XX, class 42 except 42501, 22P02, 22001), and for any code the server's own sentences ("violates … constraint", "duplicate key value", "null value in column", "could not serialize", "deadlock detected", "invalid input syntax", "value too long", "relation … does not exist"). Codes the product and the server share (class 23, 40001) are told apart by the text. A first draft treated 55 and P0 as technical and hid the product's own refusals behind "check your connection"; a table test through one panel now pins each code and text to what a person sees. Panels that save from a button, not a form, were showing the error in a toast; the ones where a person types something now hold the failure with `useSaveFailure` and show `SaveFailed` beside their button: Internal notes, Needs advisor action, Ask the prospect, Status, a field's review and correction, a quote request's answer, note, reply address and send record, and the Advisor packet's Generate. The alert is announced once as it appears; focus goes back to the button that was disabled while the save ran (it lost it); the next try clears the words; the text they typed is untouched. The fallbacks end "What you typed is still here." where something was typed. The checks a page makes itself before asking (a correction's value and reason) say so in the same place (`say`). The firm's own pages (members, settings) already said their words in an alert near their buttons from API errors that are sentences already (`MemberApiError` and the others), so only the element changed to `SaveFailed` |
| D-179 | What still reaches a person as text from elsewhere, and what was left (CRM-S2.3). Every toast and inline message that showed `error.message` for an action that is not a typed-in save (marking a thread read, the firms console's decisions, the outbox, held mail, support access, a PEO's editors) now passes `failureWords`, so a dropped network or a database timeout reads as the fallback and a rule the product raised reads in its own words. The rule is a filter on the error's code and text, not a proof: it names the server's sentences it knows (D-178), and a server sentence it does not know, under a code that is not technical, would pass. The remedy is where the newer API files already work, mapping each code to a sentence of ours (`memberApi`, `closeFirmApi`, `leaveFirmApi`, `invitationApi`, `joinByOrganisationApi`); `advisorApi`'s `fail` and its callers still hand `error.message` through, and mapping each of its calls is the story that replaces each page (a new table's story brings its own API file under the existing rule). Left as they were: the refused-query panel (D-138, its reasons are safe), the gates that wrap the whole app (`RequireFirm`, `FirmAddressGate`, the prospect's intake and packet pages: not workspace pages), the slow-load line (S2.1), the PEO editors' refusals that name what is wrong with a layout (their own sentences), and a failed background refresh over rows already on screen, which is CRM-S2.4a's (refresh in place) |
| D-180 | Deferred by CRM-S2.3 (D-24). Note for CRM-S2.4a: a table's rows query that has failed is still asked again on focus and every 60 seconds, so each ask remounts the failed-load panel: a person's un-pressed "Try again" loses focus and the alert is read again each minute. Refresh in place must decide what a failed table keeps asking. Refresh in place, the no-connection banner and the conflict dialog, with a failed refresh over rows that are on screen (today the table frame shows the failed-load panel in place of the rows, as it did): CRM-S2.4a. "This account isn't available" and the rest of the record pages' not-available state: CRM-A2.1, CRM-L2.1, CRM-O1.1 and CRM-Q3.1 use `NotAvailable`. A failed list inside a record, and a failed save on the account, contact, lead and opportunity forms: each of those stories places `PanelFailed` and builds its form on `useWorkspaceForm`, which already says a failed save beside the buttons. A failed saved view or column choice: CRM-S3.4a and CRM-S3.1a **Done by CRM-S2.4a (D-181 to D-184):** a table with nothing to show that failed is asked again only by "Try again" (not on the minute, on focus or on reconnecting, so its panel never remounts); a failed refresh over rows on screen keeps the rows and shows one polite note over them; refresh in place, the no-connection banner and the conflict dialog are built. What it left is in D-184 |
| D-181 | Refresh in place, and what a failed refresh does (CRM-S2.4a, D-49, D-144, D-180). The queue already asked again on focus and every 60 seconds through the query library, keeping its rows on screen (D-49, D-144; a test now pins that the minute's and focus's refresh brings new rows into the SAME table with the scroll position, the focused control and half-typed search words untouched). What this story decides is a failure. **Over rows on screen** (the question's own earlier answer is still there): the rows STAY, and one note, `RefreshFailed` (`states/RefreshFailed.tsx`), sits over them: "We couldn't refresh the submissions. What's below may be out of date." with "Try again" (the page's own retry, `data.retry`). It is a polite `role="status"`, not an alert, because nobody asked for this ask and the person is reading something else; it is ONE element for as long as refreshes keep failing (the table keeps asking quietly each minute and on focus, and a failed minute after a failed minute re-renders the same note, so it is not read again and a focused "Try again" is not lost), and it goes when a refresh works. It never shows the failure's own text. Pressing "Try again" says "Trying again…" (`aria-disabled`, click ignored) until the answer; if the note goes with the answer while the button had focus, focus goes to the page's `main` (`retryLanding`, as the panel's does). The frame decides: an error with a page of rows for the question on screen is the note; an error with nothing to show (never had rows, or the next question failed and there are no rows for it) is the panel as before (`LoadFailed`, or the refused-query panel of D-138). **With nothing to show** (D-180's other question): a table that failed with no rows is asked again ONLY by "Try again": not on the minute, not on focus and not on reconnecting (`keepsAsking` in `useTableRows`: the query's interval, window-focus and reconnect refetches are off while it is in the error state with no data), so the panel is never removed and drawn again by the library's own asks, a person's un-pressed "Try again" keeps focus and its alert is read once. A request that failed and then succeeds through "Try again" resumes the minute's refresh. The tab counts' queries never throw (a failed batch keeps its last numbers, D-134), so they needed no change. `useTableRows` also returns `isRetrying` (asked again while its error stands). The same rule is for every later table on the frame; none writes its own. Changes two existing tests deliberately: the two queue tests that expected the failed refresh to show an alert over the rows now expect the note **For the owner (review):** a table that failed with nothing to show while the browser still claims to be ONLINE is not asked again when the connection comes back (the browser fires no `online` event for it), only by "Try again"; the cost of the alternative is the remounting D-180 describes. The note's live region is on the page from the table's first draw (`aria-label="List refresh"`, empty) and only its words come and go, so an announcement is not lost to a region inserted with its words (the queue now has one more empty status) |
| D-182 | The no-connection banner, and saving with none (CRM-S2.4a, D-143). The browser's word, not a probe: `useOnline` (`src/components/workspace/useOnline.ts`, `navigator.onLine` with the `online` and `offline` events) says whether there is a connection. It can be wrong both ways (a network that is up but unreachable reads as online), so a request that fails is still a failed request and is said in its own place (D-175, D-178); no timer or request is made to find out. **The banner**, `ConnectionBanner` in the shell's frame on every workspace page: "You're offline. What's on screen may be out of date, and nothing can be saved until you're back online." It is ONE polite live region (`role="status"`, `aria-label="Connection"`) that is on the page from the first draw with no words, and only its words come and go: announced once when the connection goes (D-162's pattern: a region inserted with its words is announced unreliably), not again by a poll or a second `offline` event, and it says nothing when the connection comes back (the words simply go; the lists ask again by themselves on reconnect, D-181). Opening the workspace already offline shows it at once. Pages already loaded stay readable: nothing is cleared and the query library pauses asks while offline. **Saving**: every form on `useWorkspaceForm` gets it free. Offline, Save is `aria-disabled` (not `disabled`, D-143: it stays in the tab order, the click is ignored) with a visible reason beside the buttons that is also its accessible description: "You're offline. Saving is back when you are." (`OFFLINE_SAVE_REASON`). A save made another way (Enter in a field) is not sent either and says so as the failed-save alert beside the buttons: "You're offline, so this wasn't saved. Try again when you're back online. What you typed is still here." (`OFFLINE_SAVE_FAILED`), after the form's own checks, with focus back on Save and the typing untouched. The check is at the moment of saving (`navigator.onLine`), so it cannot disagree with what the button showed. The conflict dialog's Keep mine is `aria-disabled` with the same reason, and does nothing while offline. **Panels that save from a button of their own** (review fix) get the same through `useOfflineSave` (`form/useOfflineSave.tsx`: `aria-disabled`, the reason beside the button and as its description, the click ignored while offline): Internal notes (Add note), Needs advisor action, Status (Update status), Ask the prospect (Send request), a field's review buttons and Save correction, the quote request forms (Record answer, Add note, Record send, issue a new address) and the packet's Generate. Not covered: the firm pages' own buttons (members, settings, invitations, the outbox, held mail) and a button that is not a save; a later button-save uses `useOfflineSave` |
| D-183 | The conflict dialog (CRM-S2.4a, D-46, D-155). A save the database answers with `conflict: true` is not a failed save and not a toast: `save` throws `SaveConflict(conflict)` (`form/useWorkspaceForm.tsx`; a page checks the answer's `conflict` flag with `isConflict`, `src/lib/recordVersion.ts`) and the form, given `conflictDialog: { noun, fields, toValues? }`, opens `ConflictDialog` (`form/ConflictDialog.tsx`) as part of the form's `prompt`, so a page that already renders the prompt gets it. **The contract for every record form** (account, contact, lead and opportunity pass the same): `fields` is keyed by the form's own field names (a field added to the schema is a type error until it is named: its label and an optional `format` that turns a stored value into the words the form shows, a kind's code into its label; a changed field the page did not name is shown by its id in words, "Referral code", never the raw id); `toValues(current)` turns the record's editable fields, as the database answered them, into the form's values (omit it when the record's keys are the form's field names); each `changed` entry's `field` is read as a form field name. **What is compared** (review fix: the first version compared nothing but the database's `changed` list and so showed a field the person never touched as something they typed, hid the other person's edit to a field they did change, and let Keep mine put an old value back over their change unseen). The form holds the values the edit STARTED from (its defaults, `begin`'s values, or what a save left). A field is shown when EITHER side changed it: the other person's (`changed`) or the person's own (what was typed differs from the start). Columns: Field, Yours, Theirs, Changed by; a side that left the field alone says "You didn't change this" or "They didn't change this". The dialog's description also holds one line per field so the comparison is heard when it opens: "Kind of partner: you left it as Accountant, CPA or fractional CFO; Nevermore changed it to Other." (who is the history's own word: a member's email, "Nevermore", "System", never an id, D-33; times in the person's own zone). When `changed` is empty (a field changed and changed back is no difference) it says the other change was undone and still offers the choices. **Three choices, per field and not all or nothing.** **Keep mine** saves what the person CHANGED and keeps the other person's change on every field the person LEFT ALONE (those take their current values before the save), in one save from the version that holds their change: that version is sent once and becomes the form's own only if the save works (a save that fails, offline or on a refusal, leaves the form on the version it had, so the next Save finds the conflict again and opens the dialog; it never overwrites from the new version with no dialog). A third change meanwhile is a new conflict and a new dialog with the newest answer. **Keep theirs** takes their values for the fields THEY changed, starts from their version (`begin` with their values on those fields) and keeps whatever else the person typed, typed again over it; so a person who edited Name while the other changed Kind keeps their Name edit and takes the Kind. **Keep editing** closes the dialog and changes nothing: what was typed stays, the starting version is unchanged (so Save conflicts again until the person chooses), focus goes to Save. Escape, the way out with no button, IS Keep editing: nothing is chosen, saved or discarded by closing, and it does not ask "Discard your changes?" or close the row. A modal alert dialog (Radix `AlertDialog`): focus is trapped inside and starts on Keep editing (the one choice that changes nothing, which is where an alert dialog puts it), and it is named and described on opening, so it is announced once. Keep mine and Keep theirs are plain buttons and Keep editing is the dialog's cancel, so choosing one is never also a "close" that would say Keep editing. Focus afterwards: Keep theirs and Keep mine go to the form's FIRST field (the first key of the form's own values, not the answer's key order; during Keep mine's save Save is busy, so the field is where focus can be, and the save's own failure puts it back on Save), Keep editing to Save; after a save that closes a partner's row focus goes to the row's Edit button (it used to fall to the page). The old line "Someone else changed this partner while you were editing. Reload to see their change." is gone; the dialog is the same for every record that gets a version, and each form brings its own `fields` and `toValues` when it is built (D-24) |
| D-184 | What CRM-S2.4a does NOT meet, and what it left (D-24). **Criteria NOT met for things that exist today, for the owner to decide** (D-24 lets a criterion wait only for something a later epic creates, and these exist now): (1) "lists refresh on focus and every 60 seconds" holds for the queue only. The partners list, the activity page, the outbox, held mail and the members page load by their own effects and are not refreshed by themselves; putting each on the table frame (`useTableRows`) is the way, and it is not done here because each is a different shape (the outbox and activity have their own summaries and cursors; the partners page has open edit rows whose typing a refresh must not disturb, which needs its own proof with the dialog). (2) "saving is disabled with the reason" is done for every form on `useWorkspaceForm` and for the panel buttons listed in D-182, but not for the firm pages' own buttons (members, settings, invitations, the outbox, held mail). **Left to a later story (D-24)**: a selection kept through a refresh, CRM-S3.6; the conflict dialog on the account, contact, lead and opportunity forms, each form's own story (CRM-A2.1, CRM-C1.1, CRM-L2.1, CRM-O1.1), with its own `fields` and `toValues`. **Offline with a page not yet loaded** shows the banner and the loading state; the slow-load line (D-145) is what offers "Try again" |

## Epic 2 decisions (2026-11-13)

These came from reading Epic 2's stories against the code as it stands at Epic 1's tip, where P-1.1,
CRM-P2.1a to c, P-1.4, CRM-P1.3a and b, CRM-P2.3, CRM-P1.2 and CRM-P1.1 are merged and CRM-P1.5, P1.6, P1.4,
P1.8, P1.7, P2.2 and P2.4 are not. No Epic 2 story needs one of the unmerged ones. There are no tables yet for
leads, accounts, contacts, opportunities or quotes, so under D-24 each story builds its mechanism and applies it
to a page that exists: the submissions queue, a submission, referral partners, invitations, activity, the outbox.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-37 | Where the workspace's routes live. One layout route, the firm guard around the workspace shell, wraps the member pages, which keep their `/advisor/*` paths so links and end-to-end tests survive (D-8). A new section takes a top-level path when its epic ships (`/home`, `/quotes`, `/inbox`, `/leads`, `/accounts`, `/reports`); `/settings` exists now. Platform-admin pages (held mail, firms, organisations) stay outside the shell in today's look, and the shell shows a "Nevermore admin" link only to a platform admin |
| D-38 | What each menu item opens until its own epic. Quotes opens the queue `/advisor`, and a submission's page marks Quotes. Inbox opens the queue filtered to "has new PEO mail". Reports opens `/advisor/funnel`. Settings opens a new `/settings` page linking to Mailbox, Invitations, Activity (owners), Referral partners, PEOs and Outbox. New quote, Home, Leads and Accounts are not shown until the stories that build them (CRM-Q2.1, CRM-H1, CRM-L1.1, CRM-A1.1), so the menu has no dead buttons. The firm's name goes to `/advisor` |
| D-39 | What the menu's counts mean today. Quotes: quote requests with status sent, on intakes the person may see. Inbox: quote requests with new PEO mail, by the rule the queue's mark already uses. Leads has no count until CRM-L1.1 |
| D-40 | Choosing a firm. At a firm's address the browser sends that address with every request, and the database's membership helpers then also require that a row's firm is that address's firm. The address only narrows: membership is still the lock (D-18), and a request with no address behaves as today. The list of a person's own firms is not narrowed. On the bare domain a member of several firms sees "Choose a firm", a list linking to each firm's address. This ends the "kept out" rule of the ADR "the console asks for a membership" (renumbered 0014 by Epic 1), builds on ADR 0015 (at a firm's address the current firm is that firm), and is recorded as ADR 0016 in CRM-S1.1b |
| D-41 | The look. The grey-and-blue colours live in `src/index.css` under a `theme-workspace` class that the shell puts on the page's root element while it is mounted, so dialogs, sheets and messages drawn outside the shell take the same look. The employer's intake keeps today's colours |
| D-42 | The foot of the menu shows the person's name, or their email when they have no name, and their role (Owner, Lead, Agent). No team (D-20) |
| D-43 | Where a table's state lives. Page, sort, filters, view and search are in the page's address. A person's column choices are a row per firm, person and table in the database; saved views are rows a firm owner can share; built-in views live in code. Local storage is not used: "per person" means on any device |
| D-44 | Sort, filter and paging run in the database, 50 rows a page. Filters are matched by fixed SQL over a listed set of columns per table. No function builds SQL from what the browser sends |
| D-45 | A record's history and the firm's activity record are two things. The history holds a record's own fields, field by field, written only by a trigger that lists the fields to track (and, for an account's labels, by `account_labels_history`, D-237), so an intake's answers can never enter it; it is kept as long as the record and read by whoever may see the record. The activity record (CRM-P2.3) stays the record of who did what: no content, owners only, ninety days. Neither writes into the other. A Nevermore staff actor is stored and shown as in D-33 |
| D-46 | What "someone else changed it" compares. Each editable record has a whole-number version that a trigger raises on every change. A save names the version it started from; a mismatch is refused and returns the current values and who changed them |
| D-47 | CRM-S3.6 (selecting rows) and CRM-S3.7 (bulk edit) move to Epic 3, after CRM-A1.1. Every bulk action named needs something that does not exist yet: submissions have no owner (D-15), there are no labels or custom fields, and the queue's fields are intake answers or values the product works out |
| D-48 | What can be exported. Never the queue: its columns are an intake's answers (CRM-S3.8). An export runs through a database function that returns the rows and records the export in the same step, so the recorded count is true and the record cannot be skipped. The owners' alert and the fresh sign-in for a whole-table export that CRM-P2.3 and CRM-P1.3 deferred are built with it. First applied to referral partners and, for owners, the activity record |
| D-49 | Workspace lists are fetched through the query library the app already provides: refreshed on focus and every 60 seconds, keeping the rows on screen while the next arrive |
| D-50 | Small helpers (the address codec, filter operators, field rules, the CSV builder) are tested through the page or form that uses them, as D-13 says, not on their own |
| D-51 | Workspace forms are built on the form and validation libraries already installed (`react-hook-form`, `zod`, `src/components/ui/form.tsx`) |
| D-52 | At a firm's address, `/` sends a signed-in member to the workspace instead of the landing page |
| D-53 | Builders (founder, 2026-11-13, replacing D-14 from Epic 2 on). Opus builds database and server-function stories and does every review. Sonnet builds page and form stories. The orchestrator writes each story's brief, merges, and settles what a review finds |

### Epic 2 build order

A story marked a, b or c is one story of `docs/BROKER_CRM_STORIES.md` split by seam. Lanes: **Q** changes the
queue page and `src/components/workspace/table/`; **P** changes the partners and invitations pages; **D** is the
database. Stories in different lanes run side by side.

| # | Story | What it covers now, and where | Needs | Builder | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CRM-S1.1a | The shell: left menu, firm name and address, the person and role, a sheet at phone width, the current section marked, the grey-and-blue look, a title per page, the `/settings` page. Wraps every member page under `/advisor/*` | none | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 2 | CRM-S1.1b | One firm at a time (D-40): the address narrows the database's answers; a member of several firms is let in at a firm's address and chooses on the bare domain. ADR 0016 | none | Opus (D) | `20261117090000` |
| 3 | CRM-S3.3b | The queue's query in the database (D-44): sort by any column, filters as data, 50 a page, a "has new PEO mail" filter, counts for view tabs | none | Opus (D) | `20261117100000` |
| 4 | CRM-S5.2 | Record history (D-45): the table, the capture trigger, the reading function. Applied to referral partners | none | Opus (D) | `20261117110000` |
| 5 | CRM-S3.1b with S3.4b | Where column choices and saved views are kept (D-43) | none | Opus (D) | `20261117120000` |
| 6 | CRM-S1.1c | Menu counts (D-39), refreshed on focus and every 60 seconds | 1 | Opus (D) | `20261117130000` |
| 7 | CRM-S3.5a | The table frame: column definitions, the address codec, the table, "1 to 50 of N" with Previous and Next. The queue moves onto it | 1, 3 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 8 | CRM-S4.1 | Forms: "Required" in words, errors under the field, focus to the first error, asking before closing with unsaved changes, edit in place. Applied to the referral-partner and invitation forms | 1 | Sonnet (P) | none |
| 9 | CRM-S2.1 | Loading: grey rows shaped like the columns, announced once, "taking longer than usual" after ten seconds. Queue, submission, activity, outbox | 7 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 10 | CRM-S3.2a | Sort: headers that sort and show the direction; the sort is in the address | 7 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 11 | CRM-S2.4b | The version for the conflict check (D-46), on referral partners | 4 | Opus (D) | `20261117140000` |
| 12 | CRM-S2.2 | Empty: "nothing yet" and "nothing matches". Queue, activity, outbox, invitations | 9 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 13 | CRM-S3.3a | Filter: the row of filters, chips, "Add filter", operators by kind of field, filters in the address. Today's status, action and waiting links keep working | 10, 3 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 14 | CRM-S2.3 | Something went wrong: failed load with "Try again", a failed panel, a failed save, "This submission isn't available" | 12 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 15 | CRM-S2.4a | Out of date: lists that refresh in place (D-49), the no-connection banner, the conflict dialog on the partner form. Done (D-181 to D-184) | 8, 11 | Sonnet (P) | none |
| 16 | CRM-S3.1a | Columns: show, hide, reorder; the first column stays; kept per person | 13, 5 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 17 | CRM-S3.4a | Saved views: tabs with counts; today's status tabs are the built-in views; save, rename, delete, share | 16, 5, 3 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 18 | CRM-S3.8b | Export in the database (D-48), the owners' alert, the fresh sign-in | 2 | Opus (D) | `20261117150000` |
| 19 | CRM-S3.8a | The Export button and the CSV, on referral partners and the activity record | 16, 18 | Sonnet (P) | none |

Under D-105 the migration timestamps in the table above are replaced by "sort after the newest, in build order". What the built rows actually shipped as: row 4 (CRM-S5.2) `20261118130000` and `20261118130500`; row 6 (CRM-S1.1c) `20261118140000` and `20261118140500`; row 11 (CRM-S2.4b) `20261118160000`.

**What each later story does to the table frame** (CRM-S3.5a, after its reviews). "Fills in" means the story adds its part in a place the frame already has; "must change the frame" means it adds something the frame does not have yet.

| Story | Fills in, or must change the frame |
|---|---|
| CRM-S2.1 | Fills in: the loading state, now drawn BY the frame (D-146): one `DataTable` always, grey rows in its body while it waits, from the frame's own `columns`, `sort` and `renderHeader`; the frame owns the slow-load wait and hands the toolbar `{ loading, slowLine }`. Changes it made to the frame: `toolbar` may be a function, `DataTable` takes optional `columns` and `placeholder`, the frame takes optional `columns`, the `loading` slot is gone, `tableIsLoading` is exported and `useRefreshTable` cancels a stuck request first |
| CRM-S3.2a | Done (D-147 to D-151): `DataTable` takes `onSort` and draws a header button for each column with a `sortField` (`renderHeader` still overrides it); the frame passes `onSort` (a `change({ sort })` that pushes when the column is new and replaces when it only reverses, D-148), shows `address.sort ?? defaultSort` in the headers, and draws `OrderLine` ("Order: Newest first" and "Back to newest first"); `FieldDefinition.firstDirection` and `firstSortDirection` say which way a first click goes. The same `DataTable` draws the loading state, so the headers work over the grey rows |
| CRM-S2.2 | Done (D-158 to D-165): the `empty(address, { focusTable })` slot, now drawn BY the frame (D-146's pattern): one `DataTable` always, with the empty state as one cell under the real headers (`colSpan` all columns), never a different element. The frame hands `empty` the address the empty rows ANSWER (the previous one while the next answer is on its way, so the words never describe a question not yet answered) and `focusTable` for a button that makes the state go away. The Pager is not drawn when empty. Pieces: `NothingYet`, `NothingMatches`, `EmptyBody` (the one empty cell the frame and the plain tables share) and `clearLabelFor` (`states/EmptyStates.tsx`), and `filterInWords`, `inForceInWords`, `isNarrowed` (`table/words.ts`, exported from the table index) |
| CRM-S3.3a | Done (D-166 to D-173): `FilterRow` (with `FilterEditor` and `filterInput.ts`) in `src/components/workspace/table/`, which a page places in its toolbar; `TableDefinition.listedFilters` (required) names the fields shown as buttons always, and "Add filter" offers every other field. Chips are worded by `filterInWords(table.fields, filter, names)` so the empty state and the chips cannot word a filter differently. Changes it made to the frame: `changeAddress` takes `heldFilters` (which held filters stay; `AddressChange`), and the codec exports `readValue`, `escapeText` and `writeFilter` for the editor. The queue's checkboxes are gone and its empty state needed no change (D-161). Offering a person field (`any_of`, `me`, `my team`) needs the names lookup below (CRM-A1.1) |
| CRM-X3.1 | Must supply: its custom fields in `fields` with their kind, label and (for a choice) values, and `fieldsStatus` "loading" until they arrive. The filter row then offers them in "Add filter" with their kind's operators and needs nothing else; a filter on one that is not loaded yet is a held chip (D-169). It may add one to `listedFilters` through its definition hook: the type is the table's own field ids, so a definition whose `fields` type holds the loaded ids lists one with no cast |
| CRM-A1.1 | Must supply: an `owner` field of kind `person` whose people come as its `choices` (`{ value: user id, label: name }`, which the editor lists and the chip words) or as the `names` lookup (D-173; without `choices` the editor offers only "is me" and "is my team"), "Owner" in Accounts' `listedFilters`, and the database accepting person filters; also a way to name people for a person field, `filterInWords(fields, filter, names)` and `inForceInWords(table, address, names)` (an id is never shown; without `names` a person reads in quotes as written), and the "me" and "my team" filters (D-24, S3.1 and S3.3) |
| CRM-S2.3 | Done (D-175 to D-180): the `error(error, retry, address)` slot (a refused query stays the frame's own panel) is filled by every page with `LoadFailed`, and the frame gives it its own retry, so nothing in the address changes. The frame itself needed only its comment changed. The two panel-level "Loading the PEOs' profiles" lines (`PeoNeedsPanel`, `ManuscriptPanel`) now use the shared placeholder pieces (D-146, D-177) |
| CRM-S2.4a | Done (D-181 to D-184): fills in the table frame's failure slot and refresh: over rows already on screen a failed refresh is `RefreshFailed`, a note over the rows, not the panel (the frame decides: an error with a page of rows is the note, one with nothing to show is the `error` slot as before), and a table with nothing to show is asked again only by "Try again" (`useTableRows`, `keepsAsking`; `isRetrying`). Changes it made to the frame: `TableFrame` draws the note, `useTableRows` returns `isRetrying`, nothing is added to a table's definition. A page needs nothing to get it |
| CRM-S3.1a | Must change the frame: keep the first (name) column in view when the table scrolls sideways (the queue has ten columns and none is sticky; CRM-S3.2a left it to this story), wire the visible and ordered columns list into `DataTable` (which already takes an optional `columns` since CRM-S2.1) and pass the same list to `TableLoading`, keep the first column (`isName`) pinned. Done (D-217): `TableView.columns` is CRM-S3.4a's (D-142) |
| CRM-S3.4a | Must change the frame: add a view's `sort` and `columns` to `TableView`, make `queryFor` and `sameAddress` apply the view's sort (a view's sort is the table's order while the address holds none: so `shownSort` in `TableFrame`, which today is `address.sort ?? table.defaultSort`, becomes `address.sort ?? view.sort ?? table.defaultSort`; `OrderLine`'s "Back to" then goes to the VIEW's order, and its label is the view's; the sort click keeps writing `sort` to the address, and a view whose sort differs from the address's is the "modified view" `sameAddress` detects), and extend `ViewTabs` with saving, renaming and sharing; its definition hook follows the contract in D-141. A saved view stores exactly the address's filters (the same `filter` texts, `viewFilters` of it are the tab's own). While a saved view is active its filters are the tab's own, so they are not chips (D-169); a person's further filters show as chips over it, "Clear filters" removes those and leaves the view, and "modified view" is `sameAddress` differing from the view's filters. "Save this as a view" can offer the chips' filters as the new view's |
| CRM-S3.8a | Fills in: the Export button with each column's `text`, and its own server call (D-48) for every matching row, not the page on screen |


### Open questions for the owner (CRM-S3.3a review; behaviour NOT changed)

- **The Inbox mark and extra filters.** `menu.ts`'s `isInbox` marks Inbox current for any address that holds `has_new_peo_mail:is:true`, even with more filters or a tab on top, so the menu's Inbox badge (every intake with new PEO mail) and the rows shown can disagree. This predates CRM-S3.3a; the filter row makes it easier to reach. Decide whether Inbox is current only when that is the one filter in force.
- **Yes or no takes two steps.** The queue's three yes-or-no filters took one click as checkboxes; as listed filters they open an editor (Yes or No, then Apply). The editor's condition and Apply are what the story's "each shows as a chip with its value" and the other kinds need, but for a yes or no a one-click "Yes" could be offered. Decide whether to add it.

### The table address

Every workspace table keeps its whole state in the page's search parameters (D-43), read and written by one codec,
`src/components/workspace/table/address.ts`. A later table (leads, accounts, quotes, the inbox, a list inside a record) copies
this: it gives the frame a `TableDefinition` (its columns, its fields with their kinds, its built-in views, what a `view` id may be)
and gets the address, the rows, the table and the pager. Nothing writes its own parameters. The order below is the order the codec
writes; a default is never written.

| Parameter | Holds | Written as | Read leniently |
|---|---|---|---|
| `view` | A built-in view's id, or (CRM-S3.4a) a saved view's id; none is the table's "All" | `view=won` | An id the table doesn't know is dropped |
| `q` | The search words | `q=pat+gamma` | Trimmed, at most 200 characters (counted as whole characters, never cut through one); shorter than the table's minimum (3 letters for the queue, spaces not counted) is no search and is dropped |
| `filter` | One filter, repeated: a field, an operator and a value, joined by `:`, written in one canonical order (sorted by their text) | `filter=needs_action:is:true` | A field not in the table's list, an operator its kind doesn't take, a value of the wrong shape (blank text, a blank list value, text over 200 characters), a repeat, or more than 20 together with the view's own filters are dropped |
| `sort` | One field; a leading `-` means descending. A header click writes it (pushing a history entry, back to page 1); "Back to newest first" removes it | `sort=-submitted_at` | A field not in the table's list is dropped |
| `page` | The page, from 2 (page 1 has no parameter) | `page=3` | Not a whole number from 2 to 100,000 (the database's limit) is page 1; one past the end is rewritten to the last page once the total is known |

How a filter's value is written, by the operator's shape: text as it is; a number as digits; a day as `YYYY-MM-DD` (a real day);
a pair (`between`) as `a,b`; a count of days (`in_next_days`, `in_last_days`) as a whole number from 0 to 3,660; a list
(`any_of`, `none_of`, 1 to 100 values) as values joined by `,`; yes or no as `true` or `false`; an operator that takes no value
(`is_empty`, `me`, `my_team`) has none (`filter=missing_required:is_empty`). In every part of a filter (the field, the operator and each value), a backslash keeps a `:`, a `,` or a
backslash from being read as a separator (`filter=company_name:contains:Smith\, Jones`), so a future custom field's id can hold any character. Text values are trimmed as the database trims them, and a NUL (U+0000, which Postgres text cannot hold) is removed wherever text enters. The operators of each kind are those of
`src/lib/tableQuery.ts`; the database holds the same list and refuses what the codec lets through but the database won't take (its reason is shown, D-138). The limits the codec reads by (page 100,000; 20 filters; 200 characters; 100 list values; 3,660 days) are the migration's figures, and `src/lib/queueApi.test.ts` reads the migration and compares them.

Changing anything but the page (the search, a filter, the sort, the view) goes back to page 1: the writer does it
(`changeAddress`), not each control. A page change pushes a history entry; a link (a tab) pushes one; a sort pushes one when it puts a new column in the address and
replaces it when it only reverses that column; the search replaces it, and the filter row's apply, remove and clear push one (D-168). So Back undoes a page, a tab, a filter step and a
sort (however many times its column was reversed), not each keystroke.

The queue's old parameters, accepted as the same state (so old links, bookmarks and the menu's Inbox link still land on the right
rows) and rewritten as the new ones on arrival:

| Old | Now |
|---|---|
| `status=<stage>` | `view=<stage>` (the stage's own name, such as `under_review`) |
| `action=1` | `filter=needs_action:is:true` |
| `waiting=1` | `filter=waiting_on_peo:is:true` |
| `mail=new` | `filter=has_new_peo_mail:is:true` |
| `sort=oldest_waiting` | `sort=oldest_waiting_on` |
| `sort=earliest_expiry` | `sort=earliest_quote_expires_on` |
| `q`, `page` | unchanged (a search under 3 characters is dropped) |

What the page says about a view or field it dropped, and what it does with a refused query, is in D-138 and D-141. The new parameters win when an address holds both: an old parameter adds a filter only on a field the new filters don't already name. Any other parameter is dropped. The codec is tested through the queue's page
(`src/pages/advisor/AdvisorDashboardPage.test.tsx`), as D-50 says.

What a table gives the frame (`TableDefinition`): its id and noun; its `fields` (each field's label, kind and choices, the one place they live); its `columns` (each by id, with the field it sorts and filters by, and an optional plain `text` for export; a column with no `sortField` has a plain header); its `listedFilters` (the filters the row always shows) ; its `defaultSort` (or null) and `defaultSortLabel`; its `views` and `allViewLabel`; `searchMinChars`; the old-parameter mapping; and, for what is loaded from the database, `viewsStatus` and `fieldsStatus` (D-141). The page gives the frame `context` for its cells and the one call that fetches a page, which receives the effective query (D-142).

Where the later stories fill the frame (`src/components/workspace/table/`): CRM-S2.1 the `loading` slot, CRM-S2.2 the `empty` slot, CRM-S2.3 the `error` slot (done: `LoadFailed`; a refused query is the frame's own panel); CRM-S3.2a the sort headers (done: `onSort` and `OrderLine`); CRM-S3.3a the filter row in the toolbar (done: `FilterRow`; the address carries `filter`, each field's label, kind and choices are in `fields`, and `listedFilters` says which are always shown); CRM-S3.1a the columns menu (`ColumnDefinition.isName` marks the column that can't be hidden); CRM-S3.4a the saved views: it adds them to `views` through the table's hook with `viewsStatus` "loading" until they arrive, and extends `ViewTabs`; CRM-X3.1 custom fields through `fields` and `fieldsStatus`; CRM-S3.6 row selection (a marked place in `DataTable`, D-47); CRM-S3.8a the Export button, reading each column's `text`.

**What each story leaves to a later one (D-24).** S1.1a: New quote to CRM-Q2.1, Home to CRM-H1, Leads to
CRM-L1.1, Accounts to CRM-A1.1, "an opportunity marks Accounts" to CRM-O1.1, the team under the person's name to
CRM-X1.1. S1.1c: the Leads count to CRM-L1.1, unread mail across every thread to CRM-E1.4, open quotes by the new
statuses to CRM-Q3.1, the Inbox link's real filter (and removing the line the queue shows meanwhile), which CRM-S3.5a did when it moved the queue onto the table frame (D-136). S2.1 and S2.2: import as a second action to CRM-X4.1; empty lists inside a record to
CRM-A2.1, CRM-C1.1 and CRM-O1.1. S2.3: "This account isn't available" and the other record pages' not-available states to CRM-A2.1, CRM-L2.1, CRM-O1.1 and CRM-Q3.1, the failed lists and saves inside a record to the story that builds each, and a failed refresh over rows on screen to CRM-S2.4a (done, D-181). S2.4: keeping a selection through a refresh to CRM-S3.6; the conflict check on
accounts, contacts, leads and opportunities to the story that builds each one's form. S3.1 and S3.3: custom
fields to CRM-X3.1; "me" and "my team" filters to CRM-A1.1. S3.2, S3.4: each table's default sort, unsortable
columns and built-in views to that table's story (the queue's are in D-150). S3.2: "the sort is kept with the saved view" to CRM-S3.4a. S3.8: exports of accounts, leads and quotes to CRM-A1.1,
CRM-L1.1 and CRM-Q4.1; exporting a selection to CRM-S3.6. S4.1: "a contact needs an email or a phone" to
CRM-A2.2, "an opportunity needs its account" to CRM-A2.3, the phone, state and date fields to CRM-A1.2, edit in
place on a record's page to CRM-A2.1. S5.2: history on each of the five records to the story that creates its
table; a screen that shows history to CRM-A2.1 and CRM-L2.1.

**What CRM-P2.4 leaves to a later story (D-24).** "Can't export a firm's tables": exports do not exist until CRM-S3.8b (D-48), so P2.4 builds the refusal and tests that one will call it. CRM-S3.8b's export function must call `refuse_support_session(firm)` before it reads or records anything, and `supabase/tests/help_from_nevermore.sql` calls every function with "export" in its name as a support session at the firm's address and fails on one that does not raise PT423 (D-128): a call with a firm, never NULL. A file the browser builds from a firm's rows (the census, the answers as JSON, a PEO's files, the bundle) is a download the same way: during a support session the workspace offers no download control (D-123), so CRM-S3.8b's Export button is one more control that hides itself. Likewise every later story that adds a way to send as a member or to change a firm's data asks the guard (`assert_firm_writable`, or `refuse_support_session` where it has no firm write) and adds itself to the guarded list of `supabase/tests/close_a_firm.sql` and to the tables of `supabase/tests/help_from_nevermore.sql`. The two bulk actions of CRM-S3.8a (the Export button) hide themselves for the role `support`.

**Shared pieces, one owner each.** The shell, the menu's list and the theme class: S1.1a
(`src/components/workspace/`). Sending the firm address and the "Choose a firm" page: S1.1b. The table's column
type, address codec, table and pager: S3.5a (`src/components/workspace/table/`), which fixes the whole shape of
a table's address (page, sort, filters, view, search) so later stories only fill it in. Page states:
`src/components/workspace/states/`, each by the S2 story that names it. Forms:
`src/components/workspace/form/`, S4.1. Each story's calls to the database go in a file of its own under
`src/lib/`, not in `src/lib/advisorApi.ts`.

## Epic 3 decisions (2026-11-14)

These came from grilling Epic 3's stories against the glossary, the ADRs, the PRD and the code at Epic 2's tip
(`main` at the merge of #139 and #140). The founder took every recommended answer. Nothing for Epic 3 existed:
no table, page or function for accounts, contacts or opportunities. Four Epic 2 rows were never built (D-205).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-185 | A **quote** on the workspace's screens is a quote request with the quote it returns (glossary). There is no new quotes table: the quote requests are the quotes |
| D-186 | What a later epic builds stays off Epic 3's screens (D-24, D-38: no dead buttons): leads, renewals (the Renewal column and form field, the Renewals tab, "Renewing in 120 days"), custom fields, the opportunity's page, email (Email buttons, the emails panel), New opportunity, Home's tasks. Each later story adds its part where Epic 3 leaves the place for it |
| D-187 | Until the opportunity's page (CRM-O1.1, Epic 4), an opportunity's name opens its submission's page when it has one, and is plain text when it has none |
| D-188 | An opportunity made with a quote is named for its start month and "PEO search" ("March 2027 PEO search"), or "PEO search" when no start is known |
| D-189 | Builders (founder, 2026-11-14, replacing D-53 for Epic 3). Sonnet builds every Epic 3 story at high effort, database stories included. Opus reviews every story. A story that fails its review twice is rebuilt by Opus. The riskiest for Sonnet are named in the build order: CRM-Q1.1, CRM-Q1.3, CRM-S3.7, CRM-S4.3 |
| D-190 | Branches as D-2 and D-3: `crm/epic-03-accounts-contacts`, cut from `main` (Epic 2 is merged there), and one `crm/03/<story id>` per story with its pull request into the epic branch |
| D-191 | Epic 3's plan lives on this page, as Epics 1 and 2 did. No issue per story |
| D-192 | An intake an employer started gets its account, contact and opportunity when it is first certified, in the same step. The database rule is that every submission and every quote request sits on one opportunity. Amends CRM-Q1.1's "every intake belongs to one opportunity" (ADR 0025) |
| D-193 | The duplicate match is exact, never by likeness (as D-26): the web domain without `www.` and never a free-mail domain; the name with its last word dropped when that word is a legal designator (LLC, Inc, Corp, Co, Ltd, LLP, PC or PLLC, with or without punctuation), and only then in lower case, letters and digits only, with the same state, so "Costco" never matches "Cost"; or a contact's email, ignoring case (glossary) |
| D-194 | A contact made from a prospect keeps a link to that prospect, so the same person is never added twice to an account (the link is unique per account: one prospect can be a contact on two accounts); the name, email and phone are copied once and never kept in step |
| D-195 | Bringing existing submissions in (CRM-Q1.3) gives each submission its own account. Where two match by D-193, the later one is marked "Possible duplicate of" the earlier for an agent to merge or keep. Nothing is merged by guesswork |
| D-196 | An account made from a submission (CRM-Q1.1 and CRM-Q1.3) has its empty fields filled from the certified answers (legal name, address, states of operation, employees, industry, current provider), never the FEIN, the census or anything about an employee. CRM-Q1.2 (Epic 5) keeps the rest: filling again on each certification, and "The intake says 23" with "Use it" where the account differs. Once copied, a value is the firm's own field: shown, searched and exported as one |
| D-197 | An account's status in Epic 3 is a prospect account or a client (once one of its submissions is won). A former client arrives with the placement record (CRM-O1.3, CRM-A2.4, Epic 4) |
| D-198 | Labels are built in Epic 3: the firm's own list of words, made by typing one, any number on an account. With bulk edit (CRM-S3.7) and the Label column and filter on accounts. Renaming or deleting a word from the firm's list comes with Settings (Epic 9) |
| D-199 | An account's Files tab lists its intakes' files that passed the virus check, each under its own access rule. Uploading a file to an account is a new story, CRM-A2.5, for later |
| D-200 | A note can be edited only by the person who wrote it, for 24 hours; after that it is fixed and "Add to note" appends a dated addition. Calls and tasks stay editable. Anyone who may see a task's record can mark it done |
| D-201 | An account's last activity is the newest of: its creation; a call, note or task logged or completed on it, its contacts or its opportunities; a quote request's status changing; and, from Epic 6, an email. Editing a field is not activity |
| D-202 | Undoing a bulk change (ten minutes, or until the person leaves the page) puts back every row nobody has changed since, and skips and names the rest, as the dialog names skipped rows before the change |
| D-203 | A merge is made by anyone allowed to change both records. The removed record is deleted; its history moves to the kept record marked as from the merged record; its address leads to the kept record. There is no unmerge |
| D-204 | "Deleted by its owner" (CRM-A1.1) means the account's record owner or a firm owner. Its contacts, calls, notes and tasks are deleted with it. *Who may delete is now D-209's level rule (D-235)* |
| D-205 | Epic 2's rows 16 to 19 were never built and come first in Epic 3, on the pages Epic 2 named: CRM-S3.1a (the columns menu, the first column kept in view), CRM-S3.4a (saved views on screen), CRM-S3.8b (export in the database, D-48, refusing a support session, D-128) and CRM-S3.8a (the Export button and the CSV). The database half of columns and saved views exists (`table_settings`); no screen uses it |
| D-206 | Until CRM-O1.2 an opportunity's stage is worked out from its submission's pipeline stage, never stored: draft, submitted, under review and information requested are Intake under way; ready for market is Ready to send; sent to PEOs is In market; quoted is Quoted; won is Won; lost and archived are Lost. Reopening an archived submission brings its opportunity back. An opportunity is open until it is won or lost |
| D-207 | An opportunity holds at most one intake, by a constraint (D-29). Next year's renewal is a new opportunity on the same account |
| D-208 | "Prospect" stays on screens as an account's status. In writing it is "a prospect account", never the person the glossary calls the Prospect |
| D-209 | Who may change a record follows level whatever the firm's visibility: an agent their own, a lead their team's, an owner the firm's; a record with no owner, an owner only. That covers its fields, its owner, archiving, deleting and moving a contact. Adding a contact, call, note or task is open to anyone who may see the record. Owners assign unowned records, in bulk with CRM-S3.7. Single changes and bulk changes ask the same rule |
| D-210 | Only two contacts on the same account can be merged. Contacts on two accounts are moved first, or their accounts merged |
| D-211 | An account's source is set by the product and never edited: Employer's intake (with the referral partner's name when the intake came by a partner's link), Added by hand, and later New quote, Lead and Import. Brought-in submissions are Employer's intake |
| D-212 | An account's Activity tab is one list, newest first, of its calls, notes and tasks (from Epic 6 its emails) and its record history, filtered by All, Calls, Notes, Tasks or Changes. Until CRM-E1.5 it holds the history alone. It is never the firm activity record |
| D-213 | A submission's page shows "Account: <name>", linking to its account (CRM-A2.1b) |
| D-214 | Settled with the above. Changing an account's owner offers to change its open opportunities' owner too, as CRM-A1.1 says. A prospect's contact is the main contact of an account made from a submission. Records made from an employer's intake or brought in have no owner until assigned (Edgar Allan Advisory sees everything, D-15). A new account's owner is the person who made it unless they choose another. Selecting rows and bulk edit go on the accounts table only. Search's recent records are kept in the database per person (D-43). Every new table has its two-firm probe (D-28), its function registry entries and its line in `firm_closing_plan()` |
| D-215 | How three stories run side by side (D-25). Each builder works in its own working copy on its own branch, with its own Postgres cluster on its own port (`run.sh`'s database names are fixed, so two runs on one server collide) and its own `E2E_DATABASE`, `E2E_GATEWAY_PORT` and `E2E_APP_PORT`. Its migration takes the timestamp in the table below. Before it merges, the epic branch is merged into it and every SQL test's migration list is brought up to date (`scripts/sqlMigrationOrder.ts`), then the gate is run again |
| D-216 | A submission archived straight from won keeps its opportunity Won and its account a Client; archived from any other stage is Lost (decided by the last move into archived from another stage in the submission's status history; a row from archived to archived, such as marking it as needing action, is not a move). Amends D-206's "lost and archived are Lost", so D-197's "a client once won" holds when a won file is filed away (orchestrator, from CRM-Q1.1a's review) |
| D-217 | Columns (CRM-S3.1a, #144). A person's choice is kept as every shown column's id in order, the name column first. On reading, the name column is put first, an id the table does not have is dropped, and a list with no known id falls back to the table's own columns. A column added to a table later is hidden for anyone with a kept choice until they show it, so column ids are a stored contract: never renamed or reused. Nothing can be changed until the kept choice has arrived; after a failed load the usual columns show read-only with Try again. Saves are written one after another and each change saves at once. A column choice is the firm's data (`table_preferences`): a closing firm and a support session are refused (PT423) and the screen shows the database's words |
| D-218 | Export (CRM-S3.8b, #145). One database function, `export_rows(firm, table, filters, ids)`, returns the rows and records the export (`export_made`, with a `row_count` tied to the kind by a check) in one step. A closing firm can export: CRM-P1.7 offers it its tables, and the only write is the firm's record, which ADR 0023 keeps writable; a support session is refused first. A whole table is a call with no ids and asks for a sign-in within 12 hours (from the token's `amr`, failing closed); a selection of up to 1,000 ids does not, so "Select all N that match" (CRM-S3.7) goes as filters with no ids. The owners' alert fires above 500 rows or at exactly the third export by one member in an hour, ordered by an advisory lock that a two-session test proves; a member with no email is named "A member", so the alert is never lost. At most 50,000 rows a call; the firm's record takes a period. Partners export the page's two 30-day counts. The three link-free firm notices (`export_alert`, `member_left`, `firm_closing`) need only a sender (`firmNotice`), not `APP_BASE_URL`. A later table adds a registry line, a reader that applies visibility itself, its `fn_class` entry and the client's table name |
| D-219 | The CRM records (CRM-Q1.1a, #143, #146, #155). Accounts, contacts and opportunities each carry their firm and a nullable record owner; an account is seen by its own owner's level, a contact and an opportunity by their account's (CRM-S5.1). No API role writes them: every write is a later story's function. A contact needs an email, a phone or a mobile; at most one main contact per account and one contact per prospect per account are unique indexes; an opportunity holds at most one intake and an intake at most one opportunity, and the intake must be of the opportunity's firm by a composite foreign key, so an intake an opportunity holds can no longer be moved to another firm. Deleting an account is refused while it has an opportunity and deletes its contacts; deleting an intake leaves its opportunity at Qualifying. An account's source has no default and is never edited. Stage and status codes: `qualifying`, `intake_under_way`, `ready_to_send`, `in_market`, `quoted`, `won`, `lost`; `prospect`, `client`. D-216's "move into archived" ignores rows from archived to archived, which `set_submission_needs_action` and `sync_submission_workflow` both write. The web domain treats only `http:` and `https:` as a scheme without a slash. The opportunity's main-contact link is deferrable, for the merge (CRM-S4.3b) |
| D-220 | Saved views (CRM-S3.4a, #150; fixes to follow). Saving keeps the filters in force (the open view's own and the chips), the sort the rows are in and the person's kept columns. Tabs count under the filters on screen (D-137). A tab link to a view with its own sort drops the address's sort, and a sort equal to the view's own is no sort. A saved view the table cannot read (a field, operator or choice it lacks) is drawn as a tab that cannot open, saying why, and keeps Delete and Stop sharing for whoever may; it is left out of the counts so it cannot blank the others. "Keep the changes" rewrites a view through `change_saved_view`. Views are asked again on focus and every 60 seconds |
| D-221 | Process (orchestrator, 2026-11-14). The founder merges story pull requests into the epic branch once CI is green, so a story's pull request is opened only after its Opus review is Ready and its fixes are in. A story merged before its review finished gets its fixes in a follow-up pull request (`crm/03/<id>-review-fixes`). Merged migrations are not edited, even for a comment |
| D-222 | An account's page in the database (CRM-A2.1a, #158). `can_change_firm_record(firm, owner)` is the level rule every later write calls (D-209): an agent their own, a lead their own, their team's and the teams' under it (current members only), an owner the firm's; no owner or a departed owner, owners only; the visibility setting is never read and Nevermore support changes nothing. An account the caller may not see reads as nothing, the same as a missing one; its save answers PT404, and one they see but may not change 42501 in words. The Files tab lists scan verdict `clean` only (ADR 0002), with links signed per file by `documentUrl`. An archived account's fields can be edited, but nothing can be added to it: CRM-A2.2, CRM-A2.3 and CRM-E1.5 refuse new records while `archived_at` is set. The save holds the row so a second save from the same version gets the first's answer (two-session test). The source partner comes from the account's first intake |
| D-223 | The Export button (CRM-S3.8a, #160). A file holds the page's shown columns in the order shown, worded as the page words them. Cells are quoted for `,` `;` `"` and line breaks, and a cell starting with `=` `+` `-` `@`, tab, CR, LF or the full-width `＝＋－＠` gets a leading apostrophe. Files start with a byte order mark; booleans are yes and no. Export is offered only when the page has rows. A whole-table export after 12 hours steps up in place by the authenticator code, or else signs in again on this device only with the filters kept; there is no password step-up, because a password sign-in without its second factor opens no firm's data (D-19). A broker's sign-in return path keeps a safe query string |
| D-224 | Saved views, after review (CRM-S3.4a, #159). "Keep the changes" changes a view's filters and sort only, never its own columns (replaces #150's decision 6). A view the table cannot read is drawn as a tab that cannot open, with why, keeping Delete and Stop sharing, and is never counted. Column writes are tracked per firm, table and view, and the columns key carries the view's revision. Delete's focus goes to Cancel |
| D-225 | Main and Epic 3 (founder, 2026-11-14, #161). `main` built CRM-S3.1a, CRM-S3.8b and CRM-S3.8a separately (#142, #147); Epic 3's versions win. `main`'s three export migrations stay as history, byte for byte. `20261118180000_epic3_export_supersedes.sql` locks the firm's record and the outbox, holds `main`'s recorded exports, and removes `main`'s export objects. `20261119090500_epic3_export_carried.sql` puts the records back in Epic 3's shape after Epic 3's export migration. A fresh database matches Epic 3's alone. `main`'s decisions are M-185 to M-242 below. A database that ran Epic 3's migrations without `main`'s must be reset or have the five migrations marked applied (#161 lists where to check) |
| D-226 | The chain at certification (CRM-Q1.1b, #162). The chain is made at an intake's first certification, in the same transaction, in the intake's firm. That holds for a closing, suspended or support-session firm too, because certification is the employer's act: no `assert_firm_writable`. A closing firm's chain is deleted when the closing ends. A closed firm (`firm_is_closed`, after a FOR KEY SHARE lock on its row) gets no chain, and the certification still succeeds. A failure in the chain fails the certification (ADR 0025). The duplicate match (D-193) compares a website only as a host-shaped domain that is not free mail or a shared host (facebook.com, linkedin.com, sites.google.com and the others listed in `is_shared_web_host`), a listed domain's subdomains included. The website is copied to the account (beyond D-196). A name matches only with a state on both sides, the headquarters state. The earliest match is named, archived accounts included. Values come from the latest certification. The contact is left out only when the prospect has no usable email and no phone. With no company name, the sign-up name, else "Unnamed business", which never matches. One advisory lock per firm orders two chains made at once, so CRM-Q1.3 brings submissions in in batches. An intake that holds an opportunity cannot move to another firm. For Epic 5: an intake certified in a closed firm leaves no records. Two free-mail lists exist (`is_free_mail_domain`, `is_consumer_organisation`); one list when either is next restated |
| D-227 | The accounts table's query (CRM-A1.1b, #164). It changes no planner setting: every per-account column (main contact, open opportunity, quotes, status, last activity, contact text) is a `LATERAL` lookup by the account's own id, so a small firm reads only its own rows, and a test counts the rows read. The Opportunity and Quotes columns and "Waiting on PEO" are for the account's open opportunity shown: the open one with the earliest target start, one with no start after one with, the first made on a tie. "Sent" is a quote request not closed, "back" is quoted, as on the queue. Source sorts in its choice order. The firm is each call's first argument, resolved by `firm_to_act_for`. A former member owns nothing in the Owner column, filter and sort. "My team" is the caller's team and the teams under it, whoever is on them; a caller on no team has none. A current member with no name and no email reads "A member". `firm_member_choices` names every current member to any member, since the owner pickers need it. Archived accounts are in no view but Archived; an export by ids includes them. Last activity is `account_activity_times(firm, account)`, which CRM-E1.5 extends. The client checks every answer with zod, and a malformed answer is a failed load. The "my team" rule is written here and in `can_see_firm_record`; one helper when that function is next restated |
| D-228 | An account's page on screen (CRM-A2.1b, #163). Row 11 needed a migration after all: `20261119145000_account_tab.sql`, `account_tab(firm, account, tab, limit, offset)`, a read over `account_tab_rows` with the visibility rule. Tabs and Files load 50 rows a page by offset, never more than 100 a call (D-44, D-138). A page that comes back empty for the account makes the whole tab "not available". A row repeated across pages is shown once, and the tab is asked again once. A `?contact=` loads on by itself for 500 rows at most, then says so. The tab and the contact in view are in the address, and choosing a tab replaces the address. The page draws only an answer whose id is the address's, so an edit never reaches another account. On the Overview, a failed read fails the whole page. A download from Files records `document_downloaded`. `/accounts/:id` is private (no-index). A submission's "Account:" line is kept through a refresh and replaced only by a successful answer |
| D-229 | Saved views and the Export button, after review (#165). A saved view's choices are checked only against the lists the database enforces (`choicesEnforced`). Save and Keep compare view, filters and sort only (`sameQuestion`), and both wait until the current address's rows have arrived. A pending change keeps its lock until it settles, even with its dialog closed. A settled column write cancels and asks again every columns answer for the firm, table and view. A broker sign-in that names a page, `/advisor` included, returns there when the mailbox connection fails, and a notice in the shell says so on every page but the mailbox page. After "Sign in again", the export stays one click (D-223) |
| D-230 | The export handoff, after review (#166; amends D-225). `20261118180500_epic3_export_pause.sql` refuses a new export (55000, in words) while `main`'s entries are held, and `20261119090600_epic3_export_pause_end.sql` removes the pause. The held table has a unique index on its ids. The dispatcher reads an export alert in either shape, `main`'s (`table`) or Epic 3's (`table_label`). For this release, `dispatch-outbox` is deployed before the migrations are pushed. One gap remains, lasting one export's read: an owner's export of the firm's record started during the push should be made again. A database that ran Epic 3's export before #161 is reset, never pushed and never `migration repair`ed, which replaces D-225's "or have the five migrations marked applied". A database that has `20261118180000` and a later migration, but not `20261118180500`, pushes once with `--include-all` (`docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`) |
| D-231 | Bringing the existing submissions in (CRM-Q1.3, #168). A submission is an intake for which `intake_ever_certified` holds: a certification row, or a "submitted" row of its status history. An intake with no certification row takes its chain's values from its own answers, and `make_intake_chain` is restated for that alone. The run is one transaction, not batches, which amends D-226's note: a migration cannot commit between batches, and the rule needs every submission chained first. 1,500 submissions in one firm took 3.6 s. The run comes first and the rule's triggers last, with `lock_timeout` 5 s; a certification in between is chained by D-226's trigger. A brought-in record takes its first submission's time as `created_at`, while its history's "created" entry carries the push date. Where it matches an account made later, the later one is marked (D-195), and a mark already set is repointed to the earliest. The rule, every submission and every quote request sits on an opportunity, is a set of deferrable constraint triggers (D-29). Deleting or unlinking an opportunity locks the intake row first. Exempt are a closed firm, a firm closing now, a deleted intake, and a draft with no quote request. Quote requests are the screens' quotes (D-185), so no quotes table is built (Epic 5). A story that deletes opportunities (CRM-A1.1c, CRM-S4.3b) must close the race between that delete and a quote request being recorded |
| D-232 | The Accounts page (CRM-A1.1a, #167). `/accounts` sits on the table frame, which gains `TableDefinition.usual` to hold columns back by default. A table without it is unchanged, and kept choices are untouched. The views are Open opportunities, Waiting on quotes, Clients and Archived; there is no "Renewing in 120 days" (D-186). The filters are Owner, State and Employees, and the Owner filter has no default (screen 16's "My team" is an example). Search starts at 2 characters, a minimum each table sets (the queue's 3 is for File IDs). Opportunity sorts by stage, Quotes by requests sent, Owner by name. Export takes the page's filters as JSON values, is offered only on a settled page with rows, and words each column as the page does (D-223), dates in the page's time zone. An owner the member list can't name reads "A member". A sign-in again comes back to `/accounts` and `/accounts/<id>` with the query kept (`brokerNext`) |
| D-233 | Calls, notes and tasks (CRM-E1.5, #169). They live in one table, `activities`, on an account, with an optional contact and an optional opportunity of it; visibility is the account's. A call or task can be changed by whoever logged it, by the person a task is for, or under the level rule (D-209). A note can be changed by its writer only, for 24 hours (D-200). Anyone who may see a note can add to it, at any time. On an archived account nothing new is logged and no note is added to; calls and tasks can still be edited and tasks marked done (D-222). Calls, notes and tasks on a contact move with the contact, and those on an opportunity with the opportunity, through deferrable paired keys and a guard that lets the account change only by following the activity's own link. Rows 14 and 21 do the moves. These records carry no version and are last-write-wins, which amends D-46 for them. Call outcomes are Spoke, Left a message, No answer and Wrong number. A call is at most 5 minutes in the future, by the server's clock. A task is for a current member of the firm. Last activity (D-201) is one indexed `activity_at` stamp per row: when the call took place or the row was made, when the task was done, and the last addition, whichever is latest. It only moves forward. Home's due tasks wait for Home, and a lead's activities wait for leads |
| D-234 | Creating an account (CRM-A1.2, #171). The maker may choose another owner (D-214's "unless they choose another"), but only a current member they could change under D-209's level rule. An agent may choose only themselves, a lead people on their teams and teams under, an owner anyone. `account_owner_choices(firm)` lists exactly those people and the form offers only them. The chosen member's membership row is held `FOR SHARE` until the account is made, so a removal can't leave it owned by a former member. An account's phone follows the repository's `is_valid_phone` (7 to 15 digits, at most 50 characters, so the column's old 40-character limit goes). A trigger checks the phone only when it changes, compared trimmed, so an old phone never blocks an unrelated save; `update_account` and the Details editor check it the same way. Employees is at most 2,147,483,647. A hand-made account takes no "Possible duplicate" mark: the warning while typing is CRM-S4.2's. "New account" shows in the header and in the empty state, and is hidden while rows are selected. The shared form (`useWorkspaceForm`) ignores a second submit and any close while a save is in flight |
| D-235 | Archiving, deleting and changing an account's owner (CRM-A1.1c, #172; amends D-204). Who may archive, restore, delete or change the owner follows the level rule (D-209, which is later than D-204 and names deleting), so a lead may delete their team's account. An account with any opportunity can't be deleted (PT409, "archive instead"), following D-219.
