A delete never removes opportunities, and the account's contacts, calls, notes and tasks go with it (D-204, D-233). Archiving changes only `archived_at`. A new owner must be a current member, with their membership row held `FOR SHARE` to the end of the change. Removal and suspension end a membership by updating it, which KEY SHARE would not wait for. Open (not Won or Lost) opportunities take the new owner only where the person passes the level rule on them, and the skipped ones are counted and shown. The dialog starts with no owner chosen. The row actions show only on rows `accounts_can_change(firm, ids)` returns, at most 100 ids a call (D-24). The account's page uses its own `can_change` |
| D-236 | Selecting rows on the Accounts table (CRM-S3.6, #170). The selection lives in the table frame (`useRowSelection`, `SelectionBar`, a `selection` prop), in memory only and never in browser storage (D-43). It survives paging, sorting and the 60-second refresh, and is cleared by a change of view, filter or search. Rows left from the previous question can't be ticked until the new answer comes. An explicit selection is ids, and the database refuses more than 1,000 in words. "Select all N that match" sends the filters in force and the search, and no ids; bulk actions (CRM-S3.7a and S3.7b) take it the same way. Unchecking a row under "all" exports the rest by id, since D-218 takes no exception list. The "Select all N that match" line appears once the whole page is selected (the story wins over screen 35). The bar offers Export and Clear; its other actions come with CRM-S3.7 (D-24). Only the Accounts table selects (D-214) |
| D-237 | Bulk changes in the database (CRM-S3.7b, #173; amends D-45). Change owner (with the open opportunities if asked), set one optional field, add or remove a label, and archive, on up to 1,000 accounts given as ids or as "all that match" (D-236). The database counts again: over 1,000 the preview answers `too_many` and the change refuses with PT413 in words. The level rule skips an account, counted and named with the single change's words; anything else refuses and rolls back everything. Ids not seen, or deleted while the change waits for them, count as `unavailable` and are never named. A row already as asked is unchanged: no write, no history entry, not in the undo. The preview and the change ask the same checks, the phone's `is_valid_phone` included. Every changed record gets one history entry. A label is a row of its own table, so `account_labels_history` is a second writer of record history, which amends D-45. Labels are case-insensitive, 1 to 40 characters, with no cap per firm, made when an account first takes one. An archived account takes no new label (D-222); taking one off, setting a field and changing the owner are allowed. The undo (D-202) is the maker's, once, for ten minutes, and only while the membership they made it under is still current (PT404 otherwise, PT410 expired, PT409 done). It puts back what nobody changed since, judged by the field's history, and skips and names the rest: a departed previous owner, a label on an account archived since, an earlier phone that no longer passes the rule. Records deleted since are counted, not named. A label the change made that no account carries is removed. Lock order: accounts by id, the new owner's membership `FOR SHARE`, opportunities by id, then the label. The change's rows go with their account or opportunity and with a closing firm, and the firm's next bulk change clears them after a day. Exporting the selection is CRM-S3.8b's `export_rows` |
| D-238 | Adding and moving a contact (CRM-A2.2, #175). Adding is open to anyone who may see the account (D-209), except onto an archived one (D-222). Making the new contact main needs the level rule on the old main contact (42501 in words otherwise), and one main contact per account is held by a partial unique index. The owner is the maker unless they choose another current member they may give it to, whose membership row is held `FOR SHARE`. A contact's phone and mobile follow `is_valid_phone` in the function, not a trigger, so a certification never fails over a phone (ADR 0025). Moving needs three yeses under the level rule, which is stricter than the story's "by its owner" because moving changes both accounts. The yeses are the contact's own (a contact with no owner: a firm owner), the account it leaves and the account it joins, each also seen (PT404). An archived account can be left but not joined (55000). The contact's calls, notes and tasks go with it (D-233), locked in id order before they are counted. One naming an opportunity left behind loses that link, and so does a main-contact mark on such an opportunity, after a PT409 question with the count ("Move anyway"). The move is refused with 42501 when the mover may not change that opportunity. A moved main contact arrives as nobody's main. The account field on the form is shown, not chosen. The picker refuses in words when the contact can't be moved. Lock order: accounts by id, then the contact, then its activities |
| D-239 | Bulk edit on screen (CRM-S3.7a, #176). The bulk bar offers Change owner, Set a field, Label and Archive beside Export and Clear, each with its own dialog. Nevermore support gets none of them. Each dialog shows the database's preview before anything changes: the counts of accounts that change and stay as they are, the skipped accounts named with the reason, and accounts no longer available counted, never named. Screen 36's list of the accounts that will change, by name, is not built: counts and named skips are what "says exactly what will happen" means here. An owner change that reaches only open opportunities is a change. "All that match" is the matching set when the change runs, not when it was previewed (D-237), and any difference from the preview is said on the result line. A token binding the change to its preview is a possible follow-up, raised with the founder. The result and its Undo are a line over the table, replaced by the next bulk change. The ten minutes are counted by the page from the database's answer, not by the browser's clock. The selection is cleared after a change. The Label column is not a usual column, so a person with a kept column choice finds it in Columns (D-217). A Save that can't be honoured is refused in words, not disabled (D-24) |
| D-240 | Search in the database and recent records (CRM-S1.2b, #174). `search_records` finds a firm's accounts, contacts, opportunities and quotes whose own fields, or their account's, hold every word, five per kind and whether there are more. It searches by name, business, email, phone, web domain, File ID and PEO, by the caller's level (`can_see_firm_record`), and never an intake's answers, census or FEIN. Results are live before archived, then by name; opportunities and quotes newest first. Fewer than two characters is empty; words are capped at six and 200 characters. A rare word is answered from trigram indexes led by the firm, and a common one by walking the firm's records in display order, with visibility asked before anything is counted. Known limit: words that are each common but that no record holds together walk the whole firm, about 850 ms at 10,000 accounts; everything else measured is under 150 ms there. Each person keeps 20 recent records per firm in 20 places, replaced by compare-and-set, shown only while still visible. Support records none and must name the firm to search. `search_lower` is open to everyone because an index function runs as the writer. "See all" exists for accounts only (the Accounts table's search); contacts, opportunities and quotes need their own tables first |

### Epic 3 build order

Lanes: **Q** the queue and the table frame (`src/components/workspace/table/`); **P** the partners and activity
pages; **D** the database; **A** the accounts and contacts pages. A story marked a or b is one story split by
seam. "Risky" marks the stories D-189 names. Under D-105 every migration sorts after `20261118160500`, in this
order.

| # | Story | What it covers | Needs | Builder | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CRM-S3.1a | Columns: show, hide and reorder; the first column stays in view when the table scrolls sideways; kept per person. On the queue (Epic 2 row 16) | none | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 2 | CRM-S3.8b | Export in the database (D-48): one function returns the rows and records the export; the owners' alert and the fresh sign-in for a whole table; refuses a support session (D-128). Referral partners and, for owners, the activity record (Epic 2 row 18) | none | Sonnet (D) | `20261119090000` |
| 3 | CRM-Q1.1a | The records: accounts, contacts and opportunities, each with its firm and owner, row security by `can_see_firm_record` (a contact and an opportunity by their account's), history and version (`record_track`), status (CRM-A1.3, D-197) and stage (D-206) worked out, one main contact per account and one intake per opportunity as constraints, two-firm probes, the closing list | none | Sonnet (D) | `20261119100000` |
| 4 | CRM-S3.4a | Saved views on screen: tabs with counts, the queue's status tabs as its built-in views, save, rename, delete and share (Epic 2 row 17) | 1 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 5 | CRM-S3.8a | The Export button and the CSV, on referral partners and the activity record (Epic 2 row 19) | 1, 2 | Sonnet (P) | none |
| 6 | CRM-Q1.1b | The chain at the first certification (D-192): the duplicate match (D-193), a new account marked "Possible duplicate of", the account filled from the certified answers (D-196), the contact from the prospect (D-194), the opportunity named (D-188), source Employer's intake (D-211); the same step twice makes one chain | 3 | Sonnet (D), risky | `20261119110000` |
| 7 | CRM-Q1.3 | Bring the existing submissions in by the same step (D-195), reporting how many of each; run twice it makes nothing new. Then the rule that every submission and every quote request sits on one opportunity | 6 | Sonnet (D), risky | `20261119120000` |
| 8 | CRM-A1.1b | The accounts table's query: its fields, person filters (any of, me, my team; D-173), sort, 50 a page, search, view counts, and the worked-out columns (main contact; the open opportunity with the nearest target start and "+1 more"; quotes back of sent and the nearest expiry; last activity so far, D-201). `accounts` joins the saved-views and export lists | 3, 2 | Sonnet (D) | `20261119130000` |
| 9 | CRM-A2.1a | An account's page in the database: the account with each list and its count, the Files list (D-199), history readable for accounts and contacts, the save with its version (D-46) and the level rule (D-209) | 3 | Sonnet (D) | `20261119140000` |
| 10 | CRM-A1.1a | The Accounts page (`/accounts`) and its menu item: the columns, views, filters (Owner, State, Employees), page states and export | 8, 4, 5 | Sonnet (A) | none |
| 11 | CRM-A2.1b | An account's page (`/accounts/:id`): the header, tabs with counts, Overview, lists whose names open their records (D-187), edit in place, the archived banner, the Activity tab with the history (D-212), and "Account: <name>" on a submission's page (D-213) | 9 | Sonnet (A) | `20261119145000` (D-228) |
| 12 | CRM-A1.2 | Create an account (screen 23): the form, the phone, state and date rules, source Added by hand, owner its maker (D-214); it opens the new account. "Save and add a contact" arrives with row 14 | 10, 11 | Sonnet (A) | `20261119150000` |
| 13 | CRM-A1.1c | Archive, restore and delete (D-204), and change an account's owner with the offer for its open opportunities, from the table and from the account's page | 10, 11 | Sonnet (A) | `20261119160000` |
| 14 | CRM-A2.2 | Add a contact on an account (screen 24): the form, one main contact, moving a contact to another account (D-209) | 11, 12 | Sonnet (A) | `20261119170000` |
| 15 | CRM-S3.6 | Selecting rows on the accounts table: checkboxes, the page, "Select all N that match", the bulk bar | 10 | Sonnet (Q) | none |
| 16 | CRM-E1.5 | Calls, notes and tasks (screen 27) on an account and a contact: the follow-up task, D-200's note rule, the Activity tab, last activity | 11 | Sonnet (A) | `20261119180000` |
| 17 | CRM-C1.1 | A contact's page: the header, details, opportunities, calls, notes and tasks; "Do not email" is kept for Epic 6 (D-186) | 14, 16 | Sonnet (A) | `20261119190000` |
| 18 | CRM-S4.2 | Spotting a duplicate while creating an account or a contact, and the masked match for a record the person may not see | 6, 12, 14 | Sonnet (A) | `20261121090000` (D-600) |

| 22 | CRM-S4.3a | Merging on screen: from the bulk bar (exactly two) and from the possible-duplicate banner, the field-by-field choice, the old address leading to the kept record | 17, 20, 21 | Sonnet (A) | none |
| 23 | CRM-S1.2b | Search in the database: accounts, contacts, opportunities and quotes by name, business, email, phone, web domain, File ID and PEO, five per kind, by visibility, never an intake's answers; each person's recent records | 7 | Sonnet (D) | `20261119230000` |
| 24 | CRM-S1.2a | The search box in the menu: grouped results with "see all", the last five opened, "Nothing matches" with New account | 11, 12, 17, 23 | Sonnet (A) | none |

### CRM-S4.2 decisions

Built 2026-11-15 against D-193, D-209, D-219, D-226, D-238 and `20261119110000_chain_at_certification.sql`. Row 18.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-600 | The migration is `20261121090000_spot_a_duplicate.sql`, not row 18's `20261119200000`. Under D-105 a migration must sort after the newest one on main, so a file dated 20261119200000 would load before migrations already applied. It is dated the next day, 20261121, so that no Epic 4 migration built in parallel (CRM-O1.1a takes 20261120120000) shares its version |
| D-601 | The duplicate rule is written once. `duplicates_of_account(firm, web domain, name, state, email)` returns every account of the firm that matches by D-193 with the reasons (`website`, `name`, `email`), and the chain's `find_duplicate_account` now asks it for the earliest, with the same answers as before. The forms and the chain cannot disagree about what a duplicate is. Neither function can be called by an API role. A name matches only with a state on both sides, so New account asks only once there is a website, or a name and a state; Add a contact asks only for something shaped like an email address. New account has no contact email, so the email arm is the chain's and the contact form's |
| D-602 | The masked match. A record the person may not see (an account by its own level rule, a contact by its account's, D-219) is returned as `{kind, visible: false, ask}` and nothing else: no id, name, reason or link. Whom to ask is the account's owner when they are a current member of the firm, else the firm's owners (at most five, by name), the people who see a record with no owner (D-209). Names only, never an email or an id. Masks with the same people to ask are one, so nothing counts the records the person cannot see |
| D-603 | A warning, never a rule (D-193). `create_account` and `add_contact` are unchanged and a save is never refused or asked to confirm for a likeness. The warning is two reads, `duplicate_account_check` and `duplicate_contact_check`, for a member of the firm the call acts for (42501 otherwise), so a closing firm and a support session may ask as they may ask every other read. A failed check shows nothing and the form saves as it did. At most five records the person may see are named (the earliest first, then "And N more you can see"), an archived one is named and marked, and a contact on the very account the form is open on counts. A link opens the existing record in a new tab so what was typed is kept |
| D-604 | Merging is not offered (CRM-S4.3: no dead button, D-24). From the warning a person can open the existing record, or carry on and save. The story's "merge" from the warning, and the warning for a lead (Epic 5) and the import review (CRM-X4.1), wait for their stories |

## Superseded by Epic 3 (founder, 2026-11-14)

Epic 2's last four rows (its build order's rows 16 to 19: CRM-S3.1a, CRM-S3.4a, CRM-S3.8b and CRM-S3.8a) were built on two lines
that did not see each other. `main` built CRM-S3.1a, CRM-S3.8b and CRM-S3.8a (#142 and #147). The Epic 3 branch built CRM-S3.1a,
CRM-S3.8b and CRM-S3.4a, its rows 1, 2 and 4 (#144, #145 and #150); its row 5, CRM-S3.8a, is #160, which was not merged when `main`
was first merged in (321fad2), so the merged branch had no Export button until #160 landed; it is merged now (79061f5), and its
`ExportControl` is the Export button. The founder decided Epic 3's versions win. The decisions `main` recorded for its versions, numbered D-185 to D-257 there (D-185 to D-199 the Columns menu, D-200
to D-214 and D-241 to D-242 the export in the database, D-215 to D-229 and D-253 to D-257 saved views, D-230 to D-252 the Export button and the CSV),
described the implementation Epic 3 replaced. They are kept below as they were, renumbered M-185 to M-257 so that they cannot be
mistaken for Epic 3's D-185 to D-221, with their references to each other renumbered the same way; a number below D-185 in them is
the shared decision it always was. M-196 to M-199, M-215 to M-229, M-245 to M-257 were added when `main` was merged again (#156). Nothing they describe is in the product any more: `main`'s `useTableColumns`, `pinned.ts`, its saved-views pieces (`ViewBar`,
`ViewConfirm`, `ViewNameForm`, `useViewActions`), `ExportButton`, its CSV builder and `exportTable` were removed in the merge, and its database functions (`export_table` and the ones it
called, `signed_in_within`) are dropped by `20261118180000_epic3_export_supersedes.sql`. `main`'s three export migrations
(`20261118170000_export.sql`, `20261118170500_export_fixes.sql`, `20261118171000_export_fixes_again.sql`) may be applied in
production and are not edited: where their comments cite D-200 to D-214 or D-241, read M-200 to M-214 or M-241. The exports they
recorded in the firm's record keep their number, key, time, actor, table and count: `20261118180000` sets them aside and
`20261119090500_epic3_export_carried.sql` puts them back under Epic 3's rule once `20261119090000_export.sql` has added it, and the
owners' alerts they queued are rewritten into Epic 3's payload (the table in words). `supabase/tests/export_supersedes.sql` makes
exports with `main`'s functions, two of them still being made on other connections as the upgrade starts (the supersedes migration locks
the record and the outbox before it reads them, and waits for those), applies the rest, and checks they survived.

| # | Decision (superseded) |
|---|---|
| M-185 | What the Columns story builds and where it sits (CRM-S3.1a). Three pieces in `src/components/workspace/table/`, none of them in a table's definition: `useTableColumns(table, savedView?)` (the person's choice, read and kept), `ColumnsMenu` (the button and its panel) and `columnChoice.ts` (the pure parts: which columns a list of ids names, and one move). A page gives its toolbar `<ColumnsMenu table columns />` and the frame `columns` and `columnsLoading`, all from the one hook, and nothing else; the queue is the only table on the frame, and the next one copies those three lines. The page places the menu, as it places the filter row, because the toolbar is the page's. A table's `columns` are its default columns in the order shown, and each id never changes once shipped (the database keeps choices by id); no field was added to `TableDefinition`, and `TableView` gained an optional `columns` (M-193). The queue's ids, as the database keeps them: `company`, `contact`, `states`, `employees`, `submitted`, `status`, `completeness`, `quotes`, `waiting_since`, `quote_expires` |
| M-186 | The name column (CRM-S3.1a). The column marked `isName` is always first. It has no checkbox ("Always shown") and no arrows, and nothing can be moved above it (the first column after it has "Move up" unavailable). `columnsFor` puts it first whatever a kept list says, because the database keeps any list of 1 to 60 ids and cannot know which column names a record; the menu writes it first in every list it keeps. A table lists its name column first and marks it `isName` |
| M-187 | What a choice is, and how it is read (CRM-S3.1a). The columns shown, in order, as a list of ids; a column not in the list is hidden. So a column a table gains later is not shown to a person who has a choice until they show it (it is under "Not shown"), and a person with no choice follows the table's default columns. It is read leniently, as the address is (D-135): an id the table no longer has (a column since retired) is left out, an id twice is shown once, and a list that names no column the table has is no choice at all, so retiring every column of someone's list leaves them the default columns, not Company alone; a person who really chose Company alone has "company" in their list and keeps it. "Back to default columns" asks the database to forget the choice (null) instead of keeping the default list, so the person follows the table's defaults again, new columns included |
| M-188 | Changing and keeping it (CRM-S3.1a). A change is on the table at once and is kept at once: there is no Apply, because a menu of this kind has nothing to lose (D-166 says the same of the filter editor). Each save is the whole list, so saves go one after another: a quick second change waits for the first to be kept, so the last one asked for is the last one made and an older list is never kept over a newer. A save that fails does not undo what is on screen, as a failed save keeps what was typed (D-178): the table stays as the person set it for this visit and one alert beside the Columns button says so ("Couldn't keep your columns. Try again."). A firm that is closing (D-115) and a support session (D-123) are refused by the database (`set_my_table_columns` is on both guards' lists, PT423), and the alert then gives the database's own reason ("Nevermore support can look, not change, so your columns weren't kept."): `setMyColumns` says it, and until now told them "Try again" for a save that could never work. Any other failure is the plain fallback, never the database's text (D-179). The alert goes with the next change that is kept |
| M-189 | Reading it once (CRM-S3.1a). The person's choice is read when the table opens, once per firm, table and saved view, through the query library under the table's key. Refresh and the slow-load line's "Try again" ask for it again with everything else the table shows (`useRefreshTable`); the minute's refresh and the tab coming back, which refresh the rows (D-49), do not: only this person changes it, and what they chose on the page is on screen already. What the person chose on the page wins over what was read, so a late or repeated read can't undo a change; a change made on another device shows the next time the table is opened. Nothing is asked until the firm is known, as with the rows (D-49), and a page outside the shell (a test) has no firm to ask for and asks nothing |
| M-190 | Rows are never drawn in columns the person is about to lose (CRM-S3.1a, D-146). While the person's choice has not arrived the table is in its loading state, with grey rows, "Loading submissions" and the slow-load line after ten seconds (D-145), even when the rows are already here: `TableFrame` takes `columnsLoading` and `tableIsLoading` a third argument. Otherwise the rows would be drawn in the default ten columns for a moment and then collapse to the person's, on every visit of anyone who has chosen. Once the choice is known the grey rows are built from the same list as the real table, so the headers and the grey cells already have the person's columns. Meanwhile the Columns button is `aria-disabled` with the reason "Available once your columns have loaded" (D-143's way: in the tab order, the click ignored). A choice that could not be read is not a failed table: the default columns are shown with one polite line, "Your columns couldn't be loaded, so the default columns are shown." and "Try again", never the failure's own words; the line goes when the choice arrives or when the person makes one |
| M-191 | The first column stays in view (CRM-S3.1a; CRM-S3.2a left it to this story). `DataTable` pins its first column, the header cell and every row's header cell, with `position: sticky` at the left of its scroll box (`pinned.ts` holds the classes). The cell is opaque: the header and the row hover are translucent tints (`bg-muted/60`, `/50`) and a translucent pinned cell would show the columns scrolling under it, so it is the card's own colour with the same tint laid over it as an image, which is what the translucent one looks like on the card. Its right edge is an inset shadow, because a border does not travel with a pinned cell in a table whose borders collapse. `PlaceholderBody` pins its first grey cell when given `pinFirst` (the frame does), so a loading table scrolls as the real one does. It is tested in two places because jsdom has no layout: the queue's page test checks that the header and every cell of the first column carry the pin and no other does, and `e2e/columns.spec.ts` checks it in Chromium: ten columns at 900 pixels wide scrolled to the end, the name cell still at the box's left edge in the header and in a row, the last column in view, and the pinned cell not transparent |
| M-192 | The menu and the keyboard (CRM-S3.1a, D-170's way). A panel from the same `Popover` the filter row uses, opened by a "Columns" button. In it: the name column (its words and "Always shown"), each shown column with a checkbox and "Move Contact up" and "Move Contact down" buttons, then, under "Not shown", each hidden column with a checkbox, then "Back to default columns". There is no drag and drop: the arrows work by keyboard, by touch and by screen reader, and no library was added. Escape closes the panel and focus goes back to the button (the popover's own); opening puts focus in the panel. After every change focus stays on the control that was used, wherever its row went: hiding or showing sends the row to the other list, which is a new element, and that drops focus to the page, so a keyboard user's next change would start from the top; the menu puts it back by control (`data-column-control`) once the change is drawn, tested in jsdom and in Chromium (red without it). A move within a list is given the same refocus, but neither jsdom nor Chromium loses focus on an element React re-places, so that line has no red case here; it stays for a browser that does (only Chromium is installed in the build environment), and is listed as the one mutation that survived. A control that can't be used is `aria-disabled` with a description, not `disabled` (D-143): "Move up" on the first column after the name column and "Move down" on the last ("It can't move any further."), and "Back to default columns" when they are the defaults already ("These are the default columns already."). One polite status inside the panel, there empty from the panel's first draw and filled by each change, says what it did: "States is now column 2 of 10.", "Contact hidden. 9 columns shown.", "Contact shown as column 10 of 10.", "Default columns restored. 10 columns shown." The panel announces itself where the filter row does not (D-170) because nothing else says it: the arrows keep their names and places, and the table behind the panel changes silently. A closed menu adds no status to the page, so the statuses the loading tests count are unchanged |
| M-193 | Per saved view: the seam left for CRM-S3.4a (CRM-S3.1: "kept per person per table and per saved view"). `useTableColumns(table, savedView)` takes the saved view that is open, which CRM-S3.4a's page passes; the queue passes none, because its tabs are built-in views whose columns are the person's choice for the table (glossary). On a saved view the hook reads the person's choice with the view's id (the database's order: their own choice for the view, the view's own columns, their choice for the table, the table's defaults) and keeps their own choice for that view alone, never for the table; a choice made on one view is not shown on another. `TableView.columns` (new, optional) is the view's own columns: they show until the person has a choice of their own for the view, and when the database could not be asked. "Back to default columns" on a saved view forgets only the person's own choice for the view. Where that lands (the view's own columns, else their choice for the table, else the defaults) is the database's to say, so the hook shows the view's own columns at once and asks the database again for the answer (an answer that arrives after the person has chosen something else is dropped). This is tried through a small page of the test's own that calls the hook as that story will, since the queue has no saved view yet. CRM-S3.4a must pass the open saved view, give a saved view the `columns` it was saved with (`listSavedViews` already returns them), save a view's columns from the ids the hook shows (`columns.map((c) => c.id)`), and word "Back to default columns" for a view if "default" is not what it lands on |
| M-194 | What CRM-S3.1a leaves to a later story (D-24). **Custom fields** (CRM-X3.1): their columns are not offered, and a column a table offers but does not show until it is chosen needs a flag on `ColumnDefinition` (today every column a table lists is a default column). The database keeps at most 60 ids in a choice, of 1 to 64 characters each; the menu does not enforce it because no table has more than ten columns, and CRM-X3.1 must make "show" unavailable, with the reason, at 60. **A saved view's columns** (the page wiring, "Save this as a view" keeping the columns, the view's own columns on its tab): CRM-S3.4a (M-193). **Export** (CRM-S3.8a) reads each column's `text`; whether it follows the columns on screen is that story's to decide (the hook's `columns` are what is on screen). **Other lists** (partners, activity, outbox, held mail, members, the lists inside a record) have no Columns menu until each is put on the frame (D-184). **A choice changed on another device** shows the next time the table is opened (M-189). **A support session and a closing firm** can open the menu and see their choice for the visit, but it is not kept (M-188): the database refuses it and nothing in it was changed for this story |
| M-195 | Found while building (CRM-S3.1a). The queue page test's `supabase.rpc` was an empty function; it is now a small fake of the database's two functions for a column choice (`database` in `AdvisorDashboardPage.test.tsx`, whose real rules are tested in `supabase/tests/table_settings.sql`) that refuses any other call loudly, so a page that asks for something it should not is a failing test. One existing test was extended (`each request is made once`: nothing is asked before the firm is known, and the columns once after it). `PlaceholderBody`'s rows gained the `group` class so a pinned grey cell tints on hover as a real one does; no other table is changed by it. `setMyColumns` gained the PT423 words (M-188) |
| M-196 | A change is kept for the whole visit, not only while the page is drawn (CRM-S3.1a review; changes M-189's "what the person chose on the page wins over what was read"). `choose` also writes the choice into the query library's answer for that table and view, in the shape a read answers with (`{ columns, source }`: `mine` or `mine_for_view` for a list; for a forgotten choice `{ columns: null, source: "default" }`, and on a saved view the view's own columns with source `view`, which only the database's next answer can confirm). The cause was that the read is never asked again (`staleTime: Infinity`, M-189) and the app's query client keeps an answer five minutes, so a page drawn again (a row opened and Back, or another saved view chosen and the first opened again) drew the answer from BEFORE the change, and the next change was built from what was drawn: the list it kept lost the earlier change in the database as well. The component's own state (`chosen`) stays: it is what wins while the page is drawn and a late read arrives. Proved through the page (`AdvisorDashboardPage.test.tsx`: "leaving the table and coming back", and the A, B, A sequence on the saved-view test page) and in Chromium (`e2e/columns.spec.ts`: a submission opened and Back) |
| M-197 | The Columns menu waits for the firm (CRM-S3.1a review; extends M-190). `loading` is `!ready || (the firm is known and the read is pending)`: with no firm known yet the button is `aria-disabled` with the same reason ("Available once your columns have loaded"), because a change made then has no firm to be kept for, and once the firm arrived the unsaved change would have been drawn over what the database holds, with the next save built from that. A page outside the workspace shell (no workspace at all, as a test) has no firm to wait for and is as before |
| M-198 | "Back to default columns" is offered whenever the person keeps a choice (CRM-S3.1a review; changes M-192's "unavailable when they are the defaults already"). `useTableColumns` returns `hasChoice` in place of `isDefault`: the person's own kept choice for the table or the view (the read's source is `mine` or `mine_for_view`, or what they chose on the page and has not forgotten), not whether what is drawn equals the default order. A kept list that is the default order is still a kept list, and forgetting it is what lets a column the table gains later show for that person (M-187). "These are the default columns already." is said only when nothing is kept. A saved view's own columns are not the person's choice, so a person who has only those has nothing to forget |
| M-199 | Where the review's corrections are written (CRM-S3.1a review). M-185 to M-195 are not rewritten: M-196 to M-198 name what they change. The columns end-to-end test's header said M-185 to M-198; it is M-185 to M-199. Each fix was red against the build as reviewed, and each line of it was broken to see a test go red (eleven mutations; three survived the first tests and were killed by assertions on what a remounted page offers: the way back after a kept choice, nothing to go back from after forgetting, and `hasChoice` on a saved view) |
| M-200 | Where an export is recorded (CRM-S3.8b, D-45, D-48). In the firm's record of who did what (`firm_activity`), not a table of its own: the PRD lists exports among what that record holds (8.6, and `access_log` in its tables), the glossary already named the kind `export_made`, and it already gives exactly the rest of the story's rule: owners only, past the second factor; nobody changes it; ninety days; a person of Nevermore's shown as "Nevermore" (D-33). An entry is kind `export_made`, record type `table` (new, added to `firm_activity_record_check`; restated from `20261118120000` with every type it had, the one restatement of that list), the table's name as the record id, and the row count in a new column, `row_count`. A check (`firm_activity_export_check`, `NOT VALID` so no older row is judged) makes an export entry carry a count and be about a table, and no other entry carry one. Only `export_table` writes one: `record_firm_activity` (the service's door) can't, for want of a count. `firm_activity_list` returns `row_count` too (dropped and made again, with the same arguments, rules and grants; null for every other kind). No new table, so `firm_closing_plan` and the registry in `data_isolation.sql` need no line (the record is already kept through closing, line 900); the kinds list is not restated (D-104). CRM-P1.5's "what the member exported in the last 30 days" is `firm_activity_list(firm, person, 'export_made')` |
| M-201 | What can be exported (CRM-S3.8b, D-48). Two tables, chosen by name from a fixed `CASE` (`export_columns`): `referral_partners`, for any current member (the table's own rule, `is_firm_member`), and `firm_activity`, the firm's record, for its owners (`can_read_firm_activity`; 42501 "Only a firm's owners can export its record" for anyone else). `submissions` (and `queue`) is refused by name with its own sentence, "The queue can't be exported: its columns are an intake's answers" (22023); any other name, "There is no table called …" (22023). Each table's rule is asked once in `export_table` and again inside the rows (`export_rows`), so no change to one place lets rows outrun it. A later table (accounts, leads, quotes) adds one branch each to `export_columns`, `export_field_kind` and `export_rows`, and its columns to `EXPORT_COLUMNS` in `src/lib/exportApi.ts`, which `schemaSync.test.ts` keeps equal |
| M-202 | Which columns (CRM-S3.8, ADR 0006, `docs/DATA_HANDLING.md`). A fixed list per table, asked for by name, in any order and any subset, never twice: referral partners `code`, `name`, `kind`, `created_at`, `updated_at`; the record `at`, `who` (a member's email, "Nevermore" or "System", D-33, D-131), `kind`, `record_type`, `record_id`, `subject` (the member an entry is about), `role`, `rows` (an export's count). A partner's `created_by_email` and `updated_by_email` are left out: the default firm's partners were written by people of Nevermore's, whom a firm never learns. Nothing on either list holds an intake's answers, a census, a FEIN or a PEO's file; `supabase/tests/export.sql` tries every table and column name in the `public`, `auth` and `storage` schemas, and the names of an intake's content, under both table names, and only these are accepted. Moments are UTC ISO text to the millisecond |
| M-203 | The order of an export's refusals, so nothing is read or written before any of them (D-128). First `refuse_support_session(firm)`, with the firm the call names (PT423), before the table, the columns or the filters are looked at, so a support session learns nothing about what exists; then a signed-in current member of that firm at its address (`firm_role`, which holds D-19, D-40 and a suspended firm; 42501 "Only a member of this firm can export its records" for anonymous, a guest, no firm, another firm's member, a former or suspended member, a platform admin with no membership, and another firm's address); then the table (22023), its own rule (42501), the columns, filters, selection and time zone (22023); then the cap (54000); then the recent sign-in (PT428). A refusal rolls back everything, so nothing refused is recorded. An admin who is a member exports as a member and is recorded as themselves (`firm_actor_is_staff`, D-131) |
| M-204 | How big, and in what order (CRM-S3.8b, D-48). At most 10,000 rows (`export_row_cap`). A larger export is refused, never cut short: "This export would hold N rows; the most one export holds is 10000. Narrow it with a filter." (54000), and nothing is recorded, because a file cut to the cap is not "the rows that match" and its recorded count would describe a file nobody asked for. A closing firm whose table is larger exports it in parts by a date filter. Rows come in the table's own order: partners by name (ignoring case) then code, the record newest first then by entry key. There is no sort argument yet: the button sorts the CSV it builds if it wants the page's order, or a later story adds one in the queue's shape (D-44) |
| M-205 | A whole-table export and the recent sign-in (CRM-P1.3 "exporting a whole table … ask the person to sign in again if they last did more than 12 hours ago", PRD 8.7, D-48). An export is whole-table when it holds every row the caller may see in that table, and at least one: judged by what it holds, not by how it was asked, so a filter or a selection that happens to keep every row is the whole table (an intent-based rule is passed by any filter such as "created after 2000"). An export of nothing is not. A whole-table export needs a sign-in within 12 hours (`export_fresh_sign_in`), else PT428 "Sign in again to export the whole table: you last signed in more than 12 hours ago". The time is the latest `timestamp` in the token's `amr` (`signed_in_within(interval)`): Supabase Auth signs it and keeps it through refreshes, and a new sign-in, or a second factor completed again, renews it; `iat` is renewed by every refresh, so it would not work. A token without `amr` is not recent. PT428 rather than PT401: a 401 from the API reads as an ended session. `signed_in_within` is general, for the other step CRM-P1.3 asks it of (changing members), which is not built here. A known limit of judging per call is in M-242 |
| M-206 | The owners' alert (CRM-P2.3, PRD 8.6, D-35, D-102, D-106, D-118, ADR 0020). An export of more than 500 rows (`export_alert_rows`), or a member's third export within an hour, queues one outbox email of kind `export_alert` in the same transaction as the export: a firm-level row (a firm, no intake), one per export that calls for it whichever reason (both are named), deduplicated by the entry's key. Its payload is who (`member_email`), `firm_name`, `table`, `row_count`, `exported_at`, `large`, `exports_in_hour`, never a row, a column or a filter. It goes to the firm's current owners (`outbox_owner_recipients`, asked at each send, so D-35 and ADR 0020 hold as for `member_left` and `firm_closing`). It is a firm's alert (`outbox_kind_is_firm_alert`): it waits while the firm is suspended, a bounce never suppresses an owner's address, and it is ended once the firm has closed. The dispatcher builds it (`parseExportAlert`, `renderExportAlert`) only from those fields, naming the table in words ("referral partners", "the firm's record of who did what") and a table it does not know is a payload it gives up on. The database never sends an email itself |
| M-207 | What "a third export by one member within an hour" counts. The member's own `export_made` entries in that firm whose time is within the hour before this one's, this one included (another member's, another firm's and older ones are not counted), and the alert is queued when that number is exactly three (`export_alert_repeats`): a run of exports in one hour sends one email, not one per export after the third; once the oldest falls out of the hour, a later export can be the third again and send another. Two exports by the same member at once are counted one after the other: `export_table` takes a transaction-level advisory lock on the firm and the member before it counts, and the two-session case in `export.sql` goes red without it (D-32) |
| M-208 | A closing firm may export (D-115, PRD 8.5 "an owner asks to close the firm. They are offered a full export first", CRM-P1.7). `export_table` does not ask `assert_firm_writable`: it changes none of the firm's data, it appends to the firm's record and may queue an alert, which both go on while a firm is closing (D-118). It is listed as exempt, with that reason, in `close_a_firm.sql`, and as a write that refuses a support session (by `refuse_support_session`) in `help_from_nevermore.sql`. Every current member of a closing firm may export what they could before; the product gives nobody more on the way out (PRD 8.4). A suspended firm has no current member (`firm_role`), and a closed one no member at all, so neither exports |
| M-209 | Filters and the selection (D-44). Filters arrive as data in the queue's shape, `{field, op, value}`, at most 20, "and", each checked by `workspace_filter_check` (`20261117100000`) for its field's kind and matched by the same matchers; dates are days in the caller's time zone (`workspace_time_zone`). The fields: referral partners `code`, `name` (text), `kind` (a choice of the partner kinds, `is_referral_partner_kind`), `created_at`, `updated_at` (dates); the record `at` (date), `who` (text: an email, "Nevermore" or "System", which is how the activity page's person filter is carried into an export until person filters arrive with CRM-A1.1), `kind`, `record_type` (choices), `role` (owner, lead, agent). A filter on any other field is refused (22023). The selection (CRM-S3.6) is a list of 1 to 10,000 keys (a partner's code, an entry's `entry_key`), and narrows the filters; a key of another firm's, or one that does not exist, keeps nothing. There is no free-text search argument: no exportable table has one yet |
| M-210 | What CRM-S3.8a calls. `exportTable(firmId, table, columns, { filters, keys, timeZone })` in `src/lib/exportApi.ts`, which calls `public.export_table(p_firm_id uuid, p_table text, p_columns jsonb, p_filters jsonb DEFAULT '[]', p_keys jsonb DEFAULT NULL, p_time_zone text DEFAULT 'UTC') RETURNS jsonb`, and returns `{ table, columns, rows, rowCount, wholeTable, exportedAt }`: `rows` is one array per row, its values (text, a number, or null) in the order of `columns`, which is the order asked. The button sends the columns showing, in the order shown (by their database names; `EXPORT_COLUMNS` lists them), the filters in force exactly as the table frame's address holds them, the selection's keys (or none), and the browser's time zone; it builds the CSV from `rows` only, with each column's `text` for the header and cells, never from the page on screen. It throws `ExportRefusal` with a `reason`: `sign_in_again` (PT428: show the sentence and a "Sign in again" action that signs the person out and back in at the firm's address, then they press Export again), `too_many` (54000: show the database's sentence, which says how many rows, and ask for a filter), `support` (PT423: the button hides itself for the role `support`, D-123, so this is a bug), `forbidden` (42501), `invalid` (22023: the database's sentence), `failed` (anything else: "Try again"); an ended session has `unauthenticated` set and signs the person out, as every other call. On referral partners every member is offered the button; on the activity page only owners reach the page. The page reads an export's count from `firm_activity_list`'s `row_count` and the record type `table` ("Table" in `src/data/firmActivity.ts`) |
| M-211 | An export past the cap is refused before it is built (CRM-S3.8b review, amends M-204 and M-210; `20261118170500_export_fixes.sql`). The first build turned every matching row into JSON, numbered and sorted every visible row and gathered them all before comparing the count with the cap: refusing an export of 200,000 partners took 3.3 s on the test database, all of it work thrown away. Now `export_table` counts the matching rows only up to one past the cap and refuses then; counts the visible rows (for the whole-table rule, M-205) only up to one past the export's own count; and builds, sorts and gathers the rows only once both pass (4 ms for the same refusal, and for a selection of two of those rows). The rows are one SQL function with no SET clause, `export_rows(firm, table, keys)`, which Postgres folds into each query (as `quote_requests_with_new_peo_mail`, D-134), so a count reads no column it does not need and a selection is applied by key inside the rows' own query; each table's rule is asked there once per query, not once per row. It replaces `export_visible_rows`, which is dropped. Every rule, refusal, lock and the recorded count are as they were. Because the count stops at the cap, the cap's sentence no longer gives the number: "This export would hold more than 10000 rows, the most one export holds. Narrow it with a filter." (54000); CRM-S3.8a shows it as it comes. The re-check of each table's rule inside `export_rows` is a guard no input can reach (no API role can run it, and `export_table` refuses first), so removing it turns no test red: it stays as the second wall M-201 names. Correction (M-241): the sentence "Every rule, refusal, lock and the recorded count are as they were" was wrong for this build, which read the count, the visible count and the rows in three statements; `20261118171000` makes them one again |
| M-212 | An empty selection is refused, never read as "every row that matches" (CRM-S3.8b review). D-48 and CRM-S3.8 make an export the rows that match the filters or the selection; CRM-S3.6 offers the bulk actions (export among them) only once at least one row is selected, and the toolbar's Export, with nothing selected, sends no selection at all. A list that arrives empty is a selection that lost its rows: reading it as "everything" would turn that mistake into the whole table, the one export the product guards most. So `exportTable` refuses `keys: []` itself, `invalid`, "Select at least one row to export, or clear the selection to export every row that matches.", without asking the database (which refuses it too, 22023, as before), and nothing is recorded. Left out or null still means every row that matches |
| M-213 | The owners' export alert needs only the sender (CRM-S3.8b review, M-206). The dispatcher holds a kind back until the settings its email uses are valid: the prospect's confirmation, which links to nothing, waits for no `APP_BASE_URL`. The first build tied `export_alert` to the advisor readiness, which also needs `APP_BASE_URL`, though the email holds no link, so an unset app address would have held a security alert back. It now has its own readiness, `sender` (a valid `EMAIL_FROM`), in `kindReadiness`, `sendableKinds` and the building of the message. `member_left` and `firm_closing` hold no link either and are still tied to the advisor readiness, as they were before this story; they are not changed here |
| M-214 | The export alert says only the reasons it was queued for (CRM-S3.8b review, M-207). The database queues it for more than 500 rows, or for the member's export that is exactly the third in the hour, and writes the real count of the hour (`exports_in_hour`) either way. The email names the frequency only when that count is exactly three, so a large export that happens to be the fourth (or the 21st, which the first build would have called "21th") is told as a large export only; a large third names both. `parseExportAlert` refuses a payload that is neither large nor a third (a payload the database does not write), so it is given up on rather than sent with no reason |

The frame gained `TableView.sort` and `TableView.saved` (whose it is and what the person may do with it: the database's answer, `list_saved_views`), and `address.ts` gained `viewOf`, `sortFor` and `isModifiedView`. The queue's `useQueueTable()` adds the saved views after the built-in ones; its page calls `useViewActions` and hands `ViewTabs` `manage` and `onRetry`, and hands the open saved view to `useTableColumns` and `ColumnsMenu` (`view`). The next table copies those lines and adds its name to the database's `workspace_table` (one small migration). `useTableContextKey` now takes `{ id }` (it only ever read the id) |
| M-216 | What a view made from the screen keeps (CRM-S3.4; D-173's "stores exactly the address's filters"). The filters in force, the sort on screen and the columns shown, in order. The filters are the open tab's own first, then the chips (`effectiveFilters`, at most 20 together): from "All statuses" that is exactly the address's filters, as D-173 says; from a tab it is the tab's own as well, because a view saved from Won with one chip on it would otherwise lose Won, and the person saved what they were looking at. The sort is the address's, else the open view's own, else none: the table's own order is stored as no sort, so the view follows the table's order if it ever changes. The columns are `columns.map((c) => c.id)`, all of them even when they are the table's defaults (M-193: a view is what was on screen). A search and a page are not kept. The address names a view by its id and drops one the table doesn't list (D-141), so what the person sees is never a view before it exists or after it is gone, and nothing waits for the list to be read again: save and "Save changes" write the view (new or changed, with what the database will say the person may do: `canShare` is whether they are an owner) into the list the table reads (`setQueryData`); save also writes the columns the view was saved with into the columns cache (`usePrimeColumnChoice`, so opening the view draws no grey rows while the database is asked what the person already knows) and "Save changes" does not (M-257); both move the address to the view with no chip and no sort that it holds (what the person added meanwhile stays over it: M-256), and ask the list again in the background; rename and share change the view in the list; delete (and stopping to share a colleague's view, M-222) leave the address for "all" and take the view out of the list. Tests make the second read never answer (`readsOnlyOnce`) and fail without each write. The address an action ends on is the address of NOW (a ref to the latest `change`): a person who typed in the search while the database was answering keeps it; and once the page is gone (the person opened a row) the action does not move the address at all (M-253). A first draft waited for the table to list the new view before moving the address; it was removed because no test, in jsdom or in Chromium, could tell it apart, even with the library's notifications made late (`notifyManager.setScheduler`): the page reads what the library holds at every render, so the first render after the list is written already has the view. The order "address first, list second" for a view that goes is kept for the same reason it was written, and is equally unobservable |

A saved view is changed when the address holds something over it: `isModifiedView(address)` is `sameAddress` of the address, with the page and the search left out (a view keeps neither), and the bare view (its id alone), because the view's own filters and order are the tab's and are not in the address. A sort equal to the order the address has without it (the view's own, else the table's default) is no sort: the codec drops it (this extends D-147's last sentence, which said it of the table's default), so a view's own order never makes it look changed and a third click on its column goes back to it. Choosing a tab that has an order of its own clears the address's sort (the tab's link says `sort: null`), so the order it was saved in is the one shown; choosing a tab without one leaves the person's sort alone, as built-in tabs always did. `OrderLine`: while the view's own order shows it says nothing (a header marks it, as for a table whose default is one column); once another column is sorted it says "Sorted by Employees, ascending." with "Back to the order of “Hot leads”", which goes to the view's order (the table's "Back to newest first" would be wrong there); a view with no order of its own keeps the table's way back |

The partners page has no filter, so its export is every partner of the firm. The activity page's filters are the person and the kind: the kind as `{field: "kind", op: "any_of", value: [kind]}`, the person as `{field: "who", op: "is", value: <their email>}`, or "Nevermore" for everyone at Nevermore (D-33; the page's list holds an id, which the database's `who` does not). The page the reader is on (its cursor) is no filter, so the file holds every page. A person who has left has no email in the record, and every such person is the same empty `who`: asking for one would hand over the others', so for a former member chosen alone the page offers no button and says why, in one line. The time zone sent is the browser's (`Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`), in which the database counts a date filter's days; the page sends no date filter today. The filters are read when the button is pressed, not when the entries on screen were asked for |
| M-233 | The file (CRM-S3.8). CSV as RFC 4180 writes it: commas between cells, a cell in double quotes when it holds a comma, a double quote, a carriage return or a line feed, each double quote doubled, lines ended with CRLF (the last too), a header line of the page's words. **UTF-8 with a byte order mark** (`EF BB BF`): Excel, which a broker is likely to open it in, reads a file with no mark in the computer's own code page, so "José" and "北京" arrive as mojibake; the cost is one invisible character at the start of the first header for a tool that does not expect it. Declared as `text/csv;charset=utf-8`. **Moments are the ISO text the database writes**, UTC to the millisecond (M-202): not converted to the person's zone (the same export would say one thing in California and another in New York, and the file would stop agreeing with what the owners' alert and the database say), and not cut to a day. Words, not codes, wherever the page has words (M-231); a missing value is an empty cell; a number is its digits. The file is built from `rows` and nothing else: nothing on screen is read |
| M-234 | The formula guard (CRM-S3.8). A cell a spreadsheet could run as a formula when the file is opened (CSV injection) is made plain text with one apostrophe in front: **a cell whose first character, after any spaces, is `=`, `+`, `-` or `@`, or whose first character is a tab, a carriage return or a line feed**. The rule asked for `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab and carriage return; the line feed and the leading spaces are added on purpose, because which spreadsheet skips or strips what before reading a formula is not something this page controls, and a firm's names and codes are worth no rule that depends on it. Applied to every cell and header, before the quoting: `=HYPERLINK("http://x","y")` is written `"'=HYPERLINK(""http://x"",""y"")"`. A cell that only contains one of the characters (`Bay=Law`, `A-B`, `Smith @ Co`) is left as it is. The rule looks at the start of a cell only. The apostrophe shows in some spreadsheets, which is the cost of a guard that does not depend on how the file is opened, and it is accepted: a partner named `-Acme` reads `'-Acme`. The only number in an export is a row count, never negative |
| M-235 | The file's name (CRM-S3.8): `<what it holds>-<day>.csv`, `referral-partners-2026-11-14.csv` and `who-did-what-2026-11-14.csv`, and nothing else: no firm, no person, no filter. The day is the one the database says the export was made (`exportedAt`), counted in the person's own time zone, so an export made on the evening of the 14th in California is the 14th although it is the 15th in UTC. The browser adds its own number to a second file of the same name |

(the singular for one), the number the database's `rowCount` says it recorded and never the length of the rows; no toast as well, which would be read a second time. The message stays until the next press. Focus is on the button throughout. **During Nevermore support (`isSupport`) the button is replaced by the one plain line of D-123** ("Downloads are off during Nevermore support."), through `useDownloadsOff`, the one place every download control asks; the database refuses a support session all the same (D-128). **Owners only on the activity page**: the page shows the record to owners (and, read-only, to support) and to no one else, and the button is inside what it shows, so a lead or an agent is told the record is for owners and is offered nothing; the database refuses them all the same (`forbidden`, M-201) |
| M-237 | What a refusal says (CRM-S3.8, D-175, D-178, M-210, M-211). An alert (`role="alert"`) beside the button, in plain words; the page changes in no other way: the rows stay, the list is not asked again, no file is saved, focus is on Export. `sign_in_again`: the contract's sentence ("Sign in again to export the whole table: you last signed in more than 12 hours ago.") and a "Sign in again" button, which calls `signOut("expired")`: at a firm's address that ends that address's session only (D-27), and the route guard sends the person to sign in and remembers this page, as it does for any ended session, with the sign-in page's own words for it ("Your session expired. Sign in again to continue."); then they press Export again. That reuses the way an ended session is already handled rather than adding a reason for ending one, and the sign-in is the fresh one the database asks for (M-205). `too_many` and `invalid`: the database's own sentence, as it comes, never one built from a number (M-211), the only database words the page shows. `forbidden`: the words of a lost access that `LoadFailed` uses ("You no longer have access to this firm's records. If you should still have it, ask an administrator to grant it again."), never the database's. `support`: "Nevermore support can look, not export." (the button should not have been there). `failed`, and anything that is not a refusal at all (a bug of ours, a dropped connection): "We couldn't export the referral partners. This is usually a dropped connection or a problem on our side, not something you did." with a "Try again" button that exports once more and puts focus on Export. An ended session (`unauthenticated`): "Your session has expired. Taking you to sign in again…", and the person is signed out, as every other call does |

The file is handed over with a Blob and an anchor with `download` (`downloadBlob`, the helper every download of the product uses): an object URL, revoked sixty seconds later, and no request to anywhere. The app's content security policy (`vercel.json`) does not stand in its way: the enforced header sets only `frame-ancestors`, `object-src` and `base-uri`, and the report-only header blocks nothing and its fetch and form directives (`default-src`, `connect-src`, `form-action`) are about requests the page makes and forms it submits, which an export makes none of besides the database's (already in `connect-src`). A test (`AdvisorPartnersPage.export.test.tsx`) pins that the export fetches nothing, opens no tab, leaves no link in the page and lets the object URL go |

Every other entry reads as it did |
| M-240 | What CRM-S3.8a leaves to a later story (D-24). **Exporting a selection** (CRM-S3.6): the button sends no `keys` and never an empty list (M-212); the story that adds row selection sends the keys with the same call, and its bulk bar's Export hides itself for support through the same `useDownloadsOff` (D-123). **Custom fields in an export** (CRM-X3.1): they have no column in the database's list of the two tables, and arrive with the table frame's `text` (M-231). **Accounts, leads and quotes** (CRM-A1.1, CRM-L1.1, CRM-Q4.1): each adds its table to `EXPORT_COLUMNS` and the database's list (M-201) and gives the button its own columns; the queue is never exported (D-48). **Person filters beyond an email** (CRM-A1.1), for the activity page's person. **The Columns menu and saved views on these two pages**, with their move onto the table frame (D-184). **A sort argument** (M-204): the file is in the table's own order, partners by name and the record newest first, not the order of any other sort. **Clearing the message when the filters change**: "Exported 40 entries." stays until the next press, true of the export it was said for |
| M-241 | The count, the visible count and the rows are read in one statement (CRM-S3.8b second review; `20261118171000_export_fixes_again.sql`; corrects M-211). M-211's build read them in three statements, and under READ COMMITTED each statement takes its own snapshot: a partner committed between them was returned but not counted (a review probe and the two-connection case in `export.sql` both gave four rows returned, three counted and recorded), so the recorded count was not the count returned (D-48), the whole-table decision (and with it the recent sign-in, M-205) could be made on other rows than the ones returned, and the cap and the alert could judge a different export. `20261118170500`'s header (item 1) and M-211 said the count was still true; they were wrong. Now one statement with three materialized parts: the matching rows counted no further than one past the cap; the visible rows counted no further than one past that; and the rows built, sorted and gathered only when the first count is within the cap (the build is gated on it, so an over-cap export still builds nothing and is refused in milliseconds: the 200,000-row case keeps its bound). The count recorded and returned, the whole-table decision and the alert's `row_count` and `large` are all taken from the rows built. Every rule, refusal and lock is as it was. | M-242 | A known limit of "whole table" (M-205), for the owner to decide; the rule is not changed. Whole-table is judged per call, by what one export holds, so a person can export every row but one and then the last row, each without a recent sign-in: a review verified it with a sign-in 20 hours old, keys `[p1, p2, p3]` and then `[p4]` both succeeding. It follows from judging by effect rather than intent, which the docs chose; the owners' alert (M-206, M-207) still sees the third export within an hour, and the firm's record shows every export. A possible remedy, not built: count the rows the same person already exported from the same table within a window (the record holds the table and count of each export, though not which rows), and ask for a recent sign-in when the exports together reach every visible row |
| M-245 | A file is saved only to the screen it was asked on (CRM-S3.8a review; amends M-236 and M-238; "unchanged" is read as unchanged the whole time, M-250, and the change is known as it is drawn, M-251). An export is asked, the call takes a while, and the screen can change before it answers: another firm chosen, the button gone (another page, the sign-in screen, a session that ended), the person signed out or another person, Nevermore support begun (downloads are then off, D-123), or the filters or columns the button asks for changed. So the button is not there until the entries on screen answer the person and the kind now chosen (the page's `asked`), and is back with the new answer; over an answer of nothing it is not there (M-230). **The next page is no filter**: the export holds every page (M-232), so the button stays while the next page loads. The partners page has no filter and nothing on it narrows an export: after an add or a save its list is asked for again while the old one stays, and the export is of the firm's whole table either way, so it has no such case |
| M-247 | A column's text may depend on its whole row (CRM-S3.8a review; amends M-231). `ExportColumnSpec.text` is given `(value, row)`, the row by column name as the database returned it, because the page's words are not always a function of one cell. **What** is `entryLabel(kind, record_type)`, as the page's What cell is, so the close of a use of support reads "Nevermore support's access ended" and not "opened your firm's console"; the first version used the kind alone and made every end of a support session read as an opening, in a file an owner may use to check Nevermore's access. **Person it was about** is the email, "A former member" for a membership whose person's account is gone (as the page's Record cell says), and empty for every other entry, on which the page names no person.
