| M-248 | The formula guard looks past what cannot be seen (CRM-S3.8a review; amends M-234). A cell is held back when, after any run of Unicode white space (the no-break and ideographic spaces, the byte order mark, a vertical tab, a form feed, a line separator), control characters and invisible format characters (a zero-width space or joiner, a direction mark), its first character is `=`, `+`, `-`, `@` or the fullwidth `＝`, `＋`, `－`, `＠` (U+FF1D, U+FF0B, U+FF0D, U+FF20); and a cell whose first character is a tab, a carriage return or a line feed is held back whatever follows, as M-234 had it. Whether each spreadsheet reads each of these as a formula is not verified; the guard errs on the side of plain text, which costs one apostrophe, and the cells it leaves alone are every ordinary name, `A-B`, `Smith @ Co`, `12.5` and a name with accents. The rule is written with `\u{...}` codes, never as the characters themselves: an invisible character written into the source is invisible to the next reader and to a search for it |
| M-249 | Checked and left alone (CRM-S3.8a review): `Refused` signing out more than once. `signOut` gets a new identity at every render of `AuthProvider`, so the effect that ends an ended session (`[refusal, signOut]`, the pattern `LoadFailed` uses) can run again if the provider renders while the page is still on screen. A throwaway probe with the real `AuthProvider`, a faked client whose `signOut` raises SIGNED_OUT, and the partners page showing an ended session: behind a guard that takes the page away when the user is gone (as `RequireAccess` does for every workspace page) the client was signed out **once**; with the page left on screen after the sign-out (no guard) it was signed out **twice**. Every page that has the button sits behind that guard (D-37), so in the product it is once, and a second sign-out of a client that is already signed out is what a page with no guard would add. Not changed, and no test kept for a case the product does not have; the same pattern in `LoadFailed` is as it was |
| M-250 | "Unchanged" means unchanged the whole time (CRM-S3.8a second review; amends M-245). M-245 compared the screen when the database answered with the one the export was asked on, which proves only that it came back to the same value, not that it stayed: Nevermore support begun and ended (downloads off, then on), a filter changed and put back, another firm chosen and the first chosen again, another person signed in and the first back, or the columns reordered and restored, all before the answer, left the two equal, and the old file was saved. Verified before the change, one button and each of the five in turn (`ExportButton.test.tsx`): the file was saved every time. Now the button counts the screens it has been on: one number, in one ref, that goes up each time the screen it is on ends (any part of M-245's value changes, or the button goes) and is read as the press starts. The file is saved only if the number is still the one read, so the button was on one screen from the press to the answer. The value of M-245 is still compared by value, now only to know when a screen ends, so a page drawing the button again with equal arrays moves nothing and the file is saved (the unchanged control). It is the screens that were drawn that count: a change and its undoing that React draws as one (batched) is never a screen the person saw, and moves nothing; not tested, as no page does it. A refusal that arrives after the number has moved is silent, as M-245 has it. No state is added: the count is not drawn, and a state would draw the button again for nothing |
| M-251 | The end of a screen is known as it is drawn, not after (CRM-S3.8a second review; amends M-245). The count of M-250 goes up in a layout effect (`useLayoutEffect`), whose cleanup runs as React commits the change; the first version was a passive effect (`useEffect`). React 18 runs the passive effects at the end of the commit when the update is a synchronous one, as a click's is (observed here: with a click still on `window.event` they ran inside the commit); for any other update (an answer arriving, a session that ended, support begun, a firm chosen from a response) it draws the page and runs them in a later turn (observed here: the answer was read before them). An answer from the database that is read between the two found the record still naming the screen just gone, and the old file was saved to the new screen (verified: firm changed and button gone, both red with the passive effect, the answer read before the effect ran, and green with the layout one). The tests of M-245 never showed it, since each changes the screen inside `act`, which runs every effect when it ends. A layout effect is the place for a ref that code outside React's drawing reads at once. Its setup is empty on purpose: the screen is recorded by the count and by nothing else, and what changes the count is the cleanup, which runs on every change of the value and when the button goes |
| M-252 | How the gap of M-251 is shown in a test, so that it is not simplified away (CRM-S3.8a second review). Neither `rerender` (it runs inside `act`, which flushes every effect when it ends) nor `flushSync` outside `act` (a sync update, which React flushes at the end of its commit) opens the gap: both were tried against the passive effect and stayed green. The test makes it with an update that is nobody's event, made outside `act` (`IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` off for that update only) on a page that holds the button's props in state and can be changed from outside: React draws it on its own turn and leaves the passive effects to the next. The answer is resolved in a layout effect of the page, and the page then spends 20 ms, so React, which hands its work on after about 5 ms, leaves the effects to a later turn and the answer, already waiting, is read between the two. Two things in it belong to the test and not to the product: jsdom leaves the click on `window.event` after `userEvent`'s press, and React then takes the next update for the person's own and flushes its effects at once, so the test hides `window.event` for the update (a browser does not keep it); and the 20 ms is how a slow drawing is made. The test file was run eight times in a row, green each time, and the new cases are red with the passive effect (both cases) and green with the layout one. What the checks were: the passive effect for the layout one (the two new timing cases red, nothing else); the comparison of the final value only, in place of the count (the five put-back cases red, nothing else); and no increment, the count captured as always stale, the effect keyed by identity or by the firm alone, the check taken out of the refusal and out of the answer, and the count raised on arriving instead of on leaving (each red on its own tests) |
| M-253 | A saved-view action answered after the person has left the queue does not move the address (CRM-S3.4a review; amends M-217). Save, "Save changes", delete and stopping to share a colleague's view each end by moving the address (onto the new view, off the deleted one), and the database can answer after the person has opened a row or pressed Back. The move was made on whatever page was drawn then (it is the router's `navigate`, not the page's), so a person on a submission was put back on the queue and a history entry was pushed: the reviewer's probe read `/advisor/submissions/s1` before the answer and `/advisor?view=view-1` after, and each of the four actions did it. `useViewActions` keeps whether the page is still drawn (a ref set by an effect and cleared by its cleanup), and `moveAddress`, the one way an action reaches the address, does nothing once it is not. Chosen over comparing the pathname the action started on: "the page is gone" is the condition itself and needs no copy of the address. Not skipped: the writes to the list and to the columns cache, the list asked again, the status and the focus request (they are the firm's and the library's, not the page's, and the queue is drawn from them when the person comes back, with the new view among its tabs). So a person who goes back finds the queue as they left it, not opened on the view they were saving (by the code's reading the same when the answer comes after they have come back, since the page that asked is the one that is gone; not tested). Tested once per action (`AdvisorDashboardPage.savedViews.test.tsx`, "an action that is answered after the person has left the queue"): the answer is held, the person leaves for a row (through the router, because the question that delete and stopping to share put over the page makes its links unreachable), the answer is let through, and they are still on the row and Back lands on the queue (no entry was pushed). Each of the four calls put back to the plain `change`, and the check itself removed: each red |
| M-254 | A read never replaces what the person chose, in the query library either (CRM-S3.1a review; completes M-196 and the rule of M-189 that what the person chose on the page wins over what was read). M-196 made a change survive leaving the table and coming back by writing it into the query library; two reads could still write an answer from before it over that, found by a probe that opens a row and comes back. (1) On a saved view, "Back to this view's columns" asks the database again once the choice is forgotten (M-193), and that read wrote its answer whatever came after it: a column shown before it answered was kept in the database and lost on return, and the next change was built from the view's own columns and took that column out of the database too. (2) Refresh asks for the person's columns with everything else (M-189), and its read can answer before the database holds a change that is still being kept, or after it, late: the person opens a row, comes back to the columns they had, and the next change is built from those. One rule, in two places in `useTableColumns`. The read compares the library's count of writes to that answer (`dataUpdateCount`, the library's own and so the same for a read begun by a page that has since been left) from when it asked to when it was answered, and when the page wrote in between (the person chose, or a save of theirs was acknowledged) it hands back what the page wrote, not what it read. And the acknowledgment of a save writes the choice again when nothing has been chosen since, because a read that was asked and answered while the save was on its way has already replaced it and no write came after it. The same early return stops a forgotten choice being read again after a later change (that read would be answered from the database as it was before the change; no test tells it apart, because the later change's acknowledgment writes over it) and stops the earlier change's acknowledgment writing over a later one that could not be kept, which stays on the table for the visit (M-188). **Refresh keeps asking for the person's columns**, the other fix on offer being to leave the `context/columns` key out of `useRefreshTable`: the slow-load line's "Try again" is the same function and is how a person gets out of a choice that never arrives (M-190; the test "says it is taking longer than usual after ten seconds…" asks for the columns again through it), and M-189's rule is about what a repeated read may write, not a reason for it not to be asked. The suggested fix for (1) (ask with `fetchMyColumns` and write the answer only while no later change has been made) was not used, because it would leave Refresh's read, (2), able to write over the re-write when it answers late: the group's fourth test is red with only the re-write. Tested through the page ("a column choice and the answers that come after it"): the re-read answered late on a saved view; Refresh answered before the save was acknowledged; Refresh answered after it; a later change that could not be kept while an earlier one is acknowledged. Mutations, each red: the re-write after a save removed (the second and fourth tests), the comparison on reads removed (the first and fourth) or inverted, the early return removed (the third); removing the token check in the way-back's `setChosen` was first green and is red since the first test also asks that the person still has a choice of their own afterwards (the way back is still offered) |
| M-255 | A saver made an agent is told why, in sentences of ours (CRM-S3.4a review; `tableSettingsApi`). The database refuses rename, change and delete with 42501 for two reasons: the person did not save the view, or it is shared and they are no longer an owner (`20261117120500_table_settings_fixes.sql`); every 42501 was worded as the first, so an owner who saved a view and was made an agent while the page was open was told they were not its saver. The database's text is not passed through, as it is for PT409. Both product texts are written for people, but 42501 is also what Postgres raises itself, and the person can be sent that text: run against the e2e database with its real migrations, `SET ROLE anon; SELECT public.rename_saved_view(...)` says `42501: permission denied for function rename_saved_view` (these functions are revoked from `anon`; `supabase/tests/table_settings.sql` expects 42501 of that call) and the table says `permission denied for table saved_views`. D-179's rule is that a server sentence that is not the product's own does not reach a person. So `refusal` uses the database's text only to tell the two apart (`SHARED_IS_THE_FIRMS`) and says a sentence of the call's own: "A shared view is the firm's, so only an owner can rename it." (and change, delete) for the first, and for every other 42501 "Only the person who saved this view can rename it." as before; share and stop sharing have one sentence each and are as they were. Tested in `tableSettingsApi.test.ts`: the shared-view refusal of the three calls, and a raw permission refusal of all five never showing its text. Mutations, each red: the text passed through for the other 42501s, the shared-view sentence never chosen, always chosen, and its words changed. Left as it is: the bar still offers Rename and Delete to that person until the list is read again (a minute at most, M-221), because the database's answer is what draws it; and `failureWords` (D-178) still counts any 42501 text as the product's, which no call of this story hands it |
| M-256 | What an answer takes off the address, and where focus goes (CRM-S3.4a review; amends M-217 and M-226). Save and "Save changes" ended on `{ filters: [], sort: null }`: every chip and the sort in the address at the moment of the answer, not the ones the view now holds, so a filter or a column the person put on while the database was answering vanished without a word and was not in the view either. They now take off the chips that are in what was kept (`keeps.filters`, compared as the address writes them) and the sort when it is none or the same column and direction as the one kept; what the person added stays over the view, which reads as changed (M-218) and is theirs to keep or drop. The filter list is still written whole, so a filter held for a field that has not loaded would go with it, as before (the queue has none). **Focus, decided**: the new view's tab takes it only while it is still in the Save form that asked, or nowhere (the pressed button went with the change). Anywhere else (the search box the person went on to type in, a row, the Columns menu) is where they put it, and it stays: moving it sends the rest of what they type nowhere. The Save form's panel is drawn outside the tabs, so it carries `data-view-controls` for `ViewTabs`'s check. A clause that also counted the tabs and the bar as "with the controls" was written first, and no test in jsdom and none in Chromium (`e2e/saved-views.spec.ts`, the Save form by keyboard) could tell it apart, so it was removed (M-229's way); Delete and stopping to share a colleague's view are as they were (M-223): their question is modal, nothing else can have focus while it is open. Tested through the page, each way: a chip and a sort added while a view is saved and while its changes are saved (the sort in the same column the other way round, and in another column the same way round, so neither half of the comparison can go), and focus left in the search box after each; "Save changes" still sends it to the view's tab when the person stayed. Mutations, each red: everything cleared again, the chips alone, the sort alone, no chip taken off, the sort compared by column only and by direction only, never kept; focus always moved, never moved, the nowhere clause removed, the form's marker removed |
| M-257 | Two ids, and a note that was wrong (CRM-S3.4a review). `ViewTabs` and `ViewBar` described their "can't be used yet" buttons by fixed ids (`save-view-unavailable`, `save-changes-unavailable`), which a second table on a page would repeat, so a button's `aria-describedby` would point at the first table's reason; both use `useId`, as the rest of the frame does. Tested by drawing the queue twice in one document: each of the four buttons describes itself by an id that exists once and says its reason; each fixed id put back is red. Seen and left alone (not asked): the queue page's own `refresh-unavailable` is a fixed id of the same kind, and it is the page's, not the frame's. **M-217's note that "Save changes" writes the columns into the columns cache was wrong**: only save calls `primeColumns`. The note is corrected and the code is not changed. For a view this app made (it always saves its columns), what the cache holds for the view is what is on screen, which is what "Save changes" saves (the person's own choice was written when they made it; otherwise it is the view's own columns), so a write would change nothing; and the one that would change something, setting the source to "view", would be wrong for a person who has their own choice on it. By the code's reading, the cache and the database can differ afterwards only for a view with no columns of its own (the database allows one; this app does not make one) shown to a person who has a choice for the table: the database's answer for it turns from "mine" to "view" once "Save changes" gives it columns, while the cache says "mine" until it is read again, so "Back to this view's columns" is offered a little too long. No test pins that and it is not changed |

### CRM-S1.2a decisions

The search box in the menu, over CRM-S1.2b (D-240).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-620 | A record is noted as opened by the page that shows it, once the page has loaded it (`useNoteOpened`, `note_recent_record`): an account's page and a contact's page. Neither called it before this story. A failed or refused note is silent (the list of recent records is a convenience), and a note the database accepts makes the box ask for its recent records again. An opportunity and a quote have no page of their own (D-187, CRM-O1.1b), so none is noted yet; CRM-O1.1b adds its page to the same hook |
| D-621 | The box shows the last five opened (`RECENT_SHOWN`) whenever fewer than two characters are typed, with each record's kind named, and "Nothing opened yet. Type to search." when there are none. From two characters, after a 300 ms pause (the tables' own), it shows the groups Accounts, Contacts, Opportunities and Quotes, each with up to five and an empty group left out. It sits in the menu under the firm's name, on every workspace page, and ends (empties and closes) on Escape, a click elsewhere, choosing a record or any change of page |
| D-622 | Where a result opens: an account `/accounts/<id>`, a contact `/contacts/<id>`, an opportunity its submission's page, or is plain text when it has none (D-187), and a quote its submission's page. The same rule serves the recent records |
| D-623 | "See all" is offered for accounts only, and only when there are more than five: it opens the Accounts table with the words as its search (`/accounts?q=`). Contacts, opportunities and quotes have no table of their own, so a "see all" for them would go nowhere (D-240, D-186); each gets one with its table |
| D-624 | "Nothing matches" says so with the words typed and a New account button that opens CRM-A1.2's `NewAccountDialog` over the page with the words as the account's name (`initialName`); saving opens the new account (and "Save and add a contact" opens it with the contact form). A lead is not offered until leads exist (D-186). It is not said while a search is still out or failed: a failure says the database's own words |

## Epic 4 decisions (2026-11-14)

These came from grilling Epic 4's stories (CRM-O1.1, CRM-O1.2, CRM-A2.3, CRM-A2.4, CRM-O1.3 with Q-4.1) against the
glossary, the ADRs, the PRD, screens 19, 25 and 26 and the code at Epic 3's tip (`crm/epic-03-accounts-contacts` at
the merge of #143). The founder took every recommended answer. What exists: `accounts`, `contacts` and
`opportunities` (CRM-Q1.1a), an opportunity's stage worked out from its submission's pipeline stage (D-206, D-216),
quote requests with the won quote kept on the submission (D44), and the per-PEO completeness check in the browser
(`src/data/peoCompleteness.ts`). What does not: any save function for an opportunity, an account's page (Epic 3
row 11), placements, renewals, commission, and a way for an agent to start an intake (New quote, Epic 5).

Epic 4 is grilled now and built after the Epic 3 rows each story needs have merged. The decisions below hold for
the whole epic; each story's own decisions are in its section, written in its pull request, from its own range of
numbers so the five can be written side by side. A number a story does not use stays unused.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-400 | Branches as D-2 and D-3: `crm/epic-04-opportunities`, cut from Epic 3's branch, with its pull request into that branch; one `crm/04/<story id>` per story with its pull request into the epic branch. Each story's pull request holds its grilling first and its build after, on the same branch |
| D-401 | Builders as D-189: Sonnet builds every story at high effort, Opus reviews every story, and a story that fails its review twice is rebuilt by Opus. The riskiest are CRM-O1.2 (it changes the send and quote functions) and CRM-O1.3a (one step that writes five tables) |
| D-402 | What a later epic builds stays off Epic 4's screens (D-24, D-38: no dead buttons): Email the contact and the Emails column (Epic 6), Send to a PEO, Send to another PEO and Choose PEOs (Epic 6), Compare (N-3.1, Epic 7), PEOs authorized (Q-2.x, Epic 6), Remind (Epic 6), "Save and start the quote" and "Start from last year's certified answers" (New quote, Epic 5), and Home's renewals (CRM-H1). Each later story adds its part where Epic 4 leaves the place for it |
| D-403 | Until Epic 6 a send and a quote are still recorded by hand on the submission's page (D44, D-8). The opportunity's quotes panel links each row to that page |
| D-404 | Q-4.1 is built with CRM-O1.3, not after N-3.4. What it needs is a won placement, which CRM-O1.3 makes; N-3.4 (the employer chooses) changes who first names the quote, not what is recorded when it is won |
| D-405 | Epic 4's decisions take D-400 onward, so Epic 3 can keep numbering without a clash (renumbered 2026-11-14 from D-219 to D-292 when Epic 3 reached D-225). Section ranges: CRM-O1.2 D-408 to D-420; CRM-O1.1 D-421 to D-435; CRM-A2.3 D-436 to D-445; CRM-A2.4 D-446 to D-460; CRM-O1.3 with Q-4.1 D-461 to D-480. ADR 0026 is CRM-O1.2's, ADR 0027 is CRM-O1.3's |
| D-406 | Words are added to `GLOSSARY.md` when each story is built (rule 6), from the words its section settles: **Placement**, **Renewal**, **Lost reason**, **Commission basis**, **Commission payment**, **Former client**, and the opportunity's stage no longer "until CRM-O1.2" |
| D-407 | Every new table has its firm, its row security by its account's visibility (`can_see_firm_record`, as contacts and opportunities), its two-firm probe (D-28), its function registry entries, its line in `firm_closing_plan()` and, where an agent edits it, `record_track` (history and version, D-45, D-46). Every write refuses a closing firm (ADR 0023) and a support session (ADR 0024) first, and asks the level rule (D-209) through `can_change_firm_record` (D-222) |

### Epic 4 build order

Lanes as Epic 3's: **D** the database, **A** the accounts and opportunities pages. Under D-105 every migration
sorts after Epic 3's last (`20261119230000`), in this order. "Epic 3 row n" is a row of Epic 3's build order.

| # | Story | What it covers | Needs | Builder | Migration |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CRM-O1.2 | The stage: Lost without an intake kept on the opportunity, the first send and the first quote move the pipeline stage (ADR 0026), stage changes in the opportunity's history with their cause, and what would move it on | none | Sonnet (D), risky | `20261120090000` |
| 2 | CRM-O1.1a | The opportunity's page in the database: one read of the opportunity, its quotes, its intake and its contacts; the save with its version (D-46) and the level rule (D-209); CRM-E1.5's calls, notes and tasks extended to an opportunity, and the activity read (its history, its stage changes, its quote requests' events, and its calls, notes and tasks; D-427) | 1, Epic 3 row 16 | Sonnet (D) | `20261120120000` (the earlier `20261120100000` was superseded: `20261120111000` had merged) |
| 3 | CRM-A2.4a | Placements and renewals: the tables, adding, editing and deleting a renewal by hand, the account's renewals with days away, the Renewal column and the "Renewing in 120 days" view in the accounts table's query, and Former client | Epic 3 row 8 | Sonnet (D) | `20261120110000` |
| 4 | CRM-O1.3a | Won, lost and reopen in one step each: the quote chosen and the rest closed with a reason, the placement with its commission basis and the renewal, the lost reason; commission payments (Q-4.1) | 1, 3 | Sonnet (D), risky | `20261120121000` (the earlier `20261120120000` was superseded: `20261120111000` had merged, and CRM-O1.1a took that timestamp) |
| 5 | CRM-O1.1b | The opportunity's page (`/opportunities/:id`): trail, header, stage bar, quotes, intake, activity with Add a note and Add a task, details edited in place, contacts, the page states; names on the account's page open it (replaces D-187) | 2, Epic 3 row 11 | Sonnet (A) | none |
| 6 | CRM-A2.3 | New opportunity on an account (screen 25): the form and the function that makes it | 2, Epic 3 rows 11 and 14 | Sonnet (A) | `20261120130000` |
| 7 | CRM-A2.4b | Renewals on screen: the account's Renewals tab and its count, Add a renewal, Start the renewal (the New opportunity form filled in), the Renewal column and view on the accounts table | 3, 6, Epic 3 row 10 | Sonnet (A) | `20261121160000` (D-449's link of the started opportunity to its renewal: `opportunity_create` takes the renewal, which 20261120130000's comment left to this story) |
| 8 | CRM-O1.3b | Mark won or lost (screen 26), the won or lost banner with Reopen, Record a payment, and the console's Won and Lost opening the same dialog | 4, 5 | Sonnet (A) | `20261121170000` (review fixes, after CRM-A2.4b's `20261121160000`: a win and a reopening move the opportunity's version through `won_at` and `won_by`, and the outcome is one read, `opportunity_outcome`) |

The Epic 4 merge with main (not a story row) takes `20261121150000_epic4_after_merge_records.sql`, so no other story takes it. It sorts after main's `20261121100000_merge_records.sql` (CRM-S4.3b), which
restated `firm_closing_plan` without CRM-O1.3a's commission payments step and has `merge_accounts` leave `commission_payments` behind. It adds the step back (read with `pg_get_functiondef`, raising if its anchor
line is gone), makes the payments' key to their placement deferrable, and restates `merge_accounts` and `merge_preview` so a removed account's commission payments move to the kept account with their placement.

### CRM-O1.2 decisions

Grilled 2026-11-14 against D-10, D-206, D-216, D44, ADR 0007 and `20261119100000_crm_records.sql`. Builds as Epic 4
row 1.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-408 | The stage is still worked out, never stored, with one exception. With an intake it follows the submission's pipeline stage (D-206, D-216), and Won and Lost are the submission's, which CRM-O1.3 moves. With no intake it is Qualifying, or Lost when an agent marked it so: that Lost is kept on the opportunity (`lost_at`, `lost_by`, with CRM-O1.3's reason). One truth for each opportunity, never two |
| D-409 | "An agent sets the stage only while there is no intake (Qualifying), and Won or Lost at the end": with no intake the only stage there is to pick is Qualifying, and Lost comes through Mark won or lost. Won needs a quoted quote, so it needs an intake. New opportunity's Stage box shows Qualifying and cannot be changed |
| D-410 | An opportunity marked Lost with no intake is refused an intake (CRM-Q1.1b, New quote): "This opportunity was marked lost. Reopen it before starting a quote." |
| D-411 | D-10 is built as ADR 0026. Every send recorded while the submission is Ready for market moves it to Sent to PEOs, and every quote recorded while it is Sent to PEOs moves it to Quoted, each in the same transaction as the record. The rule is the stage, not "first": a submission an advisor put back to Sent to PEOs after a quote (to send to another PEO) moves to Quoted again with the next quote, which is what it then is. Only those two moves, only forward, only from that exact stage: a send or quote recorded at any other stage moves nothing. The console's one-click offer of the next step (D44) is removed for those two moves |
| D-412 | The cause. `submission_status_history` gains `cause` (null for a person's move; `quote_send` or `quote_recorded`) and `cause_quote_request_id`, additively (ADR 0007). A move made by a send or a quote names the person who recorded it as `changed_by` |
| D-413 | "Every stage change is in the opportunity's history with who or what caused it." The opportunity's history (`record_history_list` for `opportunities`) also reads the submission's status history and shows each move that changes the opportunity's stage, mapped by `opportunity_stage_of` ("Stage: Ready to send to In market, by the send to PEO B, Dana Reyes"). A move inside one stage (submitted to under review) is not a stage change and is not shown. Read, never copied: D-130 stands, so nothing but the trigger writes `record_history`. The cursor contract of `record_history_list` is unchanged: a status-history row's entry key is a UUID worked out from its own id (`md5('submission_status_history:' || id)::uuid`), so it is stable, and both kinds of entry are ordered together by time, then entry key, as today, so a page never skips or repeats an entry that shares a time Lost and reopened with no intake are tracked fields of the opportunity |
| D-414 | What would move it on, under the stage, worked out in the browser by one helper tested through the page (D-50). Qualifying: "Start the quote to move to Intake under way". Draft: "Waiting for <name> to certify". Submitted or under review: "Waiting for the review to finish". Information requested: "Waiting for <name> to answer". Ready to send: "Send to a PEO to move to In market". In market: "Waiting for a PEO to quote". Quoted: "Mark won or lost when the employer decides". Won and Lost: nothing (the banner says it). <name> is the prospect's first name, else the main contact's, else "the employer". The words are guidance, never a button a later epic owns (D-402) |
| D-415 | On screen: Qualifying, Intake under way, Ready to send, In market, Quoted, Won, Lost. The stage bar shows the first five, then Won or, for a lost one, Lost in the last place |
| D-416 | `opportunity_stage()` stays not callable by an API role (CRM-Q1.1a); the page's read (CRM-O1.1a) calls it after deciding the person may see the opportunity |
| D-417 | Archived is unchanged (D-216): archived straight from won stays Won, from any other stage is Lost, and reopening it from archived brings the opportunity back |

### CRM-O1.1 decisions

Grilled 2026-11-14 against screen 19, D-46, D-183, D-187, D-209, D-212, D44 and `src/data/peoCompleteness.ts`.
Builds as Epic 4 rows 2 (database) and 5 (page).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-421 | The address is `/opportunities/:id` in the workspace shell, with Accounts marked in the menu. An opportunity that is not there, or that the person may not see, shows the account's not-found page, with no hint which |
| D-422 | Who sees it: whoever sees its account (CRM-Q1.1a). Who changes its details: D-209's level rule on the opportunity's own record owner; with no owner, a firm owner only |
| D-423 | One read, `opportunity_page(firm, id)`: the opportunity with its stage, account, owner and main contact; its quotes; its intake's summary; its contacts. The activity is its own read, so its failure shows in its own panel with Try again (as CRM-A2.1's lists) |
| D-424 | The quotes panel, one row per quote request: PEO, status, sent (with "· N days" while sent), admin fee per employee per month, expires, and still needs. Still needs is worked out in the browser by `peoCompleteness` against the last certified version, through the same reads the submission's page makes for its PEO needs panel (`PeoNeedsPanel`: the last certified version with its provenance, verified fields, extra answers and documents, and the PEOs' requirement profiles). These load as the quotes panel's own read, not inside `opportunity_page`, which stays a summary. A quote request is matched to its PEO's profile by the PEO's name ignoring case (a quote request is unique by it); a PEO with no profile shows "Not known". It reads "Nothing", the first missing item, or "N of its own questions". The Emails column is off (D-402). Each PEO opens the submission's page (D-403) until the quote's own page (Epic 5). A closed quote shows its close reason (CRM-O1.3) |
| D-425 | The intake panel: File ID, certified by (the prospect's name) and when, version, census size (the census rows in the last certified version), and open items (the count and the first one's label), with "Open intake" to the submission's page. PEOs authorized is off (D-402) |
| D-426 | The page states. No intake: "No intake yet" and D-414's words, with no Start the quote until New quote (Epic 5). Intake under way: who is filling it in and how many answers, with no Remind (D-402). Certified, nothing sent: "Ready to send" with "Record a send" opening the submission's page (D-403) in place of Choose PEOs. Won or lost: CRM-O1.3b's banner |
| D-427 | The activity: until calls, notes and tasks reach opportunities, the opportunity's history (its fields and its stage, D-413) and its quote requests' events (sent, quoted, declined, closed), newest first, filtered as D-212. This story extends CRM-E1.5's calls, notes and tasks to an opportunity (the PRD puts them "on a lead, account, contact or opportunity"), so Add a note and Add a task show, once Epic 3 row 16 has merged |
| D-428 | The details: account (linked), stage, target start, employees, expected close, value a year, source, next step, owner. The account and the stage are shown, never edited: the stage moves only by the intake, a send, a quote, and Mark won or lost (D-408, D-411). The rest are edited in place on the form libraries (D-51), saved with the version (D-46) and the conflict dialog (D-183), the owner as D-209 says. Custom fields wait for Epic 9 |
| D-429 | "Contacts on this search": the main contact (marked Main) and the contact made from the intake's prospect (marked "fills in the intake"), one row per contact, so the usual case, where the prospect is the main contact (D-214), is one row with both marks. Each opens its contact's page. There is no per-opportunity list of contacts or roles (not asked). The main contact is changed in the details |
| D-430 | The trail is Accounts / the account / the opportunity. Every opportunity name on the account's page and its tables opens this page, replacing D-187 |
| D-431 | The header: name, stage, target start, employees, main contact and owner, with Mark won or lost once CRM-O1.3b has merged. Email the contact and Send to a PEO are off (D-402) |
| D-432 | On an archived account the opportunity's page shows the account's archived banner and nothing on it can be changed |

### CRM-A2.3 decisions

Grilled 2026-11-14 against screen 25, D-188, D-209, D-211, D-214 and `20261119100000_crm_records.sql`. Builds as
Epic 4 row 6.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-436 | New opportunity opens from the account's header and its Opportunities tab, as a dialog over the account (screen 25). The account is filled in and shown read-only: moving an opportunity to another account is not asked |
| D-437 | Who may add one: anyone who may see the account, as adding a contact (D-209). Refused on an archived account, a closing firm (ADR 0023) and a support session (ADR 0024) |
| D-438 | The fields. Name: required, at most 200, offered as "<Month YYYY> PEO search" from the target start (D-188) and kept in step with it until the person types in the name. Target start: a date. Employees in this search: a whole number, offered from the account's. Main contact: one of the account's contacts, offered as its main contact, optional. Expected close: a date. Value, a year: dollars, not negative. Source (D-439). Stage: Qualifying, read-only (D-409, in CRM-O1.2's section, #149: with no intake Qualifying is the only stage there is to pick). Owner: offered as the maker (D-214), any current member allowed to own it. Next step: at most 2000 |
| D-439 | Source is one of a fixed list kept in one module: Referral, Renewal, Existing client, Inbound, Outbound, Event, Other. Referral asks which of the firm's referral partners, kept in a new `referral_partner_id` (built as `referral_partner_code`, CRM-A2.3: a partner has no id, its code within the firm is its key and never changes) and shown "Referral · <partner>". Renewal is set by "Start the renewal" (CRM-A2.4). The function checks the word against the list |
| D-440 | An account holds any number of opportunities, several open at once: nothing refuses another, and the form says nothing about the others |
| D-441 | "Save and start the quote" and "Start from last year's certified answers" come with New quote (Epic 5), which adds them to this form. The second is offered when an earlier opportunity on the account holds a certified intake; it starts the new intake with those answers, all to be certified again. Until then the form has Cancel and Save opportunity (D-402) |
| D-442 | One function, `opportunity_create(firm, account, fields)`, makes it (stage Qualifying, no intake) and returns it; saving opens the new opportunity's page |

### CRM-A2.4 decisions

Grilled 2026-11-14 against PRD 7 and 10 ("placements and renewals"), D-197, D-209, D-212 and CRM-A2.1's states.
Builds as Epic 4 rows 3 (database) and 7 (screens).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-446 | Two new tables, as PRD 10 lists them. `placements`: what was won (the account, the opportunity, the quote request taken, the PEO's name, the start date, the broker of record, when it ended, and CRM-O1.3's commission). `renewals`: the account, what renews (`peo_placement` or `current_plan`), with whom (at most 80), the date, the placement it came from, and the opportunity working it. Both are made here; CRM-O1.3a writes placements |
| D-447 | A renewal from a won opportunity is made by Mark won (CRM-O1.3), with the date asked there and the placement's PEO as with whom. Others are added by hand on the account: what renews (A PEO placement, or The employer's current plan), with whom, the date. A PEO placement added by hand (one the firm placed before the product) makes its placement record in the same step: the PEO, a start date if known, no opportunity and no quote. So every PEO placement, won here or not, makes the account a client and can end, by one rule (D-451). The employer's current plan makes no placement |
| D-448 | Days away is worked out each time from today: "in 45 days", "today", "12 days ago". A past renewal not done shows in the warning colour |
| D-449 | A renewal names at most one opportunity working it. "Start the renewal" opens New opportunity filled in: name "<Month YYYY> renewal" from the renewal's date, target start that date, source Renewal, employees and main contact from the account. Saving links it. While that opportunity is open the renewal shows it and Start the renewal is not offered. Lost: the renewal shows "<name>, lost" and offers Start the renewal again, which links the new one. Won: the renewal is done ("Renewed"), and the win adds the next renewal. A renewal is also done when it is marked "Didn't renew" (D-451). Done is worked out from those two and nothing else |
| D-450 | The accounts table's Renewal column is the account's nearest renewal not done, as its date and days away. "Renewing in 120 days" is a built-in view of the accounts table: accounts with a renewal not done within the next 120 days, overdue included, sorted by that date. Home's part is CRM-H1 (D-402) |
| D-451 | Former client (D-197, PRD 7). An account is a client while it has a placement not ended, or a won submission with no placement (one won before Epic 4); a former client when it has placements and all of them have ended; otherwise a prospect account. "Didn't renew" on a renewal asks the date (offered as the renewal date) and keeps it on the renewal (`not_renewed_on`). That renewal is then done, so it leaves the Renewal column and the 120-day view, and the placement it came from ends on that date. Undo clears the date, which brings both back. An account's status is still worked out, never stored |
| D-452 | Who. Renewals and placements are read by their account's visibility. A renewal is added by anyone who may see the account (as a contact, D-209), and edited or deleted by whoever may change the account. A renewal made by a win is deleted only by reopening that win (CRM-O1.3) |
| D-453 | Renewals and placements get history and a version (`record_track`); the history domain gains `renewals` and `placements` |
| D-454 | The account's Renewals tab, with its count: what, with whom, the date, days away, the opportunity working it, and Add a renewal. Empty: "No renewal on file. Add a renewal" (CRM-A2.1). The Overview's renewals list shows the first five |
| D-455 | Amends D-451 (CRM-A2.4a review, 2026-11-15; the founder's standing answer). An account is a client while it has a placement not ended; with no placement at all, while it has a won submission (one won before Epic 4); a former client when it has placements and all of them have ended; otherwise a prospect account. So an account won before Epic 4 whose hand-added placement is marked "Didn't renew" becomes a former client: a won submission no longer keeps it a client once it has placements. Worked out in `account_status` and the accounts table's status, never stored |
| D-456 | For CRM-O1.3a, recorded here and not built by CRM-A2.4a. Winning the opportunity that works a renewal ends the placement that renewal came from, on the new placement's start date, so a renewal cycle never leaves a placement that cannot end (the old placement's renewal is done by the win, D-449, and the account stays a client through the new placement) |

### CRM-O1.3 with Q-4.1 decisions

Grilled 2026-11-14 against screen 26, D44's won quote (`set_submission_status`), D-204, D-209, D-216, Q-4.1 and
N-3.4. Builds as Epic 4 rows 4 (database) and 8 (screens).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-461 | Three functions, each all or none in one transaction with the version check (D-46): `opportunity_mark_won`, `opportunity_mark_lost` and `opportunity_reopen` |
| D-462 | Who. Marking won or lost: whoever may change the opportunity (D-209). Reopening ("by the owner"): the opportunity's record owner or a firm owner, as D-204 reads "its owner" |
| D-463 | Won needs a quoted quote on the opportunity's intake, and the dialog offers only quoted ones, each with its admin fee. Without one, Won shows disabled with why: "Won needs a quote the employer took. None of this opportunity's quotes is quoted yet" |
| D-464 | Marking won moves the submission to Won naming the quote (`set_submission_status`'s rule, the one place a quote is chosen). It closes every other open quote with the close reason "the employer went elsewhere" (a new `close_reason` on `quote_requests`, additive, one of `went_elsewhere` or `search_ended`), makes the placement and adds the renewal. The account becomes a client because its status is worked out (D-197, D-451). The dialog first lists each of these, naming the account, each quote it closes and the renewal date (screen 26) |
| D-465 | Defaults and rules. Start date: the quote's proposed start, else the opportunity's target start. Renewal date: a year after the start. Both are required, and the renewal must come after the start. Broker of record: text of at most 120, offered as the firm's name, required |
| D-466 | Commission (Q-4.1, D-404). The expected basis is a share of the admin fee, per employee per month (dollars) or flat a year (dollars), with its number. A share is kept as the percent a person types (20 for 20%, `numeric(5,2)`, above 0 and at most 100); dollars as `numeric(14,2)`, above 0. The dialog works out "Expected commission, a year" from it ((percent / 100) × admin fee per employee per month × employees × 12; per employee × employees × 12; flat), rounded to the cent, half up, using the opportunity's employees, else the certified census size, and shows the arithmetic. The person may overwrite the yearly figure. The placement keeps the basis, the number and the yearly figure. Commission is optional, and can be added to the placement later |
| D-467 | Commission received: `commission_payments` (the placement, received on, an amount above zero, a note of at most 500), recorded from the won banner with "Record a payment", which also shows received to date against expected. Read by whoever sees the account, never by a prospect or a PEO ("seen only by the firm"). Recorded, edited and deleted by whoever may change the opportunity, with history (`record_track`) |
| D-468 | Lost asks a reason from a fixed list: stayed with current provider, price, went direct to a PEO, no decision, other. Other requires a note of at most 500. With an intake the submission moves to Lost, which closes every open quote with the close reason `search_ended`. With no intake the opportunity keeps Lost itself (D-408). Either way the reason and note are kept on the opportunity (`lost_reason`, `lost_note`) |
| D-469 | Reopen (ADR 0027). A won opportunity goes back to Quoted. The submission moves won to quoted and the quote is no longer chosen, but stays quoted. The placement and the renewal made with it are deleted, and the account's status follows. Reopen is refused while that renewal is being worked by an opportunity ("Its renewal is being worked by <name>") or while the placement has commission payments ("Delete its payments first"), so no money record is lost. A lost opportunity goes back to the stage it was lost from (the submission's last move into lost), or to Qualifying with no intake. Its reason is cleared from the opportunity and kept in the history. Closed quotes stay closed either way |
| D-470 | One way to win or lose. The new moves (won to quoted, lost to the stage it came from) are made only by `opportunity_reopen`, and `set_submission_status` still refuses them. `set_submission_status` refuses won and lost for a submission on an opportunity ("Mark it won or lost on its opportunity"). The console's stage control (D-8) offers Won and Lost by opening the same dialog |
| D-471 | Archive keeps D-216: a won submission archived stays Won. Restoring from archived (archived to submitted) is refused by `set_submission_status` when the submission was archived straight from won or from lost, since its opportunity is then a closed search with its outcome, placement or reason: "Reopen it on its opportunity". `opportunity_reopen` restores it, with D-462's who, D-469's cleanup and refusals, and the version check: from won to quoted, from lost to the stage it was lost from. A submission archived from any other stage has no outcome to undo and is restored to submitted as today |
| D-472 | The banner (CRM-O1.1's "Won or lost" state). "Won · <PEO> · from <start> · renews <date>", with the commission line. Or "Lost · <reason>". Either has Reopen for those allowed (D-462) |
| D-473 | Won, lost, the quote taken, the reason and each reopening are in the opportunity's history with who (D-413) |
| D-474 | Winning an opportunity that works a renewal also ends the placement that renewal came from: see D-456 (CRM-A2.4a) |

### CRM-S4.3b decisions

Grilled 2026-11-15 against D-193, D-198, D-202, D-203, D-209, D-210, D-222, D-233, D-238, D-446 to D-454, CRM-S4 in the stories and the migrations named in
`20261121100000_merge_records.sql`. Builds as Epic 3 row 21 (database); the screens are row 22 (CRM-S4.3a).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-640 | What a merge is told. The kept record, the removed record, the version each was at when the person chose (both required; either having changed since refuses the merge with 40001, so a choice is never made on values that are gone), and a choice per field that differs, "kept" or "removed". A field not named keeps the kept record's value, or takes the removed one's when the kept one is blank, so nothing is lost by saying nothing; a choice that names a blank wins. The owner is the kept record's unless "removed" is chosen for it. A value no record may hold (a name, a phone the rule refuses, a contact left with no email, phone or mobile) refuses the merge in the form's words (22023) before anything moves. Accounts choose among name, trade name, website, phone, street, city, state, postal code, industry, employees, states of operation, current provider and owner; contacts among first name, last name, title, email, phone, mobile, do not email and owner. `merge_preview` reads both records with their versions, the fields that differ, what would move and, in words, why the caller may not merge them, so the screen asks nothing the merge would refuse |
| D-641 | Contacts that are one person. Merging two accounts, a contact of the removed account that is the same prospect (D-194: one contact per prospect user per account) as a contact of the kept one is merged into it, the kept account's contact winning and taking what it lacks (first name, title, email, phone, mobile), "do not email" on if either was on, and the main-contact mark if the account has no other. Every other contact moves with its calls, notes and tasks; the removed account's main contact arrives as nobody's main unless the kept account has none. Nothing else is merged by guesswork: two contacts with the same email stay two. Merging two contacts: the kept contact is main if either was, keeps the removed one's prospect link when it has none, and "do not email" is on if either was unless a choice says otherwise (the safe side for a person who asked not to be emailed) |
| D-642 | Who, and when (D-203, D-209, D-210). Two accounts: whoever may change both by the level rule, in words naming the one they may not change (42501). Not the opportunities' own owners: D-203 reads "anyone allowed to change both records", and an opportunity moves with its account as it does when the account's owner changes. The kept account must not be archived (55000: nothing can be added to it, D-222); the removed one may be. Two contacts: only on the same account (22023, in words: move one first or merge their accounts, D-210), whoever may change both by the level rule (42501), and an opportunity that names the removed contact as its main contact must be one the caller may change (42501, as move_contact's rule for the same change). Contacts of an archived account can be merged: nothing is added. Both writes ask, in the usual order, the firm (42501), a closing firm or a support session (PT423), then the records (PT404) |
| D-643 | History (D-203, D-45). The removed record's history is copied to the kept record, each entry at its own time and by its own actor, marked with the removed record's id and name (`record_history.merged_from_key`, `merged_from_name`; an entry copied by an earlier merge keeps its first mark), and the kept record gets one entry of its own, kind `merged`, naming it. The removed record's own history is deleted with it, as any deleted record's is. `record_history_list` returns `merged_from` too. A moved contact or opportunity gets the one change its own trigger writes (its account, and a contact's main-contact mark in the same change); the kept account's labels it takes are one `labels` entry; the kept account's field changes are the table's own change. A placement or a renewal tracks no account field, so its move writes nothing to its history. The history stays append only: the new entries are inserts by the merge, never an edit |
| D-644 | The old address (D-203). `merged_records` (firm, kind account or contact, the removed id, the kept id, the removed name, who, when), closed to the API, deleted with a closing firm (`firm_closing_plan`). `resolve_merged_record(firm, kind, id)` follows the chain (A into B, B later into C leads A to C) and answers the kept record's kind, id and name (a contact adds its account) only to a person who may see it, and nothing otherwise, the same as for an id never merged. A person's recent records (CRM-S1.2b) that named the removed record name the kept one, or go when the kept one is already in their list |
| D-645 | The possible-duplicate mark (D-193). Merging: an account marked a duplicate of the removed one is now marked a duplicate of the kept one; the kept account's own mark that named the removed one is cleared; a kept account with no mark takes the removed one's, so an open question about a third account is not lost. Keep separate (`account_keep_separate`): whoever may change the account by the level rule clears the mark; it shows in the account's history as any change of that field; an account with no mark is told so (22023); nothing else changes. Leads have no mark (CRM-L1.x) and are not merged here |
| D-646 | One transaction, ordered locks. Both accounts `FOR UPDATE`, lowest id first, then their contacts, opportunities, calls/notes/tasks, placements and renewals, each in id order, so add_contact, move_contact and a second merge on either account wait or are waited for (a certification's chain waits only because the chain step now holds the account it matches, D-651), and a merge of A into B and one of B into A order themselves. Two contacts: the accounts `FOR SHARE`, then the contacts `FOR UPDATE`, then their calls, notes and tasks, the order move_contact takes. The keys that name an account and a row together (an opportunity's main contact, a call's contact and opportunity, a placement's and a renewal's opportunity and a renewal's placement) are deferred inside the merge and checked once at its end; the three of placements and renewals are made deferrable here (still immediate for everyone else). A call, note or task that names neither a contact nor an opportunity moves to the kept account only while a merge of exactly that pair runs (`nevermore.merging_accounts`, as the history clean-up and a firm's closing name themselves for their own transaction), through `activities_guard`, restated with that one condition. Any failure rolls the whole merge back |
| D-647 | Where it ends. The kept account takes the removed account's contacts, opportunities (so each intake, and so its quote requests, stays on its own opportunity, and two accounts that each hold an intake keep both), calls, notes and tasks, labels (the ones in common once), placements and renewals; the removed account's bulk-change records go with it (an undo of a change to it reads them as gone). The removed account is deleted by the merge, not by `delete_account` (whose refusals protect an account that has opportunities, renewals or placements; a merge has moved them first) |
| D-648 | Migration `20261121100000_merge_records.sql`. The build order said `20261119220000`, but under D-105 migrations sort after the newest, and the newest on the epic's base is CRM-S4.2's `20261121090000` (Epic 4's own migrations use the 20261120 day, so this story stays out of it). It is loaded, with every earlier migration, at the end of every SQL test that loads CRM-A2.4a's `20261120110000`. It is written against main's placements and renewals: a placement's and a renewal's keys to an opportunity and a renewal's key to its placement are not deferrable there, so the migration makes those three deferrable (immediate for everyone else); a renewal belongs to one placement and a placement to one opportunity, so a merge never makes two of either collide; both tables delete with their account, but a merge has moved them before it deletes it |
| D-649 | Not built, and why. Emails and files that name a contact or an account (Epic 6: they will follow as the calls do); merging leads (CRM-L1.x); an unmerge (D-203); the screens (CRM-S4.3a). Contacts' and accounts' custom fields (CRM-X3.1) will join the choices when they exist. A concurrent-session test of a merge against add_contact is not written: the locks above are the order every writer already takes, and the two-session tests of those writers hold the same rows |
| D-650 | Copied history carries no version (review). A history entry's version is the record's own count of saves, and `record_version_changes` (the conflict dialog's "what changed since you opened it") picks entries by version. A copy that kept the removed record's number would be reported as a change the kept record never had. Entries copied by a merge are stored with no version, so no range matches them; the history list still shows them, marked as from the merged record. The kept record's own "merged" entry keeps its version |
| D-651 | A certification that matches an account being merged (review). The chain step (`make_intake_chain`, restated whole in this migration with one change) holds the account it matched FOR KEY SHARE before the new account names it, then looks again: an account merged away is followed through `merged_records` to the account it became (held the same way), and one that is simply gone is no match. Without it, a merge that deleted the matched account between the match and the insert made the key check fail (23503) and with it the employer's certification, which must never fail for this (ADR 0025). Tested with two real connections |
| D-652 | The owner a merge may give (review). Choosing "removed" for the owner follows the writers of the owner: for accounts `change_account_owner`'s rule and words ("Choose who will own this account." for none, "Choose a current member of the firm." for someone who has left), for contacts `update_contact`'s ("Choose one of the firm's people as the owner."); the chosen person's membership is held `FOR SHARE` to the end of the merge. Keeping the kept record's owner is never refused |

### CRM-S4.3a decisions

Merging on screen (Epic 3 row 22). The one migration is `20261121110000_account_page_duplicate_mark.sql`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-660 | The one database change. The possible-duplicate mark (D-193) was in the table but not on the account's page, so no banner could be drawn. `account_page` answers `possible_duplicate_of` inside `account`: null for no mark, `{id, name}` when the caller may see the marked account, `{id: null, name: null}` when not (the banner then says only that one exists, the way D-603 does for a match, and offers Keep separate alone). Same function, same grant, same single read; `supabase/tests/account_page.sql` holds it. Every SQL test that loads `20261121100000` loads this one after it |
| D-661 | The banner (`DuplicateBanner`). On an account marked a possible duplicate: "Possible duplicate of {name}" with a link, "Merge" and "Keep separate". The buttons are for whoever may change the account (`can_change`) and never in a Nevermore support session (ADR 0024); anyone else reads the banner. "Merge" keeps the account it names and removes this one, since the mark is on the later account; the dialog can swap. "Keep separate" acts at once (the banner says what it does) and says so in a toast, naming the other account when the person may see it. A refusal is shown in the banner in the database's words and the banner stays |
| D-662 | The old address. An account or contact page that is told "not found" asks `resolve_merged_record` once and, when it answers, replaces the address with the kept record's (`/accounts/:id`, `/contacts/:id`) and shows one note: "That address belonged to a record that was merged into {name}. This is the page it became." The note rides in the navigation state, so it is gone on the next move. An answer of nothing (never merged, or not the person's to see) and a failed lookup both read as the page's usual "isn't available": the screen never learns more than the database says |
| D-663 | The bulk bar. "Merge" is in the bar when exactly two accounts are chosen by hand (not "all that match"), not in a support session, and `accounts_can_change` returns both ids; with a different count, or a person who may not change both, it is not drawn (the preview in the dialog still says why, should rights change before it opens). The first account chosen is the one kept; the dialog can swap. A merge clears the selection and asks the table again |
| D-664 | Contacts. A contact is merged with another contact of the same account (D-210): "Merge with another contact" in the contact's page header and "Merge" beside a contact on the account's Contacts tab open `PickContactDialog`, which lists only the account's other contacts (the first 100, with the count said), then the merge dialog with the contact the person started from kept. The action is offered where the page's own `can_change` is true (the account's on the account's page, the contact's on the contact's page), also on an archived account (D-642), never in a support session; whether the person may change both contacts is the preview's to say. A contact page that was the removed one opens the kept contact |
| D-665 | The dialog (`MergeDialog`, one for both kinds). Reads `merge_preview`; shows who is kept and removed with "Keep {other} instead"; one row for each field that differs (`differs`, in the screen's order) with both values side by side as radios; says exactly what will happen (the D-203 sentence that the removed record is deleted and a merge can't be split, what moves from `moves`, that the history moves marked and the old address leads on) before anything changes. A field starts on what the database does for a field left alone (the kept value, or the removed one's where the kept is blank; "do not email" on if either is, D-641) and every differing field is sent with the choice on screen, so what is merged is what was shown. The versions sent are the preview's. Swapping resets the choices |
| D-666 | When the preview says the person may not merge (`problem`), the dialog shows the database's words and only "Close". When the records changed while choosing (40001) nothing is merged, both are asked again, the choices start over and the dialog says so. Any other refusal (a name or phone the rule refuses, a contact left with no way to be reached, an owner who has left the firm, an archived kept account) is shown in the database's own words beside the buttons, and the choices stay. Built on the form kit of CRM-S4.1: the unsaved-choices guard on closing, the failed save that keeps everything, no button disabled to point at an error |
| D-667 | After a merge the toast says what moved ("{removed} was merged into {kept}. 2 contacts, 1 opportunity, 4 calls, notes and tasks moved over."), the kept record's lists are asked again, and the removed record's cached page with them. The words live in `src/data/mergeWords.ts` |
| D-668 | Not built. Merging leads (CRM-L1.x), an unmerge (D-203), choosing the two contacts from a multi-select on the Contacts tab (a contact is merged from its own row or page), a "merge" in the account's overflow menu, and custom fields in the choices (CRM-X3.1) |

## Epics 6, 7 and 8 decisions (founder, 2026-11-15)

Grilled 2026-11-15 against `main` at Epic 4's merge (#180, #178). The founder took every recommended answer. What
exists: the receiving side of PEO mail (M-2.5, M-3.1 to M-3.6, the holding list), the broker's mailbox (M-4.1 to
M-4.3), the bundle (L-3.1 to L-3.4), accounts, contacts, opportunities, calls, notes and tasks (`activities`, which
requires an account), the level rule, bulk change and its Undo, labels on accounts, and export. What does not:
Epic 5 (New quote, the employer authorizing each PEO, M-1.1), any `leads` table, custom fields (Epic 9).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-800 | **Order, amending D-3.** Epic 5 is not built, and every send to a PEO (M-2.1 to M-2.4, Q-2.x, CRM-Q2.6) needs M-1.1. So what needs nothing from Epic 5 is built first: Epic 8 (Leads), then Epic 6's contact half (CRM-E1.1 to E1.4), then Epic 7's reading and quote work (N-1.x, N-2.x, N-3.x). The PEO half of Epic 6 and the screens of Epic 7 that sit on the quote's page (CRM-Q3.3, CRM-Q4.2) wait for Epic 5 |
| D-801 | **Branches, amending D-2 and D-3** for this line of work. The build line is the session branch `claude/admiring-keller-dd7w1a`: one commit (or a short run of commits) per slice, one draft pull request to `main`, as Epic 4's builds landed (#180) |
| D-802 | **Where slices live.** The decisions and build orders are on this page; each slice is also a GitHub issue labelled `ready-for-agent`, with its blockers. Decision numbers: Epic 6 D-600 onward, Epic 7 D-700 onward, Epic 8 D-800 onward |
| D-803 | **A lead is its own record**, the `leads` table: one row holding the person and the business (name, title, business, email, phone, state, employees, current provider, source, owner, status). A lead is not in the chain (CRM-Q1.1): it has no account, contact or opportunity until it is converted, which copies it into them. It carries its firm, row security, its two-firm probe (D-28), its line in `firm_closing_plan()` and `record_track` (history and version, D-45, D-46). Every write refuses a closing firm (ADR 0023) and a support session (ADR 0024) |
| D-804 | **Where a lead comes from** (CRM-L1.2): the source is one of Added by hand, Import, Referral partner, Firm's link. Epic 8 builds the hand form and, as its last slice, the firm's public link's "contact me" for a visitor who starts no intake. Import arrives with CRM-X4.1 (Epic 9). A linked CRM is left out: no story builds the link. A referral partner's link fills the source only where D-101 offers a link (the default firm) |
| D-805 | **Labels and custom fields on leads.** Labels are built now, from the firm's one label list (**Label**). Custom fields wait for CRM-X3.1 (Epic 9), as D-186 did for accounts; the pull request lists them as deferred |
| D-806 | **The lead bin** (CRM-L1.1). Deleting sets `leads.deleted_at`; a deleted lead is in no view but Deleted, which owners see, with Restore. A nightly `pg_cron` job removes leads deleted more than 30 days ago, with their calls, notes and tasks. A converted lead can't be deleted. Deleting from the lead's page uses the same bin |
| D-807 | **Calls, notes and tasks on a lead.** `activities` gains `lead_id`; a row belongs to exactly one of an account or a lead (a check). Converting moves a lead's rows onto the new account (and contact) in the same transaction. Added to, nothing replaced (ADR 0007) |
| D-808 | **When a lead becomes Working** (CRM-L1.3). The first logged call, the first saved change to its fields, or an agent setting it. A note or a task is not counted. Emailing a lead joins the rule in CRM-E1.2. Every lead starts New, whoever made it. Converted is set only by converting. Every status change is one history entry |
| D-809 | **Who sees and changes a lead**: the level rule as for accounts (D-209). An agent their own, a lead (role) their teams', an owner everyone's; "everyone sees everything" lets every member read. Nevermore support reads only. A lead with no owner, or whose owner has left, is changed by owners only |
| D-810 | **Converting** (CRM-L2.2). Possible matches use the duplicate match (D-193), among accounts the agent may see. A contact needs a name and an email or a phone; the agent fills one in on the form or unticks the contact. The opportunity is ticked by default, named "<Business> – PEO search", with no target start unless entered. A Not a fit lead is reopened before it can be converted. "Convert and start a quote" waits for New quote (Epic 5; D-402, no dead buttons). Custom-field values move with Epic 9. One database function does all of it or none, locking the lead row, so converting twice makes one chain |
| D-811 | **Bulk on leads** (CRM-L1.1) reuses **Bulk change** as it is: change owner, change status (New or Working), Not a fit (one reason), set a field, label, delete (to the bin; its Undo restores) and export through the existing export |
| D-812 | **Depth.** Epics 6 and 7 are split into slices now; their design questions (one message store for PEO and contact mail, the conversation's reply address, how the reply-reading model is called) are grilled just before each epic is built, after Epic 8 lands |
| D-813 | **N-1.2 moves from Epic 5 to Epic 7** (its database part: `acknowledged` and `question_open` and their moves), so N-1.1's suggestions can be accepted without Epic 5. Epic 5's CRM-Q3.1 builds on it |
| D-814 | **A lead's last activity** (CRM-L1.1a, slice 8.3). The newest moment of its calls, notes and tasks (`activities.activity_at`), or when it was made when it has none. It orders the leads table by default, newest first, as an account's last activity orders the accounts table (D-201). A change to the lead's own fields is not activity |

### Epic 6 decisions (founder, 2026-11-16)

Grilled 2026-11-16 at this branch's tip (8.1 to 8.3 built). The founder took every recommended answer. What exists: a broker's mailbox connected with send-only
permission and its token sealed (M-4.1 to M-4.3, `supabase/functions/mailbox`, `_shared/mailbox.ts`), but **no code sends through it**; PEO mail received on
quote requests (`quote_request_messages`, `receive-mail`, held mail, reply addresses `<File ID>.<PEO>.<16 hex>`); the outbox with retries
(`dispatch-outbox`). What does not: any store of mail with a contact or a lead, the employer's PEO authorization (M-1.1), a page a PEO opens by link.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-600 | **M-1.1 moves into Epic 6** as slice 6.6a, amending D-800: all of Epic 6 is built now. Its wording is draft, in the one legal-wording module (D-12), and every pull request that shows it says so. CRM-Q2.6 (New quote's step 5) still waits for New quote's steps 1 to 4 (Epic 5); until then sending to several PEOs (M-2.2) starts from the submission's page and the opportunity |
| D-601 | **Mail with contacts and leads has its own store**: `conversations` (one per contact, one per lead, shared by the firm, with its reply address) and `conversation_messages`, added beside the quote requests' threads, which are not widened (ADR 0007). A conversation names its firm, account, contact or lead; a message on it is shown on the contact, the lead, the account and the account's opportunities from that one row |
| D-602 | **One way to send**: a server function sends one message through the sender's connected mailbox (Gmail `users.messages.send`, Microsoft Graph `sendMail`). The message is recorded before it is sent and queued through the outbox with its retries. Reply-To is the thread's address first and the sender's own second; the message sets its `Message-ID`, `In-Reply-To` and `References`. Contact mail and PEO mail use it. Tests fake the provider (D-13 seam 4); a real send is confirmed only once the Google and Microsoft apps are configured for production |
| D-603 | **Epic 6 before the rest of Epic 8.** The place to write on a lead's page arrives with 8.5; moving a lead's conversation to the contact is built in 8.6 |
| D-604 | **Q-2.2 warns and never blocks**, naming nobody: "Another firm registered this employer with this PEO on Nov 3." Who owns an intake is QP-D4's, not code's |
| D-605 | **A file on an email goes as an expiring link**, never as an attachment, for contacts as for PEOs (ADR 0003, M-3.5): scanned before, revocable, each download logged. A packet or a census goes only to its own employer |
| D-606 | **A conversation's address** reads `c.<business or name, shortened>.<16 random hex>` on the receiving subdomain; the `c.` prefix can't be a File ID. `receive-mail` looks in both address tables; an address that belongs to no record holds the mail, as today. It keeps receiving after a contact moves accounts, and moves with a lead's conversation on convert (8.6) |
| D-607 | **The inbox** (CRM-E1.4) lists every message, both directions, on the quote requests, contacts and leads the person may see (level rule), newest first; views All, New, From PEOs, From contacts and leads, Sent. "New" is one mark per thread for the firm, cleared when anyone opens it, as PEO threads are. Search: sender, subject, account; never the body |
| D-608 | **M-1.1's screens.** On Certify & sign, after the certification: "Which PEOs may receive your packet?", the product's listed PEOs with a box each; none ticked is allowed and then nothing can be sent. The employer's profile has "Who can receive my packet" to add or withdraw at any time. Each change is an appended row (who, when, the PEO, the wording's version), never an edit. Withdrawing a PEO already sent to closes its quote request, revokes its link and marks the submission Needs advisor action; the PEO is sent nothing. The database refuses a send to a PEO without a live authorization. "Ask" uses the existing information request |
| D-609 | **The bundle link** (M-2.1, ADR 0003): an unguessable token kept only as its hash, 30 days. `/b/<token>` opens with no sign-in: the employer, File ID and version, each file with its own download, the open items. Each download is on the quote request's timeline. A newer version sent (M-2.3), a withdrawal (M-2.4) or the employer withdrawing the PEO (M-1.1) revokes it |
| D-610 | **Order and lanes.** Waves, at most three at once (D-25), each slice in its own working copy and its own test database, test-first (`tdd`), merged into this branch, then the full gate. Wave 1: 6.2, 6.6a, 6.6b. Wave 2: 6.1, 6.4, 6.6. Wave 3: 6.5, 6.3, 6.7. Wave 4: 6.8, 6.9, 6.10 |
| D-611 | **After Epic 6: Epic 5 and the rest of Epic 8** (founder, 2026-11-16), then Epic 7. Epic 5 (Quotes and New quote) is grilled before it is built, as every epic is; it brings CRM-Q2.6 and unblocks Epic 7's slice 7.8. Epic 8's 8.4 to 8.7 run beside it within D-25's three lanes |
| D-612 | **Decision numbers for Epic 6's later slices.** D-620 to D-699 went to 6.6a, 6.2, 6.6b, 6.1, 6.4, 6.6, 6.3 and 6.5, and D-700 onward is Epic 7's. Slices 6.7 to 6.10 take D-1600 onward, ten each: 6.7 D-1600, 6.8 D-1610, 6.9 D-1620, 6.10 D-1630 |

#### Epic 6 migrations

| Slice | Migration |
|---|---|

### 6.2 CRM-E1.2a decisions

Built 2026-11-16 (#196), test-first (`TDD_LOG.md` of the slice's lane). Migrations `20261122090000_conversations.sql` and the follow-up `20261122100500_conversations_closing.sql` (D-639); the `send-mail` function.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-630 | **A conversation names its firm and its contact or lead, never an account.** It reads its account through the contact, so it follows the contact to another account with nothing to move, and goes with the account (through the contact) or the lead. It is made with its first message, which issues its address, so the address is never empty. A contact is deleted only with its account today; if a later story deletes one alone, its conversation goes with it |
| D-631 | **The send's refusals, in order**: require_advisor, firm_to_act_for, assert_firm_writable (42501, PT423); a record the caller may not see, another firm's or none, PT404 (a lead in the bin is PT404 to an agent who no longer sees it); the record's state, PT409 (Do not email, no email or one the address rule refuses, a lead in the bin or converted); the fields, 22023; no connected mailbox, PT412, and nothing is recorded |
| D-632 | **Which failures are final and which are retried.** Retried after dispatch-outbox's delays (60 s, 5 min, 30 min, 2 h; the fifth attempt is final, kind `unavailable`): Google or Microsoft busy or failing (429, 5xx), their sign-in service not answering, the apps not set up yet. Final at once: no mailbox, a token taken back, a grant broader than sending or one that can't be opened, the connected mailbox no longer the message's From (all kind `mailbox`, so the place to write says Reconnect), 401 or 403 from the send (`mailbox`), any other refusal (`refused`), an address the rule refuses (`refused`, nothing sent), and a send whose answer was lost or cut off after 15 s (`uncertain`: neither provider takes an idempotency key, so a retry could reach the client twice; the writer is told to look in their Sent folder). A worker lost on the last attempt leaves its message `uncertain` too (the outbox's reaper ends the event; a trigger marks the message). The provider's own words are never kept; the event keeps the kind, the message the plain reason |
| D-633 | **The provider's id**: Gmail's message id. Microsoft Graph's sendMail answers 202 with no id, so a Microsoft send records our own Message-ID; so does a Gmail answer with no id that can be read (it went either way) |
| D-634 | **No mailbox stops the send before anything is recorded** (PT412), since a message with no From can never go. `conversation_for` answers `mailbox: {connected, last_send_failed}`: not connected with a newest send failed for a mailbox reason is Reconnect, not connected otherwise is Connect |
| D-635 | **The message's headers.** From is the connected mailbox's bare address (no display name yet). Reply-To is `<conversation address>@QUOTE_REPLY_DOMAIN` (the receiving subdomain receive-mail already uses), then the writer's. Message-ID is `<message id@From domain>`, set when recorded. In-Reply-To and References only when the writer names a message of the same conversation (`p_in_reply_to`): References is that message's References then its Message-ID. Plain text, UTF-8, base64; a subject that is not short ASCII goes as RFC 2047 words; every header line stays within 78 characters |
| D-636 | **Limits** kept from before: a subject is one line of at most 300 characters (trimmed), a body at most 50,000, at most 10 in CC (trimmed, empty entries dropped) |
| D-637 | **One address rule**: `public.is_mail_address` and `MAIL_ADDRESS_PATTERN` (`supabase/functions/_shared/mailAddress.ts`) hold the same pattern, compared by `src/data/schemaSync.test.ts`. The database applies it to the CC and to the person's own address when the message is written; the builder applies it to From, To and CC again and sends nothing when one fails |
| D-638 | **send-mail** is its own function on the one outbox table: kind `conversation_message`, a firm's event, carrying `{message_id}` alone. `claim_conversation_mail`, `complete_conversation_mail` and `fail_conversation_mail` are the service role's alone, with dispatch-outbox's lease (600 s) and attempt fence; a batch is 10. The claim first ends a waiting event whose message is gone (deleted with its record or a closing firm). It is worked by the service-role key (a minute schedule from the database, as dispatch-outbox's) or by any signed-in person, which only decides when the queue is worked.
