It is not one of the firm alerts that wait while a firm is suspended: a message written before a suspension is still sent |
| D-639 | **Left for later slices**: a message's status is queued, sent or failed, and its direction outbound (inbound is allowed by the column for 6.4); a support session reads conversations as an owner does and sends nothing; no lead becomes Working when written to yet, and no screen shows any of this (6.3); no file goes on a message (6.3, D-605); receiving replies is 6.4. Two first messages to the same person at the same instant: the second is refused by the one-per-contact rule and can be sent again. **A follow-up migration** outside the table's timestamps: 6.6a's `20261122100000`, built beside this slice and sorting after it, restated `firm_closing_plan()` from before 6.2, so `20261122100500_conversations_closing.sql` restates it with both slices' lines (a `_fixes`-style half step, as earlier stories used) |

### 6.1 CRM-E1.1 decisions

Built 2026-11-16 (#195), test-first (`TDD_LOG.md` of the slice's lane). No migration: it reads `my_mailbox` (M-4.3) and `conversation_for(...).mailbox` (6.2,
D-634), and connects through the existing broker sign-in. `src/components/mailbox/`: `MailboxForWriting` (the line), `useFirstSend` (the first send),
`ConnectButtons` (both use it).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-650 | **Connecting from a place to write comes back to it.** It is the existing broker sign-in (`startBrokerSignIn`, send permission only, ADR 0005), given the place to write's own page. `brokerNext` now also keeps a contact's page (`/contacts/<id>`) and the Leads page (`/leads`, with its query, which names an open lead), each with its query, under the same refusals; anything else still falls back to the queue. An opportunity's page is added by the slice that makes it (6.3) |
| D-651 | **The line's states.** It reads the person's mailbox from `my_mailbox` itself and takes `lastSendFailed` from `conversation_for(...).mailbox.last_send_failed`. No mailbox: "Connect your email to write from here", Connect Google, Connect Microsoft. Connected: "Connected" and the address the message goes from. The newest send failed for a mailbox reason: "Your last message didn't go. Google or Microsoft no longer lets Nevermore send as you. Reconnect to write from here." with Reconnect Google and Reconnect Microsoft, **also while a token is kept**: a 401 or 403 from the send leaves the token (D-632), so `connected` alone would show a healthy line over a refusing mailbox. It then names the address too. Reconnecting does not clear the mark; the next send that goes does (it is the newest send's state). It can't be checked: the panel's failure with Try again, never a claim that there is no mailbox |
| D-652 | **The first send** is `useFirstSend({returnTo, ownMail?})`, whose `beforeSend(send)` 6.3 and 6.6 call in place of sending: it checks `my_mailbox`; connected, it sends at once; not, it sends nothing and opens a dialog, "Connect your email to send this", with Connect Google and Connect Microsoft. Only a PEO send passes `ownMail` (`{replyAddress, onSendYourself}`), which adds "Send from your own mail" with the reply address shown to put in CC; a contact's or a lead's send never offers it (their reply addresses are the conversation's, and the product records only what it sends, D-634). The mailbox check failing sends nothing and says so. What `onSendYourself` records is 6.6's |
| D-653 | **The draft across a connect.** Connecting leaves the page for Google or Microsoft and comes back to it; keeping the unsent message across that trip is the place to write's own (6.3), not this slice's |
| D-654 | **Settings.** The Mailbox page (M-4.3) already shows the address, "Connected" and Disconnect, which deletes the token through the `mailbox` function, and stays as it is (D-8). Its entry in Settings said it is the mailbox you "read replies in", which ADR 0005 rules out; it now reads "Connect the email you write from. Nevermore only sends from it; your inbox stays yours." |

### 6.6a decisions

Slice 6.6a, M-1.1 (the employer authorizes each PEO), built test-first (`TDD_LOG.md`) in `20261122100000_peo_authorizations.sql`, tested in
`supabase/tests/peo_authorizations.sql`. The wording is a draft, not yet reviewed by counsel (D-12).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-620 | **The record.** `peo_authorizations`: one row per change (authorize or withdraw) per intake and listed PEO, naming the PEO (its id and the list's spelling), whether it authorizes, the wording shown and its version, who signed (the employer's id) and when the server received it. It names no packet version, so certifying again leaves it. The latest row is the PEO's state. Appended only: a trigger refuses every update and delete, the table's owner included; only deleting the intake takes its rows, through `complete_intake_deletion`, which counts them. Its firm is its intake's (D-28 probe "by intake"); the employer reads their own rows and the intake's firm's members theirs, as `intake_consents` is read; closing a firm keeps them with the intake |
| D-621 | **Saying the same again.** Ticking a PEO already authorized appends nothing and answers the row in force. Withdrawing a PEO that is not authorized (never, or already withdrawn) is refused in plain words: "<PEO> is not authorized, so there is nothing to withdraw." Changes on one intake are taken one at a time (the intake's row is locked) |
| D-622 | **Who writes.** Only the intake's own employer (`chamber_sessions.user_id`), through `set_peo_authorization`. Everyone else, the intake's own firm's members included, gets the same refusal, 42501 "Only the employer can choose who receives their packet.", so it says nothing about whose intake it is; a signed-out caller can't call it. The PEO is matched by name ignoring case and outer spaces, among PEOs listed now (L-1.1); another name is refused: "<name> is not on the list of PEOs. Ask your advisor to have it added first." The version must be the one in force (EA005, DETAIL the version), as the intake consent's is |
| D-623 | **A closing firm and a support session.** `set_peo_authorization` is the employer's own say, so it is exempt from the closing guard and the support guard, with that reason in both lists (`close_a_firm.sql`, `help_from_nevermore.sql`): a withdrawal must close that PEO's quote request even while the firm is closing, and a support session's admin is not the employer |
| D-624 | **Withdrawing a PEO already sent to.** In the same transaction (the pipeline row locked first, then the quote request, as the send locks them): every quote request of that PEO on the intake that is not closed is closed, logged as the employer's change; the submission is marked Needs advisor action with the note "The employer withdrew <PEO>. Tell <PEO> not to use the packet; nothing was sent to it.", and the mark is in the status history. Nothing is queued to send. When the submission was won with that PEO's quote, the quote is kept and the note reads "The employer withdrew <PEO>, whose quote they took. The quote is kept; talk to the employer." A PEO never sent to, or whose quote request is already closed, marks nothing. The bundle link (6.6b) reads `peo_authorized` each time it is opened, so it is revoked by the same row |
| D-625 | **The check.** `peo_authorized(session, peo)` answers the latest row; `assert_peo_authorized(session, peo)` refuses with 22023, "The employer hasn't authorized <PEO> to receive their packet. Ask them to add it." or "The employer withdrew <PEO>. Nothing more can be sent to it." No API role, the service role included, can call either. `advisor_record_quote_send` is restated from 20261120090000 with every rule kept and the check after the PEO name's checks, before anything is read or changed. A quote request recorded before this shipped stays as it is; a further send to it is refused until the employer authorizes that PEO |
| D-626 | **Each PEO's state.** `peo_authorization_states(session)`: every listed PEO, and any PEO no longer listed that the employer said something about, alphabetically, as authorized, withdrawn or not asked, with when and under which wording. For the employer and the intake's firm's members; anyone else is told "Submission not found" (PT404). It is firm-scoped (two-firm probe in `data_isolation.sql` 4e) |
| D-627 | **The screens.** One component, `PacketRecipients`: the explainer (none needs ticking; the packet carries employees' personal information; it can be changed on the profile), the words a tick records, "Draft wording, not yet reviewed by counsel", and a box per PEO. Each change is saved as it is made and the box changes only once the database has it; a refusal is shown in its own words beside the boxes, a dropped connection as "That didn't go through". It shows on Certify & sign once the intake is certified (on the confirmation after certifying and on a later visit), never before. The profile's "Who can receive my packet" shows it for each of the employer's own certified intakes, headed by the company; with none: "Once you certify your intake, you can choose here which PEOs may receive its packet." |
| D-628 | **The advisor's side.** "Who may receive the packet" on the submission's page lists each PEO as May receive it, Withdrawn by the employer or Not authorized. Each PEO not authorized has "Ask the employer to add <PEO>", which opens Ask the prospect with the draft "We'd like to send your packet to <PEO>. If you agree, tick <PEO> under "Who can receive my packet" on your profile. Nothing goes to <PEO> until you do." and no answers named; the advisor edits and sends it as any request. Nothing is sent until they do |
| D-629 | **The wording.** `src/data/legalWording.ts` holds the version (`2026-11-draft-1`, kept equal to `peo_authorization_version()` by `schemaSync.test.ts`), the question, the profile's heading, the explainer, the draft notice and the two recorded sentences (authorize, withdraw), each naming the PEO. It is in the copy guard's public files. A new version leaves signed rows in place; a PEO ticked later is recorded under the new one |

### 6.6b decisions

Slice 6.6b, the bundle link (M-2.1's third criterion, ADR 0003, D-609), built test-first in `lane/6.6b`. These settle what the brief left open; the
spec it gave (token, functions, bucket, page, guessing limit, headers) is as given.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-640 | **The token** is 32 random bytes in URL-safe base64 without padding (43 characters, 256 bits), made by the database (`gen_random_bytes`) and answered once by `issue_bundle_link`; the row keeps its SHA-256 in hex. The edge function answers a token of any other shape as gone without asking the database |
| D-641 | **Issuing asks the employer's authorization** with 6.6a's `assert_peo_authorized` (its own 22023 refusal, in its words), as a send does (ADR 0003: the database refuses a link for a PEO with no authorization). Opening and downloading ask `peo_authorized` and answer gone when it is false, before any revoke reaches the link |
| D-642 | **What a link lists** (`p_files`): one to 100 files, each `{name, what}` with a name that is one part of a path (no slash, no control character, at most 120 characters), and for a document on file its `document_id`, which must be the intake's own and through the virus check (clean or skipped), checked again at each download. A made file is stored at `bundle-links/<quote request>/<folder>/<n>-<name>`, `n` its place in the list; the browser stores every file before it issues the link, so a link never lists a file that is not there |
| D-643 | **A download goes through the function**: `bundle_link_file` answers the bucket and path to the function only; the function signs it for 60 seconds, fetches it and passes the bytes on as `application/octet-stream` with the file's name. The browser never receives a link or a path (`fileLinks.test.ts` names the function as the one server-side signer). The page posts the token (never a GET: a token in a function's URL would be in the platform's log) |
| D-644 | **Guessing** counts a token that opens nothing (unknown, expired or revoked) for the SHA-256 of the caller's address, the first of `X-Forwarded-For`, in `bundle_link_misses`, kept one hour. At 20 the address is refused for the hour, a live link too; the function shows the same "This link no longer works" for a refusal as for a gone link. A live link whose PEO was withdrawn is gone but not counted. A failure on our side is "We couldn't load this page", never "no longer works" |
| D-645 | **The token in the host's log**: the path `/b/<token>` is in Vercel's request log; accepted as SR-36 in `docs/SECURITY_REVIEW.md`, with moving it to the fragment as the way out |
| D-646 | **Both tables are closed to the API**, the service role's table access included; the advisor's screen (slice 6.6) will read a link's state through a function. The links and their files are a quote request's: kept when a firm closes, deleted with the intake (the rows by the foreign key to the quote request, the files listed by `intake_stored_files`) |
| D-647 | **The timeline**: issuing and revoking are logged by a trigger on `bundle_links` (field `bundle_link`, "Issued for version 3", "Revoked: <reason>", by whoever did it), so no write path skips it; the change log shows them in words. A download is field `bundle_download`, "<what it is> (<file name>)", no person, and the PEO thread shows it as "Downloaded <file>, by the bundle link" |
| D-648 | **The files**: the zip's builders are shared, not copied (`madeBundleFiles` in `src/lib/submissionBundle.ts`, which `buildBundle` now starts from). The link carries the packet, the census file, the workers' comp application data, the supplement, then the certified answers as JSON (`certifiedAnswersDocument`), then the documents on file by name; the open items are the plan's `missing` labels when it is issued |

### 6.4 CRM-E1.3 decisions

Built 2026-11-16 (#198), test-first (`TDD_LOG.md` of the slice's lane). Migration `20261122130000_conversation_replies.sql`; tested in
`supabase/tests/conversation_replies.sql` and `supabase/functions/receive-mail/handler.test.ts`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-660 | **One way in, and the database decides.** `receive-mail` still names every reply address of ours on a message and calls `record_inbound_mail`, restated from its latest definition (20261116240000) with every rule kept; it now looks in both address tables (D-606). A message whose addresses name one conversation and no live quote request joins that conversation: one row in `conversation_messages`, direction `inbound` (or `sent_by_hand`), status `received`, idempotent on the provider's id. The answer then names the conversation and the message (`conversation_message_id`) instead of a quote request's message, and the function follows it. A received message keeps its sender's name, whether it is automatic (the function's own rule, as for PEO mail) and whether its sender is new |
| D-661 | **Which address wins.** A live quote request's address wins over a conversation's, as before this slice, so the PEO threads are unchanged. Two conversations' addresses on one message are ambiguous and the message is held (no record is chosen by guess): for their firm when both are one firm's, else for the platform. A `c.` address that belongs to no record, never issued or a conversation deleted with its account, is held as an unknown address on the platform's list, as today: no firm can be told from it |
| D-662 | **A new sender, and who is offered as a contact.** New is: no earlier message on the conversation was from that address, to it or copied to it (so the contact written to is not new), and never an automatic reply or the firm's own copy. On a contact's conversation a new sender who is no contact on that account yet (by email, case ignored) is offered (`offer_contact` on the message); `add_sender_as_contact(message, names, title, phone, mobile, owner)` adds them to that account with their address, through `add_contact` and every rule of it (owner the caller unless they choose, by the level rule; an archived account refused). It asks `assert_firm_writable` itself first. Refused: a message the caller may not see, another firm's or none, PT404; a lead's conversation, PT409 ("A lead's conversation has no account to add a contact to."): a lead has no account, and its conversation moves to the contact on converting (8.6); a sender not offered, PT409. The message row is locked, so two people adding the same sender are ordered |
| D-663 | **The firm's own copy is sent by hand.** A sender who is a current member of the conversation's firm, by their sign-in address or their connected mailbox's, is `sent_by_hand`: never new, never new mail, never offered. A person of another firm, or one whose membership ended, writes like anyone else. A received message names no `sender_id`, so the mailbox state (`last_send_failed`) reads only the product's own sends |
| D-664 | **A received message's Message-ID.** Its own `Message-ID` header is kept when it is one (`<...>`, at most 300 characters) and no kept message has it, so the agent's answer to it (`send_conversation_message`'s `p_in_reply_to`) names it in In-Reply-To; otherwise (none, malformed, or the same message delivered twice) it is `<provider id@inbound.invalid>`. Its In-Reply-To is kept when it is one. Its References are not kept: an answer's References start from the received message |
| D-665 | **New mail on a conversation (D-607)** is one mark for the firm, kept on the conversation itself (`last_opened_at`, `last_opened_by`, added columns) rather than a table of reads: a conversation is already the firm's and row-secured. New mail is an `inbound` message that is not automatic, recorded after the conversation was last opened (`conversation_has_new_mail`, the one rule the inbox and Home will read, 6.5). `open_conversation(conversation, seen_through)` clears it for everyone through the newest message the page showed, never backwards, for a conversation the caller may see (PT404 otherwise). A support session opens it and marks nothing (ADR 0024); a closing firm may, as it may open a PEO thread, since it only marks what was read. `conversation_for` answers `conversation.new_mail` |
| D-666 | **One message on every record (D-601).** `account_conversation_messages(account or opportunity)` reads the messages of the conversations of an account's contacts, newest first, at most 500, each as `conversation_for` shows it plus its `contact_id` and its conversation's `new_mail`, from the one row each message is. An opportunity's are its account's, and both the opportunity and its account must be ones the caller may see (the level rule); PT404 otherwise. A lead's conversation is read on the lead (`conversation_for`). No screen reads any of it yet (6.3 and 6.5) |
| D-667 | **Files on a reply** follow PEO mail's rules (M-3.2, ADR 0002): the size cap, the types uploads take by name and by bytes, a scan each, the provider retrying when a scanner that was reached can't answer; one blocked file leaves the rest readable. Their own table, `conversation_message_attachments` (firm, row security, the two-firm probe, the closing's line), written only by the service role (`record_conversation_attachment`, `finish_conversation_attachment`), and their own bucket, `contact-mail`, at `<firm>/<conversation>/<message>/<attachment>-<name>`, read through a stored attachment's row by whoever reads its message, never by a support session. Closing a firm deletes the rows; the bucket's bytes are kept, unreadable once their rows are gone, because the closing deletes only from public tables and storage can't be emptied from SQL: removing them is left to a storage clean-up (as for the other buckets' files today). A malware alert from a reply names no intake. The data class of contact and lead mail becomes restricted (`src/data/dataPolicy.ts`): a reply can carry a census or a payroll file |
| D-668 | **No message's text reaches a log.** `receive-mail` logs a conversation's message by its id, counts and failure codes only, as for PEO mail (tested); `record_inbound_mail` raises nothing that carries a message's words |
| D-669 | **Left for later**: the screens (6.3 on the contact, account and opportunity; 6.5 the inbox and Home's mark); `menu_counts` does not count conversations yet (6.5). A received message's text is kept as the function cuts it (100,000 characters), with no truncation flag on a conversation's message. A closing firm still receives replies onto its conversations until the closing deletes them, as PEO mail is received for it. Held mail sent to two conversations' addresses is assigned only to a quote request (the holding list's own action); moving it onto a conversation is not built. Mail to a conversation address from the PEO side (a quote request's address and a conversation's on one message) goes to the quote request (D-661) |

### 6.6 M-2.1 decisions

Slice 6.6, M-2.1 (send to a PEO, #200), built test-first in `lane/6.6` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Migration `20261122140000_peo_send.sql`, tested in
`supabase/tests/peo_send.sql`; `send-mail` gains the PEO queue; `src/components/advisor/SendToPeo.tsx` on each PEO's card. The line every message carries
is draft wording, not yet reviewed by counsel (D-12).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-670 | **Where a sent message lives.** On the quote request's thread, `quote_request_messages`, as direction `sent` (beside `received` and `sent_by_hand`), with its `status` (queued, sent, failed), failure kind and reason, its own `message_id`, `in_reply_to`, `message_references`, `sender_id` and `bundle_link_id`; added to, nothing replaced (ADR 0007). It is already firm-scoped, kept with the intake and deleted with it, so no new table, probe or closing line. Its `provider_message_id` is its own Message-ID until Gmail answers its id (D-633). It is never "new PEO mail" (only `received` is) and can't be moved off the thread |
| D-671 | **The send is three calls, in this order, because the link needs the quote request.** `send_to_peo(session, peo, to, subject, note)` does every check and creates or continues the quote request, records the version sent, issues its reply address, moves Ready for market to Sent to PEOs (ADR 0026), and records the message queued with its outbox event (kind `peo_message`, an intake's event, so the Outbox page lists it and its retry). The browser then issues the bundle link for that quote request (`issueBundleLink`, 6.6b), and `attach_peo_link(message, link, token)` joins it. The event waits until the link has joined it; one never joined within ten minutes (the page closed between) is ended untried and the message failed: "The bundle link wasn't made, so nothing was sent. Send it again." |
| D-672 | **The send's refusals, in order**: assert_firm_writable (PT423, closing firm and support session), require_advisor (42501), the intake in the caller's firm (PT404 "Submission not found"), the PEO's name, `assert_peo_authorized` (22023, 6.6a's words), the latest certification still current (22023 "The prospect changed an answer since certifying. Sending waits until they certify again."; the version sent is that certification's), the addresses (one to ten, each by the one address rule, D-637), the subject and note (6.2's limits, D-636), no connected mailbox (PT412, nothing recorded, D-634), then the stage (Ready for market, Sent to PEOs or Quoted, as a send by hand). `attach_peo_link`: the closing and support guard, another firm's message PT404, another member's PT409 (only the writer sends it: it goes from their mailbox), a message already linked PT409, and a link that isn't the live, unexpired link of the message's own quote request opened by that token 22023 |
| D-673 | **What the stored body keeps of the link: never the token.** The body holds the note and the fixed block with a marker, "[the bundle link, sent in the email and not kept here]", where the link goes, and names the link by `bundle_link_id`. The token rides only on the outbox event's payload (closed to every API role), is read by `claim_peo_mail`, and is put in place of the marker by send-mail as it builds the email, at `APP_BASE_URL/b/<token>` (the origin is the function's setting, never the browser's). It leaves the payload when the email is sent, or when the event ends because its link stopped working; a dead event keeps it so the Outbox page's retry can still send a live link |
| D-674 | **The fixed block** is written by the database at `attach_peo_link`, so the advisor can't edit it: "-- ", "The bundle: <link>", "The link stops working on <date>.", "File ID <id>, version <n>", "Open items: <the link's open items, or none>", "<name>, <firm>, <phone>" (the name from the sign-in's full name, else the email; the phone from the advisor's profile, left out when none), and the draft line. The composer previews it line for line (`src/data/peoSend.ts`) |
| D-675 | **The draft line** (`PEO_SEND_NOTICE` in `src/data/legalWording.ts`, the one legal-wording module): "Draft wording: sent with the authorization of the employer, for this PEO alone to consider them for a quote. Please do not pass the link on." The database's copy, `peo_send_notice()`, is kept equal by `schemaSync.test.ts`, so counsel's text is still one change there that the test carries to a migration; it says "Draft wording" in the email itself until counsel's replaces it, so no PEO reads draft words as counsel's (rule 7) |
| D-676 | **Before it goes, it is checked again.** `claim_peo_mail` ends, untried and with the token taken off, a message whose link was revoked or expired ("The bundle link stopped working before this went, so nothing was sent.") or whose PEO the employer withdrew while it waited ("The employer withdrew <PEO> before this went, so nothing was sent."). Issuing a newer link for the same quote request revokes the one before (6.6b), so of two sends in quick succession to one PEO only the newer goes |
| D-677 | **Threading.** In-Reply-To and References continue the quote request's thread: the newest message on it with a Message-ID, a sent one's own, a received one's from its stored headers (`message-id`, `references`). Reply-To is the quote request's reply address at `QUOTE_REPLY_DOMAIN`, then the advisor's, as send-mail builds every message (D-635). Failure kinds, retries, the reaper's uncertain and the provider's id are 6.2's (D-632, D-633), on `claim_peo_mail`, `complete_peo_mail` and `fail_peo_mail`; the Outbox page's retry (`advisor_requeue_outbox_event`) puts the message back to queued by a trigger. No delivery or bounce events (ADR 0005) |
| D-678 | **The screens.** "Send to <PEO>" on each PEO's card in What each PEO still needs, where its bundle is built (the link is issued from the same plan as the download, with the certified answers), rather than in the PEO threads panel, which lists only PEOs with a quote request: a first send has no thread to start from. The composer: 6.1's `MailboxForWriting` line, the PEO's contacts with an email from its profile in use (`peo_send_context`), other addresses typed, a subject ("<company>: <File ID>, version <n>") and the note, then the block. Its Send goes through 6.1's `useFirstSend`: with no mailbox (or one whose permission was taken back, which deletes it) it sends nothing and offers Connect Google, Connect Microsoft and, once the quote request has a reply address and receiving is on, "Send from your own mail" with that address in CC, which opens the existing "Record it" prompt (M-2.5). The thread shows the message as "Sent from the advisor's mailbox", Queued, Sent or Not sent with the reason |
| D-679 | **Left for later slices**: the registration record (Q-2.1, 6.10) has its seam at the end of `send_to_peo`, after the message is recorded; several PEOs at once (6.7), an update (6.8), follow-up and withdraw (6.9); a PEO never sent to has no reply address yet, so "Send from your own mail" is offered from the first recorded send on; the composer keeps no draft across a connect (D-653) |

### 6.7 M-2.2 decisions

Slice 6.7, M-2.2 (send to several PEOs at once, #201), built test-first in `lane/6.7` (`TDD_LOG.md`). No migration: each PEO's send is 6.6's
`send_to_peo` and `attach_peo_link`, unchanged, called once per PEO (`sendToPeo` in `src/lib/peoSendApi.ts`). `src/components/advisor/SendToSeveralPeos.tsx`,
tested in `SendToSeveralPeos.test.tsx` and on the submission page. CRM-Q2.6 (New quote's step 5) still waits for Epic 5 (D-600).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1600 | **One action is several of 6.6's sends, one after another.** The browser calls `sendToPeo` (send_to_peo, the link, attach_peo_link) for each chosen PEO in turn, alphabetically, never in parallel: each send locks the submission's pipeline row, and the first moves Ready for market to Sent to PEOs (ADR 0026), so the rest are recorded at Sent to PEOs. No batch function in the database: every rule of a send (authorization, certification, addresses, mailbox, stage) stays in the one function, so a send to several can never skip one. The registration record (Q-2.1, 6.10) is written by each send at its seam |
| D-1601 | **No PEO sees another.** Each send is addressed only to that PEO's own contacts and its note opens with its own greeting, so no message carries another PEO's address or name; the advisor's one note is the same in each. The subject is one, "<company>: <File ID>, version <n>" (6.6's), which the advisor may change for all |
| D-1602 | **The recipients** are the PEO's contacts with an email on its profile in use (`peo_send_context`), all of them; typed addresses are the one-PEO composer's (6.6), not this one's. **The greeting** is "Hello <first names>," from those contacts ("Hello Omar and Ruth,"), `peoGreeting` in `src/data/peoSend.ts`; the note follows after a blank line. The fixed block under it (D-674) is the database's, as for one PEO, so its link, File ID, version and open items are that PEO's own |
| D-1603 | **Ready** is the existing checks: nothing missing to quote (`peoCompleteness`, the PEO needs panel's) and no eligibility check "Doesn't meet" (`peoFit`). A ready PEO starts ticked; one that isn't starts unticked, with what is missing and each check not met. Sent anyway, its link is issued from its own plan, so its message's "Open items" lists what is missing. "Can't tell yet" does not make a PEO not ready: it is a fact not sourced or an answer not given, never a failure. PEOs listed with no profile are not offered: there is nothing to check and no contact to send to (the panel lists them apart) |
| D-1604 | **What stops one PEO and lets the rest go.** Shown in the review as "Won't be sent: ..." and in the result as "Not sent. ...": the employer withdrew it or hasn't authorized it (6.6a's words, from `peo_authorization_states`), no contact with an email on its profile, or its contacts couldn't be read. Any refusal from the send itself (the database's words, through `failureWords`) stops that PEO alone too. A PEO stopped in the browser is never sent to the database; the database still refuses anything the browser missed (authorizations not read yet). "Send from your own mail" is not offered here: each PEO has its own reply address to put in CC, so that is done on its own card (6.6). No mailbox: nothing is sent to any PEO and 6.1's Connect dialog opens |
| D-1605 | **The steps.** Choose (each PEO with Ready or Not ready and its gaps, the subject and the note) and Review (for each chosen PEO an article: To, the greeting, the files as the bundle's summary says them, "Open items", checks not met, and what stops it), then "Send to N PEOs" (N those that will go), then the result, one line per chosen PEO. Close starts over: opening again reads the contacts and the employer's say afresh |
| D-1606 | **Where it is offered.** On the submission page, at the head of What each PEO still needs (where each PEO's own Send is), whenever a certification is on record. The opportunity's page (CRM-O1.1b, `/opportunities/:id`) is not built on this branch (Epic 4's rows 2 and 5 never landed), so the quotes panel's "Choose PEOs" / "Send to PEOs" that D-402 left for Epic 6 has no page to go on: not built here (rule 7). The component takes only the submission's reads (last certified version, catalog, certification), so that page can place it as it is |
| D-1607 | **The link issuer is shared**: `peoLinkIssuer(source)` (`src/lib/peoLinkIssuer.ts`) is the one way a send makes its bundle link from a PEO's plan and the certified answers; the one-PEO card (6.6) now uses it too, so both sends issue the same link |

### 6.9 M-2.4 decisions

Slice 6.9, M-2.4 (follow up and withdraw, #203), built test-first in `lane/6.9` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Migration `20261122190000_follow_up_and_withdraw.sql`, tested in
`supabase/tests/follow_up_and_withdraw.sql`; send-mail sends a PEO message that carries no link; `src/components/advisor/FollowUpOrWithdraw.tsx` on each PEO's
card and the offer in `StatusPanel`. The default texts are plain words the advisor signs (`src/data/peoFollowUp.ts`), not legal wording.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1620 | **Same thread, same queue.** A follow-up and a withdrawal are rows of `quote_request_messages`, direction `sent`, on the PEO's quote request, recorded queued before they go, through the outbox kind `peo_message` and send-mail's PEO queue, as a send is (D-670, D-671). `record_peo_thread_message` (no API role) is the one writer for both: its own Message-ID, In-Reply-To and References from the thread's newest message with a Message-ID, sent or received (D-677). Neither takes a template store; each opens with text kept in code which the advisor edits (P-3.2 brings a firm's own) |
| D-1621 | **A follow-up always carries a fresh link.** A token is kept only as its hash (D-640), so the earlier link can't be written into a new email. `follow_up_peo` answers the version already sent (`quote_requests.sent_version`), the browser issues a new 30-day link for it from the PEO card's plan (`peoLinkIssuer`), and 6.6's `attach_peo_link`, unchanged, joins it and writes the **Fixed block**. Issuing it revokes the earlier link as replaced (6.6b), so an expired link is replaced by a new 30-day one, and a live one too: the PEO's newest email holds the working link |
| D-1622 | **Revoked stays revoked.** A follow-up is refused on a closed quote request (withdrawn, or closed with its submission; sending again reopens it), for a PEO the employer no longer authorizes (6.6a's `assert_peo_authorized`, its words), and when the quote request's newest link was revoked by the advisor ("You revoked the link to <PEO>, so a follow-up can't carry one. Send to <PEO> again to issue a new link."). A link revoked because the employer withdrew the PEO is followed up only once the employer authorizes it again: the authorization check is the gate, and the employer's withdrawal already closed the quote request (D-624), so in practice the advisor sends again |
| D-1623 | **A follow-up is of the version sent.** The card's link is built from the last certified version, so Follow up is offered only while that is the version the PEO was sent; otherwise the card says "<PEO> was sent version 1 and version 2 is certified since: send it an update instead of a follow-up." (M-2.3). The database does not pin the link's version (`attach_peo_link` is 6.6's, unchanged, and 6.8 restates `issue_bundle_link` beside this slice); the screen does |
| D-1624 | **A withdrawal** (`withdraw_from_peo`) closes the quote request, records `quote_requests.withdrawn_reason` (employer_went_elsewhere, prospect_withdrew_peo, peo_not_a_fit, other; `WITHDRAWAL_REASONS` in `src/data/quoteRequests.ts`, compared by `schemaSync.test.ts`), revokes its link (`bundle_link_revoke`, reason withdrawn) and queues the message, in one transaction (the pipeline row locked first, then the quote request). Prospects read no quote request (20261110090000), so the reason is advisors' alone; it shows in the quote request's change log as "Why withdrawn". A trigger clears it when a send reopens the quote request. A withdrawal needs no authorization (it carries no link), but `claim_peo_mail` still ends one for a PEO the employer withdrew while it waited (D-608: that PEO is sent nothing) |
| D-1625 | **Won, Lost or Archived.** `set_submission_status` is restated from 20261118110000 with every rule kept and one more: every quote request of the submission but the winner loses its live link (reason submission_closed), whether or not anything is sent. The opportunity's won/lost dialog is not on this branch (D-1606; an opportunity with an intake takes its stage from the submission, D-408), so the offer is on the console's stage control, the Status panel: moving to Won, Lost or Archived lists the PEOs the move closes that the product sent to, with "Send a withdrawal to <PEOs>" ticked, a subject ("Re: " the newest sent subject) and one note. Sent, it is `close_submission_and_withdraw`: the move and a withdrawal on each of those threads, to the people that thread was last sent to, in one transaction, so a refused withdrawal (no mailbox, 6.2's limits) moves nothing. Unticked, it is the move alone. A PEO whose send was only recorded by hand has no thread to answer and is not written to; its link (none) is moot |
| D-1626 | **The same refusals as a send** (D-672): `assert_firm_writable` first (a closing firm and a support session, PT423), `require_advisor` (42501), the caller's own firm (PT404, or P0002 for the closing withdrawal, as `set_submission_status`), then the authorization (a follow-up only), addresses (one to ten, the one rule), subject and note (6.2's limits) and the mailbox (PT412, nothing recorded or moved). All three are in `close_a_firm.sql`'s guarded list, `help_from_nevermore.sql`'s writes, and `data_isolation.sql`'s registries with a two-firm probe each |
| D-1627 | **send-mail and a message with no link.** `claim_peo_mail` is restated from 20261122140000 with every rule kept: an event marked `without_link` (a withdrawal) is claimed as written and never ended for a link that never joined it. The function requires a token only when the body holds the link's marker, so a withdrawal goes as written and a send whose link never came is still refused |
| D-1628 | **Left alone**: an update (M-2.3, slice 6.8); a firm's own templates (P-3.2); the opportunity's page and its won/lost dialog (not on this branch, D-1606); a signature under a withdrawal (the advisor's note is sent as written; the Fixed block is a follow-up's alone); offering a follow-up after a PEO answers (the card offers it while the quote request is sent or quoted) |
| D-1629 | **Where they are offered.** "Follow up with <PEO>" and "Withdraw from <PEO>" on the PEO's card in What each PEO still needs, beside its Send, once the product has sent that PEO a message and while its quote request is sent or quoted; the To is who the newest message went to (editable), the subject "Re: " its subject. "Send from your own mail" is not offered for either: the first send's card offers it (6.6) |

### 6.10 Q-2.1, Q-2.2 decisions

Slice 6.10, Q-2.1 (the registration record) and Q-2.2 (the warning about a second submission), #204, built test-first in `lane/6.10` (`TDD_LOG.md`).
Migration `20261122200000_peo_registrations.sql`, tested in `supabase/tests/peo_registrations.sql`; `src/lib/peoRegistrationsApi.ts`, `src/data/peoRegistrations.ts`,
`src/components/advisor/RegistrationStatement.tsx` on Settings, and the warning in `SendToPeo` and `SendToSeveralPeos`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1630 | **The record.** `peo_registrations`: one row per send of the packet to a PEO, naming the firm, the PEO (its id and the list's spelling), who the employer is (D-1631), the File ID, the version sent and the moment (`now()`, the send's own transaction). Every send writes one, a second send of the same version too ("every send"). It is written by `register_peo_send(session, peo, version)`, which `send_to_peo` calls at the seam 6.6 left (D-679), after the message is recorded, so a send that fails writes nothing and a registration never exists without its send. `send_to_peo` is restated from 20261122140000 with every line kept and that one call added; 6.7's sends go through it, so each PEO of a send to several is registered by its own send (D-1600). The table and the writer are closed to every API role, the service role included, so no one writes a registration no send made |
| D-1631 | **What it keeps of the employer: the FEIN, read by the send.** The send runs as its owner and reads the certified answers of the version it sends, so the FEIN can be read there: the registration keeps the top-level `fein` answer (nine digits, as the workspace's box takes it), and always the company's name and its headquarters' state too (`company_name`, `hq_state`), so an employer with no FEIN is still named (Q-2.1's "name and state where there is none") and the match of D-1637 works across the two. ADR 0006 holds: the FEIN is still typed only into the workspace; the registration copies the certified value and never asks for or extracts one. The FEINs of the legal entities of an employer that is several are not registered. The whole FEIN leaves the table nowhere: the statement gives its last four digits (D-1636) and the warning never returns it |
| D-1632 | **Nobody changes or deletes one, admins included.** `reject_audit_change` refuses every update and delete, as for the other append-only tables, and a statement trigger refuses `TRUNCATE`; the superuser is refused too (tested). That function lets only an intake's deletion through, for rows naming that intake's `session_id`; a registration names no intake, so even that way is shut |
| D-1633 | **Which sends write one.** A send of a packet: 6.6's, 6.7's through it, and **an update (6.8, a newer version)**, which is a submission of that version and so writes its own registration with that version: if 6.8 sends through `send_to_peo` it is written already; if it has its own function, that function calls `register_peo_send(session, peo, version)` in its transaction. **A follow-up or a withdrawal (6.9) writes none**: neither submits the employer. A send recorded by hand (`advisor_record_quote_send`, M-2.5) writes none either: the product did not send it and can't vouch for what went |
| D-1634 | **When the intake is deleted, its registrations stay.** They are the firm's evidence of a send that happened, which the deletion of the employer's intake doesn't undo (the PEO already has the packet). The table names the File ID, never the intake row, so the deletion has nothing to follow and `data_retention`'s catalogue (every table with a `session_id` is handled by `complete_intake_deletion`) does not list it. How long one is kept after that, and what an employer who asks for deletion is told about it, is counsel's (`dataPolicy.ts` marks the class awaiting counsel, QP-D4) |
| D-1635 | **When a firm closes, its registrations stay** (`firm_closing_plan()` restated from 20261122160000 with every line, and one `keep` line): appended only, nobody deletes them, the closing included. They still warn another firm sending the same employer to that PEO, since the send did happen on that day; the warning names no firm, closed or not |
| D-1636 | **The statement** (`peo_registration_statement(firm)`): every registration of the firm, oldest first, with the database's moment it was taken; a CSV downloaded from Settings, "Registrations with PEOs", by any current member (an intake has no owner, so every member reads its sends, D-690), never another firm's (`firm_to_act_for`, D-40). A support session is refused first (PT423) and not offered the button, as for every download (D-128). The file opens with two lines, whose statement it is and "As of" the moment, then a blank line and the rows: registered at (UTC, to the second as kept), PEO, employer, state, the FEIN's last four digits, File ID, version; named `registrations-with-peos-<day>.csv`; written by the one CSV writer (`csvField`), with a byte order mark. **Not the whole FEIN**: a file taken out of the product holds no FEIN (D-48), and the last four with the name, state and File ID identify the employer; the whole one stays on the intake |
| D-1637 | **The same employer with the same PEO.** The same PEO is the same `peos` row. The same employer is the same FEIN where both registrations have one; where either has none, the same name (lower case, letters and digits only: "BAY  CAFE." is "Bay Cafe") in the same headquarters' state. Only another firm's registrations count: a firm is never warned of its own. The warning gives the first time another firm registered it with that PEO, as the timestamp; the screen shows the advisor's own day, "Nov 3" |
| D-1638 | **The warning answers only for the intake being sent** (`peo_registered_elsewhere(session)`): an advisor of the intake's own firm (`require_advisor`, then PT404 "Submission not found" for another firm's intake, as the send says it), and only from that intake's certified answers, never from anything the caller types, so no firm can probe for an employer it doesn't hold. It answers each PEO with a date and nothing else: no firm, no count, no FEIN, no version. One call per intake answers every PEO, so the one-PEO composer and the several-PEO review each read it once. **It never blocks** (D-604): `send_to_peo` does not ask it, and the second firm's send goes and is registered too (tested) |
| D-1639 | **The screens.** In 6.6's composer, under the mailbox line, and in each PEO's article of 6.7's review: "Another firm registered this employer with this PEO on Nov 3." (`registeredElsewhereWords`). A warning that couldn't be read shows nothing and stops nothing. Left alone: the warning on "Send from your own mail" and on a send recorded by hand; a registration's own page (the statement is the way to read them); PDF (CSV was chosen: it is what the firm's other files are); QP-D4's rule on who owns an intake, which this slice records and does not decide |

### 6.5 CRM-E1.4 decisions

Built 2026-11-16 (#199), test-first (`TDD_LOG.md` of the slice's lane). Migration `20261122150000_inbox.sql`; tested in `supabase/tests/inbox.sql`,
`supabase/tests/menu_counts.sql`, `src/lib/inboxApi.test.ts` and `src/pages/inbox/InboxPage.test.tsx`. No table is added: the inbox reads the two stores.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-690 | **Who sees which message.** A contact's conversation by its account's level rule, a lead's by the lead's (one in the bin by an owner or support only), exactly as `open_conversation` sees them, so every row listed can be opened. A PEO thread's messages by every current member of the firm, as the queue and the thread show them today (`quote_request_in_my_firm`): an intake has no owner, so the level rule has nothing to read there. Only the queue's rows (an intake with its workflow row that the queue does not hide as an empty guest draft), so the inbox, the queue and the menu agree. Held mail is on no thread and is never listed. One firm at a time (`firm_to_act_for`) |
| D-691 | **One row per message, from both stores** (`inbox_rows`, the one definition the list, the tabs and the menu read): its id is `c:<uuid>` for a conversation's message and `q:<id>` for a PEO thread's. Direction is received (from anyone outside the firm, automatic replies included) or sent (through the product, or a copy sent by hand). The row carries the first line with words in it (200 characters at most), never the body |
| D-692 | **New** is a message, not a mark of its own: received, not automatic, recorded after its thread was last opened, the same test as `conversation_has_new_mail` and `quote_requests_with_new_peo_mail`, so a thread has new mail exactly when one of its rows is new. Opening a message calls the thread's existing open function (`open_conversation`, `advisor_open_quote_request_thread`) through the newest message of that thread on the page, so mail that came after stays new; a support session's open marks nothing (ADR 0024) |
| D-693 | **What a message is about.** A contact's: the contact and its account, both linked. A PEO thread's: the PEO, linked to the submission, and the account of the intake's opportunity when the caller may see it, else the company the intake names, unlinked. A lead's: the lead, and its business, which stands for its account (in the Account filter and the search) until it is converted, unlinked. The owner is the account's (contact), the lead's, or the opportunity's (PEO thread), and only while they are a current member |
| D-694 | **Filters and order.** Owner (a person, with Me and My team) and Account (text on the account's name: contains, is, is empty; there is no list of accounts to pick from yet). The views are filters on three fields of their own (thread kind, direction, new); the body is no field. One order, newest first: no column sorts. No saved views, column choices or export: `workspace_table` is not widened for the inbox in this slice |
| D-695 | **The menu's Inbox** opens `/inbox` and counts the threads with new mail (PEO threads and conversations) the caller may see, read from `inbox_rows`, so it is the number of threads the New view lists. It counted intakes with new PEO mail before; `menu_counts` is restated with every key it had (quotes, inbox, leads). The queue's own "has new PEO mail" filter and its `?mail=new` links are unchanged (D-8) and are now part of Quotes in the menu. The count reads each message of the firm once per call; an index or a per-thread count is for when firms' mail grows |
| D-696 | **Where a message opens.** A contact's on the contact's page; a PEO thread's on its submission's page, which holds the thread (it is not chosen there yet); a lead has no page until slice 8.5, so a lead's opens the Leads page searched to the lead's name. A mark that fails to clear does not stop the opening: the thread stays new |
| D-697 | **No mailbox.** The banner, "Connect your email to send from here", with Connect Google and Connect Microsoft (6.1's `ConnectButtons`), reads `my_mailbox` and shows only when the answer is "not connected"; while it is on its way or if it fails, nothing shows, since the inbox lists the product's own threads whatever the mailbox. Connecting comes back to `/inbox`, which `brokerNext` now keeps, with its query |
| D-698 | **Left for later**: Home's new-mail mark (CRM-H1); the contact's and the account's pages showing their mail (6.3), so a contact's message opens on a page that does not list it yet; choosing the thread on the submission's page; saved views, column choices and export of the inbox; an account picker for the Account filter |

### 6.3 CRM-E1.2b decisions

Built 2026-11-16 (#197), test-first (`TDD_LOG.md` of the slice's lane). Migration `20261122160000_conversation_files.sql`; the edge function `message-file`; the page `/f`;
`src/components/conversations/` (`PlaceToWrite`, `EmailAContact`, `AccountEmails`).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-680 | **The place to write is one component**, `PlaceToWrite` (a contact), mounted on the contact's page above its calls, notes and tasks and, through `EmailAContact`, in a dialog from Email on the account's page. Its states: loading (the heading with grey lines, no place to write yet); "No emails yet"; no mailbox (the 6.1 line, "Connect your email to write from here", replaces the place to write); Do not email ("This contact is marked Do not email, so nothing can be written to them from here.", nothing to write, nothing to connect); no address (CRM-C1.1's "No email address on file. Add one to write from here."); sending ("Sending…", Send disabled); on its way, sent, and "Didn't go" with the reason the database keeps, the line showing Reconnect when the reason was the mailbox (D-651). A refusal of the send shows the database's words and keeps what was written. Reply fills "Re: <subject>", says "Replying to <subject>" and sends the message's id as `p_in_reply_to` (In-Reply-To and References, D-635). After a send it works the queue at once (`send-mail`, D-638) and reads the conversation again |
| D-681 | **The draft across the trip to connect** (D-653): kept per contact in the tab's session storage while it is written and forgotten once sent; where storage can't be had it is simply not kept. Email on the account comes back to `/accounts/<id>?email=<contact>` (brokerNext keeps an account's page with its query), which opens the place to write for that contact again |
| D-682 | **Email asks which contact**: the account's contacts (one list of up to 100, `account_tab`), the account's main contact first; given a first contact (`firstId`), that one first, which an opportunity's page passes as its main contact |
| D-683 | **The opportunity's page does not exist on this branch** (Epic 4 row 5, CRM-O1.1b, is not built: no route, no `opportunity_page`). So Email on an opportunity is built as `EmailAContact` with `firstId` and `AccountEmails` with `opportunityId` (6.4's `account_conversation_messages` reads an opportunity's mail), both tested, for that page to mount; `brokerNext` does not yet keep `/opportunities/<id>` (D-650 gave that to the slice that makes the page). Not a dead button: nothing is shown where there is no page |
| D-684 | **A file goes as a File link, built small, not on 6.6b's bundle link.** A bundle link is a quote request's, asks the PEO's authorization and lists made files; none of that fits a message to a contact. What is reused: the token's shape and hashing (D-640), the download through a function that signs for 60 seconds and passes the bytes on (D-643, `sha256Hex` and the client's shape), and the one guessing limit (`bundle_link_misses`, D-644), so a guesser's misses on either kind of link count together. New: `conversation_message_files` (one row per file a message carries, naming the document, closed to the API) and `conversation_file_downloads` (each download: when, which link) |
| D-685 | **Which files may go** (`conversation_file_allowed`): a document of the contact's own account's intakes, scan verdict clean (as the account's Files tab lists them, D-199), on an intake of a firm the caller belongs to (the documents bucket's read rule). Being the contact's account's own file is the packet-or-census rule: a census goes only to its own employer. A lead has no account, so a lead's message carries no file. The send refuses any other with 22023, "That file can't go with this message. Only the account's own files that passed the virus check can.", before anything is recorded; at most 10. `conversation_files_for` lists what may go. No packet is among an account's files today (it is made, not uploaded), so none can be chosen |
| D-686 | **The token is made when the message is built, each attempt.** `claim_conversation_mail` (restated) makes a fresh 32-byte token for each file of each message it claims, keeps only its SHA-256 with 30 days to run, and answers the tokens to send-mail alone, which lists each file with its link after the body: "Files (each link stops working after 30 days):" then `<name>: <APP_BASE_URL>/f#<token>`. A retry replaces the earlier attempt's tokens, so a link opens only from the message that went. The token is after `#`, so no request to the site carries it (D-645's way out); the page posts it to `message-file`. A message with files and no `APP_BASE_URL` is not sent and is tried again (kind unavailable) |
| D-687 | **Opening a link** (`conversation_file_open`, the service role only): live (issued, not revoked, within its 30 days), then the document still clean; a link not live counts as a miss for the caller's address; a live link whose file failed a later check answers nothing without a miss. Each download is a row. Nothing in the product revokes a file link yet (no screen asks; a deleted message or document takes its links with it) |
| D-688 | **The read restated**: `conversation_message_fields` (from 6.4's) adds `failure_kind`, `failure_reason`, `recorded_at`, `sent_at` and `files` (each file's place and name, never a token); `send_conversation_message` takes `p_document_ids` (its old signature dropped, every rule kept in D-631's order). Showing a conversation is opening it: the place to write clears its new-mail mark through the newest message shown (6.4's `open_conversation`). The account's Overview lists its contacts' mail newest first (6.4's `account_conversation_messages`), each naming its contact and opening the contact's page |
| D-689 | **Emailing a lead makes it Working** (D-808, closing D-639's "not yet"): a New lead written to becomes Working in the same step; Not a fit stays as it is. The lead's page and its place to write are 8.5's (D-603). Left alone: Email in the contact's header (the place to write is on the page itself), CC on the screen (the database takes it; nothing asks for it), and the inbox (6.5) |

### 6.8 M-2.3 decisions

Slice 6.8, M-2.3 (send an update, #202), built test-first in `lane/6.8` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Migration `20261122180000_peo_update.sql`, tested in
`supabase/tests/peo_update.sql`; `SendToPeo` offers the update on each PEO's card; `QuoteRequestsPanel` marks a quote given on an older version.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1610 | **An update is a send.** `send_peo_update(session, peo, to, subject, note)` asks the closing and support guard, the advisor and the intake's firm, locks the pipeline row then the PEO's quote request (as every send locks them), checks there is an update, then calls `send_to_peo` unchanged, so every check and refusal of M-2.1 (D-672) is the update's too, in the same words: the employer's authorization, a changed answer, the addresses, the subject and note, the mailbox, the stage. `send_to_peo` is not restated (6.7 builds on it); a send of a newer version through `send_to_peo` itself (6.7's several PEOs) is still a send, without the summary of D-1612, and its link's revoke still reads `newer_version` (D-1614) |
| D-1611 | **Which PEO has an update.** Its quote request is open (sent or quoted), as the queue counts one holding an older version (`advisor_list_submissions`), and its `sent_version` is older than the latest certification still current. Refused PT409 in plain words: never sent ("Nothing was sent to <PEO> yet, so there is no update. Send it the bundle."), declined, closed, or already at the latest ("<PEO> already has version <n>, the latest certified."). The message keeps the version the PEO held in `quote_request_messages.update_from_version` (added, null on every other message) |
| D-1612 | **What changed, never a value.** The database writes it under the note, before the fixed block, so the advisor can't change it and no value can get in: "Changed since version <n>: <labels>." then "Census: <a> added, <r> removed, <c> changed." (or "Census: unchanged."), or "Nothing in the answers or the census changed since version <n>." `peo_update_changes` compares the two certifications' answers key by key and names each changed one by its label from `intake_field_labels()`, a copy of `FIELD_LABELS` (the field review's labels and order) that `schemaSync.test.ts` keeps equal; the census is matched person by person by row id and only counted. The stored body and the email carry only labels and numbers |
| D-1613 | **Nothing goes until an advisor sends it.** The product offers the update on the PEO's card in What each PEO still needs ("<PEO> has version 1. Version 2 is certified." and "Send version 2 to <PEO>"), where a send starts (D-678); the composer previews "What changed since version 1: each answer by its name and the census by count, never a value". Nothing is queued, drafted or sent on certifying again |
| D-1614 | **The old link is revoked as `newer_version`.** `issue_bundle_link` is restated from 20261122110000 with every line kept; the live link before is revoked as `newer_version` when it was for an older version than the one issued, else as `replaced`. So the old link works until the update's own link is issued, a moment before it is joined and sent; of an update never joined (the page closed), D-671's ten-minute end applies and the PEO keeps no live link, as with a re-send |
| D-1615 | **A quote stays, marked with its version.** `quote_requests.quoted_version` (added) is set by a trigger on every write: the version sent when a request becomes quoted, or when a quoted request's quote itself changes (received on, expires on, proposed start, fee); null while not quoted. An update moves `sent_version` and leaves both the status and `quoted_version`, so the request reads "Quote given on version 1; version 2 sent since" until the advisor records a new answer. A note alone is no new answer. Quotes recorded before this are taken as given on the version then sent (backfilled, not logged); the change log names the column "Quote given on version" |
| D-1616 | **The queue's flag clears by itself.** "A PEO has an older version" already reads every open quote request's `sent_version` against the latest certification; an update moves it at the send (D-671), so the flag clears once every open PEO holds the latest. As with any send, the version is recorded when the send is queued, not when the provider takes it |
| D-1617 | **Registries.** `send_peo_update` is an advisor's firm-scoped write: in the three registries of `data_isolation.sql` (advisor, firm probe, secret read), `help_from_nevermore.sql`'s writes and `close_a_firm.sql`'s guarded list. `peo_update_changes` and `intake_field_labels` are no definers and no API role may call them; the trigger function is a plain trigger. No new table, so no closing line, probe or data class |
| D-1618 | **Left for later**: a census changed only under its legacy key (`employees`, intakes before the census) counts every person as added; the thread does not mark an update apart from a send (its body says "Changed since version <n>"); an update from 6.7's several-PEOs flow; M-2.4's follow-up carrying the live link |

### 8.7 CRM-L1.2b decisions

Slice 8.7, CRM-L1.2b ("A visitor on the firm's public link who asks to be contacted becomes a lead"), built test-first in `lane/8.7` (`TDD_LOG.md`).
Migration `20261122230000_request_contact.sql`, tested in `supabase/tests/request_contact.sql`; `src/lib/contactRequestApi.ts`, `src/components/ContactMeForm.tsx`, and a
section of the landing page (`LandingPage.tsx`) that shows only at a firm's address.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1660 | **Migration timestamp.** The table's `20261121150000` for 8.7 is stale: it sorts before migrations already merged (the lane brief's finding). The slice takes `20261122230000`, after 8.4's `20261122210000` and 8.5's `20261122220000`, and every SQL test that loads the whole suite loads it after `20261122200000_peo_registrations.sql` (`scripts/sqlMigrationOrder.ts` checks the order) |
| D-1661 | **What "the firm's public link" is.** The firm's address (`<address>.<the site's domain>`, D-17) shows the landing page to a signed-out visitor or a guest (`HomeRoute`). At a firm's address that exists and is not refused or closed (`firm.status === "known"`, the same answer `firm_public_name` gives), the landing page gains one section, "Rather talk to someone first?", with the form "Ask <firm> to contact me". On the bare domain, www and every host that is not a firm's, the section does not show: no firm is named, so a lead there would have no firm. No new route, so nothing in `App.tsx`, `seo.ts` or `vercel.json` |
| D-1662 | **One function, callable by anyone.** `request_contact(p_address, p_name, p_email, p_phone, p_business, p_message)` is the one new function, `SECURITY DEFINER`, granted to `anon` and `authenticated` (a guest is `authenticated` with `is_anonymous`), as `firm_public_name` and `report_client_event` are. The visitor has no grant on `leads` and reads nothing back: the answer is the word `taken` or `busy`. The firm is found from the address only, with `firm_public_name`'s own test (not refused, not closed): no browser-supplied firm id exists (D-101), and an address no firm holds, a wildcard or none is PT404 "No firm holds this address." |
| D-1663 | **What the lead is.** Source `firms_link` (Firm's link, D-804), status New (D-808), **no owner**: the link is the firm's, not an agent's, and no story builds an agent's own link (D-804: a referral partner's link fills the source only where D-101 offers one, and no slice builds that). The name is required ("Enter your name."), and an email or a phone ("Enter an email address or a phone number so the firm can reach you."), so no lead arrives that nobody can contact; every other field goes through `lead_input_error`, the hand form's words, with a business name. The visitor's message (at most 1,000 characters) is kept in the lead's note as "Asked to be contacted: <message>", or "Asked to be contacted." when there is none. A visit carrying a referral code (`?ref=`) is still Firm's link: the code is not read here |
| D-1664 | **Who is told.** The visitor is told on the page ("Thanks. <firm> has your details and will be in touch."). Inside the firm, an owner finds the lead as the newest New lead with no owner in Leads (the level rule, D-809: a lead with no owner is seen and changed by owners only, so an agent or a team lead does not see it, tested), and it counts in the menu's open leads. **No email or other alert is sent in this slice.** A new alert kind goes through the outbox, the dispatcher and the owners' recipient rule (as `export_alert` does, D-206) and so restates `outbox_kind` functions that other lanes are changing; it is left for its own story, named in the pull request. An owner assigns the lead (change owner, 8.4) so an agent sees it |
| D-1665 | **A closing firm and a closed one.** A firm that is closing is read-only (ADR 0023): the request is refused PT423 "This firm is not taking requests right now." (the visitor learns nothing a firm's own name does not already show them, since `firm_public_name` still names a closing firm); the firm's row is held `FOR KEY SHARE` first, as `assert_firm_writable` holds it, so a closure asked for at that moment waits for the lead. A firm that has closed reads as an address no firm holds. A support session cannot reach this function through a membership: it reads none (`help_from_nevermore.sql` lists it as exempt with that reason). The function is in `close_a_firm.sql`'s guarded list |
| D-1666 | **Abuse, for an anonymous caller.** The repo's pattern for a prospect-facing write is a cap counted in the database inside the function (`report_client_event`: per account, per signed-out, per hour), never a client-side one, so the same here. (1) **The same email asking again within a day is the same ask**: the visitor is told `taken` and no second lead is made, so a person pressing the button twice, or a script repeating one address, adds one lead. (2) **At most 20 requests an hour for one firm's link, and 200 an hour for every firm's together**: past it the answer is `busy`, no lead is made, the page says "Lots of requests are coming in right now. Try again in a little while.", and the visitor's words stay in the form. Both count `leads` rows of source `firms_link`, so there is no new table to keep or prune, and a lead deleted to the bin still counts. Two requests at the limit are counted one after the other (an advisory lock per firm, and one for the platform). Not done: a per-IP cap (the edge function pattern `consume_ip_quota` needs a function in front of the database, and the page calls the database directly, as `firm_public_name` is called) and a captcha; a per-firm cap of 20 an hour is what stops a flood from filling one firm's Leads, and a visitor can still fill the hour for a firm. Named for the owner to decide |
| D-1667 | **The registries.** `request_contact` is in `data_isolation.sql`'s three function registries (the callable list with audience `anyone`, `fn_class` as `platform_wide`: the caller names no firm, and nothing of a firm is read back, and the list of definer functions a signed-out caller may run), not as `firm_scoped`: its two-firm probe is about a member of one firm being refused another's rows, and here the firm is the address. Its own two-firm cases are in `request_contact.sql` (a request at one firm's address makes a lead of that firm, never another, and another firm's link is not slowed by this one's cap). `help_from_nevermore.sql` lists it among the caller's own and, for writes, exempt with the reason. `types.ts` carries the RPC |
| D-1668 | **What the page does with an answer.** `taken` shows the thanks; `busy` and every refusal show one line with the words and keep what was typed; the database's words are shown for a refused field (22023), a closing firm (PT423) and an unknown address (PT404), and nothing else of an error ever reaches the visitor ("Couldn't send your request. Try again."). The button is disabled and reads "Sending…" while the request is on its way, so a double press sends once |
| D-1669 | **Left alone.** A "contact me" for a referral partner's link (source `referral_partner`) and an agent's own link: no story builds them (D-804). The Leads page and a lead's page (8.4, 8.5). The firm's custom fields (Epic 9, D-805). The chamber and intake pages: this is for a visitor who starts no intake. An email alert to the owners (D-1664). Restated functions: none, so no other lane's definition is copied |

### 8.5 CRM-L2.1 decisions

Slice 8.5, CRM-L2.1 (a lead's page, screen 15), built test-first in `lane/8.5` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Migration `20261122220000_lead_page.sql`, tested in
`supabase/tests/lead_page.sql`; the page `/leads/:id` (`src/pages/leads/LeadPage.tsx`, `src/components/leads/`), tested in `src/pages/leads/LeadPage.test.tsx`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1650 | **The migration's timestamp is `20261122220000`**, not the build order's `20261121130000`, which is older than migrations already merged (6.x) and would sort before them. No table is added (so no closing line, probe or data class). Restated, each from its newest and only definition, `20261119180000_calls_notes_tasks.sql`, with every line kept: `can_edit_activity`, `update_activity`, `add_to_note`, `set_task_done`. `log_activity` is not restated: a lead's calls, notes and tasks are logged by a function of their own (D-1657), so the account's log keeps its signature. `firm_closing_plan()` is not restated |
| D-1651 | **Who sees and changes a lead's page.** `lead_page` answers the lead to whoever its row rule shows it (the level rule on its owner, one in the bin an owner's or support's: `lead_visible`, the policy's own test), else NULL as for a lead that is not there. `can_change` is the level rule and a lead that is not read only; `can_add` is the log's own rule. Converted, Not a fit and the bin are read, not changed (CRM-L1.1): `update_lead`, `mark_lead_not_fit`, `log_lead_activity` and the four restated activity functions refuse them with 55000 in words ("This lead is Not a fit. Reopen it to change it.", "This lead was converted. Change its account or contact instead.", "This lead is in the bin. Restore it to change it."), after PT404 and the level rule, and no row of theirs says it can be edited. Every write asks `assert_firm_writable` (ADR 0023, 0024) and is in the three registries, the support list and the closing's guarded list |
| D-1652 | **Reopen makes a Not a fit lead Working**, its reason cleared: a lead someone decided on was being worked, and Working is what "an agent setting it" makes (D-808). Both moves are in its history. Reopen is offered (`can_reopen`) to whoever the level rule lets change the lead, on a Not a fit lead not in the bin; `reopen_lead` refuses any other lead with 55000 |
| D-1653 | **"Converting makes"** names what converting (8.6) would make, by D-810: an account named after the business, or the lead's name when it has no business; a contact named after the lead's name unless that name is its business's (trimmed, any case), which is "a business and no person" and makes no contact; an opportunity "<account> – PEO search". Worked out on screen (`convertingMakes`, src/data/leadPage.ts); 8.6 must make the same three or change this panel with it |
| D-1654 | **When a lead becomes Working on its page** (D-808): a save of its fields makes a New lead Working only when a field (the owner among them) actually changed, so pressing Save on what is there moves nothing; the first call logged makes it Working in the same step, a note or a task does not; emailing it already does (D-689). Each is one history entry |
| D-1655 | **The Converted banner** links to the account, the contact and the opportunity converting made, each only where the person may see its account. The opportunity has no page on this branch (D-683), so its link opens its account's Opportunities tab (`/accounts/<id>?tab=opportunities`), where it is listed. A converted lead's page shows no place to write, no "Converting makes" and nothing to change |
| D-1656 | **The status bar** shows New, Working and Converted, each with the date it began: New when the lead was made, Working when it last became Working (from its history, `lead_status_began`; none while New), Converted when converted. The lead's own status is the current step; a Not a fit lead marks none, and its banner says since when and why |
| D-1657 | **A lead's calls, notes and tasks** (D-807) are logged by `log_lead_activity` (log_activity's checks, order and words, with no contact or opportunity to name), listed by `lead_activity` (contact_activity's shape and order) and offered people by `lead_activity_options`. On screen they are the account's own rows, columns and dialogs: `LogActivityDialog` takes a `leadId` (no With or About), and `LeadActionsProvider` gives them the account's actions context |
| D-1658 | **The place to write on a lead's page** (D-603) is 6.3's `PlaceToWrite`, its recipient now a contact or a lead (`getLeadConversation`, `send_conversation_message` with `p_lead_id`); a lead's message carries no file (D-685), so none is offered. It is not shown on a converted lead or one in the bin (the send refuses both). Email in the header is a link to it (`#emails`). `brokerNext` keeps `/leads/<id>`, so connecting a mailbox comes back to the lead. The inbox opens a lead's message on its page, replacing D-696's Leads page searched to the name. The Leads table links each name to the lead's page (in `leadsTable.tsx`; `LeadsPage.tsx` is unchanged) |
| D-1659 | **Left for later**: Convert (CRM-L2.2, slice 8.6) is not shown on the header or the table, so no button does nothing; the firm's own fields on a lead (Epic 9, D-805); deleting and restoring from the lead's page (the bin is slice 8.4's); a lead's labels on its page (8.4); the lead's change history on its page (its rows are in `record_history`; no list of them is drawn yet) |

### 8.6 CRM-L2.2 decisions

Slice 8.6, CRM-L2.2 (Convert a lead, screen 22), built test-first in `lane/8.6b` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Migration `20261122250000_convert_lead.sql`, tested in
`supabase/tests/convert_lead.sql`; the dialog `src/components/leads/ConvertLeadDialog.tsx` (tested in `ConvertLeadDialog.test.tsx`), its client `src/lib/leadConvertApi.ts`, and the Convert button on the lead's page.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1680 | **The migration's timestamp is `20261122250000`**, not the build order's `20261121140000`, which is stale: `20261121140000` is already `leads_keep_commission_history` and every merged 6.x and 8.x migration sorts after it. No table is added (so no closing line, probe or data class). Restated, from its newest definition `20261121100000_merge_records.sql`, with every line kept: `activities_guard`, with one clause added (a lead's call, note or task, which has no account, may take the account its conversion made or chose, while `convert_lead` says so for its own transaction with `nevermore.converting_lead`, as `merge_accounts` does). `create_account`, `add_contact` and `opportunity_create` are not restated: the opportunity is made by calling `opportunity_create` (so its name, source, archived-account and owner rules are the one rule); the contact is checked by `contact_input_error` and named by `crm_split_person_name`, the chain's own splitter; the account is inserted directly because `create_account` fixes its source to Added by hand and `keep_account_source` forbids changing it afterwards, and a converted lead's account is source Lead |
| D-1681 | **What moves, and what does not.** The lead's calls, notes and tasks (`activities.lead_id`) go onto the account, and onto the contact when one is made, in the same statement; their text, author and time are untouched, no opportunity is named. The lead's labels are copied onto the account (the lead keeps its own: it is history). The lead's own note field is a field, not a row, so it is carried over as a note on the account and the contact, by the person converting; the lead keeps its note too. The lead's conversation and its reply address go to the new contact with their messages (D-606), so replies to the old address still arrive; with no contact made the conversation stays on the lead and is read only. The lead's history stays with the lead (the change to Converted is one status entry, by the person converting, D-808); the account, contact and opportunity start with their own "created" entries, by the same person. Custom-field values move with Epic 9 (D-805, D-810) |
| D-1682 | **Who owns what it makes.** A new account, contact and opportunity belong to the lead's owner when that person is still a current member of the firm (their membership is held FOR SHARE to the end of the transaction, as `create_account` holds an owner's), else to the person converting: a lead with no owner converted by an owner, or whose owner left, makes records the converter owns. When an existing account is used, the contact and the opportunity belong to the person converting, who sees that account (the lead's owner may not). Who may convert is the level rule on the lead's owner (D-809): `lead_to_change`, so another agent's lead is as not there (PT404) and an owner's alone for a lead with no owner |
| D-1683 | **The account.** Made from the lead's business (its name when it has none, D-1653), city, state, employees and current provider, source Lead. Using an existing account changes nothing of it: not its name, place or size, and a new contact never displaces its main contact (it is main only when the account has none). An account the person may not see is as not there (PT404), another firm's the same; an archived one takes nothing new (55000, D-222) even with no opportunity. **Possible matches** (`lead_convert_context`) are the duplicate match (D-193) by `duplicates_of_account`: the same name and state (a lead has no website), or a contact with the lead's email; only accounts the person may see (a match they may not see is never named, as D-603 would mask it, here simply left out), none archived, the earliest first, at most ten. The dialog lists them before "Create account" and the default is making a new one |
| D-1684 | **The contact.** Named from what was typed on the form, else the lead's name split as the chain splits a prospect's (the last word is the last name, the rest the first). Its email and phone are the form's, else the lead's. A contact needs a last name and an email or a phone (`contact_input_error`, 22023 in words, before anything is written); the agent fills them in or unticks the contact. A lead whose name is its business's (trimmed, any case) makes no contact unless the person ticks it, and a ticked one then needs a last name typed (the business's own name is never split into a person). The answer's `contact` is null when none was made, which the dialog says |
