| D-1685 | **The opportunity** is made unless unticked, named "<account used> – PEO search" unless the agent typed a name, with no target start unless entered, the lead's employees, no source, and the new contact (when there is one) as its main contact. Its rules are `opportunity_create`'s, so a name over 200 characters is refused in its words and nothing is left behind. The lead page's "Converting makes" (`convertingMakes`, D-1653) and the function name the same three records; a conversion into an existing account names the opportunity for that account |
| D-1686 | **Refusals, in order**, after the firm and the write guard (`firm_to_act_for`, `assert_firm_writable`: a closing firm and Nevermore support PT423, a stranger 42501) and the level rule (`lead_to_change`): a lead already converted ("This lead was converted already."), a Not a fit lead ("This lead is Not a fit. Reopen it before converting it.", D-810) and a lead in the bin (an owner is told to restore it, 55000; an agent, who does not see it, PT404) are 55000; then the contact's words (22023), the existing account's (PT404, 55000), the opportunity's (22023). Every refusal leaves nothing: the account, contact, moved rows and the lead are one transaction. Converting twice makes one chain: the lead row is locked first, so the second caller waits and then finds it Converted |
| D-1687 | **On screen.** Convert is on the lead's page header, offered where the person may change the lead and it is New or Working and not in the bin, and not in a support session; it opens the dialog (screen 22). Converting reloads the page, which shows the Converted banner and nothing changeable. The banner's opportunity link now opens `/opportunities/<id>` (amends D-1655, whose Opportunities tab link was for a page that did not exist). The dialog checks the contact's two rules itself in the database's words, shows any refusal in the database's words and stays open with what was typed, and says so when it could not look for matches while still letting the agent make a new account |
| D-1688 | **Left alone.** "Convert and start a quote" (waits for New quote, slice 5.4; D-402, D-810: no dead buttons). Custom-field values (Epic 9). Converting in bulk (no story asks for it). Converting from the Leads table's row is built since D-1901: CRM-L1.1 does list "Row action: Convert", so "no story asks for it" was wrong for a row. Choosing another owner for what is made (D-1682 gives one rule). Naming the new account other than for the business. Merging the lead into an account later: not a conversion |
| D-1689 | **Registries and tests.** `convert_lead` and `lead_convert_context` are in the three registries of `data_isolation.sql` (callable, probed with two firms, firm-scoped), in `help_from_nevermore.sql` (the conversion is a write path, the context a read) and in `close_a_firm.sql`'s guarded list with a probe each, and every SQL test that loads the whole suite loads `20261122250000` after `20261122230000`. `convert_lead.sql` loads the whole suite, Epic 4's migrations included (the 8.5 test's list stops before them, so `opportunity_create` is not there to call) |

### 8.4 CRM-L1.1b decisions

Slice 8.4, CRM-L1.1b (bulk actions with Undo, the bin with Restore, the 30-day nightly removal), built test-first in `lane/8.4` (`TDD_LOG.md`).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1640 | **Migration `20261122210000_leads_bulk_and_bin.sql`**, not the plan table's `20261121120000`: that date is older than migrations already merged (6.x's `20261122...`), so a later-merged, earlier-dated file would load before functions it must build on. Every SQL test that loads the whole suite takes it after `20261122190000` (6.10 takes `20261122200000`, 8.5 `20261122220000`) |
| D-1641 | **One bulk change, for leads too.** `bulk_leads_preview`, `bulk_leads_change` and `bulk_leads_undo` keep the accounts' contract (CRM-S3.7b): ids or the table's own filters (never both), at most 1,000 (PT413), one preview and one change asked the same question (`bulk_lead_outcome`), all or none, one history entry per lead, ten minutes' undo by its maker, once. They share `bulk_changes` and `bulk_change_records` (added to: `lead_id` with record type `lead`, cascading with the lead; the actions `status`, `not_fit`, `delete`). A lead the caller does not see, another firm's, or one in the bin is not available (counted, never named); one they see but may not change (D-809) is skipped and named with the level rule's words. A closing firm and support are refused (PT423) |
| D-1642 | **Converted and Not a fit in bulk** (CRM-L1.1's second AC). A converted lead is skipped by every action ("It is converted, so it can be read but not changed or deleted."). A Not a fit lead is skipped by all but Change status, which reopens it (the reason is cleared), and Delete. Change status offers New or Working only: Converted is converting's, Not a fit has its own action with one reason from the short list |
| D-1643 | **Set a field on leads**: title, business, email, phone, city, state, employees, current provider, checked by the lead's own rules (`lead_input_error`); never the name, status, reason, source, owner (its own action) or the note. A bulk field change does not make a New lead Working: D-808's "saved change" is the lead's page (8.5); a bulk edit is housekeeping, as an owner change is |
| D-1644 | **Delete and its Undo.** Delete asks "Delete N leads?" with the preview's words ("This will move 1 lead to the bin. An owner can restore it for 30 days; after that it is removed for good."), names the skipped, and sets `deleted_at`. Its Undo takes the leads out of the bin unless the lead was changed since (its history has a `deleted_at` entry after the change) |
| D-1645 | **The bin on screen.** "Deleted" on the Leads page, an owner's only, opens the bin: `leads_bin` (owners, and support who reads what an owner reads; 50 a page, newest deleted first, each with `removed_on`, the day it goes) and Restore per lead (`restore_leads`, an owner's, never support's, at most 1,000, one history entry each). A dialog, not a view tab: a Deleted tab would need `leads_list` to read the bin, a restated query the 8.5 lane may also touch |
| D-1646 | **The nightly removal** is `remove_binned_leads()`, every firm's leads more than 30 days in the bin, with their calls, notes, tasks, labels, undo records (foreign keys) and history (the capture trigger). It is a step of the firm record's daily job `prune-firm-activity` (03:53 UTC), restated from `20261118120000_help_from_nevermore.sql` with every step it had, not a second scheduler (`firm_members.sql` and `help_from_nevermore.sql` count the jobs). An undo's `gone` is always 0 for leads: nothing removes a lead within ten minutes but its closing firm |
| D-1647 | **Export** (D-811): `export_table_registry` restated from `20261119130000_accounts_table_query.sql` (its newest definition) with every line and `leads`; `export_source_leads` reads `leads_rows` (the level rule, the bin left out) with codes in words. The bar's Export and the toolbar's are the shared `ExportControl`; `EXPORT_TABLES` gains `leads` |
| D-1648 | **Labels on leads.** One history entry "labels" per label on or off (`lead_labels_history`, as accounts'). Found while building it: `bulk_accounts_undo` takes a word its change made out of the firm's one list when no account carries it, and knew nothing of leads; a trigger on `labels` now keeps a word a lead still carries (the delete is skipped, the rest of that undo goes on). A leads' undo does not take a word it made out of the list. **Not built**: the Label column and filter on the leads table (it needs `leads_row` and the leads query restated; left for a slice that owns the query) |
| D-1649 | **What was restated, and from where**: `export_table_registry()` from `20261119130000_accounts_table_query.sql`; the `prune-firm-activity` job's command from `20261118120000_help_from_nevermore.sql`. No new table, so `firm_closing_plan()` is not restated (the leads, their labels and the undo records are already in it). No leads function is restated. `bulkChangeApi.ts` now exports its refusal and answer readers, which `leadsBulkApi.ts` shares; an undo with no opportunities reads as none |

### 8.7b decisions

Slice 8.7b, the owners' email for a "contact me" lead and a captcha on the form (the founder's decision after slice 8.7), built test-first in the same lane style as 8.7.
Migration `20261122240000_lead_alert_and_captcha.sql`, tested in `supabase/tests/request_contact.sql`; `supabase/functions/contact-request` (handler.ts and its test);
`supabase/functions/dispatch-outbox` (`parseLeadFromLink`, `renderLeadFromLink`); `src/components/ContactMeForm.tsx` and `src/lib/contactRequestApi.ts`. TDD log: `docs/tdd-logs/8.7b.md`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1670 | **What this amends.** The founder's decision after 8.7, recorded here and amending two earlier ones. D-1664 said "No email or other alert is sent in this slice": one is, now (D-1671, D-1672). D-1666 listed "a captcha" under "Not done": Cloudflare Turnstile stands in front of the form now (D-1673 to D-1676). Everything else in D-1660 to D-1669 stands (the lead, its owner-less level rule, the abuse caps counted in the database, the closing firm, the answers `taken` and `busy`), except D-1662's "callable by anyone" (D-1673) |
| D-1671 | **The alert is an outbox email of the kind `lead_from_link`, copied from `export_alert` (M-206).** When `request_contact` makes a lead it queues one outbox row in the same transaction, a firm-level row (a firm, no intake, `outbox_events_one_target`), deduplicated by the lead's key (`lead_from_link:<lead id>`). It goes to the firm's current owners, asked at each send (`outbox_owner_recipients`, so D-35 and ADR 0020 hold). It is a firm's alert (`outbox_kind_is_firm_alert`): it waits while the firm is suspended, a bounce never suppresses an owner's address, it ends once the firm has closed. The dispatcher builds it only from the payload's own fields (`parseLeadFromLink`, `renderLeadFromLink`) and gives up for good on a payload it cannot parse (a missing field, a lead id that is not an id, a time that is not a time). The database never sends an email itself. **Restated, each with every line and kind it had:** `outbox_events_one_target` (from `20261122090000_conversations.sql`), the `outbox_events_kind_check` kind list (from `20261122140000_peo_send.sql`), `outbox_kind_is_firm_alert` (from `20261119090000_export.sql`) and `request_contact` (from `20261122230000_request_contact.sql`, the one addition the outbox insert and, for D-1673, its grants). `claim_outbox_events` and the closing's functions are not restated: they ask `outbox_kind_is_firm_alert`, which now knows the kind |
| D-1672 | **What the email carries, and what it does not.** The payload and the email hold the visitor's name, their business when given, the firm's name, the lead's id and when (UTC): "<Name> asked <Firm> to contact them." and a button "Open the lead" to `<APP_BASE_URL>/leads/<id>`. The owners are entitled to see the lead, but the lead is in the product behind sign-in and an email is copied, forwarded and kept in inboxes the firm does not control: the visitor's email address, phone and message stay out of the payload and the email, and the email says so. Because it links into the app it needs the app's address as well as a sender (the advisor readiness: `sendableKinds`, `kindReadiness`), unlike `export_alert`, which has no link (M-213). An ask that makes no lead (the same email again, `taken`; a link past its hour, `busy`) queues nothing, so a script cannot flood the owners' inboxes past the caps that already bound the Leads table |
| D-1673 | **The database no longer answers a browser.** `request_contact` is revoked from `anon`, `authenticated` and PUBLIC and granted to `service_role` alone: the contact-request function is the one caller, so the check cannot be skipped by calling the database directly (the anon key is public). Registries: `data_isolation.sql` drops it from the callable list and from the list of definer functions a signed-out caller may run (it stays in the function registry, `platform_wide`, with its new words); `help_from_nevermore.sql` and `close_a_firm.sql` keep it (it still changes a firm's data and refuses a closing firm itself) with the words updated. The abuse caps stay in the database (D-1666) |
| D-1674 | **`contact-request`, the edge function.** `verify_jwt = false` (a visitor signs in nowhere), POST only. In order: no `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY`, `SUPABASE_URL` or `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is 503 "Asking to be contacted isn't available right now" and nothing is asked of anyone; no token (missing, blank, not text, over 2,048 characters, a body that is not JSON) is 400 "Confirm you are not a robot, then try again." and Turnstile is not asked; the token is checked with Turnstile's siteverify (`secret`, `response`, and `remoteip` as the first address of `X-Forwarded-For`): a verdict of no is 403 "The check didn't pass. Try again.", and **anything that is not a plain verdict (the provider unreachable, an error status, an answer that is not JSON or says nothing of success) is 503 and the request is refused**: it fails closed. Only then it calls `request_contact` as the service role with the address the request came from. The database's own words for a refused field (22023, 422), a closing firm (PT423, 423) and an address no firm holds (PT404, 404) are passed on; every other failure is "Couldn't send your request. Try again." (500), and the log holds the step and the code, never a message (it can hold what the visitor typed) and never the token. The token is never sent to the database |
| D-1675 | **The keys.** `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` (a function secret) and `VITE_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` (the app's build, publishable). The site key is the one the guest intake's widget already reads (`RequireAuth`): one Turnstile widget, its domain list covering the site and every firm's address under it. With either missing the function and the form both refuse (D-1674, D-1676): never open without the check. The setup script deploys the function (`FUNCTIONS`), probes it (an empty request gets its own 400, or its own 503 while the secret is unset, which is a to-do and not a failed setup), and warns when the secret is unset (`checkSecrets`); `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md` and `supabase/env.production.example` document both keys |
| D-1676 | **The form.** Turnstile's script is loaded only by the form (`TurnstileWidget` loads it when mounted), so the bare domain and every other page load nothing from Cloudflare. The button is disabled until the visitor passes the check. A token is good for one request, so after every try (taken, busy or refused) the widget is mounted anew and the next press needs a fresh check; what was typed is kept. A widget that cannot load says "The check couldn't load. Reload the page and try again." and the button stays off. With no site key the form is not offered: one line, "Asking to be contacted isn't available right now", no fields. The page posts to the function (`contactRequestApi.ts`) and no longer calls the database; the function's words for a visitor are shown as they are and any other answer reads "Couldn't send your request. Try again." |
| D-1677 | **The caps are unchanged and still the database's (D-1666).** The same email within a day is the same ask; 20 an hour for one firm's link; 200 an hour for every link together. Turnstile adds a check on the visitor, not a count, so the caps still bound what a person who passes the check (or pays a solver) can do. Not done: a per-address cap (`consume_ip_quota`) in the function. Named for the owner to decide |
| D-1678 | **Known limits, not changed here.** The firm's address is the browser's word in the request body, as it was in D-1662 (the host the visitor is at); the database finds the firm from it and refuses an address no firm holds, so a script can ask any firm that exists, within that firm's caps. The function does not check Turnstile's returned hostname or an action name against the site (the widget's domain list in Cloudflare is the control); and a token Turnstile has accepted once is rejected by Turnstile on a second use, so a replay is its refusal, not ours |
| D-1679 | **Left alone.** The lead's page and the Leads table (8.4, 8.5), the owners' alert for a lead made by hand (no story), a lead's assignment, the other outbox kinds, and the guest intake's use of the same site key (Supabase CAPTCHA stays off, `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md`) |

The withdrawal fieldset is therefore not offered with Won or Lost until 5.0b gives the dialog the offer. Tests: StatusPanel's withdrawal cases and AdvisorSubmissionPage's "offers the withdrawal when the status is changed to Lost" now choose Archived (same intent: the offer is drawn with the move, ticked, with its default text, and sent with it; never to the winner; none for a move that closes nothing), and one new case says none is offered with Won, Lost or Sent to PEOs |
| D-1719c | **Tests that were written for one line, kept in intent.** `crm_records`: the opportunities history trigger's field list ends `…referral_partner_code,won_at,won_by,` (main's CRM-O1.3b adds the two; the test loads the whole suite). `accounts_query`: an exported account row also carries `renewal_date` (null for Alpha Co; `20261121160000` adds it). `opportunity_stage`, `record_history` and `close_a_firm` keep this line's counts (lost_note; leads in the history's tables; the closing's deleted counts and its payments fixture are main's, with this line's conversation and lead counts). Leads' `LeadActionsProvider` gains the two new actions of the account's context (`newOpportunity`, `startRenewal`), never offered on a lead |
| D-1719d | **What main's Epic 4 covers of slices 5.0a, 5.0b and 5.0c, and what is left.** **5.0a (the opportunity page): done** (`/opportunities/:id`, `opportunity_page`, `opportunity_activity`, the header, details, contacts, quotes, intake and activity panels, in the menu under Accounts, `seo.ts`, `vercel.json`). Still missing from D-1700: 6.7's `SendToSeveralPeos` / Send to a PEO (the header and quotes panel say they are off, D-402), 6.3's place to write and `EmailAContact` / `AccountEmails` on the page (no Email button, no Emails column), and `brokerNext` (`src/lib/brokerAuth.ts` NEXT_PATTERN) does not yet know `/opportunities/<id>`, so a sign-in that was heading there lands on the console. **5.0b (the won/lost dialog): the dialog is done** (`CloseOpportunityDialog`, from the page and from the console's stage control, with `opportunity_mark_won`, `opportunity_mark_lost`, `opportunity_reopen`, `opportunity_outcome`). Not done: it does **not** offer 6.9's withdrawal to the PEOs still open (`close_submission_and_withdraw` is not called from it), and `opportunity_mark_won` / `opportunity_mark_lost` do **not** revoke the quote requests' bundle links (neither migration touches `bundle_links`); D-1717's "for 5.0b to fix" stands in full. **5.0c (the opportunity form): the form is done** (`NewOpportunityDialog`, `opportunity_create`, `update_opportunity`, Start the renewal). Not done: D-441's "Save and start the quote" and "Start from last year's certified answers" (the dialog's own comment defers both to New quote, Epic 5; its Save opportunity and Cancel are all there is) |

### 5.2 P-2.1 decisions

Slice 5.2 (P-2.1, a firm's link), migration `20261123110000`, decisions D-1740..D-1749. They carry out D-1704 and D-1713 and close D-101.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1740 | **The firm comes from the address the request was made at, resolved by the database.** At a firm's address the browser already names it on every database request (`x-firm-address`, 20261117090000). `keep_intake_firm` reads that header when an intake is inserted by the browser's roles and files the intake in the firm that holds the address. No column is added and no firm id is ever accepted from the browser: one sent with the insert is overwritten, and the firm can't be changed by an update, at any address. Naming another firm's address in the header files an intake in that firm and nowhere else, which is what opening that firm's public link does anyway; it reads nothing |
| D-1741 | **Which links take intakes.** A firm's link takes intakes unless the firm is refused (D-31), suspended, closing (it is read-only from the day its owner asks, ADR 0023) or closed. A firm still waiting for approval takes them: approval gates sending, not receiving. A link that takes none starts nothing: the insert is refused (`PT404`), it is never filed in the default firm instead, and nothing the visitor typed is kept. The first screen says "This link isn't taking new intakes". A closing firm that withdraws its request takes them again, and so does a suspended one once restored. An intake started before the firm closed goes on being its employer's: D-226 stands (certifying it in a closed firm leaves no records, in a closing firm makes the chain) |
| D-1742 | **No firm link means the default firm** (D-121 stands). An intake started with no `x-firm-address` (the bare domain, www, a preview host) belongs to the default firm, and so does one started at the default firm's own address. The default firm still can't close |
| D-1743 | **Referral links work for every firm** (closes D-101). A partner's link is its code on the firm link: `https://<firm address>.<domain>/?ref=<code>`. The default firm's partners keep the bare domain's link, so the links already out carry on working. The note "links come when the firm can take intakes", the missing "Their link" column and the code-only export are gone. The code is matched to a partner only within the intake's own firm, on the server (`advisor_intake_sources`, 20261116200500), which a SQL test now holds for two firms using one code. The browser keeps the `?ref=` code per origin, so a firm's link and the bare domain's keep separate codes |
| D-1744 | **The first screen and the agreement** (D-1713). At a firm's address the agreement screen opens with "You were invited by <Firm>" and a section "Who will see your answers" saying the firm will see the answers, the employees' personal information and the documents. The wording is in the one legal-wording module (`src/data/legalWording.ts`), marked draft (D-12). The agreement is recorded with that line after the wording every employer reads, so the record shows what was shown; the version of the agreement (`2026-11-draft-2`) is not bumped, since the wording everyone reads is unchanged |
| D-1745 | **Known gaps, left for the stories that follow.** (a) An employer who already has an open intake goes on with it, in the firm it was started in: opening another firm's link does not move it, and does not show the invitation. (b) An employer who has already agreed to the intake agreement (it is kept per account) is not shown the screen again, so at a second firm's link they see neither the invitation nor the line; the first-screen line is shown only while an agreement is needed. CRM-P2.5 (slice 5.13) shows the employer which firm sees each intake and lets them end it; whether the invitation should be shown once for each firm is for slice 5.3 |
| D-1746 | **"Get a quote" is an entry, not a page.** `getAQuoteUrl(address)` and `START_INTAKE_PATH` (`src/lib/firmHost.ts`) are the URL that starts an intake for a firm (`https://<address>.<domain>/start`), and `GetAQuote` (`src/components/GetAQuote.tsx`) is a link to it that shows only at a firm's address while its link takes intakes (D-402: no button that leads to a refusal). The public landing page at the firm's address is slice 8.7's: this slice neither builds nor restyles it. The merger places `<GetAQuote className="...">` beside 8.7's "Ask to be contacted" |
| D-1747 | **`firm_takes_intakes(p_address)` is callable signed out**, for the first screen and `GetAQuote`. It returns a boolean and nothing else: no name, and no reason (an unknown address, a refused firm and a closing one answer alike). It is not rate limited, like `firm_public_name`: it reads one row by exact address and answers true or false. It is registered in the three registries of `data_isolation.sql` (the allowlist as 'anyone', the privileged-function registry as 'public', and the list of signed-out SECURITY DEFINER functions). `help_from_nevermore.sql` needed no line: it reads nothing of a firm's data and writes nothing |
| D-1748 | **`keep_intake_firm` is SECURITY DEFINER** (restated from 20261116090000_firms.sql, the newest definition), because the caller can't read `firms`, `firm_suspensions` or `firm_closures`. A definer function's `current_user` is its owner, so the trigger decides "the browser's roles" from the `role` setting the API sets, which stays the caller's; the service role and the superuser still place an intake where they like. The question "does this address take intakes" is written once, in `firm_for_intake_link` (service only), which the trigger and `firm_takes_intakes` both ask |
| D-1749 | **The chain at certification needs no change** (D-226, D-1703). `make_intake_chain` already reads the intake's own firm, so an intake started at a firm's link and certified makes its account, contact and opportunity in that firm and none in the default firm; a SQL test holds it |

### 5.1 N-1.2, CRM-Q3.1 decisions

Slice 5.1 (migration `20261123100000_quote_statuses.sql`, test `supabase/tests/quote_statuses.sql`, built from 215e93d3). The database half of CRM-Q3.1 and all of N-1.2: the two new statuses, who records them, what counts them as open. The quote's page, its Record the quote and Record a decline, and Home's Needs you are other slices.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1730 | **Six statuses.** `quote_requests.status` is `sent`, `acknowledged`, `question_open`, `quoted`, `declined`, `closed` (`QUOTE_REQUEST_STATUSES`, with its sync block; `schemaSync` compares them). Words: Waiting (sent, as before), Acknowledged, Question open, Quoted, Declined, Closed. "Not sent" is not a status (D-1702): nothing is added for it |
| D-1731 | **One function records the three open statuses**: `advisor_set_quote_request_status(p_quote_request_id, p_status)`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, the advisor of the quote request's own firm only (another firm's, the employer's and a signed-out caller are refused as the other advisor functions refuse them), `assert_firm_writable` first (a closing firm and a support session record nothing). It takes `sent`, `acknowledged` or `question_open` and moves between them freely; anything else (a quote, a decline, a close) is refused with "recorded with its own form", because those carry dates, a fee or a reason and, for a quote, move the opportunity (`advisor_update_quote_request`, unchanged, which already takes a request in any non-closed status). A quoted, declined or closed request is not moved here (a closed one is reopened by sending again); the status it already has is refused, so nothing is logged for a click that changed nothing |
| D-1732 | **Who and when are the existing log's.** Every change of `quote_requests.status` is already written to `quote_request_events` by `log_quote_request_change` (who, their email, when) and that table is append-only (`reject_audit_change`), so the new statuses add no table and no column. No new table means no `firm_closing_plan()` line and no data class |
| D-1733 | **Answering a question returns it to Sent; an acknowledgement stays.** CRM-Q3.1: "Answering it, or sending again, returns it to Sent". Any message the agent sends on a PEO's thread (`quote_request_messages`, direction `sent`: a send, a send again, a follow-up, an update) returns `question_open` to `sent` through one trigger (`quote_request_messages_answer_question`, not a privileged function), so `send_to_peo`, `follow_up_peo` and `send_peo_update` needed no restatement. Recording a send made outside the product (`advisor_record_quote_send`, restated) does the same, as it already returned `closed` to `sent`. `acknowledged` stays acknowledged through both. Mail that arrives (direction `received`, `sent_by_hand`) never reaches the trigger: an email only suggests (N-1.1) |
| D-1734 | **The stage needs no restatement.** An opportunity's stage is its submission's pipeline stage (D-206, ADR 0026), moved only by a send (to Sent to PEOs) and a recorded quote (to Quoted); `opportunity_stage()`, `opportunity_stage_of()` and `submission_opportunity_stage()` are as they were. So In market stays while any quote is Sent, Acknowledged or Question open and none is Quoted, and Quoted once one is, and an acknowledgement or a question moves nothing (tested). One edge that already existed: a Quoted opportunity whose only quote is later declined or closed stays Quoted until an agent moves the submission back to Sent to PEOs |
| D-1735 | **Open means Sent, Acknowledged or Question open, everywhere a Sent one counted**, restated from their newest definitions with every line kept: `set_submission_status` (20261122200500: archiving, winning or losing closes them), `close_submission_and_withdraw` (20261122190000: the withdrawal goes to them too), `advisor_list_submissions` (20261118140500: the "Waiting on a PEO" filter, the days waiting, the older-version flag), `queue_rows` (20261117100000) and `queue_page_fast` (20261117100500) (the queue's waiting column), `accounts_rows` (20261120111000: the account table's Waiting on a PEO), `menu_counts` (20261122150000), `opportunity_activity` (20261120120000: the activity lists the two new events). In the app: `OPEN_QUOTE_REQUEST_STATUSES` and `isOpenQuoteRequest` (`src/data/quoteRequests.ts`) for the queue's older-version note, the update offer, the Won dialog's list of quotes it closes, the PEO card's Follow up and Withdraw, the console's Archived withdrawal and Record a send again |
| D-1736 | **The menu's open quotes count** (D-1707) is the quote requests Sent, Acknowledged or Question open, as it was the Sent ones. A quoted quote is not an open one. It is the number of quotes still to be answered by the PEO or by the broker |
| D-1737 | **What slice 5.0x must change** (it restates `opportunity_mark_won` and `opportunity_mark_lost`, which this slice does not): both close the quote requests "WHERE status IN ('sent', 'quoted')" (`opportunity_mark_won` also excepting the winner; `opportunity_mark_lost` with `close_reason = 'search_ended'`, `opportunity_mark_won` with `went_elsewhere`). Both lists become `IN ('sent', 'acknowledged', 'question_open', 'quoted')`. Without it a PEO that acknowledged or asked a question stays open on a won or lost opportunity (the Won dialog already lists it as closed). `opportunity_reopen` does not switch on quote status |
| D-1738 | **Not in this slice.** "Shows on Home under Needs you" (there is no Home yet) and the seven-days-before-expiry line on Home; the quote's own page; Record the quote and Record a decline (Epic 7). `peo_registered_elsewhere` does not read a status and is unchanged. The opportunity page's Quotes panel, the account's Quotes tab and the search results already word a status through `QUOTE_REQUEST_LABELS`, so they show the two new ones in words with no change; the opportunity's activity words them ("Insperity acknowledged it has the packet", "Insperity has a question open"); the quote request's card on the submission page has "Acknowledged", "Question open" and "Back to Sent" buttons for an agent |
| D-1739 | **Branch name.** The branch `lane/5.1` already existed in another working copy at an older commit (the grilling's `fbf0de60`), so this slice was built on `lane/5.1-build`, cut from 215e93d3. Functions restated and the migration each came from are listed in the slice's report |

### 5.4 CRM-Q2.1 decisions

Slice 5.4 (CRM-Q2.1, New quote step 1, and New quote in the menu), migration `20261123130000`, decisions D-1760..D-1769.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1760 | **An account made by New quote has the source `new_quote`** (D-211). `create_account` (restated from 20261119150000_create_account.sql, its only and newest definition, every line kept) gains a last parameter `p_source`, default `added_by_hand`, and accepts only `added_by_hand` or `new_quote` (22023 in words for any other: the employer's intake, a lead and an import set their own). The fourteen-argument function is dropped, so there is one `create_account`; every existing call names fourteen arguments and still means Added by hand. `accountCreateApi.createAccount` sends `p_source` only when `source: "new_quote"` is given. The data_isolation registry line names the new signature |
| D-1761 | **New quote is in the menu on every page, first, and opens over the page the agent was on.** The link carries `state.backgroundLocation`; `BackgroundRoutes` (src/components/newquote) matches the route table against that page and keeps the browser's real address for `NewQuoteHost`, which the workspace shell draws beside the page. Opened from its own address with nothing beneath (a typed or bookmarked address), the dialog stands by itself. "Save and close" returns to the page it was opened from, address and filters as they were; opened alone it goes to the opportunity's page when one was made, and to the Quotes queue (`/advisor`) when nothing was. The overlay is a full-height dialog (`h-[100dvh] w-screen`), not a route of its own beneath the shell: a route would have replaced the page |
| D-1762 | **Step 1.** The agent types a business name; accounts that could be it come from the menu search's own `searchRecords` (two characters, 300 ms) and are picked from a list. "New account" asks for the account's name (required, taken from what was typed), state and employees, and a contact: a name (required; the first word is the first name, the rest the last name, a single word the last name, because a last name is required) and an email or a phone. While the new account is typed the duplicate match (`useAccountDuplicates`, `DuplicateWarning`, D-193) warns and never blocks. A picked account offers its contacts (main contact chosen) from `opportunity_create_context` and its open opportunities (stage not won or lost, from `account_tab`), or "New opportunity" (name offered as "PEO search"). Making uses the existing functions in order: `create_account` (source `new_quote`), `add_contact` (main contact), `opportunity_create` (the contact as main contact, employees from the account, no source: "New quote" is not an Opportunity source, D-439). No new function: the three calls are not one transaction, so a refusal part-way keeps what was made (an account made and a contact refused shows the database's words and Save and close again goes on from the account) |
| D-1763 | **Founder's direction (coordinator, amending D-1709 and D-1716): steps 2 to 4 are the existing intake, not new screens.** New quote launches the intake that already exists (`/start`, `ChamberPage` with Lenore and the tabs, inside `IntakeConsentGate`, and its certified and send screens) for the opportunity's intake, "as if launched from the same portal". Nothing of those steps is built here: no steps bar, no Continue, no "not available yet" list. The steps bar and the four-step list built first were removed. Step 1 ends with "Save and close" only (D-402). The seam is `stepToOpenAt(progressOf(opportunityPage))` in src/data/newQuote.ts (D-1702: no opportunity, step 1; no intake, step 2; not certified, 3; certified and nothing sent, 4), which the Continue will use to open the existing intake for the opportunity once slice 5.3's start-intake function lands; it is tested and not yet drawn |
| D-1764 | **The opportunity's own address.** `/new-quote/:opportunityId` opens the dialog on an opportunity (its account, opportunity and main contact, read from `opportunity_page`, with Save and close). It is where the existing intake will be opened from; until then it only confirms what step 1 saved. `/new-quote` is step 1. Both routes draw nothing themselves (the shell does), are in `src/App.tsx`, `src/data/seo.ts` (title "New quote", private) and `vercel.json`'s noindex list |
| D-1765 | **The empty states.** The queue's "Submissions appear here..." (D-160) and the Accounts page's "Accounts are the businesses you quote for." offer New quote as the primary action, opening over the page; Accounts keeps New account as a second action (Import waits for CRM-X4.1). Neither shows for Nevermore support on Accounts (`canMake`); the queue page does not know the workspace, so it offers New quote to a support session too, where the database refuses the write in words (a known gap, not worth a context read there) |
| D-1766 | **Entry points that open at step 2 wait.** Started from an account, an opportunity or a lead; "Save and start the quote" on NewOpportunityDialog (D-441); "Convert and start a quote" (D-810) need the existing intake opened for an opportunity, which is slice 5.3's start-intake function; they are not built here and nothing on those screens changed. "Start from last year's certified answers" (D-441) waits with them. The account page's "No opportunities yet. New opportunity or New quote" line is also left (only the two empty states in D-1765 were asked for) |
| D-1767 | **Not built here, on purpose.** A one-transaction function that makes account, contact and opportunity together (the three existing functions were reused as asked); a "New quote" opportunity source; an owner choice in step 1 (everything is owned by the agent making it, D-214, and changed on the pages afterwards); the account's other fields (website, phone, address) in step 1 (the account's own form has them) |

| # | Decision |
|---|---|

It now reads "Certified by <name>, <title> · <time> · version N · Prepared for <PEO>" for every viewer of the page (one line, `detail.currentVersion`). Nothing else was missing |

Their tests stand in QuoteRequestsPanel.test.tsx |

Steps 4 and 5 are not built here, so a certified opportunity opens on the furthest step that exists, the certified view, whatever was sent; `stepToOpenAt` is unchanged and slice 5.8 takes the certified-and-nothing-sent case over from this view with its Continue. **Merged with 5.8:** a certified opportunity now opens on the saved summary with 5.8's choosing of PEOs (step 4); "Certified answers" opens this view and "Back to New quote" returns, so D-1770, D-1772 and D-1773 hold one click in |

### 5.0x decisions

Slice 5.0x (what main's Epic 4 opportunity page and won/lost dialog still lacked for Epic 5: D-1700, D-1717, D-1719d), rebuilt strictly test-first as branch `lane/5.0y` (`TDD_LOG.md`; the first build, `lane/5.0x`, was thrown away because about half its cycles passed on their first run). Migration `20261123090000_opportunity_close_withdraw.sql`. The decisions of the first build are kept in their idea and restated here in the words of this one; the changes are named.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1720 | **The dialog's withdrawal is two database functions, not a second call** (kept). `opportunity_mark_won_and_withdraw` and `opportunity_mark_lost_and_withdraw` take everything `opportunity_mark_won` / `opportunity_mark_lost` take, then a subject and the one note. In one transaction they ask the refusals, read who is to be told, call the plain function and record the note on each of those PEOs' own thread (`record_peo_thread_message`, no link, from the caller's own mailbox); an exception from any step undoes the rest, so a refused withdrawal moves nothing (D-1625). Rejected: the browser calling the plain function and then a second one (cannot be undone), and `close_submission_and_withdraw` (it refuses Won and Lost on an opportunity, D-1717). **Changed from the first build:** the work is split into two internal functions both wrappers call, `opportunity_withdrawal_start` (the refusals and the list, before the move) and `opportunity_withdrawal_send` (after it), rather than the first build's `opportunity_peos_to_withdraw` / `opportunity_send_withdrawals` pair, which were also callable by the service role. These two are callable by nobody (service_only in the registry). A conflict (D-46) returns as the plain function returns it and sends nothing |
| D-1721 | **Closing an opportunity revokes the links** (kept; D-1717's "for 5.0b to fix", D-1624's privacy rule). `opportunity_mark_won` is restated from `20261121170000_opportunity_outcome.sql` (its newest; it already carries `20261120121000`'s lines) and `opportunity_mark_lost` from `20261120121000_won_lost_commission.sql` (its newest; no later migration restates it), every line kept, with one step more each: every quote request of the search but the winner's loses its live bundle link (`bundle_link_revoke(..., 'submission_closed')`), whether or not anything is sent. A lost search has no winner, so every link goes. The winner's stays |
| D-1722 | **Who is told** (kept). The quote requests of the opportunity's intake still open (sent or quoted), but the winner's, that the product wrote to (a message in direction sent), each on its own thread, to the people its newest message from the product went to. A PEO whose send was recorded by hand has no thread and is not written to; one that quoted but was not chosen is told like any other still open. The browser works out the same list (`peosToTell`) only to draw the offer; the database decides again |
| D-1723 | **The same refusals as 6.9's (D-1626)**, in this order: who may act for the firm (`firm_to_act_for`, 42501) and `assert_firm_writable` (a closing firm and a support session, PT423), asked in each wrapper first; then the caller's own mailbox (PT412, "Connect your mailbox to send from here."), 6.2's limits for the subject and note (22023), and then the plain function's own (PT404, the level rule, the version, the stage). The two wrappers are in the three registries of `data_isolation.sql` (`callable`, `fprobe`, `fn_class`), `help_from_nevermore.sql`'s writes and guarded lists and `close_a_firm.sql`'s guarded list |
| D-1724 | **The offer on the dialog** (`CloseOpportunityDialog`). For Won and Lost, when a PEO would be told: "Tell the PEOs still open" with "Send a withdrawal to <PEOs>" ticked, the subject ("Re: " the newest subject the product sent them, else the account's name, `withdrawalSubject`) and the note (`closingWithdrawalNote`, naming the account) editable. For Won the list follows the quote chosen and is empty until one is. Unticked, it is the plain function; the links are revoked all the same. While the mail is still being read the dialog refuses to close with "Still checking which PEOs are open. Try again in a moment." (the refusal is shown beside the button, Save is never disabled to point at an error); if it could not be read it says "We couldn't check which PEOs are still open, so no withdrawal is offered. Their links stop working either way." and closes without one. A database refusal (no mailbox, a limit) is shown beside the button and nothing moved |
| D-1725 | **The console too.** The Status panel hands the mail it already read to `ConsoleCloseDialog`, so Won and Lost from the console make the same offer; the panel's own offer stays Archived only (D-1719b), so there is one offer, not two |
| D-1726 | **The page reads the submission once.** `useStillNeeds` is called by the page, not the quotes panel; its read (`["opportunity-needs", firm, submission]`, without opening it) serves the quotes' "Still needs", the dialog's mail and Choose PEOs. It is read when there are quotes or when the person has chosen PEOs, never for an opportunity with neither |
| D-1727 | **Choose PEOs on the quotes panel** (D-1606, D-1700): 6.7's `SendToSeveralPeos` as it is, with two optional props (`openLabel` "Choose PEOs", `startOpen`), placed by `ChoosePeos` beside the panel's heading for an intake that has been certified, for whoever can act (not a support session, not an archived account). Clicking asks the page to read the submission and opens the component once it is here; a failed read says "We couldn't read this submission, so PEOs can't be chosen yet." with Try again, and a certification that cannot say what it held says nothing can be sent from here. A send asks the page's lists again. "Record a send" on the intake panel stays |
| D-1728 | **Email and the Emails panel on the page** (D-650, D-683). `EmailAContact` in the header beside Mark won or lost, the opportunity's main contact first, over the account's contacts (`emailChoices`, now one module shared with the account's page), for whoever can act; it returns to `/opportunities/<id>?email=<contact>` after connecting a mailbox and opens the place to write again for that contact. `AccountEmails` with `opportunityId` (6.4's `account_conversation_messages`) is the Emails panel under the intake, naming each message's contact from the page's contacts |
| D-1729 | **`brokerNext` keeps `/opportunities/<id>`** with its query, as it keeps `/leads/<id>` (D-650's promise): `/opportunities` alone, a second segment, `..` and a query that leaves the site still fall back to the console. The PT412 refusal is now said in the database's words by the opportunity API (it was a failed connection). **Left alone:** "Save and start the quote" and "Start from last year's certified answers" (5.4, 5.5); an Emails column on the quotes panel and a "Send to a PEO" in the header. **For the merge with 5.1:** `opportunity_mark_won` / `opportunity_mark_lost` here close only `sent` and `quoted` quote requests (as today); 5.1's new quote statuses ('acknowledged', 'question_open') must be added to those closes, to the links revoked (this slice revokes every quote request's link but the winner's, whatever its status) and to the wrappers' read of who is open (`opportunity_withdrawal_start`) |

### 5.fix decisions

Four problems found by running the app and screenshotting it (screens 06, 09, 10, 11), fixed test-first.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1830 | **"Get a quote" is placed on the landing page at a firm's address** (D-1713, slice 5.2). `GetAQuote` takes the place of the generic "Start chatting" call to action, in the hero and in the closing section, because both lead to `/start` and a second, different way in would bypass the firm's switch for intakes. "Ask to be contacted" (8.7's section) stays: one public page, two actions. On the bare domain "Start chatting" is unchanged. Where the firm's link takes no intakes, `GetAQuote` shows nothing and the page offers only the contact form. The footer's small "Start your intake" link is left as it was. Tests: `LandingPage.contact.test.tsx` |
| D-1831 | **New quote is a dialog of width `max-w-2xl`, as tall as its content, scrolling past 90% of the window** (D-1709), like the app's other forms. The gap's cause: the content was forced to `h-[100dvh] w-screen`, and `DialogContent` is a CSS grid whose implicit rows stretch to fill a fixed height, so the free height was shared among the rows, between the title and the first field. Sizing by content removes it. The page it was opened from stays drawn behind (unchanged, `NewQuoteHost.test.tsx`). Test: `NewQuoteDialog.test.tsx` |
| D-1832 | **Mark won or lost scrolls its body, not the dialog.** The content is a column capped at 90% of the window with `overflow-hidden`; the form's fields sit in the one scrolling region and Cancel and Mark lost sit below it, always in view. Test: `CloseOpportunityDialog.test.tsx` (the buttons are outside any scrolling region, the fields inside one) |
| D-1833 | **One word for a sent quote request: "Sent"** (CRM-Q3.1's own word; its table has no "Waiting" status). `QUOTE_REQUEST_LABELS.sent` is now "Sent". The separate "Waiting N days" phrase for the longest wait (`waitingWords`) stays, as the story's "the wait is counted in days". Tests that pinned the old word were updated: `QuoteRequestsPanel.test.tsx` (two), `PeoThreadPanel.test.tsx`, `quoteRequests.test.ts`, `opportunityPage.test.ts` |

### Homepage sign in and sign up decisions

The founder: "there needs to be a login section and account sign up from the homepage". Nothing new is built: the homepage header (`Navbar`, drawn by `HomeRoute` above `LandingPage`; it is the page's only header) links to the pages that exist. Tests: `Navbar.test.tsx`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1840 | **Reuse, no new forms.** "Sign in" goes to `/login` (`AuthPage`, which also offers broker sign-in with Google and Microsoft) and "Sign up your firm" to `/firms/new` (`SignUpFirmPage`). The wording is that page's own heading and button, "Sign up your firm". |
| D-1841 | **The header is `Navbar`, not `LandingPage`.** `Navbar` is mounted only by `HomeRoute`, so the links live there, next to "How It Works" and "Start chatting", in the page's existing style. |
| D-1842 | **A person who is signed in** (a user that is not a guest) sees "Your workspace" to `/advisor` in place of "Sign in" (D-1849 says when "Sign up your firm" stays beside it). `/advisor` is where `HomeRoute` already sends a signed-in member at a firm's address and where `AuthPage` sends an advisor; `RequireFirm` there decides what a person with no firm sees (a refusal page with a Home link, or "Choose a firm"). The existing "Profile" link stays. |
| D-1843 | **A prospect's guest session is not a sign-in** (the rule `HomeRoute` and `AuthPage` already apply with `isAnonymous`): a guest still sees "Sign in" and "Sign up your firm". Gap: the header cannot tell a signed-in prospect (verified email, no firm) from a broker without a database question; such a person gets the refusal page at `/advisor` and "Profile" beside it. Not asked here. |
| D-1844 | **At a firm's address** (`useFirmAddress` not `none`, including while it is still checking) "Sign up your firm" is not shown: the visitor is at a firm, and a firm signs up on the bare domain. "Sign in" stays and is the same `/login`, which resolves on that host. "Get a quote" and "contact me" are unchanged (D-1830). |
| D-1845 | **Phone width:** the header's links are hidden below `md`, so the same links are in the hamburger menu, with the same rules (D-1842, D-1843, D-1844). |
| D-1846 | **Where each visitor lands.** Bare domain: Sign in, Sign up your firm, Start chatting. Signed in: Profile, Your workspace (or Your firm, D-1849), Sign up your firm only while they own no firm, Start chatting. A firm's address: Sign in, Start chatting (a signed-in member is redirected to `/advisor` before the page draws, D-52). |
| D-1847 | **Not done:** no change to `/login` or `/firms/new`, no new route, no "Sign up" for a prospect (a prospect starts as a guest and verifies at certification). |
| D-1848 | **The page body offers it too, bare domain only:** the landing page's footer links "Sign up your firm" to `/firms/new` (no new section; the hero stays about the prospect's intake). Not at a firm's address. Test: `LandingPage.contact.test.tsx`. Not hidden for a firm's owner (a footer link, not the main action). |
| D-1849 | **A person who owns a firm is not offered "Sign up your firm"; the link reads "Your firm"** (`useOwnedFirms`, the same answer the console already uses). Signed in and owning none (`[]`): "Your workspace" and "Sign up your firm". While ownership is unknown (`null`) a signed-in person is offered only "Your workspace". It goes to `/advisor` and not to the firm's own address because `my_owner_firms` returns the firm's id and name, not its address; `/advisor` on the bare domain opens the firm, or "Choose a firm" for several. Same in the phone menu. Test: `Navbar.test.tsx` |
| D-1850 | **Signed out at `/firms/new`:** `SignUpFirmPage` sent the person to `/login` with `{from, reason: "account_needed"}` already. `AuthPage` opens on creating an account, and `signUp`, the resent link and the emailed code all return to `/firms/new` (pinned in `AuthPage.test.tsx`, shown to fail when the code path is pointed elsewhere). Gap closed: that page said "Start your intake" and "chat with Lenore to build your packet" to a firm; for a visitor from `/firms/new` it now says "Sign up your firm" and "Create an account, then set up your firm." |
| D-1851 | **After the firm is created the done state is unchanged:** it already says the firm waits for approval by a Nevermore admin, that it can work now but cannot send to a PEO until approved, and gives its address to sign in at (`SignUpFirmPage.test.tsx`). Checked, no copy change. |
### 5.12 CRM-Q1.2 decisions

Slice 5.12 (what the intake learns goes back to the account: D-1715, extending D-196 and D-226), built test-first as branch `lane/5.12` (`TDD_LOG.md`), migration `20261123190000_intake_teaches_account.sql`. No table: the suggestions are worked out on read.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1810 | **Where "The intake says 23" is kept: nowhere. It is worked out on read** (`account_intake_says(p_firm_id, p_account_id)`) from the account's latest certification: the newest `chamber_certifications` row, not superseded, of any intake on one of the account's opportunities. No column, no table, so nothing to keep in step, close with a firm or add to the data policy. A suggestion exists exactly while the account's value differs from the certified one, so it ends when the value is used (D-1812), when someone types the same value, and when the next certification changes the value it names (the newer certification replaces the older). Two things follow and are meant: a field an agent empties or changes by hand shows the certified value again, and there is no "dismiss" (the story has none) |
| D-1811 | **Filling on every certification.** `teach_account_from_intake(intake)` runs in the same transaction, from `chain_at_certification()` (restated from `20261119110000` with one added line) right after `make_intake_chain`, which is not restated: the chain already answers the chain that is there, and a first certification's new account is filled by the chain itself, so the teaching adds nothing there. It fills the account of the intake's opportunity (an account the chain made, one an agent made for New quote, or a merged one) in the D-196 list: legal name, address, states of operation, employees, industry, current provider, and the website (D-226). "Empty" is NULL (states: the empty list). "Address" is what the intake certifies: the street and the headquarters state (city, ZIP and phone are not certified). The legal name is never empty on an account (it is required), so it is only ever suggested. One UPDATE, and only if a field changes: an account the certification adds nothing to keeps its `updated_at` and version (a test of 20261119110000, "certifying again changed nothing", caught the first version rewriting it). An archived account is left alone (D-222). No `assert_firm_writable`: certification is the employer's act (D-226); a closed firm has no opportunity, so nothing happens |
| D-1812 | **"Use it" is the edit that already exists.** The page saves the account with `update_account`, every field as it is and the one field the intake's, from the version the page holds: the level rule (D-209), the closing firm, the version conflict (answered in words, nothing saved) and record history all apply with no new write function. "Used" for the history is therefore an ordinary entry, "changed from X to Y", by the person who clicked; the filling at certification is an entry by the employer's prospect (a member-kind actor). A suggestion shown to someone who may not change the account has no Use it |
| D-1813 | **What "differs" means.** Text: trimmed and case ignored ("dental practice " is "Dental practice"). States of operation: the same set in any order. Employees: the number. The certified side is read as the chain reads it (`crm_answer_text`, valid state codes only through `crm_certified_states`, the provider only when the employer says they are in a PEO). A field the certification says nothing of is no suggestion: the intake never proposes emptying a field |
| D-1814 | **A withdrawn certification says nothing.** When the employer changes an answer the certification is superseded (existing behaviour), and until they certify again no suggestion is offered: what a certification no longer stands behind is not offered. The next certification's values replace it |
| D-1815 | **The certifier becomes a contact** by add_contact's rules, without calling it (it needs a signed-in firm member; the employer is not one). The match is by email, case ignored, against any contact of the account, and by `prospect_user_id` (D-194); no match adds one with the profile's name (else the signer's), the auth email, the profile's phone, owned by nobody as the chain's contact is. Main only when the account has no main contact: an existing main contact is never displaced. Not added: with no name, or neither an email nor a phone (the `contacts_reachable` check; a test failed on that), and to an archived account (D-222, "nothing can be added"). A certification never fails for any of this (ADR 0025) |
| D-1816 | **ADR 0006 holds by construction and by test.** `account_intake_says` builds its answer from eight fixed keys (the account's own fields) and the filling writes only those; a test certifies an intake with a FEIN, a census and a named employee and searches the account, its contacts and the answer for them, and `chain_at_certification.sql`'s check stays green |
| D-1817 | **Who is told.** The same rule as the account's page (`can_see_firm_record`): NULL, the same as for an account that is not there, for anyone else, the employer's prospect included and another firm's member; the API function is for `authenticated` only |
| D-1818 | **Registries.** `account_intake_says` is `firm_scoped` (and `own` in the callable list, with a probe); `teach_account_from_intake` and `crm_certified_states` are `service_only`; the restated trigger function keeps its `trigger` class. No table, so no `firm_closing_plan` line and no data class. It is a read, so it is in `help_from_nevermore.sql`'s reads, not the guarded writes |
| D-1819 | **Reused, and gaps.** Reused: the chain's trigger and its certification reads (`crm_answer_text`, the latest certification), `crm_split_person_name`, the contacts' unique and reachable rules, `update_account` and record history for Use it, `can_see_firm_record`, the account page's Details panel with its rows, `updateAccount`/`formOf`/`editOf` and the form's version rule, the account queries' keys (a save asks the suggestions again). Existing test changed by this slice: `chain_at_certification.sql`'s agent-made-account case said the certifier is no contact; it now says the certifier is the main contact. Gaps: the suggestion's "Use it" sends the value the page read (a certification made in the next seconds is not seen until the page asks again); the Overview shows suggestions in the Details panel only (not the accounts table or the opportunity page) |
### 5.13 CRM-P2.5 decisions

Slice 5.13 (CRM-P2.5, P-2.3: the employer ends a firm's access to their intake), migration `20261123200000_employer_ends_access.sql`, test `supabase/tests/employer_ends_access.sql`, decisions D-1820..D-1829. They carry out D-1714 and D-1703. Built from 43af73be, test-first (`TDD_LOG.md`, `docs/tdd-logs/5.13.md`).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1820 | **Where an intake goes, and how "every submission sits on an opportunity" (D-192, D-219, D-231) still holds.** The intake moves to the default firm, Nevermore's own, which is "no outside firm" (D-1742; P-2.1: "an intake started without a firm link belongs to no outside firm"). The rule is not exempted and not weakened. In one transaction `employer_end_firm_access` (1) lets the firm's opportunity go (`intake_id` null, which D-219's firm key needs before the intake can change firm; the opportunity goes back to Qualifying by `opportunity_stage` as it is, D-408, and keeps its account, contacts, notes, owner, value and any won or lost mark), (2) moves the intake's `firm_id` under a one-update bypass in `keep_intake_firm` (restated from 20261123110000 with every line kept: the setting `nevermore.ending_firm_access` names the intake, is set local to the function, and only this function sets it), and (3) when the intake is a submission (`status = 'submitted'` or `intake_ever_certified`) calls `make_intake_chain` as it stands (20261121100000), which makes the account, contact and opportunity in the intake's new firm, the default firm, as for any intake started with no firm link (D-1742). The rule's constraint trigger on the old opportunity (`opportunities_keep_intake_unlink`) is deferred for the statement and asked again before the function returns, when the intake sits on the default firm's opportunity, so the rule holds at every commit and the refusal of the rule is not weakened for anything else. A draft nobody has certified has no opportunity and gets none (the rule asks submissions only, as it never asked drafts). The default firm cannot close (D-121), so the chain it now holds is never deleted by a closing |
| D-1821 | **The employer's view.** `my_intake_firms()` (authenticated, SECURITY DEFINER) answers `(session_id, firm_name, company)` for the caller's own intakes whose firm is not the default firm: an employer cannot read a firm's name by any policy, so this is how the profile names it. The profile's `MyFirmAccess` ("Who can see my intake") sits under 6.6a's `MyPacketRecipients` in `ProfilePage` and shows "<Company> is seen by <Firm>." with "End <Firm>'s access", for every such intake, certified or not. It asks first (an alert dialog with `endFirmAccessText`, the draft notice, "Keep" and "End"), says "<Firm> can no longer see <Company>." when done, shows the database's own words when it refuses and "That didn't go through" for a dropped connection, and says "No firm can see your intake." when the list is empty and "We couldn't load..." when it could not be read |
| D-1822 | **The record.** `intake_access_ends` (firm, firm name as it was, intake, the opportunity that held it, the employer, when) is appended only (`reject_audit_change`, the table's owner included), closed to the API (no policy, no grant: the firm reads the note, D-1825), and holds no answers. It has no foreign key to the intake, so deleting the intake leaves it (it is the firm's record, like the history that "outlives the rows it names"). Firm: its own `firm_id` (the firm that lost the intake), with its probe and matrix row in `data_isolation.sql`, a `keep` line in `firm_closing_plan()` (restated from 20261122200500_main_merged.sql with every line kept), a data class in `src/data/dataPolicy.ts` |
| D-1823 | **No further send, by anyone.** `peo_authorized` and `assert_peo_authorized` (restated, each from its only definition, 20261122100000, every line kept) answer "no" and refuse with 22023, "The employer ended a firm's access to this intake. Nothing more can be sent for it.", for an intake that has a record in `intake_access_ends`. They are the one gate `send_to_peo`, `advisor_record_quote_send`, `follow_up_peo`, `send_peo_update` and `issue_bundle_link` already ask, and the bundle link reads `peo_authorized` each time it is opened, so a link is dead whether or not it was revoked too. The firm that lost the intake cannot reach it at all (its members get PT404 from the firm scope, a test holds it); the default firm that holds it now is refused with the sentence above. The intake is not re-attached by any later step: the employer invites a firm again by that firm's link (5.2), which starts a new intake (D-1745a), or by 5.3's invite; a certified intake is not moved back |
| D-1824 | **Live bundle links are revoked** with `bundle_link_revoke(quote_request_id, 'access_ended')` for each quote request of the intake. `bundle_links_revoked_check` and `bundle_link_revoke` (restated from 20261122110000 with every line kept) gain the reason `access_ended`; the quote request's change log says "the employer ended the firm's access" (`src/data/quoteRequests.ts`) |
| D-1825 | **What the firm keeps.** The account, its contacts, its notes, calls and tasks and the opportunity stay with the firm untouched (a test holds each). What was sent: the quote requests, their mail and the pipeline rows are the intake's (by intake: D-28, 20261116110000), so they go with the intake into the default firm and the firm no longer reads them. The firm keeps its own copy of what it sent as one note on the opportunity, written in the same transaction: "The employer ended <Firm>'s access to their intake on <date>. Sent to <PEO> on <date> (<status>). ... The intake and its answers are no longer shown to your firm; the account, its contacts and your notes stay." (or "Nothing had been sent to a PEO."). It is a note by no one (author cleared), so it shows in the account's and the opportunity's own timeline with no new screen |
| D-1826 | **The firm's notes on the submission** (`advisor_notes`) are appended only and by intake, so they cannot be taken out of the intake and go with it. They are copied into the same note on the opportunity, oldest first, "<date>, <author>: <text>", with the whole note capped at the note's 5000 characters. **Known gap, for counsel and the founder:** the originals, the quote requests' own notes (`quote_request_notes`) and the PEO mail threads, being the intake's, are readable by members of the default firm once the intake is theirs, and a PEO's later reply to a quote request lands in the default firm's thread, not the firm's. Only the firm's advisor notes are copied back; moving the rest would break "by intake" and was not asked |
| D-1827 | **The wording.** `endFirmAccessText(firm)` in `src/data/legalWording.ts` (what ending means: the firm no longer sees the answers, the employees' personal information or the documents; nothing more is sent to a PEO; the firm keeps its own records of the company and of what it already sent, and cannot undo it; a firm can be invited again) is draft wording with `DRAFT_WORDING_NOTICE` beside it on the dialog (D-12). The note on the opportunity is the product's own sentence to the firm, not legal wording |
| D-1828 | **A closing firm cannot stop it.** `employer_end_firm_access` is the employer's own say, so it is exempt from the closing guard and from the support guard, with that reason in both lists (`close_a_firm.sql`, `help_from_nevermore.sql`: own_or_platform, and `my_intake_firms` among the reads), as `set_peo_authorization` is (D-623). It is registered in the three registries of `data_isolation.sql` (the callable allowlist 'own', the privileged-function registry 'own', and `intake_access_ended` as 'service_only'). Refused (22023, "No firm sees this intake, so there is nothing to end.") when the intake is already the default firm's, which also makes ending twice a refusal and not a second record |
| D-1829 | **Reuse and gaps (D-1763).** Reused: 6.6a's `set_peo_authorization` shape (own-employer refusal, locks, 42501), its `peo_authorized` gate and the bundle link's read of it, 6.6b's `bundle_link_revoke`, `make_intake_chain` and the rule's own triggers, `keep_intake_firm`'s role test, `reject_audit_change`, `MyPacketRecipients`'s page and place, `useSaveFailure`/`SaveFailed`/`failureWords`, `AlertDialog`, `DRAFT_WORDING_NOTICE`, `peoAuthorizationsApi`. Functions restated from their newest definition, every line kept: `keep_intake_firm` (20261123110000_firm_link), `peo_authorized` and `assert_peo_authorized` (20261122100000_peo_authorizations), `bundle_link_revoke` (20261122110000_bundle_links), `firm_closing_plan` (20261122200500_main_merged). Not built: an e2e flow of the profile (the page is tested as a component, the database as SQL); a firm-side screen beyond the note (the opportunity's intake panel shows "no intake" as it does for any opportunity without one); re-attaching an intake to a firm; the broker-started intake (slice 5.3, untouched) |
### 5.3 P-2.2, P-2.4 decisions

Slice 5.3 (P-2.2 a broker starts an intake for a client, P-2.4 a broker talks to Lenore, with the invite and Remind), migration `20261123120000`, decisions D-1750..D-1759. They carry out D-1703, D-1710, D-1711 and D-1712 on ADR 0025. The broker's own screens for entering answers, inviting and Remind are New quote steps 1 to 3 (slices 5.4, 5.5, 5.7, D-402): this slice makes the existing chamber work for a broker (D-1751), builds the server functions and the API helpers (`src/lib/brokerIntake.ts`) those screens will call, and on the employer's side the page the link opens (`/intake-invite`) and "Your broker entered this".

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1750 | **A broker-started intake has no employer user, and the opportunity holds it from the start.** `chamber_sessions.user_id` may now be empty and `started_by` names the member who started it (set only by the database: the browser's roles can neither set nor change it). `start_broker_intake(opportunity)` makes the intake in the opportunity's firm and links it (`opportunities.intake_id`) in the same step, so the chain rule holds from the start (ADR 0025) and certifying it later makes no second account or opportunity (a test pins it). It is refused for another firm's opportunity and one the caller may not see (PT404, one answer), an archived account (55000), an opportunity that already holds an intake (PT409), a closing firm or a support session (PT423). `keep_intake_firm` is restated from 20261123110000 with one branch: an intake with `started_by` set keeps the firm its function chose. An intake with no user is read by nobody as its owner (row security compares `user_id`, which an empty value never equals) and by the firm's members as every intake of the firm |
| D-1751 | **A broker works the chamber we already have** (founder's direction for 5.3). A broker-started intake runs in the existing intake: `/start`'s `ChamberPage` (Lenore and the tabs), `useChamberSession` and its save, `chamber-extract`, `applyExtraction` and the provenance, not a new intake screen and not a parallel set of answer-writing functions. **Reused unchanged:** the chamber's tabs and fields, Lenore's chat and voice, the extraction and its merge rules, the document candidates, the save (versioned update of `chamber_data`, `field_provenance`, `transcript`), the validation triggers, `provenance` and the required items, certification, the chain, and the employer's side of the same chamber. **Changed in it:** (1) row security lets a current member of the intake's firm update an intake the firm started while it is `in_progress` (`Firm members can work an intake their firm started`); (2) a trigger, `work_broker_started_intake`, decides what that member's save may change and marks it: every answer whose value changed becomes a broker entry (D-1710) naming the member, status inferred, the message and words kept when the chamber wrote it from a message; an answer the employer typed, closed or gave Lenore, and the FEIN (42501, the whole save), are not changed; the employer (`user_id`) and the PEO choice stay as they were; a closing firm or a support session is refused (PT423); (3) `useChamberSession` opens an intake the caller does not own when it was started by a firm and `broker_may_enter_intake` says the caller may enter answers there (`brokerEntry`), and its one funnel `updateIntake` marks every change as a broker entry (`markBrokerEntries`, the same rules, so the screen shows what the database will keep); (4) `ChamberPage` takes an `intakeId` and `LiveWorkspace` a `brokerEntry` flag: the page says "Your client's intake", leaves out Start over and the employer's Certify & sign tab, and `ProvenanceField` offers no box for the FEIN; (5) the route `/client-intake/:intakeId` (the same `ChamberPage`, which reads the id from the address; behind `RequireFirm`, no consent gate: the agreement is the employer's) opens it, with no button that leads to it until New quote step 2 (slice 5.5) uses it (D-402); (6) `chamber-extract` lets such a caller talk to Lenore (it asks `broker_may_enter_intake`; anyone else is "not found" as before). There is no `broker_enter_answers`: the first build of this slice had one, and it was folded into the save above |
| D-1752 | **A broker's entry ranks lowest** (`src/data/provenance.ts`; `markBrokerEntries` is what the chamber applies to a broker's change): the employer's own word (4), a document they applied (3) and their conversation with Lenore (2) stand over it (1), and a differing broker value is only noted beside theirs; a broker's later entry replaces their earlier one rather than making a conflict. An answer the employer changes or confirms in the workspace gets their own source (the existing edit and confirm), so it is theirs. The employer's page says "Your broker entered this" for an unconfirmed broker entry (the badge replaces "Lenore captured this"), and for a confirmed one keeps that line beside "Confirmed". The firm's own view says "Entered by the firm" |
| D-1753 | **Only the employer certifies, and certifying confirms the broker's entries.** A BEFORE trigger refuses (42501) a certification, written by the browser's roles, from a current member of the intake's firm (a member of another firm certifies an intake of their own at this firm's link as any employer does; the service role and jobs are not refused). Another BEFORE trigger, sorted ahead of the certification triggers that read the provenance, makes every entry that still carries a broker's source `confirmed` with `employer_confirmed_at`, and leaves the source standing so step 3 can show what the employer changed (CRM-Q2.4); an entry the employer changed has their source and is not touched. The browser keeps `employer_confirmed_at` when it saves |
| D-1754 | **The employer's browser can't write a broker's name on an answer.** A save from the browser's roles may not give a field a broker-entry source the intake did not already hold for it (42501); the employer resends the whole provenance on every save, so the entries already held pass (only the source is compared, so the confirmation certification adds and the employer confirming do not trip it), changing an answer replaces its entry with the employer's own source and dropping one passes |
| D-1755 | **The invitation** (D-1711): a table `intake_invitations`, one row for each link, closed to the API (members read where it stands through `intake_invite_status`, never the link or its hash), kept as the SHA-256 of a token the server function makes. A link lasts 14 days and works once; at most one is waiting for an intake (a unique index; a second invitation is PT409 "Remind them instead"). **Remind** replaces the waiting link: the old row is marked `replaced` (opened later it says so, PT410, rather than "not valid"), the new row carries `invited_at`, `last_opened_at` and the count of sends over, so step 3 shows when the client was first invited and last opened a link. Inviting and Remind are refused for an intake that is closed (55000), one that already has its employer (55000, invite only), a closing firm (PT423), another firm's intake, an employer's own and one not there (PT404), and an address that could never be emailed (22023 in words, before anything is made). "The contact does it all" is the same invitation with nothing entered: nothing requires an entry first |
| D-1756 | **Mail goes out as "<Firm> via Nevermore" by Nevermore's own sender, and is not queued in `outbox_events`** (carries out D-1712 as the member invitation does): the token must travel in the email and be kept nowhere (the reason 20261116190000 gives for sending a member invitation from its own function), and a row of the submission outbox is tied to an intake and keeps its whole payload. So the new `invite-client` server function copies `invite-member`: it makes the token, hands the database only its hash, emails the link at the firm's address (`https://<firm address>.<domain>/intake-invite#token=...`, the secret after the #, D-34) and records how the send went (`sent`, `failed`, `unknown`, retried once with the same key after an unclear answer) as the service role against that link, dropping a result that arrives for a link since replaced. The firm's name in the display name is cleaned of brackets and quotes. No firm mailbox is needed, so a firm with none connected can still start quotes. The outbox's kinds are untouched |
| D-1757 | **Opening the link signs the person up or in and the intake becomes theirs** (D-1703, D-1711). The page `/intake-invite` (at the firm's address, outside sign-in) first asks `peek_intake_invite(hash, address)`, which a signed-out visitor may call and which answers the firm's name, who it is for and when it ends, never the address, and records `last_opened_at`; it is registered in the three registries beside `firm_takes_intakes` (D-1747) as the one other thing a firm's address says before sign-in. The signed-in person then presses "Open your intake", which calls `accept_intake_invite(hash, address)`: bound to the firm's own address (PT422 first, so a link at another address learns nothing), refusing in turn a link that is not valid (PT404), replaced, withdrawn, used or expired (PT410), a person not signed in with a verified email (42501), another email than the one the link was sent to (PT403, as a member invitation does), a **member of the firm (PT403: the firm can't take the employer's place)**, and a person who already has an intake in progress (PT409: `refuse_second_open_draft` guards inserts, so the same lock and question are asked here; the link stays waiting). It sets `user_id`; the employer then opens `/start`, behind the intake agreement as any employer is |
| D-1758 | **What a broker says to Lenore** (P-2.4) is the existing chat: `chamber-extract` answers a member of the firm in an intake the firm started (D-1751), the answer goes through `updateIntake` like any extraction, and `markBrokerEntries` makes it a broker entry with the message and the words it came from, never the employer's confirmed answer. Lenore's prompt is unchanged: she still speaks to "the prospect" |
| D-1759 | **Known gaps and registries.** (a) The broker's own uploads (the census and documents, P-2.2, K-2.4) are not marked as the broker's: document intake runs through `document-upload` and the intake's own `documents` rows, which this slice does not touch; New quote step 2 (5.5) must add it. (b) A person with an intake in progress can't accept a link until they finish or archive it (D-1745 (a) is the same gap for the firm link). (c) Concurrent edits: the employer's tab and the firm both writing the same intake rely on the existing `data_version` of the browser's saves; the broker's save locks the row and merges field by field. (d) Registries: `intake_invitations` in the isolation matrix, its probe and the two-firm probes of the five member functions (`supabase/tests/data_isolation.sql`), `firm_closing_plan()` restated from 20261122200500 with one line (the invitations are deleted with the firm; the intake stays with its employer), `help_from_nevermore.sql`'s lists (reads: `intake_invite_status`; writes: the four member functions; the caller's own: `accept_intake_invite`), `close_a_firm.sql`'s guarded list (the four writes; `accept_intake_invite` and `peek_intake_invite` exempt, with the reason: the employer's own act and a visitor opening a link), `src/data/dataPolicy.ts` (`intake_invitations`), `src/integrations/supabase/types.ts`, `/intake-invite` in `src/App.tsx`, `src/data/seo.ts` and `vercel.json`, and GLOSSARY.md (Broker entry, Broker-started intake, Intake invitation) |
| D-1759a | **A member's save is refused, not marked, when it writes anything but the chamber's own columns** (slice 5.3b, migration `20261123120100`; closes the hole D-1759 left).
