The trigger `work_broker_started_intake` (D-1751) normalised only `chamber_data`, `field_provenance` and `transcript` (and kept `user_id` and `chosen_peo`), so a current member of the firm could still write, through a crafted direct update under the firm's row-security rule, the intake's `extra_answers` and every other column that the policy's `WITH CHECK` does not look at: the submission, the expiry, `firm_id`, `started_by`, the File ID, the referral code and campaign tags, the schema version, the consent link and `data_version` (a probe showed each of them stuck). **Decision: refused (42501, the whole save, as the FEIN is), not marked as a broker entry.** Marking does not fit: only `chamber_data` answers carry a provenance map the readers, the required items and the certification's confirmation (D-1753) know how to treat as a broker's, and an extra answer's own `source` is read by none of them, so a marked extra answer would stay unconfirmed after the employer certifies; and the answers to a PEO's questions follow the PEO choice, which a member can't make (D-1751 (2)). The chamber's own save is unaffected (it sends the extra answers as they stand, so nothing differs). The check compares the whole row but the five columns the save sends (`chamber_data`, `field_provenance`, `transcript`, and `user_id` and `chosen_peo`, which stay as they were, as before), so a column added later is guarded without being named. The function is restated from 20261123120000 with every line kept and the one check added after the FEIN's. `status` is also refused by the policy's `WITH CHECK`; its test is a second lock on the same door |
### 5.8 CRM-Q2.5, CRM-Q2.6 decisions

Slice 5.8 (CRM-Q2.5, New quote step 4, with CRM-Q2.6, step 5, the send), decisions D-1780..D-1789, built test-first in `lane/5.8` (`TDD_LOG.md`). No migration, no SQL. Founder's rule (D-1763): reuse what exists, add only what is missing, as small additions to the existing components.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1780 | **Reused: step 4 is not a screen.** New quote on an opportunity whose intake is certified (`stepToOpenAt(progressOf(page)) === 4`, D-1702) draws the opportunity page's own `ChoosePeos` (D-1727) inside the overlay, opened at once (new prop `startChosen`), reading the submission through `useStillNeeds` as the page does (D-1726). "Save and close" stays. No steps bar, no second picker. An intake with none or not certified draws nothing of it (steps 2 and 3 are the intake, 5.5 and 5.7) and reads no submission |
| D-1781 | **Reused as it was (CRM-Q2.5 already met by 6.7 and neighbours):** each PEO's readiness and what it still needs to quote (`peoCompleteness`, "Not ready: ..."); the PEO's own fit checks (`peoFit`, 4.x's EligibilityChecks sentences); a PEO that fails a check is unticked but can be ticked, the warning ("A check that isn't met is a warning...") shown in the review and the send never stopped by it (D-1603); the employer's authorizations (`peo_authorization_states`, 6.6a) which stop an unauthorized PEO in the review and in the send ("Won't be sent"); the "Ask the employer to add <PEO>" wording and the information request (`advisor_request_information`, D-608/D-628); Q-2.2's registered-elsewhere warning; the mailbox line, connect and reconnect (`MailboxForWriting`, `useFirstSend`, 6.1) |
| D-1782 | **Gap built: fit in the choosing step.** Each PEO shows its outcome (Meets, Can't tell yet, Doesn't meet, `fitOutcome`) with the first check that says so, as that check's own sentence ("TriNet takes groups of 20 or more employees; this employer has 12."). Before, only a failed check's label showed in "Not ready", and "can't tell yet" and "fits" showed nothing. A PEO whose every check is met shows "Meets" with no reason. No change to who is ticked or ready (D-1603 stands: "can't tell yet" is not a failure) |
| D-1783 | **Gap built: the employer's say and the firm's contact in the choosing step.** Each PEO shows "May receive it", "Not authorized" or "Withdrawn by the employer" (`STATE_WORDS`, 6.6a's own words, now with `askToAddPeo` in `src/data/peoAuthorizationWords.ts` so the panel and the choosing step share them) and "Contact: <names (role)>" from the contacts the component already reads for the review (D-1602). Nothing new is read |
| D-1784 | **Gap built: an unauthorized PEO can't be ticked.** Once the employer's say is read, a PEO not authorized (not asked or withdrawn) has a disabled box and starts unticked even if ready. Unread (the read failed), nothing is blocked in the browser and the database still refuses (D-1604). The review's and the send's "Won't be sent" stops stay as the guard for a race and for a PEO ticked before the read landed |
| D-1785 | **Gap built: "Ask <employer>" from step 4.** An unauthorized PEO offers "Ask <employer> to add <PEO>", which sends the information request on this intake (`requestInformation` with PacketRecipientsPanel's `askToAddPeo` words, no fields) without leaving the overlay; it then reads "Asked <employer> to add <PEO>." and the ask is not offered twice; a refusal is shown in the database's words with the ask still on offer (`failureWords`). The employer is the certifier's first name ("Lucia"), "the employer" when the certification names nobody. The submission page's panel still opens Ask the prospect with the draft; this is the same words sent at once |
| D-1786 | **Gap built: no PEO authorized.** With the employer's say read and every listed PEO unauthorized, "<employer> hasn't authorized a PEO yet." with "Ask <employer>" (one request naming the listed PEOs, `askToAddAnyPeo`: "tick one or more of ..."), and the choosing step's Continue, which in the existing component is **Review**, is disabled. CRM-Q2.5's "Continue" is Review, and CRM-Q2.6's step 5 is the existing review and send (D-1605), so there is no second Continue |
| D-1787 | **Gap built: no PEOs listed.** An empty list of PEOs with a profile reads "No PEOs are listed yet. Ask Nevermore to list the PEOs you quote with." and nothing else (no Review, no blame on the employer). Who to ask is Nevermore: the PEO directory is a platform's, edited by an admin (the PEOs page, `/advisor/peos`). A catalog with PEOs but none with a profile reads the same, as no PEO can be offered (D-1603) |
| D-1788 | **CRM-Q2.6 met by reuse, one gap built.** One email per PEO shown before sending, from the agent's own address to that PEO's contact, the subject and note editable, that PEO's files (D-1602, D-1605); a PEO that still needs something can be sent to and the message names what is missing (D-1603); a failure of one send leaves the others (D-1600); sending makes a quote request per PEO at sent, writes the registration record (6.10) and the opportunity's stage follows from the quote requests (`opportunity_stage_of`, ADR 0026), so In market is derived, not written here. **Gap built: "Try again"**: a PEO whose send itself failed shows "Try again with <PEO>" on its result line, which sends that PEO alone (through the same mailbox check) and replaces its line; a PEO stopped before the send (not authorized, no contact) has none, as trying again would be refused the same way. The overlay reads the opportunity again after a send (`onSent`) |
| D-1789 | **Left alone, on purpose.** "Send from my own mail" with the quote's reply address in CC stays on each PEO's own card, not in the several-PEOs flow (D-1604: each PEO has its own reply address); the disconnected-mailbox state is the existing Connect or Reconnect line (6.1), not a new "Your email is no longer connected" screen; a quote opened at step 4 with some quotes already sent still opens the choosing (D-1702's function names step 4 for any certified intake, so a later PEO can be added); step 3's certified view and the intake itself (5.5, 5.7) are other lanes' and untouched. **Note on the tests:** the older "stops a PEO the employer hasn't authorized" test still passes only because it clicks before the employer's say is read; the browser's new block (D-1784) is tested on its own |
### 5.9 CRM-Q3.2 decisions

Slice 5.9, CRM-Q3.2 (a quote's page, screen 11), built test-first in `lane/5.9` (`TDD_LOG.md`). No migration, no function restated, no SQL touched: the page is a composition of reads and pieces that exist (D-1763).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1790 | **The page's read is the reads that exist** (`getQuotePage`, `src/lib/quotePageApi.ts`; D-185: a quote is a quote request, no quotes table). The quote request's own row (`quote_requests`, by its number, which row security limits to the firm) says which submission it is on; `getOpportunityOfSubmission` gives that submission's opportunity (none for an account the person's level does not reach, D-421); `getOpportunityPage` gives the account, the opportunity and whether the person may change it; `loadSubmission(id, false)` gives the threads, notes, events, addresses and the sent version. The submission is read, never opened: looking at a quote is not opening the intake, so no opening is written to the firm's record (D-1726's rule). An address that is not a whole number from 1, a quote request that cannot be read, a submission on no visible opportunity, and a request missing from its submission are all "This quote isn't available" (D-180), never a failure; only a read that fails is "We couldn't load this quote" with Try again |
| D-1791 | **What is reused, and what is new.** Reused as they are: `PeoThreadPanel` (the thread, its new-mail mark, the attachment rule: "Open" only for a file that passed its scan, a blocked one leaves the message readable, 6.3/6.4), `ReplyAddress` and `OpenStatusButtons` (exported from `QuoteRequestsPanel`), `FollowUpOrWithdraw` (6.9), `SendToPeo` and `RecordSendPrompt` (6.6, M-2.5), `useStillNeeds` (the opportunity panel's "still needs", D-424/D-1726), `useOpportunityOutcomeQuery` and `quoteCloseWords` (why a quote closed), `quoteStatusWords`, `olderVersionNote`, `adminFeeWords`, the states pieces (`RecordLoading`, `NotAvailable`, `LoadFailed`). New: the page, `QuoteHeader`, `QuoteDetails`, `QuoteFigures`, `WhatWasSent`, `CloseQuote`, `ClosedBanner`, `QuoteSendActions` (all under `src/components/quotes`), `quotePage.ts` (the words), `quotePageApi.ts` |
| D-1792 | **Close is the existing close.** `updateQuoteRequest(id, {status: "closed", note})` (`advisor_update_quote_request`, the call the submission page's answer form makes), with the agent's optional reason as the note. It is offered on a quote request still open (sent, acknowledged, question open), because the database clears a quote's figures when a request is closed, so a quoted one is closed by Withdraw (6.9) or left; never on a closed or declined one. Not offered in a Nevermore support session (ADR 0024), on an archived account's opportunity (D-432) or to a person the level rule does not let change the account's records (D-209); the database asks the same. A failed close says why in words and keeps the form and what was typed |
| D-1793 | **The reply is Follow up, from the agent's own address.** The story's "place to reply from the agent's address" is 6.9's Follow up or Withdraw on the PEO's own thread (through the agent's connected mailbox, the thread continued by In-Reply-To), with the reply address to copy where a send was made from the agent's own mail. No second composer is written. It is offered only where the submission's PEO card offers it (the product has written to the PEO, the quote request is open or quoted, the submission is certified and the PEO has a profile), built from `useStillNeeds`' certified version, catalog and submission. Connecting a mailbox from it comes back to the quote (D-1796) |
| D-1794 | **Send again exists, so it is offered.** A closed quote request is sent to again through 6.6's `SendToPeo` (labelled "Send again": a new optional `label` prop), whose send reopens the quote request (D-1622); with no mailbox its send-from-your-own-mail path is `RecordSendPrompt`, as on the PEO card, so nothing offered is dead. Offered only for a certified submission and a PEO with a profile |
| D-1795 | **The states.** Loading: the header and each panel's heading with grey lines, one polite status. No reply yet: "No reply yet · waiting N days" under the thread while the request is open (sent, acknowledged, question open) and no email has come back (`direction received`). No quote recorded: each line of the Quote panel reads "Not yet". Sent outside the product: a send date and no message the product sent (`direction sent`) says "Sent outside the product: the send was recorded by hand" with the reply address to copy. Closed: a banner with why (the withdrawal's reason, D-1624; else the opportunity's close reason, "The employer went elsewhere" or "The search ended"; and the note the closing carried). The PEO contact is the addresses of the newest sent email, else who last wrote from the PEO, else "Not known". What was sent lists each email the product sent (when, to whom, subject, "With a link to the packet", the update's earlier version) and the version and day, with M-2.3's "Sent v3, current v4"; a link's token is kept only as a hash (D-1621), so no link is shown |
| D-1796 | **Where the quote request lists lead here.** The opportunity's quotes panel (main's `QuotesPanel`, which opened the submission's page, D-424), the account's Quotes tab, the submission's quote requests ("Open <PEO>'s quote page" on each) and the menu search's quote rows open `/quotes/<number>`; the menu marks Quotes as the current section there; `brokerNext` keeps `/quotes/<number>` so a mailbox connected from the page returns to it; the route is in `App.tsx`, `seo.ts` ("Quote", private) and `vercel.json`'s noindex list |
| D-1797 | **Left alone.** Record the quote and Record a decline (slice 7.8, D-1706, D-402: a test pins that no such button is on any status). The inbox's PEO rows still open the submission: the inbox read carries no quote request number, which is a SQL change this slice does not make. New quote and the chamber (other lanes). A per-email bundle link version (the request's sent version is shown instead) |
| D-1798 | **Changes to shared pieces, all optional props or exports.** `PeoThreadPanel`: `ofRequest` (one quote request's thread open at once, marked read, no chooser, headed "Emails") and `footer`; `SendToPeo`: `label`; `QuoteRequestsPanel`: `ReplyAddress` and `OpenStatusButtons` exported and the quote-page link per request (its tests now render inside a router) |
### 5.6 CRM-Q2.3 decisions

Slice 5.6 (CRM-Q2.3, what the employer sees when a broker invited them to finish an intake), migration `20261124110000_employer_sees_firm.sql`, test `supabase/tests/employer_sees_firm.sql`, decisions D-1880..D-1889. Built from e5074598, test-first (`TDD_LOG.md`, `docs/tdd-logs/5.6.md`). Almost all of the story was already built by 5.2, 5.3 and 6.6a (D-1763); this slice pins that and builds the one gap, the person who invited the employer.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1880 | **Reuse and gaps (D-1763), by acceptance criterion.** (1) *Firm's name, who invited them, and that the firm can see their answers.* Reused: 5.2's "You were invited by <Firm>" and "Who will see your answers" (`IntakeConsentGate`, `firmSeesAnswersText`, `FIRM_SEES_ANSWERS_HEADING`), 5.3's `/intake-invite` page (`IntakeInvitePage`, `peek_intake_invite`), 5.13's `my_intake_firms` and `listMyIntakeFirms`, `DRAFT_WORDING_NOTICE`. **Gap built:** the person. Neither the invitation page nor the intake said which member of the firm invited the employer, and the invitation page said nothing of the firm seeing the answers. (2) *"Your broker entered this", can be changed.* Reused and already pinned: `ProvenanceField`, `statusBadges`, the broker-entry tests in `ProvenanceField.test.tsx` (the badge, the employer's change making it theirs, confirmed after certifying) and `broker_intake.sql` 40..42. Nothing built. (3) *Certify & sign, the signature and the PEO ticks, in counsel's draft wording.* Reused and already pinned: `ReckoningTab` (the signature, the certification's consent wording with its own `CONSENT_DRAFT_LABEL` from `src/data/certification.ts`), `PacketRecipients` (the boxes, `peoAuthorizationText`, `DRAFT_WORDING_NOTICE`), D-1708 (the boxes stay just after certifying). Nothing built, and the certification's consent wording is not moved into `legalWording.ts` (it has its own versioned draft marking, `isDraftConsent`, tested; moving it would rebuild what works). (4) *Only the employer can certify.* Reused and already pinned: D-1753's trigger and `broker_intake.sql` 40 (a member of the firm, even one the intake is "theirs" on paper for, gets 42501 and the intake stays in progress). Nothing built |
| D-1881 | **The database says who invited them.** An employer can read neither a firm nor its invitations (`intake_invitations` is closed to the API), so two functions gain one column, `invited_by_name`: the member who first invited them (the earliest link made for the intake: Remind's later links name whoever reminded, a test holds it), by `person_display_name` (D-42: the name in their sign-up, else their email, else "A member"). `my_intake_firms()` (the employer's own intakes, 5.13) also answers `invited_at` (when the client was first invited); it answers `invited_by_name` null, and no claim of an invitation, for an intake started at the firm's link and never invited. `peek_intake_invite` (what the link shows before sign-in) answers the name too; it still answers nothing but the firm's name, who it is for, when it ends and now who sent it, never the address the link was sent to. A member with no name in their sign-up shows as their email address to the person who holds the link and was sent it by that member; this is as every other screen names a member (D-42) |
| D-1882 | **Where the employer reads it.** (a) The invitation page: "You were invited by <Firm>" stays, the line under it says "<Member> of <Firm> started this PEO intake for <Contact>" and a section "Who will see your answers" with 5.2's `firmSeesAnswersText` and `DRAFT_WORDING_NOTICE` (counsel's wording, draft, D-12; no wording of mine). (b) The employer's own intake (`/start`, `ChamberPage`): `FirmSeesBanner` above the chamber says "You were invited by <Member> of <Firm>." (or "This intake is with <Firm>." when nobody invited them, as at a firm's link), the same `firmSeesAnswersText` and the draft notice. It reads `my_intake_firms` through `listMyIntakeFirms` and shows nothing for an intake no outside firm sees (the employer's own or the default firm's, D-1742), for another of their intakes' firm, and when the read fails (the notice is information; the intake still opens, as the profile's `MyFirmAccess` fails on its own). The broker working the client's intake (`brokerEntry`) is not shown it. It closes D-1745 (b) for an invited employer: the first-screen line is shown only while an agreement is needed, so an employer who had already agreed at another firm saw neither the invitation nor the line; the banner is shown on every visit to the intake |
| D-1883 | **Functions restated, each from its only definition, every line kept.** `my_intake_firms` (20261123200000_employer_ends_access.sql) and `peek_intake_invite` (20261123120000_broker_intake.sql). A function's result columns can't change in place, so each is dropped and made again with the same grants (`my_intake_firms`: authenticated; `peek_intake_invite`: anon and authenticated). No caller is lost: 5.13's profile and 5.3's page read named columns, and `listMyIntakeFirms`' schema takes the new ones as optional |
| D-1884 | **Registries.** No new function and no new table, so no new row in the allowlist, the privileged-function registry, `help_from_nevermore.sql`, `close_a_firm.sql`'s lists or `firm_closing_plan()`. One line: `my_intake_firms()` now reads `intake_invitations`, so it is in `data_isolation.sql`'s `secret_readers` (it returns no hash; the sweep calls it as every caller). `src/integrations/supabase/types.ts` has the two return types. Every whole-suite SQL test loads the new file after 20261124090000 (`scripts/sqlMigrationOrder.ts` holds the order) |
| D-1885 | **Known gaps.** (a) No e2e flow of the employer's page: the page is tested as a component, the database as SQL (the banner is a stand-in in `ChamberPage.test.tsx`, its own test reads the database's answer through the API helper). (b) The inviter's name is the member's name as it is now, not as it was when they invited: a member who renames themselves is named by the new name. (c) A member who has since left the firm is still named as the inviter (it was true when they invited, and the employer would otherwise be told nobody invited them). |
### 5.10 CRM-Q3.4 decisions

Slice 5.10 (CRM-Q3.4, add a quote to an opportunity), decisions D-1890..D-1895, built test-first in `lane/5.10` (`TDD_LOG.md`). No migration, no function restated, no SQL changed: the database already lets another PEO be added at every stage before Won or Lost and refuses after, and a second send continues the PEO's quote; two sections of `peo_send.sql` pin it. Founder's rule (D-1763): reuse.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1890 | **"Send to another PEO" is a door, not a screen.** A link (`SendToAnotherPeo`) beside Choose PEOs on the opportunity's quotes panel opens `/new-quote/<id>` over the page (`backgroundLocation`, D-1709), which opens at step 4 for a certified intake (D-1702, D-1780) with 5.8's `ChoosePeos` and `SendToSeveralPeos`; 6.7's send is step 5. Offered only where Choose PEOs is (a certified intake, not a Nevermore support session, not an archived account, D-432, ADR 0024), once at least one quote was sent (before that Choose PEOs is the first send), and never once the opportunity is Won or Lost. Choose PEOs on the page stays (D-1727); the old pin that no "Send to another PEO" is drawn (D-402) now reads Compare only |
| D-1891 | **The PEOs already sent to are marked** in the choosing step: "Already sent on <day>. Sending again continues its quote." under each, matched to the quote request's PEO name ignoring case (new optional `alreadySent` on `SendToSeveralPeos`, passed by `ChoosePeos`, by `OpportunitySaved` and by the opportunity page's own Choose PEOs, so both doors agree; `sentToPeos` and `alreadySentWords` in `src/data/peoSend.ts`). A quote request with no day sent is not marked. Marking changes nothing about who is ticked or ready: an already-sent PEO can be ticked, and the send continues its quote request with the new version (D-1622, `peo_send.sql` 11) |
| D-1892 | **New quote opened on a Won or Lost opportunity draws no choosing** (the address can be typed or kept): `OpportunitySaved` shows the saved summary and reads no submission. The database refuses the send there regardless (`peo_send.sql` 26) |
| D-1893 | **Pinned, not rebuilt** (`peo_send.sql` 25, 26, neither red-first, as the behaviour existed): a send to another PEO at Sent to PEOs makes its own quote request and leaves the first as it was; the same at Quoted; once the opportunity is Lost the send is refused ("Move this submission to Ready for market first. It is lost") and records nothing. `send_to_peo` is not restated (newest definition 20261122200000). Each pin was mutated against that definition: all killed |
| D-1894 | **Reused:** the quotes panel and its `action` slot, New quote and its address, 5.8's step 4, 6.7's send, `useStillNeeds`, `formatDay`. **Built:** `SendToAnotherPeo`, the `alreadySent` mark, the won/lost guard in `OpportunitySaved`. The quote's page (5.9) needed nothing: its Send again and Follow up are for a PEO already sent to (D-1794) |
| D-1895 | **Left alone:** a PEO added from the quote's page (5.9) rather than the opportunity's; an explanation line when New quote's address is opened on a closed opportunity (the summary alone shows, D-402: no dead buttons); the account's Quotes tab has no entry (it lists quotes, not an opportunity) |
### 7.1 N-1.4 decisions

Slice 7.1, N-1.4 (a decline keeps its reason), built test-first in `lane/7.1` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Epic 7 has no grilled decisions yet: where the story left a choice open the brief's recommendation was taken, and each is here for the founder to review. One migration, `20261124130000_quote_decline_reason.sql`; two functions restated (below).

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-710 | **The reasons.** Eight, in one list the database owns (`quote_decline_reasons()`, a CHECK on the column) and `DECLINE_REASONS` in `src/data/quoteRequests.ts` repeats (schemaSync compares them): `industry` (outside their appetite), `too_small`, `too_large`, `claims_history`, `states` (a state they do not cover), `missing_information`, `pricing` (will not price it competitively), `other`. The words a broker reads are `DECLINE_REASON_LABELS` |
| D-711 | **Required, with the PEO's words optional.** Moving a quote request to declined needs a reason from the list (22023, "Say why the PEO declined"); the PEO's words are plain text, up to 500 characters after trimming (22023 over it), stored trimmed and as nothing when blank. A reason or words sent with any other status are refused (22023), as a fee with a decline is, so nothing is dropped silently. The form refuses a decline with no reason before it asks the database |
| D-712 | **Kept, and replaced by the next decline.** Two columns on `quote_requests`, `decline_reason` and `decline_words`. Moving on from declined (to quoted, say) leaves both; the next decline overwrites both, the words included when the new decline gives none. A closed quote request cannot be declined (as before). Every change is in the quote request's history by the trigger that already logs each column, so no history code was written for it |
| D-713 | **Where the answer is made: `advisor_update_quote_request`, not `advisor_set_quote_request_status`.** The brief named the second; that one refuses declined (it records Sent, Acknowledged and Question open only, 20261123100000), and a decline has always been recorded by the first, from the submission page's answer form. Restated from its newest definition, 20261120090000, every line kept, with two new arguments at the end (`p_decline_reason`, `p_decline_words`, default null). The old seven-argument function is dropped, since two would make every call that names its arguments ambiguous; the grants are restated. The three registries, `help_from_nevermore.sql` and `close_a_firm.sql` already list the function by name |
| D-714 | **Where a declined quote shows its reason.** A new `DeclineReason` component (`src/components/quotes/DeclineReason.tsx`, "Declined: Too small" and the words in quotation marks), used on the submission page's quote request card and on the quote's page (5.9, above its header); the opportunity's activity line ("Insperity declined: Too small ("Not in Oregon")"), where `opportunity_activity` (restated from 20261123100000, every line kept) adds `decline_reason` and `decline_words` to a quote's declined event, read from that decline's own history rows by time so a PEO that declined twice shows each reason on its own line. Shown only while the status is declined: a quote that moved on keeps the reason in the database but does not say "Declined" |
| D-715 | **Reused, not rebuilt (D-1763).** The submission page's answer form (`AnswerForm` in `QuoteRequestsPanel`) is the one reason picker; the quote's page has no decline button of its own (slice 7.8, D-1706, and a test already pins it) and gets the reason through the page's own read, which is the whole `quote_requests` row. The change log reads the new fields in words through `describeQuoteRequestEvent`. No new screen, route or read function |
| D-716 | **A decline recorded before this slice has no reason** and none is invented: its card and line say "declined" as they did. Opening its answer form and saving asks for a reason, as any decline does |
| D-717 | **Left alone.** The quotes table's status column and the opportunity's quotes panel (they show the status word as before; the reason is one click away on the quote's page); a count of declines by reason (nothing asks for one yet); the PEO's words are not searched |

## Creating a firm end to end

Branch `lane/tenant-admin` (`TDD_LOG.md`), migration `20261124090000_firm_signup_emails.sql`, SQL test `supabase/tests/firm_signup_emails.sql`. Until now a person could sign a firm up at `/firms/new` and a platform admin could approve it at `/advisor/firms`, but nobody told the admins a firm was waiting, nobody told the founder the outcome, and the first admin needed SQL. Three gaps, closed with the outbox every other email uses; no new sending path.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1860 | **Every platform admin is emailed when a firm signs up** (outbox kind `firm_waiting`, `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md`). It is queued by the trigger `firms_queue_signup_emails` (AFTER INSERT on `firms`, only for a firm someone signed up and that is undecided), in the same transaction as the sign-up, so a sign-up that rolls back mails nobody. A trigger, not a restated `sign_up_firm`: that function has been restated four times already, and a trigger also covers any other path that signs a firm up. A firm with no signer (seeded, or made by an operator) is not announced. The payload is the firm's name, its address and the signer's email (lower case), nothing else (`parseFirmWaiting` reads those three and gives up on a payload that lacks one); the key is `firm_waiting:<firm id>`, so a firm is announced once. It is a firm-level row (a firm, no intake: `outbox_events_one_target`, restated from `20261122240000` with every kind it had) |
| D-1861 | **Who is told is asked at each send.** `outbox_admin_recipients()` (service role only; registered `service_only`) answers every account holding the `admin` role that has an email, once each, in order. `dispatch-outbox` asks it each time it builds or resends the email (`adminRecipients`), so an admin whose role was taken away stops receiving it, as D-35 holds for every alert; the first addresses are fixed on the row (`first_recipients`) like any advisor alert. With no platform admin the email waits (`no_recipients`), is retried and then dead; the firm still shows on `/advisor/firms` whenever an admin exists, and `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md` says to grant the first admin before the first firm signs up. The link is `<APP_BASE_URL>/advisor/firms` (https://nevermorepeo.com/advisor/firms in production); the email needs `EMAIL_FROM` and `APP_BASE_URL`, the advisor readiness |
| D-1862 | **The person who signed the firm up is emailed the decision** (kind `firm_decision`). The trigger `firms_queue_decision_email` (AFTER UPDATE OF `decision`, when it first becomes set and the firm has a signer) queues it in the transaction of `admin_decide_firm`, so a decision that is refused (already decided, not an admin, an admin of that firm) queues nothing. It goes to the signer, not to the firm's current owners: ownership can pass while a firm waits (D-21), and it is the signer who asked. The signer's address is read when the decision is made and kept in the payload (`recipient_email`, with the firm's name and address, `approved`, and the admin's note as the decision keeps it: trimmed, none when blank). A firm whose signer's account is gone queues none: there is nobody to tell, and the decision stands. Keyed by `firm_decision:<firm id>` |
| D-1863 | **What the decision email says.** Approved: "<firm> has been approved.", the firm's link (`https://<address>.nevermorepeo.com`) and the next step, "Sign in at your firm's address, open Invitations and invite your team", and the support address. Refused: "<firm> was not approved.", the admin's note if given ("What we noted: ..."), the support address to write to, and no link: the address is not theirs to use (D-31). Both are plain, in the voice of the other emails, and escape what a person typed (a firm name, the note). The firm's link is the address in front of `APP_BASE_URL`'s host (`firmAddressUrl`: https://nevermorepeo.com makes https://<address>.nevermorepeo.com, http://localhost:8080 makes http://<address>.localhost:8080), so the function holds no second copy of the site's domain. It needs `EMAIL_FROM`, `SUPPORT_EMAIL` and `APP_BASE_URL`, the readiness the draft reminder has (`prospectLink`); the reply-to is the support address |
| D-1864 | **Neither kind is a firm's alert, and neither is a prospect email.** `outbox_kind_is_firm_alert` is not restated: a firm that waits has no owners to tell, a refused firm's founder must still be told, and a suspended firm's decision is the platform's own business, so none is held back for a suspension or ended by a closure. `outbox_kind_reaches_prospect` is not restated either: a bounce on a founder's address does not silence their address for other mail, and an earlier bounce does not hold a decision they asked for. Both are firm-level rows and so appear on no firm's Outbox page (those list by intake), which keeps the admins' addresses from a firm's members. Both kinds are in the client's labels (`src/data/outbox.ts`), which `schemaSync.test.ts` holds to the database's list |
| D-1865 | **`--grant-admin <email>` on `scripts/supabase-setup.mjs`** mirrors `--grant-advisor` through the same statement (`roleGrantSql`; `advisorSql` and `adminSql` only name the role): it needs the access token and the project, not the secrets file or Docker; it says "Granted", "They already had it" or "No account has that email yet: sign up on the site's /login page, confirm the email, then run this again"; both options may be given in one run. Documented in `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md` ("After it passes"), with the two emails and the settings each needs |
| D-1866 | **Left alone, on purpose.** The sign-up page and the Firms page (no new copy or screen); an in-app notice; resending a dead admin email by hand (a firm-level row has no Outbox page); a digest or a reminder for a firm that waits too long; telling the other admins who decided. No new table, so no new firm column, matrix row or data class, and no function an API role can call, so nothing in the callable list, the firm probes, `help_from_nevermore.sql` or `close_a_firm.sql`; the three new functions are registered `service_only` (`outbox_admin_recipients`) and `trigger` (the two queueing functions) in `data_isolation.sql` |
### 5.11 CRM-Q4.1 decisions

Slice 5.11 (CRM-Q4.1, the quotes table, and Quotes in the menu opening it, D-1707), migration `20261123180000_quotes_table.sql`, decisions D-1800..D-1809. No new table: every quote row is worked out from the quote requests, the opportunities and the intakes that exist, so there is no data class, no row-security probe and no line in `firm_closing_plan()`.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1800 | **One database read, never stored.** `quotes_rows(firm, search)` is the table: one row for each quote request of the firm's opportunities (a PEO of an opportunity), and one for each opportunity left half done (an intake and no quote request, status `not_sent`, no PEO, no quote request id: D-1702), unless it is Won or Lost or its account is archived. A row's key is `q<quote request id>` or `o<opportunity id>`; selections, bulk changes and the export name rows by it. Who sees a row is the level rule on the account's owner (`can_see_firm_record`, asked once for each owner the firm's accounts have), the rule search and the opportunity page read |
| D-1801 | **The order is "needs me first" (D-1705), and a sort is a field.** With no sort: new PEO mail first, then a quoted quote by its soonest expiry (an expired one first of them: nothing moves by itself), then an open request by its longest wait, then by account. A sort on a field goes either way with a blank last in both directions; a status sorts by its place in the list. `quotes_list` and `quotes_view_counts` answer as `leads_list` and `leads_view_counts` do (50 a page, the filters `workspace_filter_check` reads, one firm at a time through `firm_to_act_for`). The page's field list and statuses are the database's: `src/lib/quotesApi.test.ts` compares them with the migration's `sync` blocks |
| D-1802 | **The views follow 5.1's statuses.** Waiting on a PEO is the open statuses (sent, acknowledged, question open: D-1730, D-1736, the menu's count, which 5.1 already restated and so needs no change here); Open is everything not finished (those, Not sent yet, and Quoted); Not sent yet, Quoted, Declined and Closed are one status each. "Expired" is not a status: a Quoted row past its expiry shows it under its status (D-1705) |
| D-1803 | **Bulk: change owner, close, export, and nothing else.** `bulk_quotes_change(firm, action, params, filters, ids)`: rows chosen by their keys or as "all that match" (the table's own filters and search), at most 1,000, never both. Change owner changes the opportunity's owner, once however many of its rows are chosen; Close closes each chosen quote request with one of M-2.4's four reasons (`WITHDRAWAL_REASONS`, reused) and stops its PEO's link as a withdrawal does, writing to no PEO (a withdrawal that writes to PEOs is the opportunity's own: D-1727). A status, a figure and a date are recorded on a quote's own page and never in bulk (CRM-Q4.1). The answer says how many changed, how many were already as asked, which were skipped and why (named), and how many keys were no row the caller may see. It asks `assert_firm_writable` (a closing firm and Nevermore support change nothing, ADR 0023, 0024) and the level rule on each opportunity's owner; a won or lost opportunity and an archived account are skipped. There is no preview and no undo (D-1804) |
| D-1804 | **No preview, no Undo.** Accounts and leads preview a bulk change and offer ten minutes of Undo. Closing a quote request is as a withdrawal is, whose own undo is sending to the PEO again, and changing an owner is a field the opportunity page changes back; so the dialog asks for the one choice it needs, says what happened and who was skipped, and offers nothing it cannot take back. A gap if the founder wants the same Undo: it is the leads' `bulk_leads_undo` over `quote_request` history, a later slice |
| D-1805 | **Export is the existing export.** `export_table_registry()` (restated from `20261122210000_leads_bulk_and_bin.sql`, the newest, with its four lines and a fifth: `quotes`, for any current member) and one reader, `export_source_quotes`, which reads `quotes_rows` (so a person exports what they may see, in the table's own order, filtered and searched as the table is) with the status in words; closed to the API like the others. `EXPORT_TABLES`, the words and the file name in `src/data/exportTables.ts` follow |
| D-1806 | **Registries.** `data_isolation.sql` (callable, function probe with two firms, function class), `help_from_nevermore.sql` (the reads `quotes_list`, `quotes_view_counts`; the write `bulk_quotes_change`, and support's refused attempt) and `close_a_firm.sql` (guarded list, and a closing firm's refused attempt) name each new function; `quotes` joins `workspace_table`, so saved views and a person's columns work as for the leads. `\i` lines in every whole-suite SQL test, after `20261123130000`. Restated, each from its newest definition with every line kept: `export_table_registry` (`20261122210000_leads_bulk_and_bin.sql`) and the `workspace_table` domain's check (`20261121110500_leads_table_query.sql`, with `quotes` added) |
| D-1807 | **The page is the table frame, reused (D-1763).** `src/pages/quotes/QuotesPage.tsx` and `src/components/quotes/quotesTable.tsx` are `LeadsPage` and `leadsTable` with the quotes' columns, views and words: the same search, filter row, Columns menu, saved views, selection bar, "Select all N that match", Refresh, `ExportControl`, `NothingYet`, `NothingMatches` and `LoadFailed`. Reused as they are: `useNewQuoteAction` (5.4's launcher, over the page), `WITHDRAWAL_REASONS`, `dollarsWords`, `formatDay`, `quoteStatusLabel`, `bulkChangeApi`'s refusals and selection type, `classifyApiError`. Built: the quotes' API and bulk API, the bulk dialog (the leads' dialog is tied to its previews), and the result line |
| D-1808 | **The links.** The account opens the opportunity (`/opportunities/<id>`); the PEO opens the quote's page (`/quotes/<quote request id>`, slice 5.9, merging separately: linked by its path, so until it merges the link opens the app's not-found page); a half-done row has no PEO and so no second link. Quotes in the menu opens `/quotes` (D-1707) and is current there, under `/quotes/` and under the queue; its count is the database's, which 5.1 already made the open statuses. A "Submissions" link on the page opens the old `/advisor` queue until it is retired. `/quotes` is in `src/App.tsx`, `src/data/seo.ts` (title "Quotes", private) and `vercel.json`'s noindex list; `/quotes/:id` is slice 5.9's to add to the same three |
| D-1809 | **States.** Loading: the frame's headings, views and grey rows. Nothing yet (a bare address, no rows): "Your quotes will be listed here" with New quote (5.4's launcher; not for Nevermore support). The story's "three steps of a quote" are not shown: D-1763 removed the steps bar, and steps 2 to 4 are the existing intake. Nothing matches: "No quotes match these filters" with the way back. A failed load: "We couldn't load the quotes." with Try again. |

### 5.5 CRM-Q2.2 decisions

Slice 5.5 (CRM-Q2.2, New quote step 2, the intake with Lenore; with CRM-Q2.4's first AC, the waiting line on step 3), migration `20261124100000`, decisions D-1870..D-1879, built test-first in `lane/5.5` (`TDD_LOG.md`). Founder's rule (D-1763): reuse what exists; the intake is the existing chamber, not a new screen.

**Reused, recorded against each acceptance criterion of CRM-Q2.2**

| Acceptance criterion | Where it already stands (reused) | Gap, built here |
|---|---|---|
| The step is the intake as it is built: Lenore's conversation and voice on one side, the ten tabs on the other | `ChamberPage` takes `intakeId` (5.3, D-1751): Lenore's chat and voice, the tabs, `useChamberSession`, the save | Drawn inside New quote (D-1872), with `embedded` so it fills the dialog instead of the window |
| What the account holds is written in as the agent's entry before the agent types anything | The broker-entry source, its provenance and its confirmation at certification (5.3, D-1710, D-1753) | `start_broker_intake` seeds the intake from the account (D-1870) |
| The agent can tell Lenore, paste, upload the census and documents, and type on any tab but FEIN and Certify and sign; each answer is the agent's, never the employer's confirmed answer | Telling Lenore and typing: `chamber-extract` for a member, `work_broker_started_intake` marking each changed answer as a broker entry, the FEIN refusal, the chamber leaving out the employer's own steps (5.3, D-1751, D-1752, D-1758). Pasting goes through Lenore's chat | **Not met, see D-1879:** uploads of the census and documents |
| "Invite the contact to finish and certify" emails the contact a link to the firm's address; "The contact does it all" sends the same link with nothing entered | `invite_intake_client`, `invite-client`, `inviteClient`, the page `/intake-invite` (5.3, D-1755..D-1757) | The two buttons in New quote (D-1873) |
| The step shows how many answers are in, who entered them, and what is still needed before it can be certified | `requiredItems` / `certificationBlockingItems`, the open-answer rule of `opportunity_page` (`answered`) | One line in the chamber for the firm (D-1871) |
| CRM-Q2.4, first AC: "Waiting for Lucia to certify", invited, last opened, Remind | `intake_invite_status`, `remind_intake_invite`, `remindClient` (5.3, D-1755) | The waiting line in New quote (D-1874) |

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1870 | **The account's facts are the agent's first entry.** `start_broker_intake` (restated from 20261123120000_broker_intake.sql, its only definition, every line kept) writes what the account holds into the new intake in the same step: company name, industry, employee count, states of operation, headquarters state, street, website, and the current provider with `in_peo` true when there is one. Each is a broker entry naming the member who started it, status `inferred` (D-1710): the employer's certification confirms it (D-1753) and nothing counts as theirs before. A blank field, an empty list, and an account with no provider write nothing (so the chamber never counts a blank as given). Tested in `supabase/tests/new_quote_intake.sql` |
| D-1871 | **How far it is, drawn in the chamber for the firm.** `entrySummary` (`src/data/intakeEntries.ts`, pure) counts the answers with the database's own rule (not null, not blank text, not an empty list or object; an explicit false or 0 counts), splits them into entered by the firm (a broker entry stands on the answer) and by the contact (their typing, what Lenore heard from them, a document they applied, anything with no record), and names what holds certification back (`certificationBlockingItems`' labels; the recommended census never does). `IntakeProgress` shows it as one line under the chamber's top bar when the chamber is a broker's (`brokerEntry`): "2 answers in · 1 entered by the firm · 1 by the contact · Still needed: States of operation, Pay frequency". It reads what the chamber already holds, so it follows every save with no new read. Counts, not names: who within the firm entered an answer is the member id on the entry, and the chamber has no list of names to turn it into |
| D-1872 | **Step 2 opens inside New quote.** An opportunity with no intake shows "Start the intake" (`startBrokerIntake`, 5.3) and reads the opportunity again; an opportunity whose intake is not certified draws `ChamberPage intakeId=<submission> embedded` under the saved summary. The dialog widens (`max-w-[95vw] h-[90vh]`) while the chamber is open (`onWide`) and keeps the 5.fix width (`max-w-2xl`) for every other view. `ChamberPage` gained one prop, `embedded`: its root fills its container (`h-full`) instead of the window (`h-screen`). `stepToOpenAt` is unchanged (D-1763): an intake not yet certified is step 3 there, and this view carries steps 2 and 3 together (the chamber with the waiting line above it) |
| D-1873 | **The hand-over.** Above the chamber, `IntakeInvite` offers "Invite the contact to finish and certify" (a form with the main contact's email, which is the default, sent with "Send invitation") and "The contact does it all" (the same `inviteClient` call, sent at once to the main contact's email, with nothing entered; a contact with no email opens the form instead of sending to a blank address). Nothing is offered until the invitation's state has been read, so an intake already invited is never offered again. A refusal (a bad address, a closing firm, a second invitation) is shown in the words the server gave, with the form kept; after a send the state is read again |
| D-1874 | **Step 3's waiting line (CRM-Q2.4, first AC; closes D-1771's seam).** Once an invitation exists the same place reads "Waiting for <first name> to certify" (the invitation's contact name, else its email), "Invited <time> · Last opened <time>" or "Not opened yet", and Remind (`remindClient`: a fresh link, the old one revoked, 5.3), with "Reminder sent to <first name>." after it and the state read again. An invitation whose email failed says "The email didn't go out. Remind sends it again."; one past its 14 days says "The link expired <time>. Remind sends a new one." An accepted invitation still reads as waiting: the employer has the intake and has not certified (the certified view takes over once they do) |
| D-1875 | **Registries: nothing new.** No table, no new function: `start_broker_intake` keeps its signature, so the registries in `data_isolation.sql`, `help_from_nevermore.sql` and `close_a_firm.sql` already name it (5.3, D-1759 (d)) and `firm_closing_plan()` is unchanged. The migration is loaded after `20261124090000` by every SQL test that loads the whole suite (39 files) and by `new_quote_intake.sql` |
| D-1876 | **Tests that moved with the view.** `NewQuoteDialog.test.tsx` and `NewQuoteChoosePeos.test.tsx` draw an uncertified intake, which now opens the chamber: each gained a stand-in for `ChamberPage` (as they already had for the submission page); no assertion of theirs changed |
| D-1877 | **Why the account is seeded in the database, not the browser.** The chamber's own save would also mark the seeded answers as the agent's (the trigger marks any changed answer), but it would run only when someone opened the chamber, after the agent could already have seen an empty intake, and twice if two members opened it. Done in the start, once, inside the transaction that links the intake to the opportunity |
| D-1878 | **Left alone, on purpose.** The chamber's own chrome in the embedded view (its "Nevermore /" link, Sign out and the legal links) is unchanged; "Start from last year's certified answers" (D-441) and the entry points that open New quote at step 2 from an account, an opportunity or a lead (D-1766) stay with their own slices; a name for the member behind each entry (D-1871) |
| D-1879 | **Not met: the agent's own uploads of the census and documents (CRM-Q2.2's third AC, in part).** The census and documents go through `document-upload`, which inserts the document row and the stored file as the uploader, and the row security of `documents` and of the `documents` bucket (20261021100000_session_documents.sql) lets only the intake's own user (`chamber_sessions.user_id`) write them. A broker-started intake has no user until the contact accepts, so a member's upload is refused (D-1759 (a) named this gap for this slice). Closing it is a change to those policies and to what the employer, who later owns the intake, can see of a file a member uploaded, to the data deletion's stored-file count, and to `document-upload` and `document-extract`: a slice of its own (documents on a broker-started intake), not folded into this one. What does work today: typing on any tab, telling or pasting to Lenore, and the account's own facts |

## Issue clean-up decisions (2026-11-17)

Found while checking every open issue against `main` (01f8cbb), most of which were already built, and fixing the gaps that check found: #190, #198, #237, #238, and an approval rule the stories state and nothing enforced. Decision numbers D-1900 onward, so they cannot meet another lane's block. The founder's standing instruction (take the recommended answer on each decision a scout finds, and keep going) was followed for #237; each answer is here to be reopened.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-1900 | **A firm sends nothing to a PEO until a platform admin has approved it** (P-1.3, Q-1.2, D-26, D-27: "approval gates sending, not signing in"; `20261124160000_firm_approval_gates_sending.sql`). Nothing enforced it: `firms.approved_at` was read only by the invitation limits (D-34, D-36), and `send_to_peo`, `follow_up_peo` and `withdraw_from_peo` never looked at it. The rule is a trigger on the message a send records (`direction = 'sent'`), not three restated functions: no write path can skip it, and the functions keep every rule they have (D-104). A send is recorded in the same transaction as its quote request, stage move, outbox row and registration, so the refusal (42501, in plain words) undoes all of them. **Not stopped**, because they send nothing: `advisor_record_quote_send` (a send the agent made outside the product), mail received, and a message sent by hand. A refused firm (decision `refused`, never approved) is stopped too. **Deploy note:** a firm waiting for approval, or refused, that could send before this migration can no longer. Edgar Allan Advisory, the default firm, is approved from the start |
| D-1901 | **Convert is the leads table's row action** (CRM-L1.1: "Row action: Convert"; #190; `20261124170000_leads_can_convert.sql`). D-1659 and D-1688 left it off until a lead could be converted (slice 8.6) and it was never added back. `leads_can_convert(firm, ids)` says which of a page's leads (at most 100) the caller may convert, by exactly what `convert_lead` checks before it changes anything: the level rule on change (D-809), New or Working (a Not a fit lead is reopened first, D-810), not in the bin, a firm that is not closing and no support session (ADR 0023, 0024). The table offers a Convert button on those rows only (D-24, D-402): none until the database has answered, none if it fails, none to Nevermore support, and no "Actions" column in a view where nothing can be converted. The button opens the Convert dialog over the table; after a conversion the table and the menu counts are read again and the person is told where the lead went. **Still not built:** the Label column and filter (D-1648, which needs the leads query restated: #245), and "Convert and start a quote" (#242) |
| D-1902 | **An opportunity's Activity shows its intake's events** (#237; `20261124150000_opportunity_activity_intake.sql`). The answer to #237's two questions: yes, an Intake kind; and the events are the ones the database already records, so nothing new is written: a member started the intake for the client (`chamber_sessions.started_by`), the client was invited and reminded (`intake_invitations`: the first link is Invited, each later one Reminded), and each certification, the first and every later version (`chamber_certifications`, dated by `recorded_at`, the database's clock, not the signer's browser's). They are shown under All and Changes, as the quote requests' events are, and counted by the page. A row never carries the invitation link's token, and names the invitee by name or, where none is on file, by address. A certification is by the employer's signer (`who_kind` "client": not a member, not named by their account). `opportunity_activity` is restated from `20261123100000` with every rule it had; the restatement removes no line of the original. The mockup's "from the account's details" in the started row is not drawn: the database records who started it, not from what |
| D-1903 | **A new sender is offered as a contact on the account** (CRM-E1.3, slice 6.4; #198). The database marked the sender (`new_sender`) and offered them (`offer_contact`), and `add_sender_as_contact` was tested in SQL, but no screen called it. A received message the database offers now shows "New sender" and **Add as contact** (the glossary's words) on the contact's emails and on an account's and an opportunity's Emails. The dialog takes the name from the message, shows the address it will use and does not let it be changed (the database adds the message's own From address), and says the database's refusals in its own words. After an add the list is read again, and the database offers nothing once they are a contact. An account's Emails words a new sender's message as "Received from <them> on <contact>'s emails" rather than from the contact. A lead's conversation offers nothing (D-662) |
| D-1904 | **Left alone, and what the whole-suite tests were given.** `data_isolation.sql` fails unless it loads every migration, so it loads the three of this clean-up and registers what they add: `leads_can_convert` (callable, own firm, with a two-firm probe and its class), `guard_firm_approved_to_send` (a trigger function), and `opportunity_activity` as a secret reader, since it now names `intake_invitations` (its sweep creates an invitation to plant a secret in when no test made one, and goes red if the function returns the link's token). `help_from_nevermore.sql` and `close_a_firm.sql` do not load these migrations yet; the first change that makes them must add `leads_can_convert` to the reads support may call. Left alone: (1) the Label column and filter on the leads table (D-1648, #245); (2) "Convert and start a quote" and New quote's step 2 (#242, D-1766); (3) deleting, restoring, labels and change history from a lead's own page (D-1659); (4) the `docs/prd-screens/` images, not regenerated (its README lists where they differ, #238) |

## Rules for building one story

1. **Branch.** `crm/<epic number>/<story id in lower case>` (for example `crm/01/crm-p1.2`), cut from the epic's
   branch. One pull request, into the epic's branch, titled `<story id>: <what a person can now do>`.
2. **Read first.** `GLOSSARY.md`, the ADRs for the area, the story and every story it names, and the screens it
   names in `docs/prd-screens/`.
3. **Test-driven** (the `tdd` skill): one failing test, then only the code that passes it, at the seams in D-13.
   Name the break each test catches. Mutate what you changed before you finish.
4. **The gate**, all green before the pull request is opened:
   `npm run lint && npm run typecheck:functions && npm run typecheck:app && npm test && npm run build`, then
   `PGHOST=localhost PGPORT=5432 PGUSER=postgres PGPASSWORD=postgres supabase/tests/run.sh`, and
   `npm run test:e2e` when the story touches a flow in `e2e/`.
5. **Database.** A new migration file per story, never an edit to an old one. Every new table carries its firm
   and its row security in the same migration, with its SQL test.
6. **Words.** Add each new term to `GLOSSARY.md` in the story that introduces it. Record a decision that
   reverses an earlier one as an ADR in `docs/adr/`.
7. **What can't be built as written.** Stop and say so in the pull request, naming the story line and what is
   missing. Never invent a fact about a PEO, and never present draft legal wording as counsel's.
8. **Working beside other stories.** Each story is built in its own lane. Its migration file takes the timestamp it was given, so two stories never pick the same name. Before a story merges, the epic's branch is merged into it, conflicts are settled, and the gate is run again.
9. **The pull request says:** what a person can now do; the acceptance criteria met, each with its test; any
   criterion not met and why; what was not asked for and was left alone.

### 7.2 N-2.1 decisions

Slice 7.2 (N-2.1, a quote holds the whole offer; the database and API half, the on-screen form is 7.8), migration `20261124140000_quote_offer.sql`, decisions D-720..D-729. Epic 7 has no grilled decisions: each is a recommendation for the founder to review.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-720 | **A new `quotes` table, one row for each quote received on a quote request.** A revised quote (7.3, N-2.4) is a second row; only the newest is current. The quote request stays what it was (status, dates, per-month fee, note); `quotes` adds what it could not hold. Reused: `advisor_update_quote_request` (the status and the quote request's own fields), `intake_in_my_firm`, `quote_request_in_my_firm`, `require_advisor`, `assert_firm_writable`, `is_us_state_code`. Built: the table, `record_quote`, five check functions |
| D-721 | **"Not quoted" is NULL, never 0.** A cost area a quote leaves out (the administrative fee, one-off fees, workers' comp, unemployment tax, medical) is NULL; an area that is there is a non-empty list. 0 is a real answer (a waived fee) and is accepted. The API sends an empty area as null, not `[]` (`recordQuote`). N-3.1's comparison reads "Not quoted" from NULL |
| D-722 | **`kind` is required: `estimate` or `firm_offer`.** Nothing else, no default, so the comparison never shows an estimate as a firm offer (N-3.1). The client's `QUOTE_KINDS` is compared with the table's by `schemaSync.test.ts` |
| D-723 | **The administrative fee and its basis come together**: per employee per month, or percent of payroll. Bounds: 0 to 100000 a month, 0 to 100 percent. Numeric, held both by `record_quote` and by a table check, so no writer can store a fee that cannot be compared |
| D-724 | **The other cost areas are jsonb lists, each line checked by an immutable function the table's check and `record_quote` both call.** One-off fees: a name and an amount (0 to 10000000), each name once. Workers' comp: a state, a class code and a rate per 100 dollars of payroll (0 to 1000), each state and class code once. Unemployment tax: per state, the employer's own rate or the PEO's rate with its percent of taxable wages. Medical: a plan, a tier (`employee_only`, `employee_spouse`, `employee_children`, `family`) and a monthly rate per employee. Shapes with extra keys, wrong types or repeated lines are refused (22023, never 22P02). jsonb rather than a child table for each area: a quote is read whole, and 7.7's comparison reads the lines of one quote at a time. If 7.7 needs to query across quotes by class code, a child table can be made from these lists then |
| D-725 | **One writer, `record_quote`** (SECURITY DEFINER, authenticated only). It checks, before anything about the quote is said: the firm is writable (ADR 0023, 0024), the caller is an advisor, the quote request is in the caller's firm (another firm's and not found are both PT404). Then it validates, calls `advisor_update_quote_request` with status `quoted` (which keeps the quote request's dates, per-month fee and note in step, and moves the pipeline stage, ADR 0026), and inserts the row. The fee is copied to the quote request's `admin_fee_pepm` only for the per-employee-per-month basis; a percent of payroll leaves it empty. A closed quote request takes no quote |
| D-726 | **Mark quoted keeps working and adds no row.** `advisor_update_quote_request` is not restated; a quote request marked quoted that way has no `quotes` row, which the comparison shows as quoted with nothing itemised. A later `record_quote` is the first row |
| D-727 | **Firm scope is the intake's.** Row security reads through the quote request's intake (`intake_in_my_firm(session_id)`), as `quote_requests` does; `session_id` is copied onto the row. No API role writes the table. Closing a firm keeps it (a quote request's, so the intake's and the employer's): `firm_closing_plan` restated from its newest definition (`20261123200000_employer_ends_access.sql`) with every line kept and one added. Data class `advisor_work` in `dataPolicy.ts` |
| D-728 | **Registries.** `data_isolation.sql` (matrix row, two-firm probe, fixture row, function registries), `help_from_nevermore.sql` (`record_quote` a write, refused to support), `close_a_firm.sql` (guarded write list, a closing firm). `record_quote` is in `types.ts` and `recordQuote` in `advisorApi.ts` |
| D-729 | **Not built here (7.8, 7.3):** the Record a quote form, the quote document's file (7.3) and the revision flow, which are the next rows of the table; nothing is shown on screen by this slice |

### 7.4 N-1.1 decisions

Slice 7.4 (N-1.1, suggest what a PEO's reply means: the message is data, outcomes are recorded without content), migration `20261125100000_reply_suggestions.sql`, test `supabase/tests/reply_suggestions.sql`, decisions D-740..D-749. Needs 7.0 (N-1.2, slice 5.1). Epic 7 has no grilled decisions: each is a recommendation for the founder to review.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-740 | **One suggestion for each reply, one of four words: acknowledged, question open, quoted, declined; or none.** "Nothing to change", an unreadable answer, a status the reply cannot mean and any sentence are all "no suggestion" and leave no row. Held mail, a message the firm sent, a replayed webhook (read once) and mail with no quote request are never read. Automatic replies are read (an auto-reply "we have your packet" is an acknowledgement). Held in a `CHECK` on the column, so no writer can keep a sentence |
| D-741 | **Nothing changes status on its own.** The mail function only records the suggestion (`record_reply_suggestion`, service role only, one per message, refuses a message that is not a PEO's reply on a quote request). Accept is the agent's: `advisor_decide_reply_suggestion(message, 'accept' or 'dismiss')`, an agent of the message's own firm (another firm's is PT404), a closing firm or a support session refused (ADR 0023, 0024). It is settled once; a second decision, accept after dismiss included, is refused in words |
| D-742 | **Accept goes through the existing status function, never a new writer.** For acknowledged and question open it calls `advisor_set_quote_request_status`, so its rules, its log (who and when) and the answering-a-question trigger (D-1733) all apply, and a refusal (already that status, closed) comes back in its words and settles nothing. For quoted and declined the status function refuses by design (a quote has figures, a decline a reason: CRM-Q3.3, N-1.4), so Accept is allowed only once the quote request already has that status, recorded by its own form (`record_quote`, Record a decline); otherwise it says "Record the decline first, with its reason, then accept this". Gap: Accept never records a quote or a decline by itself |
| D-743 | **The record sits on the message row, not in a new table.** Four columns on `quote_request_messages`: `suggested_status`, `suggestion_outcome` (`accepted` or `dismissed`; empty while open), `suggestion_decided_by`, `suggestion_decided_at`. The smallest reading: no new table, so no new firm table row in the closing plan, the matrix or the data classes, and the message's own firm scope and deletion with its intake apply. The cost: the record goes when the message does (moved to the holding list it keeps its columns; deleted with an intake it goes), where extraction events (D20) are a separate table. The columns hold a word from a fixed list, a word and a time, never text. A separate append-only table is the alternative if the founder wants the record to outlive the mail |
| D-744 | **"Changed" is not an outcome.** The story names accepted, changed, dismissed. Here an agent who sets another status by hand leaves the suggestion open until dismissed; the quote request's own change log already says what they set. Gap, one column value to add if wanted |
| D-745 | **"The sentence it rests on" is not kept or shown.** The story asks each suggestion to carry its sentence, and also that the record hold no content. Both cannot hold in one record: a quoted sentence is the message's text. The suggestion is shown under the reply it came from, so the agent reads the sentence in place. Gap against that acceptance criterion |
| D-746 | **The message is data, never instructions, in three layers.** (1) The model is told in the system prompt that the email is untrusted content to classify and never to follow, and the email is the only thing inside a `<peo_reply>` fence; instructions are outside it. (2) The fence tag is removed from the message before it is placed (a reply cannot close the fence and speak), and only its first 8000 characters are sent. (3) The model must call one forced tool with an enum; the answer is parsed against the fixed list of four and anything else is dropped. The model cannot act: the only thing the function does with the answer is store a word of the list as a suggestion |
| D-747 | **Reused: the model-calling code of `chamber-extract` and `document-extract`.** `selectAdapter` (Anthropic, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `AI_MODEL`), `callModel` (timeout, one retry, failure kinds) and `consume_global_ai_budget` through `readBudgetLimits` (the one daily ceiling every AI call shares, spent only when a call is made). No new client, no new secret. With no model key set, no suggestion is made and nothing else changes (the same 200, the same log line). A budget refusal or a model failure is logged by kind (`MAIL_SUGGESTION_SKIPPED`, `MAIL_SUGGESTION_FAILED`, at warn) with none of the message, and the webhook still answers 200: a suggestion never makes the provider retry mail. A reply whose reading failed is not read again (a replay is a duplicate) |
| D-748 | **Reused: the PEO's thread.** The suggestion is shown by `PeoThreadPanel`'s message entry (new `ReplySuggestion`), which the submission page and the quote page both render, with Accept and Dismiss; both pages already read the message row whole, so no new read. After a decision the panel's existing "reload" callback runs (the quote page now passes its refetch). The settled suggestion stays under the reply as one line ("Suggested: Acknowledged, accepted."). Gap: nothing on Home or the quotes table says a reply has a suggestion waiting; the message's new-mail mark does that |
| D-749 | **Registries and privacy.** The advisor function is in `data_isolation.sql` (function table, two-firm probe with a fixture reply, writes list), `help_from_nevermore.sql` (a write, refused to support) and `close_a_firm.sql` (guarded list, asks `assert_firm_writable` first); the service function is in the service-only list and its probe. The data policy's PEO correspondence class says the AI provider reads each reply and what is kept. Not built: N-1.3 (an underwriter's question becomes an information request), which builds on this |
### 7.7 N-3.1, N-3.2 decisions

Slice 7.7 (N-3.1, the broker's comparison of quotes, and N-3.2, notes on it; the read, the notes and the component with its tests; the button that opens it is 7.8), migration `20261125110000_quote_comparison.sql`, decisions D-770..D-779. Epic 7 has no grilled decisions: each is a recommendation for the founder to review.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-770 | **The comparison is for one opportunity: one column for each quote request of its intake, alphabetical (as `opportunity_page` lists them), showing the PEO's current quote, the newest `quotes` row of that quote request (D-720).** The earlier rows stay in the table and are not shown. A quote request with no `quotes` row (not yet quoted, or marked quoted by hand, D-726) is a column with its status and a null quote. Reused: `quote_requests`, `quotes`, `opportunity_page`'s visibility rule. Built: `opportunity_quote_comparison` |
| D-771 | **What a column says.** A NULL cost area reads "Not quoted" (D-721), never 0, blank or an empty list; the read keeps it JSON null and the screen draws it. A 0 is a real answer: a waived fee reads $0.00. A quote is labelled Estimate or Firm offer by `quoteKindLabel` (D-722). An expired quote reads "Expired <date>" in the Expires row (its expiry is before today); a quote with none reads "Not given"; a PEO with no quote reads "Not yet quoted". The expiry is judged on the screen against today, as the quotes panel does, so the read holds no clock. The fee reads with its basis ($40.00 per employee per month, 3.5% of payroll, D-723); the four lists read one line each (one-off fee: name and amount; workers' comp: state, class code and rate per $100 of payroll; unemployment: the employer's own rate or the PEO's rate with its percent of taxable wages; medical: plan, tier and a monthly rate) |
| D-772 | **One read, firm-scoped like `opportunity_page`:** `opportunity_quote_comparison(p_firm_id, p_opportunity_id)`, STABLE SECURITY DEFINER, answering `{can_add, columns, notes}`. Seen by whoever sees the opportunity's account (D-422); the one refusal PT404 for an opportunity that is not there, is another firm's or may not be seen. `can_add` is false for an archived account (D-432). Gap: the guard is written once more here and in `add_comparison_note` rather than shared, because restating `opportunity_page` to share a helper is out of this slice |
| D-773 | **Reuse and gaps on screen (D-1763).** Reused: the opportunity page's panel frame (`ListPanel`, `EmptyLine`), `PanelFailed` and `PlaceholderLines` for the panel's loading and failure, `formatDay`, `today`, `quoteStatusLabel`, `quoteKindLabel`, `OpportunityError`, `opportunityKey`. Not reused: the table frame in `src/components/workspace/table` (a table of records with saved views, filters and columns) and `Rows` (one record a row): the comparison is the other way round, one PEO a column and one cost area a row, so it has its own table markup, with row and column headers, scrolling sideways on a narrow screen. `QuoteComparison` draws what it is given and sends a note through `onAddNote`; `QuoteComparisonPanel` asks the read, shows loading, "That opportunity isn't available" (no Try again, nothing a retry can change), or a failure with Try again, and reads the list again after a note |
| D-774 | **Notes: a new table, `quote_comparison_notes`, and one writer, `add_comparison_note`.** Neither existing notes pattern fits: `quote_request_notes` is the record of a call or email with one PEO (a kind, a date; no internal or client mark, no cost area, and one quote request, not an opportunity's comparison) and `activities` (`log_activity`) is a note on an account, contact or lead (free text, no target, no mark). Changing either would change two merged tables and their registries, so a small table of its own. A note is about an opportunity's comparison; optionally about one PEO (a quote request of that opportunity's own intake, 22023 otherwise) and, with or without a PEO, one of five cost areas; marked `internal` or `client` (required, no default in the database; the form starts on internal); words trimmed, 1 to 2000 characters. Never edited or deleted (gap: the story asks for neither) |
| D-775 | **Who may add a note:** the rules of the account, as `log_activity` has them: anyone who may see the opportunity's account, in the firm the call acts for (`firm_to_act_for`), when the firm is writable (`assert_firm_writable`: a closing firm and a support session get PT423, ADR 0023, 0024); an archived account takes nothing (55000, D-432). No level rule beyond seeing the account. The firm and the author come from the session, never from the browser. The order of refusals: not a member (42501), closing or support (PT423), the mark, the area, the words (22023), then the opportunity (PT404), the archive (55000), then the PEO (22023) |
| D-776 | **Notes marked for the client are stored and marked, and shown nowhere to the client.** N-3.3 (the employer's comparison, needs QP-D2) shows a shared comparison with the broker's notes for the client and never an internal one; this slice only keeps the mark right. The table is closed to every API role: the comparison reads it and the writer writes it, both SECURITY DEFINER |
| D-777 | **Registries.** `quote_comparison_notes` is the firm's own working data: `firm_closing_plan` restated from `20261124140000_quote_offer.sql` with every line kept and one added, so closing a firm deletes its notes before its opportunities (ord 59); the quotes and the intake stay with the employer as before. Data class `crm_records` in `dataPolicy.ts`. `data_isolation.sql` (matrix row, two-firm probe, fixture row, callable, fprobe and fn_class rows for both functions), `help_from_nevermore.sql` (the read in the reads list, the writer in the writes and guarded lists), `close_a_firm.sql` (the guarded list, and a note in each firm's fixture whose deletion and survival it counts). Both functions are in `types.ts`. A lane that also restates `firm_closing_plan` must be merged after this one's line is added to its restatement, or its restatement drops this line (D-104) |
| D-778 | **Not built here (a recommendation each):** the annual total worked out from the certified census and payroll with the arithmetic shown, and the current costs in the first column (N-3.1's second criterion, K-4.1), which need the intake's payroll and current-cost answers read into a column: a slice of their own; a note on one exact figure rather than on a PEO and a cost area; editing or deleting a note; the quote's own history (the earlier rows of a revised quote are kept, 7.3 draws them) |
| D-779 | **Not mounted.** The "Compare quotes" button and its place in the menu are 7.8 (CRM-Q4.2), and the opportunity page's quotes panel keeps its Compare off until then (D-402), so `QuoteComparisonPanel` is on no screen and no button leads to it. 7.8 mounts it with `<QuoteComparisonPanel firmId opportunityId />` |
### 7.3 N-2.2, N-2.4 decisions

Slice 7.3 (N-2.2, keep the quote document; N-2.4, a revised quote), migration `20261125090000_quote_document.sql`, SQL test `supabase/tests/quote_document.sql`, decisions D-730..D-739, built test-first in `lane/7.3` from 10308e73 (`TDD_LOG.md`, "## 7.3"). Epic 7 has no grilled decisions: each is a recommendation for the founder to review. The founder's reuse rule (D-1763) applies: a quote document is kept the way a file that arrived by mail is kept.

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-730 | **The quote document is a mail attachment, named by the quote.** No new table, bucket or store: `quotes.document_attachment_id` (nullable, one document for each quote, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) points at a `quote_request_message_attachments` row, which already holds the name, size, scan verdict, `peo-mail` path and the signed link (`mailAttachmentUrl`, 60 seconds). `attach_quote_document(quote, attachment)` (SECURITY DEFINER, authenticated only) asks, in this order: the firm is writable (ADR 0023, 0024), the caller is an advisor, the quote is in the caller's firm (another firm's and not found are both PT404), the file sits on a message of this quote's own quote request (22023: never another PEO's file, held mail or another firm's), and the file is `stored`, which is scanned clean (22023: never blocked, pending or not kept). A document that already arrived by mail is linked, never uploaded again. Naming another file replaces the first (the earlier file stays on the thread). Reused: `require_advisor`, `assert_firm_writable`, `quote_request_in_my_firm`, the attachments table, the bucket and its read policy, `mailAttachmentUrl`, `describeAttachment`. Built: the column, the function |
| D-731 | **A quote recorded by hand can have a document uploaded, kept the same way.** `start_quote_document_upload(quote, name, type, size)` (same three checks as D-730) records a message `sent_by_hand` on the quote request's thread (provider id `upload:<uuid>`, from the agent's own address, subject "Quote document uploaded by hand") with one `pending` attachment, and answers where the file goes (`<intake>/<message>` in `peo-mail`, as mail files are). The new function `quote-document-upload` (multipart: `quote_id`, `file`; the agent's own sign-in) then does what `receive-mail` does with an attachment, with the same helpers: a scanner must be set up (503 before anything is recorded), the bytes are checked (`sniffUpload`: not empty, 20 MB, a type uploads take, in fact as in name; 415), the upload is started as the agent (the database's refusals answer 404, 403, 423 in its words), the file is scanned, a clean one is stored with the service role and settled `stored` (`finish_inbound_attachment`) and named as the quote's document (`attach_quote_document`), an infected one is settled `blocked` with its signature, its bytes dropped, alerted (`MALWARE_DETECTED source=quote-document-upload`), and named nowhere. A scanner that can't answer (502 "upload again") stores nothing and leaves the attachment pending, which the thread already shows as "being checked, can't be opened yet". Gaps: the upload shows on the PEO thread as a message "Sent by hand" (a direction of its own, `uploaded`, would need the thread panel and the shape check changed; left), and a pending upload that is never repeated stays pending (mail has the provider's retry; an upload has the agent's) |
| D-732 | **Registries.** No new table, so no matrix row, data class or closing-plan line (7.2's `quotes` line stands). The two functions are in `data_isolation.sql` (the callable list, the class registry as `firm_scoped`, a two-firm probe each), `help_from_nevermore.sql` (writes, and refused to support) and `close_a_firm.sql` (the guarded list), each seen red before its edit. `quote-document-upload` joins `scripts/supabase-setup-lib.mjs` (18 functions deployed), `docs/SUPABASE_SETUP.md` and `docs/RUNBOOK.md`. `attach_quote_document`, `start_quote_document_upload` and `quotes.document_attachment_id` are in `types.ts`; `attachQuoteDocument`, `uploadQuoteDocument` and `loadQuotes` in `advisorApi.ts`. Deleting an intake (which removes its mail attachments before its quote requests) leaves a quote, which then goes with its quote request; deleting a file alone leaves its quote with no document (SQL test 11) |
